2. Notation

Analysis on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. We use the bracket notation

\langle x\rangle=\sqrt{1+|x|^{2}}.

We introduce a smooth cut-off function
\chi
, satisfying
\chi(x)=1
if
|x|\leq 1
and
\chi(x)=0
if
|x|\geq 2
. For any
K>0
and
a\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, we denote

\chi_{{}_{a,K}}(x)=\chi\Big(\frac{x-a}{K}\Big),\quad\chi_{{}_{K}}(x)=\chi\Big(\frac{x}{K}\Big).

(2.1)

The reader should not be confused with the notation
f_{\nu,a}
below, as we always use
\chi
for the cut-off function throughout this paper. We let
\zeta^{*}=10
, introduce a fixed constant
\zeta_{*}\ll 1
and let

\quad\chi^{*}(x)=\chi_{\zeta^{*}}(x),\quad\chi_{{}_{*}}(x)=\chi_{\frac{\zeta_{*}}{\nu}}(x).

(2.2)

Symmetries and stationary states
. For
\nu>0
and
a\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and for any function
f
, we write

f_{\nu,a}(x)=\frac{1}{\nu^{2}}f\Big(\frac{x-a}{\nu}\Big),\quad f_{\nu}(x)=\frac{1}{\nu^{2}}f\Big(\frac{x}{\nu}\Big).

The associated scaling generator is

\Lambda w(x)=\nabla\cdot(xw)=2w(x)+x\cdot\nabla w(x).

(2.3)

We introduce the following notation for the two stationary states,

U_{\nu}(z)=\frac{8\nu^{2}}{(\nu^{2}+|z|^{2})^{2}},\quad U_{1,\nu}=U_{\nu}(z-a),\quad U_{2,\nu}(z)=U_{\nu}(z+a).

The Poison field they generate is

\nabla\Phi_{U_{\nu}}(z)=-\frac{4z}{\nu^{2}+|z|^{2}},\quad\nabla\Phi_{U_{1,\nu}}(z)=\nabla\Phi_{U_{\nu}}(z-a),\quad\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}(z)=\nabla\Phi_{U_{\nu}}(z+a).

To shorten the writing, we introduce

U_{12,\nu}(z)=U_{1,\nu}(z)U_{2,\nu}(z),\quad U_{1+2,\nu}(z)=U_{1,\nu}(z)+U_{2,\nu}(z).

(2.4)

The asymptotic position of the stationary states is

a_{\infty}=(2,0).

Self-similar variables
. Given
T>0
the parabolic self-similar variables are

z=\frac{x}{\sqrt{T-t}}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\tau=-\log(T-t).

(2.5)

The corresponding renormalization of the unknown is

u(x,t)=\frac{1}{T-t}w(z,\tau)\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\nabla_{x}\Phi_{u}(x,t)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{T-t}}\nabla_{z}\Phi_{w}(z,\tau).

(2.6)

Then
u
solves the Keller-Segel system (
1.1
) if and only if
w
solves the self-similar equation

w_{\tau}=\nabla\cdot(\nabla w-w\nabla\Phi_{w})-\beta\Lambda w,\qquad\beta=\frac{1}{2},

(2.7)

Linearized operators.
Linearizing
w
around
U_{1,\nu}+U_{2,\nu}
leads to the linearized operator in parabolic variables

\mathscr{L}^{z}\varepsilon=\Delta\varepsilon-\nabla\varepsilon\cdot\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}-\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}\cdot\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon}+2U_{1+2,\nu}\varepsilon-\beta\Lambda\varepsilon,\quad\beta=\frac{1}{2},

(2.8)

The linearized operator around one bubble
U(y)
is denoted by
\mathscr{L}_{0}
,

\mathscr{L}_{0}u=\nabla\cdot\big(\nabla u-u\nabla\Phi_{U}-U\nabla\Phi_{u}\big)=\nabla\cdot\big(U\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}u\big),

(2.9)

where

\mathscr{M}_{0}u=\frac{u}{U}-\Phi_{u}.

(2.10)

The linearized operator with truncated Poisson field is

\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}\varepsilon=\Delta\varepsilon-\nabla\varepsilon\cdot\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}-\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}\cdot\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}\varepsilon}+2U_{1+2,\nu}\varepsilon-\beta\Lambda\varepsilon.

(2.11)

Inequalities
. We write
a\lesssim b
if there exists
C>0
such that
a\leq Cb
uniformly in the parameters’ range at stake, and
a\sim b
if simultaneously
a\lesssim b
and
b\lesssim a
.

Weights
. We denote by
L^{2}_{g}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
the weighted
L^{2}
space with general weight function
g
, equipped with the scalar product

\langle u,v\rangle_{g}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}uv\;gdz,\quad\|u\|_{g}=\sqrt{\langle u,u\rangle_{g}}.

When
g\equiv 1
, we simply write

\langle u,v\rangle\equiv\langle u,v\rangle_{1}\quad\quad\|u\|\equiv\|u\|_{1}.

We use the weight functions

\displaystyle\gamma_{\nu}(z)=\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1+2,\nu}(z)},

(2.12)

\displaystyle\omega_{\nu}(z)=\frac{\nu^{4}}{U_{12,\nu}(z)}e^{-\beta z^{2}/2},\qquad\nabla\ln\omega_{\nu}=-\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}-\beta z,

(2.13)

\displaystyle\rho_{\nu}(z)=\nu^{2}\frac{U_{1+2,\nu}(z)}{U_{12,\nu}(z)}e^{-\frac{\beta z^{2}}{2}}=\nu^{-2}U_{1+2,\nu}\omega_{\nu}(z),\quad\rho(z)=e^{-\frac{\beta|z|^{2}}{2}}.

(2.14)

The function
\gamma_{\nu}
is the weight function for the inner zone (near the soliton scale)
|z\pm a|\ll 1
where the linear part
\Lambda
is considered as a small perturbation, the function
\omega_{\nu}
is the exact weight function for the outer zone
|z\pm a|\gtrsim 1
where the Poisson field
\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon}
is negligible, and
\rho_{\nu}
is a global weight function.

3. The two approximate eigenfunctions

In this section we find suitable approximate eigenfunctions of the linearized operator in parabolic self-similar variables around two concentrated stationary states
\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}
defined by (
2.11
). The reason why we consider
\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}
and not the full operator
\mathscr{L}^{z}
is because the eigenfunctions will grow at spatial infinity what will prevent their Poisson field to be well-defined, which is why we localize the source term in the Poisson field in
\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}
. The error terms created by this localization will be estimated later on. We will find two couples
(\lambda_{0},\phi_{0})
and
(\lambda_{1},\phi_{1})
such that for
i=0,1
, the function
R_{i}
defined as the error in the eigenfunction equation

R_{i}=\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}\phi_{i}-\lambda_{i}\phi_{i}

(3.1)

is small. For
\beta>0
, the equilibrium position for the stationary states is denoted by
a_{\infty}=\left(\sqrt{\frac{2}{\beta}},0\right)
. We introduce the parameter
\alpha=\frac{2}{|a|^{2}}
, so that
|\alpha-\beta|\lesssim|a-a_{\infty}|
for
a
close to
a_{\infty}
.

Proposition 3.1

(Two approximate eigenfunctions)
.

There exists
\tilde{\epsilon}>0
and
\zeta_{*}>0
small enough such that the following holds true for all
1/4\leq\beta\leq 1
,
0<\nu\ll 1
small enough and
a=(|a|,0)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
with
|a-a_{\infty}|\lesssim\nu
. There exist two smooth functions
\phi_{0}[\nu,a]
and
\phi_{1}[\nu,a]
and two approximate eigenvalues

\displaystyle\lambda_{0}=2\beta\Big(1+\tilde{\lambda}_{0}\Big),\qquad\tilde{\lambda}_{0}(\nu)=\frac{\gamma_{0}}{\ln\nu}+\mathcal{O}(|\ln\nu|^{-2}),\quad\gamma_{0}\in\mathbb{R},

(3.2)

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}=2\beta\tilde{\lambda}_{1},\qquad\tilde{\lambda}_{1}(\nu)=\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\ln\nu}+\mathcal{O}(|\ln\nu|^{-2}),\quad\gamma_{1}=\frac{3}{16}-16A,

(3.3)

where
A<0
is given in Proposition
3.12
, and where the exact expression of
\tilde{\lambda}_{i}
is given by (
3.271
), that satisfy the following.

(i)
(Approximate eigenfunctions)
We have the decomposition

\displaystyle\phi_{i}(z)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{16\nu^{4}}\sum_{\pm}\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}\Big(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu}\Big)\chi_{\pm a,\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}}}(z)+\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z)\big(1-\sum_{\pm}\chi_{\pm a,\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}}}\big),

(3.4)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\pm}\left(-\frac{1}{16\nu^{4}}\Lambda U\Big(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu}\Big)\pm\frac{L_{i}}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U\Big(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu}\Big)\right)\chi_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}}(z)+\tilde{\phi}_{i}(z),

(3.5)

where
\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}
,
\phi^{\textup{ex}}_{i}
are given explicitly in Proposition
3.4
and Proposition
3.12
with the constants being given by (
3.271
), (
3.272
) and (
3.306
) and where we introduced
L_{i}=-\frac{\alpha^{1/2}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}}{16}
that satisfies
|L_{0}|\lesssim 1
and
|L_{1}|\lesssim|\ln\nu|
. In particular, we have for
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle|\nabla^{k}\phi_{i}(z)|+|\nabla^{k}\nu\partial_{\nu}\phi_{i}(z)|\lesssim\sum\frac{\langle z\rangle^{C_{k}}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{4+k}},\qquad\mbox{for }k\in\mathbb{N},

(3.6)

\displaystyle|\nabla^{k}\Phi_{\chi^{*}\phi_{i}}(z)|\lesssim\sum\frac{1+i|\ln\nu|}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2+k}}\langle z\rangle^{2},\qquad\mbox{for }k\geq 1.

(3.7)

\displaystyle|\nabla^{k}\tilde{\phi}_{i}(z)|\lesssim\sum\frac{\langle z\rangle^{C_{k}}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2+k}},\quad|\nabla^{k}\nu\partial_{\nu}\tilde{\phi}_{i}(z)|\lesssim\sum\frac{\langle z\rangle^{C_{k}}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2+k}}\left(\frac{\nu^{\delta_{0}}}{(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{\delta_{0}}}+\frac{1}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\right)

(3.8)

\displaystyle|\nabla^{k}\Phi_{\chi^{*}\tilde{\phi}_{i}}(z)|\lesssim\sum\frac{1+i|\ln\nu|}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{k}},\qquad\mbox{for }k\geq 1.

(3.9)

\displaystyle|\nabla^{k}\partial_{a}\phi_{i}|\lesssim\sum\frac{\langle z\rangle^{C_{k}}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{5+k}},\quad|\nabla^{k}\partial_{a}\tilde{\phi}_{i}|\lesssim\sum\frac{\langle z\rangle^{C_{k}}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{3+k}}.

(3.10)

(iii)
(Improved estimates near
\pm a
)
For
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\displaystyle|\nabla^{k}(\phi_{1}-\phi_{0})|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{|\ln\nu|\langle z\rangle^{C_{k}}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{3+k}},\quad|\nabla\Phi_{\phi_{1}-\phi_{0}}(z)|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{|\ln\nu|\langle z\rangle^{C}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)}.

(3.11)

and

\displaystyle|\nabla^{k}\nu\partial_{\nu}(\phi_{1}-\phi_{0})|\lesssim\sum\frac{|\ln\nu|}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{3+k}}\langle z\rangle^{C_{k}},

(3.12)

(iv)
(Pointwise estimates of
R_{i}
)
. The errors in the approximate eigenfunction equation

R_{0}=\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}\phi_{0}-\lambda_{0}\phi_{0}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad R_{1}=\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}\phi_{1}-\lambda_{1}\phi_{1}

(3.13)

satisfy

\sum_{\pm}|\langle R_{i},\mathbbm{1}_{|z\pm a|<1}\rangle|\lesssim\frac{1}{|\ln\nu|},\quad|\nabla^{k}R_{i}(z)|\lesssim\frac{1}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2+k}}\langle z\rangle^{C_{k}}.

(3.14)

Remark 3.2
.

Our approximate eigenvalues are precise up to the order
|\ln\nu|^{-1}
. The exact identity (
3.271
) we take for
\tilde{\lambda}_{i}
does not give the correct next order
|\ln\nu|^{-2}
term. This is because this identity is useful in our construction of the approximate eigenfunctions, but that this approximate eigenfunctions are only correct up to an order
|\ln\nu|^{-2}
.

Note that in the approximate eigenfunction equation (
3.13
) the estimate (
3.14
) quantify how much
R_{i}
is negligible compared with
\lambda_{i}\phi_{i}
. We have roughly
|\phi_{i}|\approx(\nu+|z-a|)^{-4}(\nu+|z+a|)^{-4}\langle z\rangle^{C}
by Proposition
3.4
and Proposition
3.12
, so that (
3.14
) shows that
R_{i}
gains a
|\log\nu|^{i-2}
factor, as well as a
(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{2}
factor near
\pm a
.

The proof of Proposition
3.1
consists of several parts represented in different subsections:

•

In subsection
3.1
, we construct an inner approximate eigenfunction located on
|z\pm a|\ll 1
, where the linearized operator behaves like the one generated by linearization around one bubble. The Poison field generated by other bubble can be approximated by a truncated Taylor expansion, from which we can compute its effect up to any accuracy order.

•

In subsection
3.2
, we construct an outer approximate eigenfunction located on
|z\pm a|\sim 1
, where we eliminate the effect of the nonlocal term, for which the linearized operator behaves like the one of Fokker-Planck type operator with a singular potential.

•

In subsection
3.3
, we perform a matched asymptotic expansion on the common region that gives approximate eigenvalues. We also derive various estimate concerning the approximate eigenfunctions and the generated error.

3.1. Inner expansion of the eigenfunctions

In this subsection we obtain approximate eigenfunctions
\phi^{\textup{in},z}_{i}
near
a
and
-a
for
i=0,1
. We replace in (
3.1
)
\Phi_{\chi^{*}\phi^{\textup{in},z}_{i}}
by a solution
V^{\textup{in},z}_{i}
of
-\Delta V^{\textup{in},z}_{i}=\phi_{i}^{\textup{in},z}
. This yields the following inner approximate eigenfunction system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l l}&R_{i}^{\textup{in},z}=\mathscr{L}^{z}(\phi_{i}^{\textup{in},z},V_{i}^{\textup{in},z})-\lambda_{i}\phi_{i}^{\textup{in},z},\\
&-\Delta V_{i}^{\textup{in},z}=\phi_{i}^{\textup{in},z},\end{array}\right.

(3.15)

that will be studied in this subsection, where

\mathscr{L}^{z}(\phi,V)=\Delta\phi-\nabla\phi\cdot\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}-\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}\cdot\nabla V+2U_{1+2,\nu}\phi-\beta\Lambda\phi.

3.1.1. Renormalised error in the eigenfunction equation

Consider
i=0
or
i=1
and define the new variables
z_{\pm}
centred at
\pm a
and its rescaled version

z_{\pm}=z\mp a,\qquad y_{\pm}=\frac{z_{\pm}}{\nu},

where
\nu>0
. In the polar coordinates, we write

\theta_{\pm}=\frac{z_{\pm}}{|z_{\pm}|}=\frac{y_{\pm}}{|y_{\pm}|},\qquad r_{\pm}=|y_{\pm}|,\qquad\zeta_{\pm}=|z_{\pm}|

so that

z_{\pm}=\zeta_{\pm}(\cos\theta_{\pm},\sin\theta_{\pm}),\qquad y_{\pm}=r_{\pm}(\cos\theta_{\pm},\sin\theta_{\pm}).

We define the renormalized inner eigenfunction
\phi^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}
, Poisson field
V^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}
and error
R^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}
as

\phi^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}(y_{\pm})=\nu^{4}\phi_{i}^{\textup{in},z}(z),\qquad V^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}(y_{\pm})=\nu^{2}V_{i}^{\textup{in},z}(z),\qquad R^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}(y_{\pm})=\nu^{6}R_{i}^{\textup{in},z}(z).

By writing

\displaystyle U_{1+2,\nu}(z)=\frac{1}{\nu^{2}}\Big(U(y\pm)+U\big(y_{\pm}\pm\frac{2a}{\nu}\big)\Big),\qquad\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}(z)=\Phi_{U}(y_{\pm})+\Phi_{U}\big(y_{\pm}\pm\frac{2a}{\nu}\big).

this transforms (
3.15
) into the renormalized inner approximate eigenfunction system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l l}&R_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}=\mathscr{L}_{0}(\phi^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm},V_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}})-\nu^{2}\big(\beta\Lambda+\lambda_{i}\big)\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}-\nabla.\Big(\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}\big[\nabla\Phi_{U}(y_{\pm}\pm\frac{2a}{\nu})\pm\nu\beta a\big]\Big)\\
&\qquad\qquad-\nabla.\Big(U(y_{\pm}\pm 2a/\nu)\nabla V^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}\Big)\\
&-\Delta V_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}=\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}\end{array}\right.

(3.16)

where

\mathscr{L}_{0}(\phi^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm},V^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm})=\nabla.\Big(\nabla\phi^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}-\phi^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}\nabla\Phi_{U}-U\nabla V^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}\Big).

3.1.2. Leading order system and decomposition of the error
R_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}

To extract the leading order terms in the right-hand side of (
3.16
) we perform the Taylor expansion of
\nabla\Phi_{U}(y_{\pm}\pm\frac{2a}{\nu})
. For sake of simplicity, we write

y=y_{\pm},\quad r=r_{\pm},\quad\theta=\theta_{\pm},

wherever there is no need to specify the sign.

Lemma 3.3
.

For
a=(|a|,0)
with
|a|\approx 1
, there holds

\nabla\Phi_{U}(y\pm 2a/\nu)=\mp\alpha a\nu+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\alpha^{\frac{i+1}{2}}\nu^{i+1}G_{i,\pm}(y)+\tilde{G}_{\pm}(y),

(3.17)

where
|\nabla^{k}\tilde{G}_{\pm}(y)|=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{5}\langle y\rangle^{4-k})
for
|y|\ll\nu^{-1}
, and
G_{i,\pm}(y)
are homogeneous in
y
of degree
i
. In particular, we have

\displaystyle G_{1,\pm}(y)

\displaystyle=G_{1}(y)=\frac{1}{2}(y_{1},-y_{2})=\frac{1}{4}\nabla(y_{1}^{2}-y_{2}^{2})=\frac{1}{4}\nabla\big(r^{2}\cos 2\theta\big),

(3.18)

\displaystyle G_{2,\pm}(y)=\pm G_{2}(y),\quad G_{2}(y)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}\Big(y_{1}^{2}-y_{2}^{2},-2y_{1}y_{2}\Big)+\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}(1,0)

(3.19)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{12\sqrt{2}}\nabla(y_{1}^{3}-3y_{1}y_{2}^{2})+\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}\nabla y_{1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{12\sqrt{2}}\nabla\big(r^{3}\cos 3\theta\big)+\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}\nabla(r\cos\theta),

and

\displaystyle G_{3,\pm}(y)=G_{3}(y)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{16}\Big(3y_{1}y_{2}^{2}-y_{1}^{3},3y_{1}^{2}y_{2}-y_{2}^{3}\Big)+\frac{1}{16}(-3y_{1},y_{2})

(3.20)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{64}\nabla\big(6y_{1}^{2}y_{2}^{2}-y_{1}^{4}-y_{2}^{4}\big)+\frac{1}{32}\nabla(-3y_{1}^{2}+y_{2}^{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{64}\nabla\big(r^{4}\cos 4\theta\big)-\frac{1}{32}\nabla\big(r^{2}(1+2\cos 2\theta)\big).

Proof.

Let
F(b)=\nabla\Phi_{U}(by\pm 2a/\nu)
and use the Taylor expansion for the function
F(b)
at
b=0
, then, let
b=1
to get the desired result. See Appendix
B
for a computation which can be simply verified by Matlab symbolic.
∎

Using Lemma
3.3
, and anticipating that
|a-a_{\infty}|\lesssim\nu
so that
|\alpha-\beta|\lesssim\nu
, we decompose the error
R^{\textup{in}}_{\pm}
as

\displaystyle R^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}

\displaystyle=\tilde{R}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}+\bar{R}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm},

where
\tilde{R}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}
contains the leading order terms defined by the following renormalized inner approximate eigenfunction system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{r l}\tilde{R}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}=&\mathscr{L}_{0}(\phi^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm},V^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm})-\nu^{2}\alpha\left(\big(\Lambda+\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\beta}\big)\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}+\nabla.\Big(\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}G_{1,\pm}\Big)\right)\\
&-\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}\nu^{3}\nabla.\Big(\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}G_{2,\pm}\Big)-\alpha^{2}\nu^{4}\nabla.\Big(\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}G_{3,\pm}\Big),\\
-\Delta V^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}=&\phi^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}\end{array}\right.

(3.21)

and
\bar{R}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}
contains the subleading remainding terms defined by

\displaystyle\bar{R}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}

\displaystyle=\pm\nu(\alpha-\beta)a.\nabla\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}+\nu^{2}(\alpha-\beta)\left(\Lambda+\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\beta}\right)\ \phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}

\displaystyle\qquad-\nabla.\Big(U(y_{\pm}\pm 2a/\nu)\nabla V^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}\Big)-\nabla.\Big(\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}\tilde{G}_{\pm}\Big).

(3.22)

3.1.3. First truncated expansion for the leading system

Our aim is to construct a first version of the inner eigenfunction and Poisson field
(\phi^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm},V^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm})
so that the error
\tilde{R}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}
in the leading system (
3.21
) is small in some suitable function space. There holds
\lambda_{i}=2\beta(1-i)+2\beta\tilde{\lambda}_{i}
. Since we expect
|\tilde{\lambda}_{i}|\ll 1
and
|a-a_{\infty}|\lesssim\nu
so that
|\alpha-\beta|\lesssim\nu
, we write (
3.23
) as

\left\{\begin{array}[]{r l}\tilde{R}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}(\phi^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm},V^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm})=&\mathscr{L}_{0}(\phi^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm},V^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm})-\alpha\nu^{2}\left(\Lambda_{i}\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}+\nabla.\Big(\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}G_{1,\pm}\Big)\right)\\
&-\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}\nu^{3}\nabla.\Big(\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}G_{2,\pm}\Big)-\alpha^{2}\nu^{4}\nabla.\Big(\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}G_{3,\pm}\Big)-2\alpha\nu^{2}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}},\\
-\Delta V^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}=&\phi^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm},\end{array}\right.

(3.23)

where
\Lambda_{i}
is defined by

\Lambda_{i}=\Lambda+2(1-i)=z.\nabla+2(2-i).

We look as a first attempt for an approximate inner eigenfunction of the form of a truncated power series in the small parameters
\nu
and
\tilde{\lambda}_{i}
,

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}

\displaystyle=\Lambda U+\alpha\nu^{2}T_{2}^{(i)}\pm\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}\nu^{3}T_{3}+\alpha^{2}\nu^{4}T_{4}^{(i)}+\alpha\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\nu^{2}\hat{T}_{2}+\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\alpha^{2}\nu^{4}\hat{T}_{4}^{(i)},

(3.24)

\displaystyle\tilde{V}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}

\displaystyle=\Phi_{\Lambda U}+\alpha\nu^{2}V_{2}^{(i)}\pm\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}\nu^{3}V_{3}+\alpha^{2}\nu^{4}V_{4}^{(i)}+\alpha\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\nu^{2}\hat{V}_{2}+\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\alpha^{2}\nu^{4}\hat{V}_{4}^{(i)},

(3.25)

where we require

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{0}(T_{2}^{(i)},V_{2}^{(i)})=\Lambda_{i}\Lambda U+\nabla.(\Lambda UG_{1}),

(3.26)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{0}(\hat{T}_{2},\hat{V}_{2})=2\Lambda U,

(3.27)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{0}(T_{3},V_{3})=\nabla.\big(G_{2}\Lambda U\big),

(3.28)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{0}(T_{4}^{(i)},V_{4}^{(i)})=\Lambda_{i}T_{2}^{(i)}+\nabla.(G_{3}\Lambda U)+\nabla.\big(T_{2}^{(i)}G_{1}\big),

(3.29)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{0}(\hat{T}_{4}^{(i)},\hat{V}_{4}^{(i)})=2T_{2}^{(i)}+\nabla.(G_{1}\hat{T}_{2})+\Lambda_{i}\hat{T}_{2},

(3.30)

\displaystyle-\Delta V_{2}^{(i)}=T_{2}^{(i)},\;\;-\Delta V_{4}^{(i)}=T_{4}^{(i)},\;\;-\Delta V_{3}=T_{3},\;\;-\Delta\hat{V}_{2}=\hat{T}_{2},\quad-\Delta\hat{V}_{4}^{(i)}=\hat{T}_{4}^{(i)}.

(3.31)

Plugging (
3.26
)-(
3.30
) in (
3.23
) and recalling
\mathscr{L}_{0}(\Lambda U,\Phi_{\Lambda U})=0
, we obtain that
\tilde{R}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}(\tilde{\phi}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm},\tilde{V}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm})
consists of the terms of magnitude order
\mathcal{O}(\tilde{\lambda}_{i}^{2}\nu^{4}+\nu^{5})
or higher,

\displaystyle\tilde{R}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}(\tilde{\phi}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm},\tilde{V}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm})=

\displaystyle-2\alpha^{2}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}^{2}\nu^{4}\hat{T}_{2}

\displaystyle+\alpha^{\frac{5}{2}}\nu^{5}\left(\mp\Lambda_{i}T_{3}\mp\nabla.(T_{3}G_{1})\mp\nabla.(T_{2}^{(i)}G_{2})\right)+\alpha^{\frac{5}{2}}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\nu^{5}\left(\mp\nabla.(\hat{T}_{2}G_{2})\mp 2T_{3}\right)

\displaystyle+\alpha^{3}\nu^{6}\left(-\Lambda_{i}T_{4}^{(i)}-\nabla.(T_{4}^{(i)}G_{1})-\nabla.(T_{3}G_{2})-\nabla.(T_{2}^{(i)}G_{3})\right)

\displaystyle+\alpha^{3}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\nu^{6}\left(-\Lambda_{i}\hat{T}_{4}^{(i)}-\nabla.(\hat{T}_{4}^{(i)}G_{1})-\nabla.(\hat{T}_{2}G_{3})-2T_{4}^{(i)}\right)-2\alpha^{3}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}^{2}\nu^{6}\hat{T}_{4}^{(i)}

\displaystyle+\alpha^{\frac{7}{2}}\nu^{7}\left(\mp\nabla.(T_{4}^{(i)}G_{2})\mp\nabla.(T_{3}G_{3})\right)\mp\alpha^{\frac{7}{2}}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\nu^{7}\nabla.(\hat{T}_{4}^{(i)}G_{2})

\displaystyle-\alpha^{4}\nu^{8}\nabla.(T_{4}^{(i)}G_{3})-\alpha^{4}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\nu^{8}\nabla.(\hat{T}_{4}^{(i)}G_{3}).

(3.32)

We then compute the remainder associated to the subleading terms by plugging (
3.26
)-(
3.30
) in (
3.22
) and using
U\Big(\frac{2a}{\nu}\Big)=\frac{\nu^{4}}{2|a|^{4}}+O(\nu^{6})
:

\displaystyle\bar{R}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}(\tilde{\phi}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm},\tilde{V}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm})

\displaystyle=\pm\nu(\alpha-\beta)a.\nabla\Lambda U+\nu^{2}(\alpha-\beta)\left(\Lambda+\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\beta}\right)\Lambda U\pm\nu^{3}(\alpha-\beta)\alpha a.\nabla(T_{2}^{(i)}+\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\hat{T}_{2})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\nu^{4}}{2|a|^{4}}\Lambda U\pm\nu(\alpha-\beta)a.\nabla(\tilde{\phi}_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}-\Lambda U-\alpha\nu^{2}T^{(i)}_{2}-\alpha\nu^{2}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\hat{T}_{2})

\displaystyle\quad+\nu^{2}(\alpha-\beta)\left(\Lambda+\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\beta}\right)(\tilde{\phi}_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}-\Lambda U)+\left(U\Big(y\pm\frac{2a}{\nu}\Big)-\frac{\nu^{4}}{2|a|^{4}}\right)\Lambda U

\displaystyle\quad+U\Big(y\pm\frac{2a}{\nu}\Big)(\tilde{\phi}_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}-\Lambda U)-\nabla U\Big(y\pm\frac{2a}{\nu}\Big).\nabla\tilde{V}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}-\nabla.(\tilde{\phi}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}\tilde{G}_{\pm}).

Above, the first line gathers terms of order
\nu|a-a_{\infty}|
,
\nu^{2}|a-a_{\infty}|
,
\nu^{4}
and
\nu^{3}|a-a_{\infty}|
which are not small enough for
|y|\lesssim 1
, but which are subleading for
|y|\gg 1
in comparison with
\tilde{R}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}
due to a stronger decay. The other lines gather terms of order
\nu^{5}
or
\nu^{4}|a-a_{\infty}|
and higher which are small enough. In order to avoid the terms on the first line, we will add corrections in a second truncated expansion.

3.1.4. Second expansion with corrections for subleading remainder and Poisson field

We now look for an expansion that manages simultaneously the main order error terms in
\tilde{R}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}
and
\bar{R}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}
. We also want to include corrections that allow the Poisson field
V^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}
to behave like
y_{1}
or
(y_{1}^{2}-y_{2}^{2})
as
|y|\to\infty
. This is because the Poisson field for the full solution will actually depend from the outer part of the solution, and that the main part of this nonlocal effect in the Poisson equation will be located on these first two harmonic polynomials. We look for an expansion of the form

\displaystyle\phi^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\phi}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}+\bar{\phi}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}

(3.33)

\displaystyle\bar{\phi}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}

\displaystyle=\pm\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\partial_{x_{1}}U\pm\nu(\alpha-\beta)\mathcal{S}_{1,1}+\nu^{2}(\alpha-\beta)\mathcal{S}_{2,1}+\nu^{2}(\alpha-\beta)^{2}\mathcal{S}_{2,2}\pm\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}\nu^{3}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\mathcal{S}_{3,0}

\displaystyle\quad\pm\nu^{3}(\alpha-\beta)\mathcal{S}_{3,1}+\nu^{4}\mathcal{S}_{4,0}+\alpha^{2}\nu^{4}M_{i}^{\textup{in}}\mathcal{S}_{4,0}^{\prime},

(3.34)

\displaystyle V^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}

\displaystyle=\tilde{V}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}+\bar{V}_{i,\pm}

(3.35)

\displaystyle\bar{V}_{i,\pm}

\displaystyle=\pm\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\partial_{x_{1}}\Phi_{U}\pm\nu(\alpha-\beta)\mathcal{W}_{1,1}+\nu^{2}(\alpha-\beta)\mathcal{W}_{2,1}+\nu^{2}(\alpha-\beta)^{2}\mathcal{W}_{2,2}

\displaystyle\quad\pm\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}\nu^{3}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\mathcal{W}_{3,0}\pm\nu^{3}(\alpha-\beta)\mathcal{W}_{3,1}+\nu^{4}\mathcal{W}_{4,0}+\alpha^{2}\nu^{4}M_{i}^{\textup{in}}\mathcal{W}_{4,0}^{\prime},

(3.36)

The real constants
L_{i}^{\textup{in}},M_{i}^{\textup{in}}
are for the moment free parameters. They will be adjusted later on to match with the outer Poisson field. All profiles will actually depend in an affine way on
L_{i}^{\textup{in}}
, but we chose to hide it in the notation except fo
\partial_{x_{1}}U
and
\mathcal{S}_{3,0}
which will give the key contribution involving
L_{i}^{\textup{in}}
. Also, the profiles in
\bar{\phi}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}
and
\bar{V}_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}
above depend on
i
but we do not include it in the notation for sake of clarity. We require

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{0}(S_{1,1},\mathcal{W}_{1,1})=-a.\nabla\Lambda U,

(3.37)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{0}(S_{2,1},\mathcal{W}_{2,1})=-(\Lambda_{i}+2\tilde{\lambda}_{i})\Lambda U-\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}a.\nabla\partial_{x_{1}}U,

(3.38)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{0}(\mathcal{S}_{2,2},\mathcal{W}_{2,2})=-a.\nabla\mathcal{S}_{1,1},

(3.39)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{0}(S_{3,0},\mathcal{W}_{3,0})=(\Lambda_{i}+2\tilde{\lambda}_{i})\partial_{x_{1}}U+\nabla.(\partial_{x_{1}}UG_{1}),

(3.40)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{0}(\mathcal{S}_{3,1},\mathcal{W}_{3,1})=-\alpha a.\nabla(T_{2}^{(i)}+\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\hat{T}_{2})+\alpha((\Lambda_{i}+2\tilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{S}_{1,1}+\nabla.(\mathcal{S}_{1,1}G_{1}))

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}(\Lambda_{i}+2\tilde{\lambda}_{i})\partial_{x_{1}}U,

(3.41)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{0}(\mathcal{S}_{4,0},\mathcal{W}_{4,0})=-\frac{1}{2|a|^{4}}\Lambda U+\alpha^{2}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\nabla.(\partial_{x_{1}}UG_{2}),

(3.42)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{0}(\mathcal{S}_{4,0}^{\prime},\mathcal{W}_{4,0}^{\prime})=0,\qquad-\Delta\mathcal{W}_{4,0}^{\prime}=\mathcal{S}_{4,0}^{\prime},

(3.43)

\displaystyle-\Delta\mathcal{W}_{m,n}=\mathcal{S}_{m,n}\quad\mbox{for }(m,n)\in\{(1,1),(2,1),(2,2),(3,0),(3,1),(4,0)\}.

(3.44)

We recall that
\mathscr{L}_{0}(\partial_{x_{1}}U,\Phi_{\partial_{x_{1}}}U)=0
. Plugging (
3.26
)-(
3.30
) and (
3.37
)-(
3.44
) in (
3.22
) and (
3.23
) we obtain:

\displaystyle R^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}

\displaystyle=\tilde{R}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}(\tilde{\phi}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm},\tilde{V}^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm})

\displaystyle\qquad-\alpha\nu^{2}\Bigg((\Lambda_{i}+2\tilde{\lambda}_{i})\left(\bar{\phi}_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}\mp\nu(\alpha-\beta)\mathcal{S}_{1,1}\mp\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\partial_{x_{1}}U\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\nabla.\left(\left(\bar{\phi}_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}\mp\nu(\alpha-\beta)\mathcal{S}_{1,1}\mp\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\partial_{x_{1}}U\right)G_{1,\pm}\right)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad-\nabla.\left(\Big(\bar{\phi}_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}\mp\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\partial_{x_{1}}U\Big)\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}\nu^{3}G_{2,\pm}\right)-\nabla.\left(\bar{\phi}_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}\alpha^{2}\nu^{4}G_{3,\pm}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\pm\nu(\alpha-\beta)a.\nabla(\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}-\Lambda U-\alpha\nu^{2}T^{(i)}_{2}-\alpha\nu^{2}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\hat{T}_{2}\mp\nu(\alpha-\beta)\mathcal{S}_{1,1}\mp\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\partial_{x_{1}}U)

\displaystyle\qquad+\nu^{2}(\alpha-\beta)\left(\Lambda+\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\beta}\right)(\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}-\Lambda U\mp\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\partial_{x_{1}}U)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(U\Big(y\pm\frac{2a}{\nu}\Big)-\frac{\nu^{4}}{2|a|^{4}}\right)\Lambda U+U\Big(y\pm\frac{2a}{\nu}\Big)(\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}-\Lambda U)

\displaystyle\qquad-\nabla U\Big(y\pm\frac{2a}{\nu}\Big).\nabla V^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}-\nabla.(\phi^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}\tilde{G}_{\pm}).

(3.45)

3.1.5. Elliptic equations in spherical harmonics

To ease the notations, we write

r=r_{\pm},\quad\theta=\theta_{\pm},

wherever there is no need to precise the sign. Using the change of coordinates
\partial_{y_{1}}=\cos\theta\partial_{r}-r^{-1}\sin\theta\partial_{\theta}
and
\partial_{y_{2}}=\sin\theta\partial_{r}+r^{-1}\cos\theta\partial_{\theta}
yields the elementary identity

\displaystyle\nabla.\Big[f(r)\cos(m\theta)\nabla g(r)\cos(k\theta)\Big]

\displaystyle=\Big[\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}(rf\partial_{r}g)-\frac{k^{2}fg}{r^{2}}\Big]\cos(m\theta)\cos(k\theta)

\displaystyle\quad+(mk)\frac{fg}{r^{2}}\sin(m\theta)\sin(k\theta).

(3.46)

Elliptic equations for the leading terms.

Since the source terms in (
3.26
) - (
3.30
) depends on spherical harmonics, we decompose the profiles
T_{2}^{(i)},T_{3},T_{4}^{(i)},\hat{T}_{2},
and
\hat{T}_{4}^{(i)}
accordingly,

\displaystyle T_{2}^{(i)}=T_{2,0}^{(i)}(r)+\mathsf{T}_{2,2}(r)\cos(2\theta),

(3.47)

\displaystyle V_{2}^{(i)}=V_{2,0}^{(i)}(r)+\mathsf{V}_{2,2}(r)\cos(2\theta),

(3.48)

\displaystyle T_{3}=\mathsf{T}_{3,1}(r)\cos(\theta)+\mathsf{T}_{3,3}(r)\cos(3\theta),

(3.49)

\displaystyle V_{3}=\mathsf{V}_{3,1}(r)\cos(\theta)+\mathsf{V}_{3,3}(r)\cos(3\theta),

(3.50)

\displaystyle T_{4}^{(i)}=T_{4,0}^{(i)}(r)+\mathsf{T}_{4,2}^{(i)}(r)\cos(2\theta)+\mathsf{T}_{4,4}(r)\cos(4\theta)

(3.51)

\displaystyle V_{4}^{(i)}=V_{4,0}^{(i)}(r)+\mathsf{V}_{4,2}^{(i)}(r)\cos(2\theta)+\mathsf{V}_{4,4}(r)\cos(4\theta),

(3.52)

\displaystyle\hat{T}_{2}(y)=\hat{T}_{2}(r),\qquad\quad\hat{T}_{4}^{(i)}=\hat{T}_{4,0}^{(i)}(r)+\hat{\mathsf{T}}_{4,2}(r)\cos(2\theta),

(3.53)

\displaystyle\hat{V}_{2}(y)=\hat{V}_{2}(r),\qquad\quad\hat{V}_{4}^{(i)}=\hat{V}_{4,0}^{(i)}(r)+\hat{\mathsf{V}}_{4,2}(r)\cos(2\theta),

(3.54)

Note that we slightly abuse notations for radial functions by identifying
f(y)\equiv f(r)
. The notation
f_{m,k}(r)
means that the term of magnitude order
\nu^{m}
and angular dependence
\cos(k\theta)
. We also note in the above decomposition of the profiles that the pairs
(\mathsf{T}_{k,k},\mathsf{V}_{k,k})_{k=2,3,4}
and
(\hat{\mathsf{T}}_{4,2},\hat{\mathsf{V}}_{4,2})
are independent of
i
.

For radial modes
T(y)=T(r)
with suitable decay at infinity,
\Phi_{T}
is well defined. In particular, we have the explicit formula

\partial_{r}\Phi_{T}=-r^{-1}\int_{0}^{r}T(\tilde{r})\tilde{r}d\tilde{r}.

(3.55)

We extend
T\mapsto\nabla\Phi_{T}
by the above formula to all radial
T\in L^{1}_{loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, noting that it is well defined (and abusing notations since
\Phi_{T}=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\ln r*T
might not be well defined). We extend accordingly

\mathscr{L}_{0}(T)=\Delta T-\nabla.(T\nabla\Phi_{U})-\nabla.(U\nabla\Phi_{T}),

in the distributional sense to all radial functions
T\in L^{1}_{loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
by defining
\nabla\Phi_{T}
via (
3.55
). Whatever the choice of smooth radial functions
T(y)=T(r)
and
V(y)=V(r)
with
-\Delta V=T
we have then the identity:

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{0}(T,V)=\mathscr{L}_{0}(T).

For higher order spherical harmonics, if
-\Delta(\omega\cos(k\theta))=\psi\cos(k\theta)
, we have

\mathscr{L}_{0}(\psi\cos(k\theta),\omega\cos(k\theta))=\cos(k\theta)\mathscr{L}_{0,k}(\psi,\omega),

and

\Delta(\omega\cos(k\theta))=\cos(k\theta)\Delta_{k}\omega,

where

\mathscr{L}_{0,k}(\psi,\omega)=\Delta_{k}\psi+\dfrac{4r}{1+r^{2}}\partial_{r}\psi+\dfrac{32r}{(1+r^{2})^{3}}\partial_{r}\omega+\dfrac{16\psi}{(1+r^{2})^{2}},

(3.56)

and

-\Delta_{k}\omega=\psi\quad\textup{with}\quad\Delta_{k}=\partial_{r}^{2}+\dfrac{\partial_{r}}{r}-\dfrac{k^{2}}{r^{2}},

We use here the convention
\mathscr{L}_{0,0}\equiv\mathscr{L}_{0}
and
\Delta_{0}\equiv\Delta
.
From Lemma
3.3
and (
3.46
), we decompose the source terms in (
3.26
) - (
3.30
) into the spherical harmonics,

\nabla.(\Lambda UG_{1})=\Sigma_{2,2}(r)\cos(2\theta),\qquad\Sigma_{2,2}=\frac{1}{2}r\partial_{r}\Lambda U,

(3.57)

and

\nabla.(\Lambda UG_{2})=\Sigma_{3,1}(r)\cos\theta+\Sigma_{3,3}(r)\cos(3\theta)

where

\Sigma_{3,1}(r)=\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}\partial_{r}\Lambda U,\quad\Sigma_{3,3}(r)=-\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}r^{2}\partial_{r}\Lambda U.

(3.58)

We have

\displaystyle\Lambda_{i}T_{2}^{(i)}+\nabla.(G_{1}T_{2}^{(i)})+\nabla.(G_{3}\Lambda U)=\Sigma_{4,0}^{(i)}(r)+\Sigma_{4,2}^{(i)}(r)\cos(2\theta)+\Sigma_{4,4}(r)\cos(4\theta),

where

\displaystyle\Sigma_{4,0}^{(i)}

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{i}T_{2,0}^{(i)}+\frac{1}{4r}\partial_{r}(r^{2}\mathsf{T}_{2,2})-\frac{1}{16r}\partial_{r}(r^{2}\Lambda U),

(3.59)

\displaystyle\Sigma_{4,2}^{(i)}

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{i}\mathsf{T}_{2,2}+\frac{1}{2}r\partial_{r}T_{2,0}^{(i)}-\frac{r}{16}\partial_{r}\Lambda U,

(3.60)

\displaystyle\Sigma_{4,4}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}r\partial_{r}\mathsf{T}_{2,2}-\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{T}_{2,2}+\frac{1}{16}r^{3}\partial_{r}\Lambda U,

(3.61)

and

\displaystyle 2T_{2}^{(i)}+\nabla.(G_{1}\hat{T}_{2})+\Lambda_{i}\hat{T}_{2}=\hat{\Sigma}_{4,0}^{(i)}(r)+\hat{\Sigma}_{4,2}(r)\cos(2\theta),

where

\hat{\Sigma}_{4,0}^{(i)}=2T_{2,0}^{(i)}+\Lambda_{i}\hat{T}_{2},\qquad\hat{\Sigma}_{4,2}=2\mathsf{T}_{2,2}+\frac{1}{2}r\partial_{r}\hat{T}_{2}.

(3.62)

Now, the equations (
3.26
) -(
3.31
) are transformed into the following systems of radial functions:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}&\mathscr{L}_{0}(T_{2,0}^{(i)})=\Lambda_{i}\Lambda U,\\
&-\Delta V_{2,0}^{(i)}=T_{2,0}^{(i)},\end{array}\right.\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}&\mathscr{L}_{0}(\hat{T}_{2})=2\Lambda U,\\
&-\Delta\hat{V}_{2}^{(i)}=\hat{T}_{2,0}^{(i)}.\end{array}\right.

(3.63)

\mathscr{L}_{0}(T_{4,0}^{(i)})=\Sigma_{4,0}^{(i)},\quad\mathscr{L}_{0}(\hat{T}_{4,0}^{(i)})=\hat{\Sigma}_{4,0}^{(i)},

(3.64)

\displaystyle k=2,3,4,\quad

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}\mathscr{L}_{0,k}(\mathsf{T}_{k,k},\mathsf{V}_{k,k})=\Sigma_{k,k},\\
-\Delta_{k}\mathsf{V}_{k,k}=\mathsf{T}_{k,k},\end{array}\right.\qquad\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}\mathscr{L}_{0,1}(\mathsf{T}_{3,1},\mathsf{V}_{3,1})=\Sigma_{3,1},\\
-\Delta_{1}\mathsf{V}_{3,1}=\mathsf{T}_{3,1},\end{array}\right.

where
(\Sigma_{k,k})_{k=2,3,4}
and
\Sigma_{3,1}
are defined in (
3.57
), (
3.58
), (
3.61
), and

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}\mathscr{L}_{0,2}(\mathsf{T}_{4,2}^{(i)},\mathsf{V}_{4,2}^{(i)})=\Sigma_{4,2}^{(i)},\\
-\Delta_{2}\mathsf{V}_{4,2}^{(i)}=\mathsf{T}_{4,2}^{(i)},\end{array}\right.\qquad\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}\mathscr{L}_{0,2}(\hat{\mathsf{T}}_{4,2},\hat{\mathsf{V}}_{4,2})=\hat{\Sigma}_{4,2},\\
-\Delta_{2}\hat{\mathsf{V}}_{4,2}=\hat{\mathsf{T}}_{4,2},\end{array}\right.

where
\Sigma_{4,2}^{(i)}
and
\hat{\Sigma}_{4,2}
are defined in (
3.60
) and (
3.62
).

Elliptic equations for the subleading terms.

We next decompose the profiles
\mathcal{S}_{1,1}
,
\mathcal{S}_{2,1}
,
\mathcal{S}_{2,2}
,
\mathcal{S}_{3,0}
,
\mathcal{S}_{3,1}
,
\mathcal{S}_{4,0}
and
\mathcal{S}_{4,0}^{\prime}
and their Poisson fields in spherical harmonics

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{1,1}=\mathsf{S}_{1,1,1}(r)\cos(\theta),\qquad\mathcal{W}_{1,1}=\mathsf{W}_{1,1,1}(r)\cos(\theta)

(3.73)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{2,1}=\mathsf{S}_{2,1,0}(r)+\mathsf{S}_{2,1,2}(r)\cos(2\theta),\qquad\mathcal{W}_{2,1}=\mathsf{W}_{2,1,0}(r)+\mathsf{W}_{2,1,2}(r)\cos(2\theta)

(3.74)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{2,2}=\mathsf{S}_{2,2,0}(r)+\mathsf{S}_{2,2,2}(r)\cos(2\theta),\qquad\mathcal{W}_{2,2}=\mathsf{W}_{2,2,0}(r)+\mathsf{W}_{2,2,2}(r)\cos(2\theta)

(3.75)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{3,0}=\mathsf{S}_{3,0,1}(r)\cos\theta+\mathsf{S}_{3,0,3}(r)\cos(3\theta),\qquad\mathcal{W}_{3,1}=\mathsf{W}_{3,0,1}(r)\cos\theta+\mathsf{W}_{3,0,3}(r)\cos(3\theta)

(3.76)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{3,1}=\mathsf{S}_{3,1,1}(r)\cos\theta+\mathsf{S}_{3,1,3}(r)\cos(3\theta),\qquad\mathcal{W}_{3,1}=\mathsf{W}_{3,1,1}(r)\cos\theta+\mathsf{W}_{3,1,3}(r)\cos(3\theta)

(3.77)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{4,0}=\sum_{j=0,2,4}\mathsf{S}_{4,0,j}(r)\cos(j\theta),\qquad\mathcal{W}_{4,0}=\sum_{j=0,2,4}\mathsf{W}_{4,0,j}(r)\cos(j\theta),

(3.78)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{4,0}^{\prime}=\mathsf{S}_{4,0,2}^{\prime}(r)\cos(2\theta),\qquad\mathcal{W}_{4,0,2}=\mathsf{W}_{4,0,2}(r)\cos(2\theta).

(3.79)

We decompose the source terms in (
3.37
)-(
3.44
) as

\displaystyle-a.\nabla\Lambda U=-|a|\partial_{r}\Lambda U\cos(\theta),

\displaystyle-(\Lambda_{i}+2\tilde{\lambda}_{i})\Lambda U-\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}a.\nabla\partial_{x_{1}}U=-(\Lambda_{i}+2\tilde{\lambda}_{i})\Lambda U(r)-\frac{\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}|a|\left(\partial_{r}^{2}U+\frac{\partial_{r}U}{r}\right),

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}|a|\left(\partial_{r}^{2}U-\frac{\partial_{r}U}{r}\right)\cos(2\theta)

\displaystyle-a.\nabla\mathcal{S}_{1,1}=-\frac{|a|}{2}\left(\partial_{r}\mathsf{S}_{1,1,1}+\frac{\mathsf{S}_{1,1,1}}{r}\right)+\frac{|a|}{2}\left(\frac{\mathsf{S}_{1,1,1}}{r}-\partial_{r}\mathsf{S}_{1,1,1}\right)\cos(2\theta).

\displaystyle(\Lambda_{i}+2\tilde{\lambda}_{i})\partial_{x_{1}}U+\nabla.(\partial_{x_{1}}UG_{1})=\left(\frac{5}{4}r\partial_{r}^{2}U+\frac{17-8i-8\tilde{\lambda}_{i}}{4}\partial_{r}U\right)\cos\theta+\left(\frac{r}{4}\partial_{r}^{2}U-\frac{1}{4}\partial_{r}U\right)\cos(3\theta),

\displaystyle-\alpha a.\nabla(T_{2}^{(i)}+\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\hat{T}_{2})+\alpha((\Lambda_{i}+\tilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{S}_{1,1}+\nabla.(\mathcal{S}_{1,1}G_{1}))-\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}(\Lambda_{i}+2\tilde{\lambda}_{i})\partial_{x_{1}}U

\displaystyle\qquad=\alpha\Big(2r\partial_{r}\mathsf{S}_{1,1,1}+(2(2-i)+2\tilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathsf{S}_{1,1,1}-\partial_{r}\mathsf{T}_{2,0}^{(i)}-\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\partial_{r}\hat{T}_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{\partial_{r}\mathsf{T}_{2,2}}{2}-\frac{\mathsf{T}_{2,2}}{r}-\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}(\Lambda_{i}+2\tilde{\lambda}_{i})\partial_{r}U\Big)\cos\theta

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\alpha(r\partial_{r}\mathsf{S}_{1,1,1}-\mathsf{S}_{1,1,1}+|a|(\frac{\mathsf{T}_{2,2}}{r})-\frac{\partial_{r}\mathsf{T}_{2,2}}{2})\cos(3\theta),

and

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2|a|^{4}}\Lambda U+\alpha^{2}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\nabla.(\partial_{x_{1}}UG_{2})=-\frac{1}{2|a|^{4}}\Lambda U+\frac{\alpha^{2}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}}{8\sqrt{2}}\left(\partial_{r}^{2}U+\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}U\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(-\frac{3}{24\sqrt{2}}(r^{2}\partial_{r}^{2}U+r\partial_{r}U)+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}^{2}U-\frac{1}{2r}\partial_{r}U\right)\cos(2\theta)-\frac{3}{24\sqrt{2}}(r^{2}\partial_{r}^{2}U-r\partial_{r}U)\cos(4\theta)

The equations (
3.31
)-(
3.37
) are transformed into

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}&\mathscr{L}_{0,1}(\mathsf{S}_{1,1,1},\mathsf{W}_{1,1,1})=-|a|\partial_{r}\Lambda U,\\
&-\Delta_{1}\mathsf{W}_{1,1,1}=\mathsf{S}_{1,1,1},\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}&\mathscr{L}_{0}(\mathsf{S}_{2,1,0})=\Sigma_{2,1,0},\\
&-\Delta\mathsf{W}_{2,1,0}=\mathsf{S}_{2,1,0},\end{array}\right.\quad\Sigma_{2,1,0}=-(\Lambda_{i}+2\tilde{\lambda}_{i})\Lambda U(r)-\frac{|a|}{2}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}(\partial_{r}^{2}U+\frac{\partial_{r}U}{r})

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}&\mathscr{L}_{0}(\mathsf{S}_{2,1,2})=\Sigma_{2,1,2},\\
&-\Delta_{2}\mathsf{W}_{2,1,2}=\mathsf{S}_{2,1,2},\end{array}\right.\quad\Sigma_{2,1,0}=-\frac{\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}|a|(\partial_{r}^{2}U-\frac{\partial_{r}U}{r})

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}&\mathscr{L}_{0}(\mathsf{S}_{2,2,0})=\Sigma_{2,2,0},\\
&-\Delta\mathsf{W}_{2,2,0}=\mathsf{S}_{2,2,0},\end{array}\right.\qquad\Sigma_{2,2,0}=-\frac{|a|}{2}(\partial_{r}\mathsf{S}_{1,1,1}+\frac{\mathsf{S}_{1,1,1}}{r})

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}&\mathscr{L}_{0,2}(\mathsf{S}_{2,2,2})=\Sigma_{2,2,2},\\
&-\Delta_{2}\mathsf{W}_{2,2,2}=\mathsf{S}_{2,2,2},\end{array}\right.\qquad\Sigma_{2,2,2}=\frac{|a|}{2}(\frac{\mathsf{S}_{1,1,1}}{r}-\partial_{r}\mathsf{S}_{1,1,1})

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}&\mathscr{L}_{0,1}(\mathsf{S}_{3,0,1},\mathsf{W}_{3,0,1})=\Sigma_{3,0,1},\\
&-\Delta_{1}\mathsf{W}_{3,0,1}=\mathsf{S}_{3,0,1},\end{array}\right.\qquad\Sigma_{3,0,1}=\frac{5}{4}r\partial_{r}^{2}U+\frac{17-8i-8\tilde{\lambda}_{i}}{4}\partial_{r}U,

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}&\mathscr{L}_{0,1}(\mathsf{S}_{3,0,3},\mathsf{W}_{3,0,3})=\Sigma_{3,0,3},\\
&-\Delta_{3}\mathsf{W}_{3,0,3}=\mathsf{S}_{3,0,3},\end{array}\right.\qquad\Sigma_{3,0,3}=\frac{r}{4}\partial_{r}^{2}U-\frac{1}{4}\partial_{r}U,

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}&\mathscr{L}_{0,1}(\mathsf{S}_{3,1,1},\mathsf{W}_{3,1,1})=\Sigma_{3,1,1},\\
&-\Delta_{1}\mathsf{W}_{3,1,1}=\mathsf{S}_{3,1,1},\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}&\mathscr{L}_{0,3}(\mathsf{S}_{3,1,3},\mathsf{W}_{3,1,3})=\Sigma_{3,1,3},\\
&-\Delta_{3}\mathsf{W}_{3,1,3}=\mathsf{S}_{3,1,3},\end{array}\right.\quad\Sigma_{3,1,3}=\alpha(r\partial_{r}\mathsf{S}_{1,1,1}-\mathsf{S}_{1,1,1}+|a|(\frac{\mathsf{T}_{2,2}}{r}-\frac{\partial_{r}\mathsf{T}_{2,2}}{2}))

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}&\mathscr{L}_{0}(\mathsf{S}_{4,0,0})=\Sigma_{4,0,0},\\
&-\Delta\mathsf{W}_{4,0,0}=\mathsf{S}_{4,0,0},\end{array}\right.\qquad\Sigma_{4,0,0}=-\frac{1}{2|a|^{4}}\Lambda U+\frac{\alpha^{2}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}}{8\sqrt{2}}\left(\partial_{r}^{2}U+\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}U\right)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}&\mathscr{L}_{0}(\mathsf{S}_{4,0,2})=\Sigma_{4,0,2},\\
&-\Delta_{2}\mathsf{W}_{4,0,2}=\mathsf{S}_{4,0,2},\end{array}\right.\qquad\Sigma_{4,0,2}=-\frac{3}{24\sqrt{2}}(r^{2}\partial_{r}^{2}U+r\partial_{r}U)+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}^{2}U-\frac{1}{2r}\partial_{r}U

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}&\mathscr{L}_{0}(\mathsf{S}_{4,0,4})=\Sigma_{4,0,4},\\
&-\Delta_{4}\mathsf{W}_{4,0,4}=\mathsf{S}_{4,0,4},\end{array}\right.\qquad\Sigma_{4,0,4}=-\frac{3}{24\sqrt{2}}(r^{2}\partial_{r}^{2}U-r\partial_{r}U)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}&\mathscr{L}_{0}(\mathsf{S}_{4,0,2}^{\prime})=0,\\
&-\Delta_{2}\mathsf{W}_{4,0,2}^{\prime}=\mathsf{S}_{4,0,2}^{\prime},\end{array}\right.

where

\Sigma_{3,1,1}=\alpha\left(2r\partial_{r}\mathsf{S}_{1,1,1}+2(2-i)\mathsf{S}_{1,1,1}-\partial_{r}\mathsf{T}_{2,0}^{(i)}-\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\partial_{r}\hat{T}_{2}-\frac{\partial_{r}\mathsf{T}_{2,2}}{2}-\frac{\mathsf{T}_{2,2}}{r}-\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}(\Lambda_{i}+2\tilde{\lambda}_{i})\partial_{r}U\right).

3.1.6. The inner approximate eigenfunction

The following proposition provides an approximate expansion of the eigenfunction
\phi_{i}
in the inner zone
|y_{\pm}|\ll\nu^{-1}
.

Proposition 3.4

(Inner expansion of
\phi_{i}
)
.

There exists
\epsilon>0
such that the following holds true. Let
i=0,1
and pick any constants
\tilde{\lambda}_{i},L_{i}^{\textup{in}},M_{i}^{\textup{in}}\in\mathbb{R}
with
|\tilde{\lambda}_{i}|\lesssim|\ln\nu|^{-1}
and
|\tilde{L}_{i}^{\textup{in}}|+|\tilde{M}_{i}^{\textup{in}}|\lesssim|\ln\nu|
. There exists a smooth solution
(\phi^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm},V^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm},R^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm})
of (
3.16
) with the following properties.

(i)
Decomposition
. There holds

\displaystyle\phi^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}

\displaystyle=\Lambda U+\alpha\nu^{2}T_{2}^{(i)}\pm\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}\nu^{3}T_{3}+\alpha^{2}\nu^{4}T_{4}^{(i)}+\alpha\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\nu^{2}\hat{T}_{2}+\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\alpha^{2}\nu^{4}\hat{T}_{4}^{(i)}

(3.106)

\displaystyle\qquad\pm\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\partial_{x_{1}}U\pm\nu(\alpha-\beta)\mathcal{S}_{1,1}+\nu^{2}(\alpha-\beta)\mathcal{S}_{2,1}+\nu^{2}(\alpha-\beta)^{2}\mathcal{S}_{2,2}\pm\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}\nu^{3}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\mathcal{S}_{3,0}

\displaystyle\qquad\pm\nu^{3}(\alpha-\beta)\mathcal{S}_{3,1}+\nu^{4}\mathcal{S}_{4,0}+\alpha^{2}\nu^{4}M_{i}^{\textup{in}}\mathcal{S}_{4,0}^{\prime},

\displaystyle V^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}

\displaystyle=\Phi_{\Lambda U}+\alpha\nu^{2}V_{2}^{(i)}\pm\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}\nu^{3}V_{3}+\alpha^{2}\nu^{4}V_{4}^{(i)}+\alpha\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\nu^{2}\hat{V}_{2}+\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\alpha^{2}\nu^{4}\hat{V}_{4}^{(i)}

(3.107)

\displaystyle\qquad\pm\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\partial_{x_{1}}\Phi_{U}\pm\nu(\alpha-\beta)\mathcal{W}_{1,1}+\nu^{2}(\alpha-\beta)\mathcal{W}_{2,1}+\nu^{2}(\alpha-\beta)^{2}\mathcal{W}_{2,2}\pm\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}\nu^{3}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\mathcal{W}_{3,0}

\displaystyle\qquad\pm\nu^{3}(\alpha-\beta)\mathcal{W}_{3,1}+\nu^{4}\mathcal{W}_{4,0}+\alpha^{2}\nu^{4}M_{i}^{\textup{in}}\mathcal{W}_{4,0}^{\prime},

where the functions above are solutions of (
3.26
)-(
3.31
) and (
3.38
)-(
3.44
) given by the formulas (
3.47
)-(
3.53
) and (
3.73
)-(
3.79
) and where the functions in these formulas are given by Lemmas
3.5
,
3.6
,
3.7
,
3.7
,
3.8
,
3.9
and
3.10
and Proposition
3.11
.

(ii)
Expansion
. We have,

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{16\nu^{4}}\phi^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}(y_{\pm})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\zeta_{\pm}^{4}}+\frac{\alpha}{\zeta_{\pm}^{2}}\left(\frac{i}{2}+\frac{1}{4}\cos 2\theta_{\pm}\right)\mp\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}\frac{1}{12\sqrt{2}\zeta_{\pm}}\cos(3\theta_{\pm})

(3.108)

\displaystyle+\alpha^{2}\left(\frac{i}{4}-\frac{7}{64}+\frac{2i-1}{8}\cos(2\theta_{\pm})+\frac{1}{32}\cos(4\theta_{\pm})\right)

\displaystyle+\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\left(-\frac{\alpha}{2\zeta_{\pm}^{2}}+\alpha^{2}\left((\frac{i}{2}-\frac{1}{4})(\ln\zeta_{\pm}-\ln\nu)+\frac{1-10i}{16}-\frac{1}{4}\cos(2\theta_{\pm})\right)\right)+\phi_{i,\pm,\sharp}^{\textup{in},z}(z_{\pm}),

where for
\nu\lesssim|z_{\pm}|=\zeta_{\pm}\lesssim 1
:

|\nabla^{k}\phi_{i,\pm,\sharp}^{\textup{in},z}(z_{\pm})|\lesssim\nu^{\epsilon}(\nu+\zeta_{\pm})^{-4-k-\epsilon}

(3.109)

and

\displaystyle\frac{-1}{16\nu^{2}}V^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}(y_{\pm})

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{4\zeta^{2}_{\pm}}\pm\frac{\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}}{4}\frac{1}{\zeta_{\pm}}\cos(\theta)

\displaystyle+\alpha\Bigg(\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{i}-i}{4}\ln^{2}\zeta_{\pm}+\left(\frac{i-\tilde{\lambda}_{i}}{2}\ln\nu+\frac{2i+1-2\tilde{\lambda}_{i})}{4}\right)\ln\zeta_{\pm}+\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{i}-i}{4}\ln^{2}\nu+\frac{2\tilde{\lambda}_{i}-1-2i}{4}\ln\nu+\frac{1}{16}\cos(2\theta)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\mp\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}\zeta_{\pm}\left(L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\frac{1-4i+4\tilde{\lambda}_{i}}{32}\cos\theta+\frac{1}{96\sqrt{2}}\cos(3\theta_{\pm})\right)

\displaystyle+\alpha^{2}\zeta^{2}_{\pm}\Bigg(\frac{1-2i}{16}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\ln\zeta_{\pm}+\frac{2i-1}{16}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\ln\nu+\frac{2\tilde{\lambda}_{i}+1-2i}{32}\ln\zeta_{\pm}\cos(2\theta_{\pm})+\frac{2i-1-\tilde{\lambda}_{i}}{32}\ln\nu\cos(2\theta)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{16}\left(\frac{7}{16}-i+\tilde{\lambda}_{i}(\frac{9}{2}i-\frac{5}{4})\right)+\frac{5-10i+10\tilde{\lambda}_{i}-8M_{i}^{\textup{in}}}{128}\cos(2\theta_{\pm})+\frac{1}{384}\cos(4\theta_{\pm})\Bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad+V^{\textup{in},z}_{i,\pm,\sharp}(z_{\pm})

(3.110)

where for
\nu\lesssim|z_{\pm}|=\zeta_{\pm}\lesssim 1
for

Moreover, assuming

(3.112)

\displaystyle|\nabla^{k}\nu\partial_{\nu}(\phi^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}-\Lambda U\mp\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\partial_{x_{1}}U)|\lesssim\nu\langle y\rangle^{-2-k},\quad|\nabla^{k}\partial_{a}(\phi^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}-\Lambda U\mp\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\partial_{x_{1}}U)|\lesssim\langle y\rangle^{-2-k}.

(3.113)

\displaystyle\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}-\Lambda U-\alpha\nu^{2}T^{(i)}_{2}-\alpha\nu^{2}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\hat{T}_{2}\mp\nu(\alpha-\beta)\mathcal{S}_{1,1}\mp\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\partial_{x_{1}}U

\displaystyle\qquad=\pm\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}\nu^{3}T_{3}+\alpha^{2}\nu^{4}T_{4}^{(i)}+\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\alpha^{2}\nu^{4}\hat{T}_{4}^{(i)}+\bar{\phi}_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}\mp\nu(\alpha-\beta)\mathcal{S}_{1,1}\mp\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\partial_{x_{1}}U

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{3}\langle y\rangle^{-1})+\mathcal{O}(\nu^{4})+\mathcal{O}(\nu^{4})+\mathcal{O}(\nu^{3}\langle y\rangle^{-2})=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{3}\langle y\rangle^{-1}),

(3.143)

\displaystyle\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}-\Lambda U\mp\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\partial_{x_{1}}U=\mathcal{O}(\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\langle y\rangle^{-2})+\mathcal{O}(\nu^{3}\langle y\rangle^{-1})+\mathcal{O}(\nu^{2}\langle y\rangle^{-2})=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{2}\langle y\rangle^{-2}),

(3.144)

so that

\displaystyle\pm\nu(\alpha-\beta)a.\nabla(\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}-\Lambda U-\alpha\nu^{2}T^{(i)}_{2}-\alpha\nu^{2}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\hat{T}_{2}\mp\nu(\alpha-\beta)\mathcal{S}_{1,1})

\displaystyle\qquad+\nu^{2}(\alpha-\beta)\left(\Lambda+\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\beta}\right)(\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}-\Lambda U)=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{5}\langle y\rangle^{-2}).

(3.145)

For the fifth, we have that
U\Big(y\pm\frac{2a}{\nu}\Big)=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{4})
and
U\Big(y\pm\frac{2a}{\nu}\Big)-\frac{\nu^{4}}{2|a|^{2}}=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{5}\langle y\rangle)
for
|y|\ll\nu^{-1}
. Hence by (
3.144
):

\displaystyle\left(U\Big(y\pm\frac{2a}{\nu}\Big)-\frac{\nu^{4}}{2|a|^{4}}\right)\Lambda U+U\Big(y\pm\frac{2a}{\nu}\Big)(\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}-\Lambda U)

(3.146)

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{5}\langle y\rangle)\mathcal{O}(\langle y\rangle^{-4})+\mathcal{O}(\nu^{4})\mathcal{O}(\nu^{2}\langle y\rangle^{-2})=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{5}\langle y\rangle^{-3}).

(3.147)

For the sixth, by (
3.108
) and (
3.109
), and (
3.110
) and (
3.111
), and (
3.3
) and
\nabla U(y\pm\frac{2a}{\nu})=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{5})
for
|y|\ll\nu^{-1}
:

\displaystyle-\nabla U\Big(y\pm\frac{2a}{\nu}\Big).\nabla V^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}-\nabla.(\phi^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}\tilde{G}_{\pm})

(3.148)

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{5})(\mathcal{O}(\langle y\rangle^{-3})+\mathcal{O}(\nu^{2}\langle y\rangle^{-1}\langle\ln\langle y\rangle\rangle)+\nabla.(\mathcal{O}(\langle y\rangle^{-4})\mathcal{O}(\nu^{5}\langle y\rangle^{4}))=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{5}\langle y\rangle^{-1})

(3.149)

Injecting (
3.139
), (
3.141
), (
3.142
), (
3.145
), (
3.146
) and (
3.148
) in (
3.45
) shows the desired estimate (
3.114
), as all estimates in the
\mathcal{O}()
’s above propagate to higher order derivatives.

∎

3.1.7. Computation of the leading profiles

In what follows, to lighten notation, the notation
\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}()
will implicitly include estimates for derivatives. Namely, for
f\in C^{\infty}((0,\infty),\mathbb{R})
and
g:(0,\infty)\rightarrow(0,\infty)
we will write

f(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(g(r))

if for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we have for all
r\geq 1
:

|\partial_{r}^{k}f(r)|\lesssim r^{-k}g(r).

Throughout the analysis, we will treat with special care two natural obstacles for solving the systems (
3.63
), (
3.64
), (
3.1.5
) and (
3.1.5
). Indeed, the formal adjoint of
\mathscr{L}_{0}
in
L^{2}
is
\mathscr{L}_{0}^{*}\varepsilon=\mathscr{M}_{0}\nabla.(U\nabla\varepsilon)
, and
\mathscr{L}_{0}^{*}(1)=\mathscr{L}_{0}^{*}(y_{1})=0
(from
[
RS14a
]
, Lemma 2.4), which comes from the conservation of mass and momentum of the Keller-Segel system (
1.1
). Therefore, if the source terms in these elliptic systems have zero mass and momentum, we will find decaying solutions, while if not we will find solutions which grow as
|y|\to\infty
.

Computing
(T_{2,0}^{(i)},V_{2,0}^{(i)})
and
(\hat{T}_{2},\hat{V}_{2})
:

We claim the following.

Lemma 3.5
.

Let
i=0,1
and for any constants
c_{2,0}^{(0)},c_{2,0}^{(1)},\hat{c}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
, the equations (
3.63
) admit smooth solutions
(T_{2,0}^{(i)},c_{2,0}^{(i)}+V_{2,0}^{(i)})
and
(\hat{T}_{2},\hat{c}_{2}+\hat{V}_{2})
such that

T_{2,0}^{(0)}=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}\Big(\frac{\ln r}{r^{4}}\Big),\quad V_{2,0}^{(0)}=-4\ln r+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}\Big(\frac{\ln r}{r^{2}}\Big),

(3.150)

T_{2,0}^{(1)}=-\frac{8}{r^{2}}+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}\Big(\frac{\ln^{2}r}{r^{4}}\Big),\quad V_{2,0}^{(1)}=4\ln^{2}r-12\ln r+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}\Big(\frac{\ln^{2}r}{r^{2}}\Big),

(3.151)

\hat{T}_{2}=\frac{8}{r^{2}}+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-4}|\ln r|^{2}),\quad\hat{V}_{2,0}=-4\ln^{2}r+8\ln r+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}\Big(\frac{\ln^{2}r}{r^{2}}\Big),

(3.152)

and

|r^{2}T_{2,0}^{(i)}|+|r\partial_{r}V_{2,0}^{(i)}|=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(r^{2}),\qquad|r^{2}\hat{T}_{2,0}|+|r\partial_{r}\hat{V}_{2,0}|=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(r^{2}).

Moreover, these estimates propagate for higher order derivatives.

Proof.

Equation (
3.63
) can be solved by introducing the partial mass

m_{2,0}^{(i)}(r)=\int_{0}^{r}T_{2,0}^{(i)}(s)sds,\quad T_{2,0}^{(i)}(r)=r^{-1}\partial_{r}m_{2,0}^{(i)},\quad\partial_{r}V_{2,0}^{(i)}(r)=-r^{-1}m_{2,0}^{(i)}.

Here,
m_{2,0}^{(i)}
solves the equation

\mathscr{A}_{0}m_{2,0}^{(i)}=g^{(i)}_{2,0}\quad\textup{with}\;\;g^{(i)}_{2,0}=\int_{0}^{r}\Lambda_{i}\Lambda U(\zeta)\zeta d\zeta=r^{2i-1}\partial_{r}\big(r^{4-2i}U\big).

where
\mathscr{A}_{0}
is the second order operator

\mathscr{A}_{0}=\partial_{r}^{2}-\frac{\partial_{r}}{r}+\frac{Q}{r}\partial_{r}+\frac{\partial_{r}Q}{r}\quad\textup{with}\quad Q=m_{U}=\frac{4r^{2}}{1+r^{2}}.

(3.153)

We compute by using Lemma
A.1
with
g=g^{(1)}_{2,0}
to get

\displaystyle m_{2,0}^{(1)}(r)

\displaystyle=8(r^{-2}+O(r^{-4}))\int_{1}^{r}(\zeta+O(\zeta^{-1}\ln\zeta))d\zeta

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{1}{2}(1+O(r^{-2}\ln r))\int_{0}^{r}\zeta^{2}\partial_{\zeta}(\zeta^{2}U)d\zeta,

\displaystyle=4+\mathcal{O}\Big(\frac{\ln^{2}r}{r^{2}}\Big)+\frac{1}{2}r^{4}U(r)-8\ln r+4+\mathcal{O}\Big(\frac{\ln^{2}r}{r^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle=-8\ln r+12+\mathcal{O}\Big(\frac{\ln^{2}r}{r^{2}}\Big)\quad\textup{as}\;\;r\to\infty,

(3.154)

where we used
\int_{0}^{r}\zeta^{3}Ud\zeta=4\big(\log(1+r^{2})+(1+r^{2})^{-1}-1\big)
.
By noting that
g_{2,0}^{(0)}
has a better decay than
g_{2,0}^{(1)}
at infinity, which is
g_{2,0}^{(0)}=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-4})
, we then compute from Lemma
A.1
,

\displaystyle m_{2,0}^{(0)}(r)

\displaystyle=8(r^{-2}+O(r^{-4}))\int_{1}^{r}O(\zeta^{-1})d\zeta+\frac{1}{2}(1+O(r^{-2}\ln r))\int_{0}^{r}\partial_{\zeta}(\zeta^{4}U)d\zeta,

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\Big(\frac{\ln r}{r^{2}}\Big)+\frac{1}{2}r^{4}U(r)=4+\mathcal{O}\Big(\frac{\ln r}{r^{2}}\Big)\quad\textup{as}\;\;r\to\infty.

(3.155)

Similarly, we solve (
3.63
) by applying Lemma
A.1
with
g=2m_{\Lambda U}=2r^{2}U
to obtain

\displaystyle\hat{m}_{2}

\displaystyle=-8(r^{-2}+O(r^{-4}))\int_{1}^{r}(\zeta+O(\zeta^{-1}\ln\zeta))d\zeta+(1+O(r^{-2}\ln r))\int_{0}^{r}\zeta^{3}Ud\zeta,

\displaystyle=8\ln r-8+O(r^{-2}|\ln r|^{2}),\quad\mbox{as }r\to\infty.

(3.156)

The asymptotic behavior of
m_{2,0}^{(0)}
,
m_{2,0}^{(1)}
and
\hat{m}_{2}
near zero is a direct consequence of Lemma
A.1
and we omit the details. All estimates propagate for higher order derivative from a direct check at the identities (
3.154
) and (
3.155
) using the formulas (
A.3
) and (
A.2
). This concludes the proof of Lemma
3.5
.
∎

Computing
\mathsf{T}_{2,2}
and
\mathsf{V}_{2,2}
:

We are going to apply Lemma
A.2
and
A.3
to solve the system (
3.1.5
).

Lemma 3.6
.

There exists a smooth solution
(\mathsf{T}_{2,2},\mathsf{V}_{2,2})
of (
3.1.5
) for
k=2
such that

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}_{2,2}(r)=-4r^{-2}+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-\sqrt{8}}),\qquad\mathsf{V}_{2,2}(r)=-1+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{2-\sqrt{8}}),

(3.157)

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}_{2,2}(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(r^{2}),\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\mathsf{V}_{2,2}(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(r^{2}).

(3.158)

Proof.

We apply Lemma
A.3
with
\ell=2
and get that the solution is of the form

\mathsf{T}_{2,2}(r)=\sum_{k=1}^{4}h_{2,k}(r)\gamma_{2,k}(r),\quad\mathsf{V}_{2,2}(r)=\sum_{k=1}^{4}g_{2,k}(r)\gamma_{2,k}(r),

(3.159)

where
(h_{2,k},g_{2,k})_{1\leq k\leq 4}
are given by Lemma
A.2
and

\displaystyle\gamma_{2,k}(r)

\displaystyle=\bar{\gamma}_{2,k}+(-1)^{k+1}\int_{0}^{r}\frac{\Sigma_{2,2}(s)W_{2,k}(s)}{W_{2}}ds

\displaystyle=\bar{\gamma}_{2,k}+\frac{(-1)^{k}}{3\cdot 2^{12}}\int_{0}^{r}\Sigma_{2,2}(s)W_{2,k}(s)s^{2}(1+s^{2})^{2}ds,

for arbitrary constants
(\bar{\gamma}_{2,k})_{1\leq k\leq 4}
. We recall that from (
A.16
) and (
A.18
),
(W_{2,k})_{1\leq k\leq 4}
admit the following asymptotics, for non-zero constants
(c_{2,k,0})_{1\leq k\leq 4}
:

\displaystyle W_{2,1}

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}c_{2,1,0}r^{-3}(1+o_{r\to 0}(1)),\\
-3\cdot 2^{8}r^{-5}(1+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{2-\sqrt{8}})),\end{array}\right.\quad W_{2,3}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}c_{2,3,0}r^{-3}(1+o_{r\to 0}(1)),\\
-3\cdot 2^{6}C_{2}r^{-2\sqrt{2}-3}(1+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{2-\sqrt{8}})),\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle W_{2,2}

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}c_{2,2,0}r(1+o_{r\to 0}(1)),\\
2^{8}r^{-1}(1+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{2-\sqrt{8}})),\end{array}\right.\quad W_{2,4}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}c_{2,4,0}r(1+o_{r\to 0}(1)),\\
-3\cdot 2^{6}K_{2}r^{2\sqrt{2}-3}(1+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{2-\sqrt{8}})).\end{array}\right.

Since
h_{2,2}
and
h_{2,4}
are singular at the origin, we choose
\bar{\gamma}_{2,2}=\bar{\gamma}_{2,4}=0
. We further choose for
k=1,3
,

\bar{\gamma}_{2,k}=\frac{1}{3\cdot 2^{12}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Sigma_{2,2}(s)W_{2,k}(s)s^{2}(1+s^{2})^{2}ds<+\infty,

to avoid growth at infinity. We have by (
3.57
),

\Sigma_{2,2}(r)=32r^{-4}+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-6}).

We then obtain

\displaystyle\gamma_{2,1}(r),\gamma_{2,3}(r)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(1),\quad\gamma_{2,2}(r),\gamma_{2,4}(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(r^{6}),

and as
r\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\gamma_{2,1}(r)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{3\cdot 2^{12}}\int_{r}^{\infty}2^{13}\cdot 3s^{-3}(1+\mathcal{O}_{s\to\infty}(s^{2-\sqrt{8}}))ds=-r^{-2}(1+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{2-\sqrt{8}})),

\displaystyle\gamma_{2,2}(r)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{3\cdot 2^{12}}\int_{0}^{r}2^{13}s(1+\mathcal{O}_{s\to\infty}(s^{2-\sqrt{8}}))ds=-\frac{1}{3}r^{2}(1+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{2-\sqrt{8}})),

\displaystyle\gamma_{2,3}(r)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{3\cdot 2^{12}}\int_{r}^{\infty}2^{11}\cdot 3C_{2}s^{-\sqrt{8}-1}(1+\mathcal{O}_{s\to\infty}(s^{2-\sqrt{8}}))ds=-\frac{C_{2}}{4\sqrt{2}}r^{-\sqrt{8}}(1+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{2-\sqrt{8}})),

\displaystyle\gamma_{2,4}(r)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{3\cdot 2^{12}}\int_{r}^{\infty}2^{11}\cdot 3K_{2}s^{\sqrt{8}-1}(1+\mathcal{O}_{s\to\infty}(s^{2-\sqrt{8}}))ds=-\frac{K_{2}}{4\sqrt{2}}r^{\sqrt{8}}(1+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{2-\sqrt{8}})).

Using the asymptotic behaviors of
(h_{2,k},g_{2,k})
given in Lemma
A.2
, we directly get (
3.158
) and obtain, as
r\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}_{2,2}(r)=\sum_{k=1}^{4}\gamma_{2,k}h_{2,k}

\displaystyle=-r^{-2}(1+O(r^{2-\sqrt{8}}))8r^{-2}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{-2}))-\frac{1}{3}r^{2}(1+O(r^{2-\sqrt{8}}))24r^{-6}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{-2}))

\displaystyle-\frac{C_{2}}{4\sqrt{2}}r^{-\sqrt{8}}(1+O(r^{2-\sqrt{8}}))16K_{2}r^{\sqrt{8}-2}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{-2}))-\frac{K_{2}}{4\sqrt{2}}r^{\sqrt{8}}(1+O(r^{2-\sqrt{8}}))16C_{2}r^{-\sqrt{8}-2}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{\sqrt{8}-4}))

\displaystyle=-4r^{-2}+\mathcal{O}(r^{-\sqrt{8}})

where we used in the last line
C_{2}K_{2}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}
, and

\displaystyle\mathsf{V}_{2,2}(r)=\sum_{k=1}^{4}\gamma_{2,k}g_{2,k}

\displaystyle=-r^{-2}(1+O(r^{2-\sqrt{8}}))r^{2}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{-2}))-\frac{1}{3}r^{2}(1+O(r^{2-\sqrt{8}}))3r^{-2}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{-2}))

\displaystyle-\frac{C_{2}}{4\sqrt{2}}r^{-\sqrt{8}}(1+O(r^{2-\sqrt{8}}))(-4K_{2}r^{\sqrt{8}}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{-2})))-\frac{K_{2}}{4\sqrt{2}}r^{\sqrt{8}}(1+O(r^{2-\sqrt{8}}))(-4C_{2}r^{-\sqrt{8}})(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{\sqrt{8}-4}))

\displaystyle=-1+\mathcal{O}(r^{2-\sqrt{8}}).

We notice that the estimates indeed propagate for higher order derivatives by a direct check at the formulas (
3.159
) because of the asymptotic behaviour of the functions in Lemmas
A.3
and
A.2
. This concludes the proof of Lemma
3.6
.
∎

Computing
T_{3}
and
V_{3}
:

We use Lemmas
A.2
and
A.3
to solve (
3.1.5
) for
k=3
.

Lemma 3.7
.

There exist smooth solutions
(\mathsf{T}_{3,k},V_{3,k})
to (
3.1.5
) for
k=1,3
such that

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}_{3,1}(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(r),\quad\mathsf{V}_{3,1}(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(r),

(3.168)

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}_{3,1}(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}\Big(r^{-3}\Big),\quad\mathsf{V}_{3,1}(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(\frac{\ln r}{r}).

(3.169)

and

\mathsf{T}_{3,3}(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(r^{3}),\quad\mathsf{V}_{3,3}(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(r^{3}),

(3.170)

\mathsf{T}_{3,3}(r)=\frac{4}{3\sqrt{2}}r^{-1}+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}\Big(r^{2-\sqrt{13}}\Big),\quad\mathsf{V}_{3,3}(r)=\frac{1}{6\sqrt{2}}r+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{4-\sqrt{13}}).

(3.171)

The estimates propagate for higher order derivatives.

Proof.

We recall from (
3.1.5
),

\displaystyle k=1,3,\quad\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}\mathscr{L}_{0,k}(\mathsf{T}_{3,k},\mathsf{V}_{3,k})=\Sigma_{3,k},\\
-\Delta_{k}\mathsf{V}_{3,k}=\mathsf{T}_{3,k}.\end{array}\right.

Since solving for
\big(\mathsf{T}_{3,k},\mathsf{V}_{3,k}\big)
for
k=1,3
is similar as solving for
\big(\mathsf{T}_{2,2},\mathsf{V}_{2,2}\big)
that we have detailed in the proof of Lemma
3.6
, we will only detail the main new points.

Step 1
.
Computing
\mathsf{T}_{3,1}
. There is a surprising cancellation for the source term, as it has no horizontal momentum:

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}y_{1}\nabla.(\Lambda UG_{2})dy=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Lambda U(1,0).G_{2}dy=\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Lambda U(y_{1}^{2}-y_{2}^{2}-1)dy=0

where we used that
\Lambda U
is spherically symmetric with
\int\Lambda U=0
, which implies in polar coordinates

\int_{0}^{\infty}\Sigma_{3,1}(r)r^{2}dr=0.

(3.174)

This cancellation permits to find a smooth and decaying solution
(\mathsf{T}_{3,1}\mathsf{V}_{3,1})
of (
3.1.5
). Namely, by applying Lemma
A.3
with
k=1
we have a solution of the form

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}_{3,1}=\sum_{k=1}^{4}\gamma_{1,k}h_{1,k},\quad\mathsf{V}_{3,1}=\sum_{k=1}^{4}\gamma_{1,k}g_{1,k},

(3.175)

where, using
\Sigma_{3,1}=O_{r\to 0}(r)
and
\Sigma_{3,1}=O_{r\to\infty}(r^{-5})
,

\displaystyle\gamma_{1,1}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{r}\frac{\Sigma_{3,1}W_{1,1}}{W_{1}}ds=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(\int_{0}^{\infty}sds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(r^{2}),\\
\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(\int_{r}^{\infty}s\langle s\rangle^{-2}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(\ln r),\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\gamma_{1,2}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{r}\frac{\Sigma_{3,1}W_{1,2}}{W_{1}}ds=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(\int_{0}^{r}s^{3}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(r^{4}),\\
\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(\int_{r}^{\infty}s^{-3}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-2}),\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\gamma_{1,3}

\displaystyle=-\int_{r}^{\infty}\frac{\Sigma_{3,1}W_{1,3}}{W_{1}}ds=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(\int_{0}^{\infty}s\langle s\rangle^{-\sqrt{5}-3}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(1),\\
\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(\int_{r}^{\infty}s^{-\sqrt{5}-2}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-\sqrt{5}-1}),\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\gamma_{1,4}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{r}\frac{\Sigma_{3,1}W_{1,4}}{W_{1}}ds=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(\int_{0}^{r}s^{3}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(r^{4}),\\
\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(\int_{0}^{r}r^{3}\langle r\rangle^{\sqrt{5}-5})=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{\sqrt{5}-1}).\end{array}\right.

In the second identity, we used that
\int_{0}^{r}\frac{\Sigma_{3,1}W_{1,2}}{W_{1}}ds=-\int_{r}^{\infty}\frac{\Sigma_{3,1}W_{1,2}}{W_{1}}ds
by (
3.174
) and the fact that
\frac{W_{1,2}}{W_{1}}=\frac{2\sqrt{5}+1}{24}r^{2}
from (
A.14
). Using the asymptotic behaviors of
(h_{1,k},g_{1,k})
given in Lemma
A.2
and the above asymptotic estimates yield the desired estimates (
3.168
) and (
3.169
).

Step 2
.
Computing
\mathsf{T}_{3,3}
. We apply Lemma
A.3
with
\ell=3
and
f=\Sigma_{3,3}
, where we compute from (
3.58
),

\Sigma_{3,3}=O_{r\to 0}(r^{3}),\qquad\Sigma_{3,3}(r)=-\frac{16}{\sqrt{2}}r^{-3}+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-5}).

We then obtain a solution of the form

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}_{3,3}=\sum_{k=1}^{4}\gamma_{3,k}h_{3,k},\quad\mathsf{V}_{3,3}=\sum_{k=1}^{4}\gamma_{3,k}g_{3,k}.

(3.176)

We choose the integration constants so that

\displaystyle\gamma_{3,1}

\displaystyle=-\int_{r}^{\infty}\frac{\Sigma_{3,3}W_{3,1}}{W_{3}}ds,\qquad\gamma_{3,2}=-\int_{0}^{r}\frac{\Sigma_{3,3}W_{3,2}}{W_{3}}ds

\displaystyle\gamma_{3,3}

\displaystyle=-\int_{r}^{\infty}\frac{\Sigma_{3,3}W_{3,3}}{W_{3}}ds,\qquad\gamma_{3,4}=-\int_{0}^{r}\frac{\Sigma_{3,3}W_{3,4}}{W_{3}}ds.

The regularity of
(\mathsf{T}_{3,3},\mathsf{V}_{3,3})
as
r\to 0
can be checked the same way we did for
(\mathsf{T}_{3,1},\mathsf{V}_{3,1})
. To obtain the expansion as
r\to\infty
we compute from (
A.18
):

\displaystyle W_{3,1}(r)=-3\cdot 2^{9}r^{-6}(1+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{3-\sqrt{13}})),\qquad W_{3,2}(r)=-3\cdot 2^{8}(1+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{3-\sqrt{13}})),

\displaystyle W_{3,3}(r)=-3\cdot 2^{8}C_{\ell}r^{-\sqrt{13}-3}(1+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{3-\sqrt{13}})),\qquad W_{3,4}(r)=-3\cdot 2^{8}K_{\ell}r^{\sqrt{13}-3}(1+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{3-\sqrt{13}}).

Recalling that
W_{3}(r)=-9\cdot 2^{13}r^{-6}+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-8})
we obtain

\displaystyle\gamma_{3,1}=\int_{r}^{\infty}\frac{1}{3\sqrt{2}}s^{-3}(1+\mathcal{O}(s^{3-\sqrt{13}}))ds=\frac{1}{6\sqrt{2}}r^{-2}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{3-\sqrt{13}})),

\displaystyle\gamma_{3,2}=\int_{0}^{r}\frac{1}{6\sqrt{2}}s^{3}(1+\mathcal{O}(s^{3-\sqrt{13}}))ds=\frac{1}{24\sqrt{2}}r^{4}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{3-\sqrt{13}})),

\displaystyle\gamma_{3,3}=\int_{r}^{\infty}\frac{C_{\ell}}{6\sqrt{2}}s^{-\sqrt{13}}(1+\mathcal{O}(s^{3-\sqrt{13}}))ds=\frac{C_{\ell}}{6\sqrt{2}(\sqrt{13}-1)}r^{1-\sqrt{13}}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{3-\sqrt{13}})),

\displaystyle\gamma_{3,4}=\int_{0}^{r}\frac{K_{\ell}}{6\sqrt{2}}s^{\sqrt{13}}(1+\mathcal{O}(s^{3-\sqrt{13}}))ds=\frac{K_{\ell}}{6\sqrt{2}(\sqrt{13}+1)}r^{1+\sqrt{13}}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{3-\sqrt{13}})),

from what we deduce, using Lemma
A.2
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}_{3,3}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{4}\gamma_{3,k}h_{3,k}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(r^{-2})\mathcal{O}(r^{-1})+\mathcal{O}(r^{4})\mathcal{O}(r^{-7})+\frac{C_{\ell}r^{1-\sqrt{13}}}{6\sqrt{2}(\sqrt{13}-1)}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{3-\sqrt{13}}))16K_{\ell}r^{\sqrt{13}-2}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{-2}))

\displaystyle+\frac{K_{\ell}r^{1+\sqrt{13}}}{6\sqrt{2}(\sqrt{13}+1)}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{3-\sqrt{13}}))16C_{\ell}r^{-\sqrt{13}-2}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{\sqrt{13}-5}))

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{3\sqrt{2}}r^{-1}(1+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{3-\sqrt{13}}))

where we used that
K_{3}C_{3}=\frac{3}{\sqrt{13}}
, and

\displaystyle\mathsf{V}_{3,3}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{4}\gamma_{3,k}g_{3,k}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6\sqrt{2}}r^{-2}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{3-\sqrt{13}}))2r^{3}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{-2}))+\frac{1}{24\sqrt{2}}r^{4}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{3-\sqrt{13}}))4r^{-3}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{-2}))

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{\ell}r^{1-\sqrt{13}}}{6\sqrt{2}(\sqrt{13}-1)}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{3-\sqrt{13}}))(-4K_{\ell}r^{\sqrt{13}-2})(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{-2}))

\displaystyle+\frac{K_{\ell}r^{1+\sqrt{13}}}{6\sqrt{2}(\sqrt{13}+1)}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{3-\sqrt{13}}))(-4C_{\ell}r^{-\sqrt{13}-2})(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{\sqrt{13}-5}))

\displaystyle=\frac{r}{6\sqrt{2}}(1+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{3-\sqrt{13}}))

This concludes the proof of (
3.170
) and (
3.171
). The estimates (
3.168
), (
3.169
), (
3.170
) and (
3.171
) propagate for higher order derivative from a direct check at the identities (
3.175
) and (
3.176
) using the asymptotic behaviours of the functions in Lemmas
A.3
and
A.2
.
∎

and

Lemma 3.8
.

Let
i=0,1
and
T_{2,0}^{(0)}
,

,
\hat{T}_{2}
and

be described as in Lemmas

and

. For any constants

,
\hat{c}_{4,0}^{(0)},\hat{c}_{4,0}^{(1)}
, the systems (
3.64
) admit smooth solutions
(T_{4,0}^{(i)},c_{4,0}^{(i)}+V_{4,0}^{(i)})
and
(\hat{T}_{4,0}^{(i)},\hat{c}_{4,0}^{(i)}+\hat{V}_{4,0}^{(i)})
such that for
r\to\infty
,

T_{4,0}^{(i)}=\frac{7}{4}-4i+\mathcal{O}(r^{2-\sqrt{8}}),\quad V_{4,0}^{(i)}=\Big(i-\frac{7}{16}\Big)r^{2}+\mathcal{O}(r^{4-\sqrt{8}}),

(3.177)

\displaystyle\hat{T}_{4,0}^{(i)}

\displaystyle=(4-8i)\ln r+10i-1+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-2}\ln^{2}r),

(3.178)

\displaystyle\hat{V}_{4,0}^{(i)}

\displaystyle=(2i-1)r^{2}\ln r+\Big(\frac{5}{4}-\frac{9}{2}i\Big)r^{2}+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(\ln^{3}r),

(3.179)

where
B_{2}
is the free parameter introduced in Lemma
3.6
. The estimates propagate to higher order derivatives.

Proof.

Note that there is a tail cancellation in
\Lambda T_{2}
(since
\Lambda(r^{-2})=0
), from which we introduce two different profiles
T_{4}
and
\hat{T}_{4}
. Roughly speaking,
T_{4}
and
\hat{T}_{4}
gather all terms of the order
\mathcal{O}(\nu^{4})
and
\mathcal{O}(\tilde{\lambda}_{1}\nu^{4})
respectively, so that the generated error term will be of the order
\mathcal{O}(\tilde{\lambda}_{i}^{2}\nu^{4})\sim\mathcal{O}(\nu^{4}|\ln\nu|^{-2})
in the blowup variables, which is the typical size to successfully control the remainder.

Since
\Sigma_{4,0}^{(i)}
is radial, we use the partial mass to solve for
T_{4,0}^{(i)}
. We recall from (
3.59
),

\Sigma_{4,0}^{(i)}=\Lambda_{i}T_{2,0}^{(i)}+\frac{1}{4}\Lambda\mathsf{T}_{2,2}-\frac{1}{16}\Lambda^{2}U,\quad\Lambda_{i}=\Lambda+2(1-i).

From (
3.151
), (
3.150
), (
3.157
), (
3.155
) and the identities
\Lambda_{i}f(r)=r^{2i-3}\partial_{r}(r^{4-2i}f)
, we get

\displaystyle g_{4,0}^{(i)}(r)=\int_{0}^{r}\Sigma_{4,0}^{(i)}(\zeta)\zeta d\zeta

\displaystyle=r^{2}T_{2,0}^{(i)}+2(1-i)m_{2,0}^{(i)}+\frac{1}{4}r^{2}\mathsf{T}_{2,2}-\frac{1}{16}r^{2}\Lambda U

\displaystyle=7-16i+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}\Big(r^{2-\sqrt{8}}\Big),

Applying Lemma
A.1
to solve
\mathscr{A}_{0}m^{(i)}_{4,0}=g_{4,0}^{(i)}
yields

m_{4,0}^{(i)}=\Big(\frac{7}{8}-2i\Big)r^{2}+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{4-\sqrt{8}}),

hence,

\displaystyle T_{4,0}^{(i)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial_{r}m_{4,0}^{(i)}}{r}=\frac{7}{4}-4i+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{2-\sqrt{8}}),

(3.180)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}V_{4,0}^{(i)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{m_{4,0}^{(i)}}{r}=\Big(2i-\frac{7}{8}\Big)r+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{3-\sqrt{8}}).

(3.181)

Similarly, solving
\hat{T}_{4,0}^{(i)}
is reduced to solving
\mathscr{A}_{0}\hat{m}_{4,0}^{(i)}=\hat{g}_{4,0}^{(i)}
, where we compute asymptotically from (
3.62
) and Lemma
3.5
,

\displaystyle\hat{g}_{4,0}^{(i)}

\displaystyle=m_{2T_{2,0}^{(i)}+\Lambda_{i}\hat{T}_{2}}=2m_{2,0}^{(i)}+m_{\Lambda_{i}\hat{T}_{2}}

\displaystyle=2m_{2,0}^{(i)}+r\partial_{r}\hat{m}_{2}+2(1-i)\hat{m}_{2}=(16-32i)\ln r+32i+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-2}\ln^{2}r),

where we used (
3.154
) (
3.155
) and (
3.156
). Applying Lemma
A.1
with
g=g_{4,0}^{(i)}
yields

\displaystyle\hat{m}_{4,0}^{(i)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2r^{2}}\int_{r}^{1}\zeta^{3}\big[(16-32i)\ln\zeta+32i\big]d\zeta+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{r}\zeta\big[(16-32i)\ln\zeta+32i\big]d\zeta+\mathcal{O}(\ln^{2}r)

\displaystyle=(2-4i)r^{2}\ln r+\big(7i-\frac{3}{2}\big)r^{2}+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(\ln^{2}r).

hence,

\displaystyle\hat{T}_{4,0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial_{r}\hat{m}_{4,0}^{(i)}}{r}=(4-8i)\ln r+10i-1+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-2}\ln^{2}r),

(3.182)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\hat{V}_{4,0}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\hat{m}_{4,0}^{(i)}}{r}=(4i-2)r\ln r+\big(\frac{3}{2}-7i\big)r+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-1}\ln^{2}r).

(3.183)

This ends the proof of Lemma
3.8
. The estimates (
3.179
), (
3.178
) and (
3.177
) propagate to higher order derivatives by a direct check at the identities (
3.180
), (
3.181
), (
3.182
) and (
3.183
) because of the asymptotic behaviour of the functions in Lemmas
A.3
and
A.2
.
∎

Lemma 3.9
.

Let
i=0,1
and
T_{2,0}^{(i)}
,
\mathsf{T}_{2,2}
and
\hat{T}_{2}
be given by Lemmas
3.5
and

. The systems (
3.1.5
) admit smooth solutions
(\mathsf{T}_{4,2}^{(i)},\mathsf{V}_{4,2}^{(i)})
and
(\hat{\mathsf{T}}_{4,2},\hat{\mathsf{V}}_{4,2})
depending such that for

:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{T}}_{4,2}^{(i)}

\displaystyle=2-4i+\mathcal{O}(r^{2-\sqrt{8}}),\quad{\mathsf{V}}_{4,2}^{(i)}=(i-\frac{1}{2})r^{2}\ln r+(\frac{5}{4}i-\frac{5}{8})r^{2}+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{4-2\sqrt{2}})),

(3.184)

\displaystyle\hat{\mathsf{T}}_{4,2}

\displaystyle=4+\mathcal{O}(r^{2-\sqrt{8}}),\quad\hat{\mathsf{V}}_{4,2}=-r^{2}\ln r-\frac{5}{4}r^{2}+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{4-2\sqrt{2}})).

(3.185)

The estimates propagate to higher order derivatives.

Proof.

From Lemma
A.3
with
\ell=2
, we choose the solution of the form

\mathsf{T}_{4,2}^{(i)}(r)=\sum_{k=1}^{4}h_{2,k}(r)\gamma_{4,k}(r),\quad\mathsf{V}_{4,2}^{(i)}(r)=\sum_{k=1}^{4}g_{2,k}(r)\gamma_{4,k}(r),

where
(h_{2,k},g_{2,k})_{1\leq k\leq 4}
are given in Lemma
A.2
,
W_{2}=-3.2^{12}r^{-2}(1+r^{2})^{-2}
and with the following choice of integration constants

\displaystyle\gamma_{4,1}(r)

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{3\cdot 2^{12}}\int_{0}^{r}\Sigma_{4,2}^{(i)}(s)W_{2,1}(s)s^{2}(1+s^{2})^{2}ds,\quad\gamma_{4,1}(r)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3\cdot 2^{12}}\int_{0}^{r}\Sigma_{4,2}^{(i)}(s)W_{2,2}(s)s^{2}(1+s^{2})^{2}ds,

\displaystyle\gamma_{4,3}(r)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3\cdot 2^{12}}\int_{r}^{\infty}\Sigma_{4,2}^{(i)}(s)W_{2,1}(s)s^{2}(1+s^{2})^{2}ds,\quad\gamma_{4,1}(r)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3\cdot 2^{12}}\int_{0}^{r}\Sigma_{4,2}^{(i)}(s)W_{2,2}(s)s^{2}(1+s^{2})^{2}ds.

Injecting (
3.150
), (
3.151
) and (
3.157
) in (
3.60
), we have

\Sigma_{4,2}^{(i)}(r)=\Lambda_{i}\mathsf{T}_{2,2}+\frac{1}{2}r\partial_{r}\mathsf{T}_{2,0}^{(i)}-\frac{r}{16}\partial_{r}\Lambda U=\frac{(-8+16i)}{r^{2}}+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-\sqrt{8}}).

(3.186)

The smoothness of
(\mathsf{T}_{4,2}^{(i)},\mathsf{V}_{4,2}^{(i)})
as
r\to 0
can be checked as for that of
(\mathsf{T}_{2,2},\mathsf{V}_{2,2})
in the proof of Lemma
3.6
. To obtain the expansion as
r\to\infty
we use (
3.1.7
) and (
3.1.7
) and compute

\displaystyle\gamma_{4,1}=-\frac{1}{3\cdot 2^{12}}\int_{0}^{r}-3\cdot 2^{11}(-1+2i)\langle s\rangle^{-1}(1+\mathcal{O}_{s\to\infty}(s^{2-\sqrt{8}}))ds=\frac{-1+2i}{2}\ln r+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{2-\sqrt{8}}),

\displaystyle\gamma_{4,2}=\frac{1}{3\cdot 2^{12}}\int_{0}^{r}2^{11}(-1+2i)s^{3}(1+\mathcal{O}_{s\to\infty}(s^{2-\sqrt{8}}))ds=\frac{-1+2i}{24}r^{4}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{2-\sqrt{8}}))

\displaystyle\gamma_{4,3}=\frac{1}{3\cdot 2^{12}}\int_{r}^{\infty}-3\cdot 2^{9}C_{2}(-1+2i)s^{1-2\sqrt{2}}(1+\mathcal{O}_{s\to\infty}(s^{2-\sqrt{8}}))ds=-\frac{C_{2}(-1+2i)}{16(\sqrt{2}-1)}r^{2-2\sqrt{2}}(1+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{2-\sqrt{8}}))

\displaystyle\gamma_{4,4}=\frac{1}{3\cdot 2^{12}}\int_{0}^{r}-3\cdot 2^{9}K_{2}(-1+2i)s^{1+2\sqrt{2}}(1+\mathcal{O}_{s\to\infty}(s^{2-\sqrt{8}}))ds=-\frac{K_{2}(-1+2i)}{16(\sqrt{2}+1)}r^{2-2\sqrt{2}}(1+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{2-\sqrt{8}})).

From the asymptotic behaviors of

given in Lemma
A.2
and the relation

, we end up with

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(\ln r)\mathcal{O}(r^{-2})+\mathcal{O}(r^{4})\mathcal{O}(r^{-6})-\frac{C_{2}(-1+2i)}{16(\sqrt{2}-1)}r^{2-2\sqrt{2}}(1+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{2-\sqrt{8}}))16K_{2}r^{\sqrt{8}-2}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{-2}))

\displaystyle-\frac{K_{2}(-1+2i)}{16(\sqrt{2}+1)}r^{2-2\sqrt{2}}(1+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{2-\sqrt{8}}))16C_{2}r^{-\sqrt{8}-2}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{\sqrt{8}-4}))

\displaystyle=2-4i+\mathcal{O}(r^{2-\sqrt{8}})

and

\displaystyle\mathsf{V}_{4,2}^{(i)}(r)=\sum_{k=1}^{4}\gamma_{2,k}g_{2,k}

\displaystyle=\frac{2i-1}{2}\ln r+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{2-\sqrt{8}})r^{2}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{-2}))+\frac{2i-1}{24}r^{4}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{2-\sqrt{8}}))3r^{-2}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{-2}))

\displaystyle-\frac{C_{2}(2i-1)}{16(\sqrt{2}-1)}r^{2-2\sqrt{2}}(1+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{2-\sqrt{8}}))(-4K_{2}r^{\sqrt{8}}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{-2})))

\displaystyle-\frac{K_{2}(2i-1)}{16(\sqrt{2}+1)}r^{2-2\sqrt{2}}(1+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{2-\sqrt{8}}))(-4C_{2}r^{-\sqrt{8}})(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{\sqrt{8}-4}))

\displaystyle=(i-\frac{1}{2})r^{2}\ln r+(\frac{5}{4}i-\frac{5}{8})r^{2}+\mathcal{O}(r^{4-\sqrt{8}}).

This shows the desired identities (
3.184
).

The same computation also works for
(\hat{\mathsf{T}}_{4,2},\hat{\mathsf{V}}_{4,2})
. Indeed, by (

), (

) and (

) we have

Notice that this is exactly as

given by (
3.186
), up to a constant factor. This concludes the proof of
3.9
.
∎

Lemma 3.10
.

Let
\hat{T}_{2}
and
\mathsf{T}_{2,2}
be described in Lemmas
3.5
and
3.6
. The system (

) with

such that

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}_{4,4}(r)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{4-\sqrt{20}}),

(3.187)

\displaystyle\mathsf{V}_{4,4}(r)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{24}r^{2}+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{6-\sqrt{20}}).

(3.188)

The estimates propagate to higher order derivatives.

Proof.

The proof is the same as for Lemma
3.9
by applying Lemma

with

and

, where we compute from (

(3.189)

From Lemma
A.3
, we choose the following solution to (
3.1.5
),

\displaystyle(\mathsf{T}_{4,4},\mathsf{V}_{4,4})=\sum_{k=1}^{4}\gamma_{4,k}(h_{4,k},g_{4,k}),

where we choose the following integration constants

\displaystyle\gamma_{4,1}=\frac{1}{15\cdot 2^{14}}\int_{r}^{\infty}\Sigma_{4,4}(s)W_{4,1}(s)s^{2}(1+s^{2})^{2}ds,\quad\gamma_{4,2}=\frac{1}{15\cdot 2^{14}}\int_{0}^{r}\Sigma_{4,4}(s)W_{4,2}(s)s^{2}(1+s^{2})^{2}ds,

\displaystyle\gamma_{4,3}=\frac{1}{15\cdot 2^{14}}\int_{r}^{\infty}\Sigma_{4,4}(s)W_{4,3}(s)s^{2}(1+s^{2})^{2}ds,\quad\gamma_{4,4}=\frac{1}{15\cdot 2^{14}}\int_{0}^{r}\Sigma_{4,4}(s)W_{4,4}(s)s^{2}(1+s^{2})^{2}ds.

To obtain the expansion as

, we recall that from (

),

\displaystyle=-2^{9}5r^{-7}\big(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{4-\sqrt{20}}\big),\quad W_{4,2}=-3\cdot 2^{9}r\Big(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{4-\sqrt{20}})\Big),

(3.190)

\displaystyle W_{4,3}

\displaystyle=-15\cdot 2^{7}C_{4}r^{-2\sqrt{5}-3}\Big(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{-4-\sqrt{20}})\big),\quad W_{4,4}=-15\cdot 2^{7}K_{4}r^{2\sqrt{5}-3}\Big(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{4-\sqrt{20}})\big).

(3.191)

We compute thanks to (
3.189
), (
3.190
) and (
3.191
):

\displaystyle\gamma_{4,1}=\frac{1}{15\cdot 2^{14}}\int_{r}^{\infty}-5\cdot 2^{12}s^{-3}(1+\mathcal{O}(s^{4-\sqrt{20}}))ds=-\frac{1}{24r^{2}}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{4-\sqrt{20}})),

\displaystyle\gamma_{4,2}=\frac{1}{15\cdot 2^{14}}\int_{0}^{r}-3\cdot 2^{12}s^{5}(1+\mathcal{O}(s^{4-\sqrt{20}}))ds=-\frac{r^{6}}{120}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{4-\sqrt{20}})),

\displaystyle\gamma_{4,3}=\frac{1}{15\cdot 2^{14}}\int_{r}^{\infty}-15\cdot 2^{10}C_{4}s^{-\sqrt{20}+1}(1+\mathcal{O}(s^{4-\sqrt{20}}))ds=-\frac{C_{4}}{32(\sqrt{5}-1)}r^{2-\sqrt{20}}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{4-\sqrt{20}})),

\displaystyle\gamma_{4,4}=\frac{1}{15\cdot 2^{14}}\int_{0}^{r}-15\cdot 2^{10}K_{4}s^{\sqrt{20}+1}(1+\mathcal{O}(s^{4-\sqrt{20}}))ds=-\frac{K_{4}}{32(\sqrt{5}+1)}r^{2+\sqrt{20}}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{4-\sqrt{20}})),

From the definition of

given in Lemma

\displaystyle-\frac{K_{4}}{32(\sqrt{5}+1)}r^{2+\sqrt{20}}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{4-\sqrt{20}}))16C_{4}r^{-\sqrt{20}-2}(1+\mathcal{O}(r^{4-\sqrt{20}})),

Proposition 3.11
.

)-(

and
