\displaystyle|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathsf{S}_{3,0,3}(r)|\lesssim r^{-3-k},\qquad\qquad\qquad|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathsf{W}_{3,0,3}(r)|\lesssim r^{-1-k},

(3.199)

\displaystyle|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathsf{S}_{3,1,1}(r)|\lesssim(1+|L_{i}|)r^{-1-k},\qquad|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathsf{W}_{3,1,1}(r)|\lesssim(1+|L_{i}|)r^{1-k}\ln r,

(3.200)

\displaystyle|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathsf{S}_{3,1,3}(r)|\lesssim(1+|L_{i}|)r^{-1-k},\qquad|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathsf{W}_{3,1,3}(r)|\lesssim(1+|L_{i}|)r^{1-k},

(3.201)

\displaystyle|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathsf{S}_{4,0,0}(r)|\lesssim(1+|L_{i}|)r^{-2-k},\qquad|\mathsf{W}_{4,0,0}(r)|\lesssim(1+|L_{i}|)r^{-k}\ln^{2-\delta_{k\geq 1}}r,

(3.202)

\displaystyle|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathsf{S}_{4,0,2}(r)|\lesssim(1+|L_{i}|)r^{-2-k},\qquad|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathsf{W}_{4,0,2}(r)|\lesssim(1+|L_{i}|)r^{-k},

(3.203)

\displaystyle|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathsf{S}_{4,0,4}(r)|\lesssim r^{-2-k},\qquad\qquad\qquad|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathsf{W}_{4,0,4}(r)|\lesssim r^{-k}.

(3.204)

Proof.

To ease the presentation, we prove all bounds below under the assumption that
|L_{i}^{\textup{in}}|\lesssim 1
. The dependance on
L_{i}^{\textup{in}}
in the final identities (
3.194
)-(
3.204
) then follows directly from the affine dependance on
L_{i}^{\textup{in}}
in the equations (
3.1.5
)-(
3.1.5
).

To lighten notation, the notation
\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}()
will again implicitly include estimates for derivatives. Namely, for
f\in C^{\infty}((0,\infty),\mathbb{R})
and
g:(0,\infty)\rightarrow(0,\infty)
we will write

f(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(g(r))

if for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we have for all
r\geq 1
:

|\partial_{r}^{k}f(r)|\lesssim r^{-k}g(r).

Step 1
.
Computation of the first spherical harmonics profiles
.

Determination of
(\mathsf{S}_{1,1,1},\mathsf{W}_{1,1,1})
. We notice that by (
3.58
) we have
-|a|\partial_{r}\Lambda U=-4\sqrt{2}|a|\Sigma_{3,1}
. By (
3.1.5
), (
3.1.5
) and Lemma
3.7
we thus choose
(\mathsf{S}_{1,1,1},\mathsf{S}_{1,1,1})=(\mathsf{T}_{3,1},\mathsf{W}_{3,1})
. The desired estimates (
3.194
) then follow from (
3.169
).

Determination of
(\mathsf{S}_{3,0,1},\mathsf{W}_{3,0,1})
. We first notice that the horizontal momentum of the source term is non-zero by (
3.18
) and by integrating by parts:

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}y_{1}\left((\Lambda_{i}+2\tilde{\lambda}_{i})\partial_{x_{1}}U+\nabla.(\partial_{x_{1}}UG_{1})\right)dy=4(4i-1-4\tilde{\lambda}_{i})\pi.

In polar coordinates, this corresponds to

\int_{0}^{\infty}\Sigma_{3,0,1}(r)r^{2}dr=4(4i-1-4\tilde{\lambda}_{i}).

(3.205)

We next estimate by (
3.18
)

\Sigma_{3,0,1}(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(r),\qquad\Sigma_{3,0,1}(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-6}).

(3.206)

We deduce by (
3.205
) and (
A.14
) that

\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\Sigma_{3,0,1}W_{1,2}}{W_{1}}dr=2i-\frac{1}{2}-2\tilde{\lambda}_{i}.

(3.207)

We apply Lemma
A.3
with
\ell=1
and
f=\Sigma_{3,1,1}
to solve (
3.1.5
) and choose the following solution

(\mathsf{S}_{3,1,1},\mathsf{W}_{3,1,1})=\sum_{k=1}^{4}\gamma_{3,1,1,k}(h_{1,k},g_{1,k})

(3.208)

where, using (
3.206
) and (
3.207
):

\displaystyle\gamma_{3,0,1,1}=-\int_{r}^{\infty}\frac{\Sigma_{3,0,1}W_{1,1}}{W_{1}}ds=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(\int_{0}^{\infty}\langle s\rangle^{-2}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(r^{2}),\\
=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(\int_{0}^{r}s^{-2}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-1}),\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\gamma_{3,0,1,2}=-\int_{0}^{r}\frac{\Sigma_{3,0,1}W_{1,2}}{W_{1}}ds=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(\int_{0}^{r}s^{3}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(r^{4}),\\
=\frac{1}{2}-2i+2\tilde{\lambda}_{i}+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(\int_{r}^{\infty}s^{-4}ds)=\frac{1}{2}-2i+2\tilde{\lambda}_{i}+\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-3}),\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\gamma_{3,0,1,3}=-\int_{r}^{\infty}\frac{\Sigma_{3,0,1}W_{1,3}}{W_{1}}ds=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(\int_{0}^{\infty}s\langle s\rangle^{-1-\sqrt{5}-3}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(1),\\
=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(\int_{r}^{\infty}s^{-\sqrt{5}-3}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-2-\sqrt{5}}),\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\gamma_{3,0,1,4}=-\int_{0}^{r}\frac{\Sigma_{3,1,1}W_{1,4}}{W_{1}}ds=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(\int_{0}^{r}s^{3}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(r^{4}),\\
=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(\int_{0}^{r}s^{\sqrt{5}-3}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{\sqrt{5}-2}),\end{array}\right.

Injecting the asymptotic expansions of Lemma
A.3
in (
3.208
) we obtain as
r\to\infty

\displaystyle\mathsf{S}_{3,0,1}=\mathcal{O}(r^{-1})\mathcal{O}(r^{-5})+\mathcal{O}(1)\mathcal{O}(r^{-3})+\mathcal{O}(r^{-2-\sqrt{5}})\mathcal{O}(r^{\sqrt{5}-2})+\mathcal{O}(r^{\sqrt{5}-2})\mathcal{O}(r^{-\sqrt{5}-2})=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-3})

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}_{3,0,1}=\mathcal{O}(r^{-1})\mathcal{O}(r^{-1})+(\frac{1}{2}-2i+2\tilde{\lambda}_{i})(r+\mathcal{O}(r^{-1}\ln r))+\mathcal{O}(r^{-2-\sqrt{5}})\mathcal{O}(r^{\sqrt{5}})+\mathcal{O}(r^{\sqrt{5}-2})\mathcal{O}(r^{-\sqrt{5}})

\displaystyle=(\frac{1}{2}-2i+2\tilde{\lambda}_{i})r+\mathcal{O}(r^{-1}\ln r).

This is (
3.192
).

Estimate for
(\mathsf{S}_{3,1,1},\mathsf{W}_{3,1,1})
. We first compute by (
3.1.5
) and (
3.194
), (
3.150
), (
3.151
), (
3.152
), (
3.158
), (
3.157
) and
|\tilde{\lambda}_{i}|\lesssim|\ln\nu|^{-1}
that

|\Sigma_{3,1,1}(r)|=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(r),\qquad|\Sigma_{3,1,1}(r)|=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-3})

(3.209)

We apply Lemma
A.3
with
\ell=1
and
f=\Sigma_{3,1,1}
to solve (
3.1.5
) and choose the following solution

(\mathsf{S}_{3,1,1},\mathsf{W}_{3,1,1})=\sum_{k=1}^{4}\gamma_{3,1,1,k}(h_{1,k},g_{1,k})

where

\displaystyle\gamma_{3,1,1,1}=\int_{0}^{r}\frac{\Sigma_{3,1,1}W_{1,1}}{W_{1}}ds=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(\int_{0}^{r}sds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(r^{2}),\\
=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(\int_{0}^{r}sds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{2}),\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\gamma_{3,1,1,2}=-\int_{0}^{r}\frac{\Sigma_{3,1,1}W_{1,2}}{W_{1}}ds=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(\int_{0}^{r}s^{3}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(r^{4}),\\
=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(\int_{0}^{r}s^{3}\langle s\rangle^{-4}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(\ln r),\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\gamma_{3,1,1,3}=-\int_{r}^{\infty}\frac{\Sigma_{3,1,1}W_{1,3}}{W_{1}}ds=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(\int_{0}^{\infty}s\langle s\rangle^{-1-\sqrt{5}}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(1),\\
=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(\int_{r}^{\infty}s^{-\sqrt{5}}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{1-\sqrt{5}}),\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\gamma_{3,1,1,4}=-\int_{0}^{r}\frac{\Sigma_{3,1,1}W_{1,4}}{W_{1}}ds=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(\int_{0}^{r}s^{3}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(r^{4}),\\
=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(\int_{0}^{r}s^{\sqrt{5}}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{1+\sqrt{5}}),\end{array}\right.

where we used (
3.209
) and the bounds of Lemma
A.3
to obtain the above estimates. The desired estimate (
3.200
) for
k=0
then follows from the above estimates and that of Lemma
A.2
. They are propagated to higher order derivatives
k\geq 1
by the same method.

Step 2
.
Computation of the radial profiles
. We first notice the mass cancellation for all source terms with radial components

\displaystyle 0=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}-(\Lambda_{i}+2\tilde{\lambda}_{i})\Lambda U-\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}a.\nabla\partial_{x_{1}}U

\displaystyle\qquad=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}-a.\nabla\mathcal{S}_{1,1}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}-\frac{1}{2|a|^{4}}\Lambda U+\alpha^{2}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\nabla.(\partial_{x_{1}}UG_{2})

which in polar coordinates corresponds to

0=\int_{0}^{\infty}r\Sigma_{2,1,0}(r)dr=\int_{0}^{\infty}r\Sigma_{2,2,0}(r)dr=\int_{0}^{\infty}r\Sigma_{4,0,0}(r)dr.

(3.210)

By (
3.1.5
), (
3.1.5
) and (
3.1.5
), and (
3.194
) we have for
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
r\geq 1
,

|\partial_{r}^{k}\Sigma_{2,1,0}(r)|+|\partial_{r}^{k}\Sigma_{2,2,0}(r)|+|\partial_{r}^{k}\Sigma_{4,0,0}(r)|\lesssim r^{-4-k}.

(3.211)

In view of (
3.210
) and (
3.211
), we will only compute
(\mathsf{S}_{2,2,0},\mathsf{W}_{2,2,0})
, and the proof will apply simultaneously to
(\mathsf{S}_{2,1,0},\mathsf{W}_{2,1,0})
and
(\mathsf{S}_{4,0,0},\mathsf{W}_{4,0,0})
. Since the system (
3.1.5
) corresponds to the radial case, in can be solved by appealing to Lemma
A.1
. Introducing
m_{2,2,0}(r)=\int_{0}^{r}\mathcal{S}_{2,2,0}(r^{\prime})r^{\prime}dr^{\prime}
and
\sigma_{2,2,0}(r)=\int_{0}^{r}\Sigma_{2,2,0}(r^{\prime})r^{\prime}dr^{\prime}
we choose

m_{2,2,0}=\frac{1}{2}\psi_{0}\int_{r}^{1}\frac{\zeta^{4}+4\zeta^{2}\ln\zeta-1}{\zeta}\sigma_{2,2,0}(\zeta)d\zeta+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\psi}_{0}\int_{0}^{r}\zeta\sigma_{2,2,0}(\zeta)d\zeta.

(3.212)

By (
A.3
), the function
\mathsf{S}_{2,2,0}(r)=r^{-1}\partial_{r}m_{2,2,0}(r)
is then smooth at the origin. We have by (
3.210
) and (
3.211
) that for
k\in\mathbb{N}
, for any
r>1
,

|\partial_{r}^{k}\sigma_{2,2,0}(r)|=|\partial_{r}^{k}\int_{r}^{\infty}\Sigma_{2,2,0}(r^{\prime})dr^{\prime}|\lesssim r^{-2-k}.

(3.213)

We have by (
3.212
)

|\mathsf{S}_{2,2,0}(r)|=\left|\frac{1}{2r}\partial_{r}\psi_{0}\int_{r}^{1}\frac{\zeta^{4}+4\zeta^{2}\ln\zeta-1}{\zeta}\sigma_{2,2,0}(\zeta)d\zeta+\frac{1}{2r}\partial_{r}\tilde{\psi}_{0}\int_{0}^{r}\zeta\sigma_{2,2,0}(\zeta)d\zeta\right|\lesssim r^{-2}.

This extends by the same computation to the case
k\geq 1
, which shows the first inequality in (
3.197
). Using
\mathsf{W}_{2,2,0}(r)=C-\int_{0}^{r}\frac{m_{2,2,0}(r^{\prime})}{r^{\prime}}dr^{\prime}
for some
C\in\mathbb{R}
and
|\partial_{r}^{k}m_{2,2,0}(r)|\lesssim r^{-k}\ln r
from (
3.212
) and (
3.213
), we obtain the second inequality in (
3.197
). As we mentioned above, this proof shows simultaneously (
3.195
) and (
3.202
).

Step 3
.
Computation of second spherical harmonics profiles
. First, from Lemma
A.2
we simply define
(\mathcal{S}_{4,0,2}^{\prime},\mathcal{W}_{4,0,2}^{\prime})=(h_{2,1},g_{2,1})
which immediately implies the desired behaviour (
3.193
).

We now compute
(\mathcal{S}_{2,1,2},\mathcal{W}_{2,1,2})
,
(\mathcal{S}_{2,2,2},\mathcal{W}_{2,2,2})
and
(\mathcal{S}_{4,0,2},\mathcal{W}_{4,0,2})
. We have by (
3.1.5
), (
3.1.5
), (
3.1.5
) and (
3.194
)

\Sigma_{2,1,2}(r),\Sigma_{2,2,2}(r),\Sigma_{4,0,2}(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(r^{2}),

(3.214)

where we used for
\Sigma_{2,2,2}
that near the origin
\mathsf{S}_{1,1}(r)=cr+O(r^{3})
for some constants
c\in\mathbb{R}
as
\mathcal{S}_{1,1}
is smooth near the origin. We also have

\Sigma_{2,1,2}(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-6})=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-4}),\qquad\Sigma_{2,2,2}(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-4}),\qquad\Sigma_{4,0,2}(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-4}).

(3.215)

Because of (
3.214
) and (
3.215
) we will only compute
(\mathsf{S}_{2,2,2},\mathsf{W}_{2,2,2})
, and the proof will apply simultaneously to
(\mathsf{S}_{2,1,2},\mathsf{W}_{2,1,0})
and
(\mathsf{S}_{4,0,2},\mathsf{W}_{4,0,2})
. We could show more decay as
r\to\infty
for
(\mathsf{S}_{2,1,2},\mathsf{W}_{2,1,0})
thanks to the extra
r^{-2}
decay in
\Sigma_{2,1,2}
but the decay we prove is sufficient.

We solve (
3.1.5
) thanks to Lemma
A.3
with
\ell=2
and
f=\Sigma_{2,2,2}
and get a solution of the form

(\mathsf{S}_{2,2,2},\mathsf{W}_{2,2,2})=\sum_{k=1}^{4}\gamma_{2,2,2,k}(h_{2,k},g_{2,k})

where we choose the integration constants so that

\displaystyle\gamma_{2,2,2,1}=-\int_{r}^{\infty}\frac{\Sigma_{2,2,2}W_{2,1}}{W_{2}}ds=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(\int_{0}^{\infty}\langle s\rangle^{-3}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(1),\\
=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(\int_{r}^{\infty}s^{-3}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-2}),\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\gamma_{2,2,2,2}=-\int_{0}^{r}\frac{\Sigma_{2,2,2}W_{2,2}}{W_{2}}ds=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(\int_{0}^{r}s^{5}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(r^{6}),\\
=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(\int_{0}^{r}s\langle s\rangle^{-4}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{2}),\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\gamma_{2,2,2,3}=-\int_{r}^{\infty}\frac{\Sigma_{2,2,2}W_{2,3}}{W_{2}}ds=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(\int_{0}^{\infty}\langle s\rangle^{-1-\sqrt{8}}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(1),\\
=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(\int_{r}^{\infty}s^{-1-\sqrt{8}}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-\sqrt{8}}),\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\gamma_{2,2,2,4}=-\int_{0}^{r}\frac{\Sigma_{2,2,2}W_{2,4}}{W_{2}}ds=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(\int_{0}^{r}s^{5}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(r^{6}),\\
=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(\int_{0}^{r}s^{\sqrt{8}-1}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{\sqrt{8}}),\end{array}\right.

and where the estimates follow from (
3.216
) and the bounds of Lemma
A.3
. The estimates (
3.201
) for
k=0
then follow from the above estimates and that of Lemma
A.2
, and are propagated for
k\geq 1
by the same computations. As we explained, this proves also simultaneously (
3.196
) and (
3.203
).

Step 4
.
Computation of third spherical harmonics profiles
. We estimate by (
3.1.5
), (
3.1.5
), (
3.157
) and (
3.194
)

\Sigma_{3,0,3}(r),\Sigma_{3,1,3}(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(r^{3}),\qquad\Sigma_{3,0,3}(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-5}),\qquad\Sigma_{3,1,3}(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-3})

(3.216)

where we used that since
\mathcal{S}_{1,1}
and
T_{2,2}
are smooth near the origin then in polar coordinates
\mathsf{S}_{1,1}(r)=cr+O_{r\to 0}(r^{3})
and
\mathsf{S}_{2,2}(r)=c^{\prime}r^{2}+O_{r\to 0}(r^{4})
for some constants
c,c^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
and that these estimates propagate for higher order derivative. We now solve (
3.1.5
) and (
3.1.5
) simultaneously. Let
j\in\{0,1\}
. Then by (
3.216
) we have
\Sigma_{3,j,3}=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{2j-5})
. Applying Lemma
A.3
with
\ell=3
and
f_{j}=\Sigma_{3,j,3}
to solve (
3.1.5
) and (
3.1.5
) we get the following solutions

(\mathsf{S}_{3,j,3},\mathsf{W}_{3,j,3})=\sum_{k=1}^{4}\gamma_{3,j,3,k}(h_{3,k},g_{3,k})

where we choose

\displaystyle\gamma_{3,j,3,1}=-\int_{r}^{\infty}\frac{\Sigma_{3,j,1}W_{3,1}}{W_{3}}ds=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(\int_{0}^{\infty}\langle s\rangle^{2j-5}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(1),\\
=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(\int_{r}^{\infty}s^{2j-5}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{2j-4}),\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\gamma_{3,j,3,2}=-\int_{0}^{r}\frac{\Sigma_{3,j,1}W_{3,2}}{W_{3}}ds=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(\int_{0}^{r}s^{7}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(r^{8}),\\
=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(\int_{0}^{r}s^{2j+1}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{2j+2}),\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\gamma_{3,1,3,3}=-\int_{r}^{\infty}\frac{\Sigma_{3,1,1}W_{3,3}}{W_{3}}ds=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(\int_{0}^{\infty}\langle s\rangle^{-\sqrt{13}+2j-2}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(1),\\
=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(\int_{r}^{\infty}s^{-\sqrt{13}+2j-2}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{2j-1-\sqrt{13}}),\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\gamma_{3,1,3,4}=-\int_{0}^{r}\frac{\Sigma_{3,1,1}W_{3,4}}{W_{3}}ds=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(\int_{0}^{r}s^{7}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(r^{8}),\\
=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(\int_{0}^{r}s^{\sqrt{13}+2j-2}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{2j-1+\sqrt{13}}),\end{array}\right.

and where the estimates follow from (
3.216
) and the bounds of Lemma
A.3
. The estimates (
3.199
) and (
3.201
) for
k=0
then follow from the above estimates and that of Lemma
A.2
, and are propagated for
k\geq 1
by the same computations.

Step 5
.
Computation of the fourth spherical harmonics profile
. We estimate by (
3.1.5
):

\Sigma_{4,0,4}(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(r^{4}),\qquad\Sigma_{4,0,4}(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-4}).

(3.217)

Applying Lemma
A.3
with
\ell=4
and
f=\Sigma_{4,0,4}
to solve (
3.1.5
) we get the following solution

(\mathsf{S}_{4,0,4},\mathsf{W}_{4,0,4})=\sum_{k=1}^{4}\gamma_{4,0,4,k}(h_{4,k},g_{4,k})

where we choose

\displaystyle\gamma_{4,0,4,1}=-\int_{r}^{\infty}\frac{\Sigma_{4,0,4}W_{4,1}}{W_{4}}ds=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(\int_{0}^{\infty}\langle s\rangle^{-5}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(1),\\
=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(\int_{r}^{\infty}s^{-5}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-4}),\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\gamma_{4,0,4,2}=-\int_{0}^{r}\frac{\Sigma_{3,0,4}W_{4,2}}{W_{4}}ds=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(\int_{0}^{r}s^{9}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(r^{10}),\\
=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(\int_{0}^{r}s^{3}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{4}),\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\gamma_{4,0,4,3}=-\int_{r}^{\infty}\frac{\Sigma_{4,0,4}W_{4,3}}{W_{4}}ds=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(\int_{0}^{\infty}\langle s\rangle^{-\sqrt{20}-1}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(1),\\
=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(\int_{r}^{\infty}s^{-\sqrt{20}-1}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{\sqrt{20}}),\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\gamma_{4,0,4,4}=-\int_{0}^{r}\frac{\Sigma_{4,0,4}W_{4,4}}{W_{4}}ds=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(\int_{0}^{r}s^{9}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to 0}(r^{10}),\\
=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(\int_{0}^{r}s^{\sqrt{20}-1}ds)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{\sqrt{20}}),\end{array}\right.

and where the estimates follow from (
3.217
) and the bounds of Lemma
A.3
. The estimates (
3.204
) and (
3.204
) for
k=0
then follow from the above estimates and that of Lemma
A.2
, and are propagated for
k\geq 1
by the same computations.

∎

3.2. Outer expansion of the eigenfunctions

In this subsection, we compute an approximate eigenfunction
\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}
in the exterior zone
\cap_{\pm}|z\pm a|\gg\nu
. To do so, we replace in (
3.1
)
\Phi_{\chi^{*}\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}}
by a more general solution
V_{i}^{\textup{ex}}
of the Poisson equation
-\Delta V_{i}^{\textup{ex}}=\chi^{*}\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}
, so that the approximate exterior eigenfunction system is

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l l}&R_{i}^{\textup{ex}}=\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}(\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}},V_{i}^{\textup{ex}})-\lambda_{i}\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}},\\
&-\Delta V_{i}^{\textup{ex}}=\chi^{*}\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}.\end{array}\right.

(3.218)

In order to find a solution of (
3.218
) such that
R_{i}^{\textup{ex}}
is suitably small, we identify the leading order part. We approximate the derivative of the Poisson field generated by
U_{1+2,\nu}
by
-\sum_{\pm}4(z\pm a_{\infty})/|z\pm a_{\infty}|^{2}
so that the linearized operator can be decomposed as

\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}(\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}},V_{i}^{\textup{ex}})=\mathscr{H}_{\beta}\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}+\nabla\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}.\left(-\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}-4\sum_{\pm}\frac{z\pm a_{\infty}}{|z\pm a_{\infty}|^{2}}\right)-\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla V_{i}^{\textup{ex}}+2U_{1+2,\nu}\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}

(3.219)

where the leading order part is

\mathscr{H}_{\beta}:=\Delta+\left(\frac{4(z-a_{\infty})}{|z-a_{\infty}|^{2}}+\frac{4(z+a_{\infty})}{|z+a_{\infty}|^{2}}\right).\nabla-\beta\Lambda.

(3.220)

Using (
3.219
) we can rewrite (
3.218
) with the following decomposition

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l l l}R_{i}^{\textup{ex}}&=\mathscr{H}_{\beta}\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}-\lambda_{i}\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}&\}:=\tilde{R}_{i}^{\textup{ex}},\\
&\quad+\nabla\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}.(-\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}-4\sum_{\pm}\frac{z\pm a_{\infty}}{|z\pm a_{\infty}|^{2}})-\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla V_{i}^{\textup{ex}}+2U_{1+2,\nu}\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}&\}:=\bar{R}_{i}^{\textup{ex}}\\
-\Delta V_{i}^{\textup{ex}}&=\chi^{*}\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}.\end{array}\right.

(3.221)

where
\tilde{R}_{i}^{\textup{ex}}
denotes the remainder in the leading part of the system and
\bar{R}_{i}^{\textup{ex}}
gathers negligible terms. We will first solve the leading system obtained by discarding
\bar{R}_{i}^{\textup{ex}}
in (
3.221
), which will provide the outer solution
\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}
. Then we will estimate the negligible terms in
\bar{R}_{i}^{\textup{ex}}
. This will produce a solution with the following properties.

Proposition 3.12

(Outer expansion of
\phi_{i}
)
.

Let
i=0,1
and
\lambda_{i}=2\beta(i-1)+2\beta\tilde{\lambda}_{i}
, and pick any constants
A^{\textup{ex}}_{i},B^{\textup{ex}}_{i},C^{\textup{ex}}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
. There exists a smooth solution
(\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}},V_{i}^{\textup{ex}},\tilde{R}_{i}^{\textup{ex}})
of the exterior eigenfunction system (
3.218
) on
\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{-a_{\infty},a_{\infty}\}
that has the following properties.

(i) Asymptotics as
\zeta_{\pm}=|z_{\pm}|=|z\mp a_{\infty}|\to 0
. Let
\theta_{\pm}=\frac{z_{\pm}}{|z_{\pm}|}
. We have

\displaystyle\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z)=\frac{1}{\zeta_{\pm}^{4}}+\frac{\beta}{\zeta_{\pm}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{2}i+\frac{1}{4}\cos(2\theta_{\pm})-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\right)\mp\frac{\beta^{\frac{3}{2}}}{12\sqrt{2}\zeta_{\pm}}\cos(3\theta_{\pm})+\beta^{2}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\left(\frac{i}{2}-\frac{1}{4}\right)\log\zeta_{\pm}

(3.222)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\beta^{2}}{32}\left(3i+(8i-4)\cos(2\theta_{\pm})+\cos(4\theta_{\pm})\right)+4\beta^{2}A_{i}+2\beta^{2}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\left(-\frac{1}{8}\cos(2\theta_{\pm})+B_{i}\right)+\phi_{i,high}^{\textup{ex}}(z),

\displaystyle|\nabla^{k}\phi_{i,high}^{\textup{ex}}(z)|+|\nabla^{k}\nu\partial_{\nu}\phi_{i,high}^{\textup{ex}}(z)|\lesssim\zeta^{\sqrt{5}-2-k}_{\pm}\qquad\mbox{for }k\in\mathbb{N},

(3.223)

where
A_{i},B_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
with in particular

-1\leq A_{1}\leq-0.9,

(3.224)

and

\displaystyle V_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z)=-\frac{1}{4\zeta_{\pm}^{2}}-\left(\frac{\beta}{4}i-\frac{\beta}{4}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\right)(\log\zeta_{\pm})^{2}+\frac{\beta}{16}\cos(2\theta_{\pm})\mp\frac{\beta^{3/2}}{96\sqrt{2}}\zeta_{\pm}\cos(3\theta_{\pm})+\beta^{2}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\frac{1-2i}{16}\zeta_{\pm}^{2}\log\zeta_{\pm}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\beta^{2}\zeta_{\pm}^{2}\left(-\frac{3i}{128}-A_{i}+\left(\frac{2i-1}{16}-\frac{B_{i}}{2}\right)\tilde{\lambda}_{i}+\frac{1-2i+2\tilde{\lambda}_{i}}{32}\log\zeta_{\pm}\cos(2\theta_{\pm})+\frac{1}{384}\cos(4\theta_{\pm})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\pm A_{i}^{\textup{ex}}\frac{1}{\zeta_{\pm}}\cos(\theta_{\pm})+B_{i}^{\textup{ex}}\log\zeta_{\pm}+C_{i}^{\textup{ex}}\pm D_{i}^{\textup{ex}}\zeta_{\pm}\cos(\theta_{\pm})+E_{i}^{\textup{ex}}\zeta_{\pm}^{2}\cos(2\theta_{\pm})+V_{i,high}^{\textup{ex}}(z)

(3.225)

\displaystyle|\nabla^{k}V_{i,high}^{\textup{ex}}(z)|\lesssim\zeta_{\pm}^{\sqrt{5}-k}\qquad\mbox{for }k\in\mathbb{N},

(3.226)

where
D_{i}^{\textup{ex}}=D_{i,0}^{\textup{ex}}+D_{i,1}^{\textup{ex}}A_{i}^{\textup{ex}}+D_{i,2}^{\textup{ex}}B_{i}^{\textup{ex}}
and
E_{i}^{\textup{ex}}=E_{i,0}^{\textup{ex}}+E_{i,1}^{\textup{ex}}A_{i}^{\textup{ex}}+E_{i,2}^{\textup{ex}}B_{i}^{\textup{ex}}
for some constants
D_{i,1}^{\textup{ex}}=-\frac{\beta}{8}
and
D_{i,0}^{\textup{ex}},D_{i,1}^{\textup{ex}},D_{i,2}^{\textup{ex}},E_{i,0}^{\textup{ex}},E_{i,1}^{\textup{ex}},E_{i,2}^{\textup{ex}}\in\mathbb{R}
.

(ii) Asymptotics as
|z|\to\infty
. Assuming
|\partial_{\nu}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}|\lesssim 1
, we have

\displaystyle|\nabla^{k}\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z)|+|\nabla^{k}\nu\partial_{\nu}\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z)|\lesssim|z|^{C_{k}},\qquad\partial_{a}\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}=0,

(3.227)

and

\displaystyle|V_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z)|\lesssim(1+|A_{i}^{\textup{ex}}|+|B_{i}^{\textup{ex}}|+|C_{i}^{\textup{ex}}|)\langle\log\langle z\rangle\rangle^{\delta_{k=0}}\langle z\rangle^{-k}.

(3.228)

(iii) Estimate for the remainder
. There exists
\tilde{\epsilon}_{0}>0
such that the error
R_{i}^{\textup{ex}}
satisfies if
|\tilde{\lambda}_{i}|\lesssim|\ln\nu|^{-1}
and
|a-a_{\infty}|\lesssim\nu
and
|A_{i}^{\textup{ex}}|+|B_{i}^{\textup{ex}}|+|C_{i}^{\textup{ex}}|\lesssim|\ln\nu|
:

|\nabla^{k}R_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z)|\lesssim\frac{1}{|\log\nu|^{2}}\sum_{\pm}\frac{\langle z\rangle^{C_{k}}}{|z\pm a|^{2+k}}\qquad\mbox{for }z\in\cap_{\pm}\{|z\pm a|\gtrsim\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}_{0}}\}.

(3.229)

Proof.

Step 1
.
The ansatz for the leading system
. We first solve the leading part of the system (
3.221
):

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l l l}\tilde{R}_{i}^{\textup{ex}}&=\mathscr{H}_{\beta}\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}-\lambda_{i}\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}},\\
-\Delta V_{i}^{\textup{ex}}&=\chi^{*}\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}.\end{array}\right.

(3.230)

As
\lambda_{i}=2\beta(1-i)+2\beta\tilde{\lambda}_{i}
with
|\tilde{\lambda}_{i}|\ll 1
we look for a solution with the following decomposition

\displaystyle\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z)=\Omega_{i}(z)+2\beta\tilde{\lambda}_{i}Z_{i}(z),

(3.231)

\displaystyle V_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z)=V_{i,\Omega}^{\textup{ex}}(z)+2\beta\tilde{\lambda}_{i}V_{i,Z}^{\textup{ex}}(z),

(3.232)

where
\Omega_{i}
,
Z_{i}
,
V_{i,\Omega}^{\textup{ex}}
and
V_{i,Z}^{\textup{ex}}
solve

\displaystyle\Big(\mathscr{H}_{\beta}-2\beta(1-i)\Big)\Omega_{i}=0,\qquad\Big(\mathscr{H}_{\beta}-2\beta(1-i)\Big)Z_{i}=\Omega_{i},

(3.233)

\displaystyle-\Delta V_{i,\Omega}^{\textup{ex}}=\chi^{*}\Omega_{i},\qquad\qquad\qquad-\Delta V_{i,Z}^{\textup{ex}}=\chi^{*}Z_{i}.

(3.234)

Injecting (
3.231
) and (
3.233
) in (
3.230
) we find

\tilde{R}_{i}^{\textup{ex}}=-4\beta^{2}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}^{2}Z_{i}.

(3.235)

Step 2
.
Solving (
3.233
) and proof of (
3.222
) and (
3.227
) for
i=1
. We make the change of variables for
i=0,1
,

\Omega_{i}(z)=4\beta^{2}\tilde{\Omega}_{i}(\tilde{z}),\qquad Z_{i}(z)=\beta\tilde{Z}_{i}(\tilde{z}),\qquad\tilde{z}=z\sqrt{2\beta},

(3.236)

that transforms
\mathscr{H}_{\beta}=2\beta\mathscr{H}_{\frac{1}{2}}
. Hence, (
3.233
) becomes

\Big(\mathscr{H}_{\frac{1}{2}}-(1-i)\Big)\tilde{\Omega}_{i}=0,\quad\Big(\mathscr{H}_{\frac{1}{2}}-(1-i)\Big)\tilde{Z}_{i}=2\tilde{\Omega}_{i}.

(3.237)

The existence and asymptotic behaviors of
\tilde{\Omega}_{1}
and
\tilde{Z}_{1}
have been rigorously proved in
[
SSV13
]
(see Lemmas 4.1 and 4.6). There it is showed that there exists a solution with as
\tilde{z}_{\pm}=\tilde{z}\mp(2,0)\to 0
,

\tilde{\Omega}_{1}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})=\frac{1}{\zeta_{\pm}^{4}}+\tilde{\Omega}_{1,1}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})\pm\tilde{\Omega}_{1,2}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})+\tilde{\Omega}_{1,3}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})+A+\tilde{\Omega}_{1,high}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})

(3.238)

with
A<0
computed numerically and

\displaystyle\tilde{\Omega}_{1,1}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})=\frac{1}{8\tilde{\zeta}_{\pm}}[1+2\cos^{2}\tilde{\theta}_{\pm}]=\frac{1}{8\tilde{\zeta}_{\pm}^{2}}[2+\cos(2\tilde{\theta}_{\pm})],

(3.239)

\displaystyle\tilde{\Omega}_{1,2}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})=\frac{1}{48}\frac{\cos(\tilde{\theta}_{\pm})}{\tilde{\zeta}_{\pm}}[3-4\cos^{2}\tilde{\theta_{\pm}}]=-\frac{1}{48\tilde{\zeta}_{\pm}}\cos(3\tilde{\theta}_{\pm}),

(3.240)

\displaystyle\tilde{\Omega}_{1,3}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})=\frac{1}{16}\cos^{4}\tilde{\theta}_{\pm}=\frac{1}{128}(3+4\cos(2\tilde{\theta}_{\pm})+\cos(4\tilde{\theta}_{\pm})),

(3.241)

\displaystyle|\nabla^{k}\tilde{\Omega}_{1,high}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})|\lesssim\tilde{\zeta}_{\pm}^{\sqrt{5}-2-k},\qquad\mbox{for }k\in\mathbb{N}.

(3.242)

Actually, (
3.242
) is only proved for
k=0
in
[
SSV13
]
, but the estimate for
k\geq 1
follows directly from the Fourier series representation formula in polar coordinates obtained on page 339 in
[
SSV13
]
. It is also showed that

\tilde{Z}_{1}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})=-\frac{1}{2\tilde{\zeta}^{2}_{\pm}}+\frac{1}{8}\log\tilde{\zeta}_{\pm}-\frac{1}{8}\cos(2\tilde{\theta}_{\pm})+Cte+\tilde{Z}_{1,high}(\tilde{z}_{\pm}).

(3.243)

where

|\nabla^{k}\tilde{Z}_{1,high}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})|\lesssim\tilde{\zeta}_{\pm}^{\sqrt{5}-2-k},\qquad\mbox{for }k\in\mathbb{N}.

(3.244)

Again, the proof of (
3.244
) follows from a similar Fourier series expansion as the aforementioned one.

The desired expansion (
3.222
) and bound (
3.223
) then follow from injecting (
3.238
)-(
3.244
) in (
3.236
) and (
3.231
). The bound
|\nabla^{k}\tilde{\Omega}_{1}|+|\nabla^{k}\tilde{Z}_{1}|\lesssim|\tilde{z}|^{C_{k}}
is proved in
[
SSV13
]
for
k=0
using a comparison principle argument, and the same argument can be used to obtain this bound for
k\geq 1
. The bound (
3.227
) follows from standard elliptic regularity estimates.

Step 3
.
Solving (
3.233
) and proof of (
3.222
) and (
3.227
) for
i=0
. The construction of
\tilde{\Omega}_{0}
that solves the first equation in (
3.237
) is exactly as in the proof of Lemma 4.1 in
[
SSV13
]
for
\tilde{\Omega}_{1}
. Hence we only show the key estimate for the approximate solution, as the construction of the full solution follows from the exact same arguments. We look for a solution
\tilde{\Omega}_{0}
of the first equation in (
3.233
) of the form

\tilde{\Omega}_{0}(\tilde{z})=\sum_{\pm}(\frac{1}{\tilde{\zeta}_{\pm}^{4}}+\tilde{\Omega}_{0,1}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})\pm\tilde{\Omega}_{0,2}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})+\tilde{\Omega}_{0,3}(\tilde{z}_{\pm}))\chi(\tilde{z}\mp a)+\tilde{\omega}_{0,high}(\tilde{z}).

(3.245)

In what follows, many calculations are simply verified by Matlab symbolic. We begin with a Taylor expansion using
\tilde{a}_{\infty}=(2,0)
,

F(\tilde{z}_{\pm})=\frac{(\tilde{z}_{\pm}\pm 2\tilde{a}_{\infty})}{|\tilde{z}_{\pm}\pm 2\tilde{a}_{\infty}|^{2}}\mp\frac{\tilde{a}_{\infty}}{2}=\sum_{k=1}^{3}(\pm 1)^{k+1}F_{k}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})+\mathcal{O}_{|\tilde{z}_{\pm}|\to 0}(|\tilde{z}_{\pm}|^{4}),

(3.246)

where
F_{k}(\tilde{z})
is homogeneous of order
k
with

\displaystyle F_{1}(\tilde{z})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}(-\tilde{z}_{1},\tilde{z}_{2}),\quad F_{2}(\tilde{z})=\frac{1}{16}\big(\tilde{z}_{1}^{2}-\tilde{z}_{2}^{2},-2\tilde{z}_{1}\tilde{z}_{2}\big),

\displaystyle\quad F_{3}(\tilde{z})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{64}\big(-\tilde{z}_{1}(\tilde{z}_{1}^{2}-3\tilde{z}_{2}),\tilde{z}_{2}(3\tilde{z}_{1}^{2}-\tilde{z}_{2}^{2})\big).

We then rewrite

\mathscr{H}_{\frac{1}{2}}-1=\Big(\Delta+\frac{4\tilde{z}_{\pm}}{|\tilde{z}_{\pm}|^{2}}.\nabla\Big)-\Big(\frac{1}{2}\tilde{z}_{\pm}.\nabla+2\Big)+\sum_{k=1}^{3}(\pm 1)^{k+1}F_{k}(\tilde{z}_{\pm}).\nabla+\tilde{F}(\tilde{z}_{\pm}).\nabla,

where
\tilde{F}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})=\mathcal{O}(|\tilde{z}_{\pm}|^{4})
as
|\tilde{z}_{\pm}|\to 0
. We note the cancellation

\Big(\Delta+\frac{4\tilde{z}_{\pm}}{|\tilde{z}_{\pm}|^{2}}.\nabla\Big)|\tilde{z}_{\pm}|^{-4}-\Big(\frac{1}{2}\tilde{z}_{\pm}.\nabla+2\Big)|\tilde{z}_{\pm}|^{-4}=0,

(3.247)

and then search for
(\tilde{\Omega}_{0,k})_{k=1,2,3}
satisfying

\displaystyle\Big(\Delta+\frac{4\tilde{z}_{\pm}}{|\tilde{z}_{\pm}|^{2}}.\nabla\Big)\tilde{\Omega}_{0,1}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})=-F_{1}.\nabla(|\tilde{z}_{\pm}|^{-4}):=-|\tilde{z}_{\pm}|^{-4}\cos(2\tilde{\theta}_{\pm}),

(3.248)

\displaystyle\Big(\Delta+\frac{4\tilde{z}_{\pm}}{|\tilde{z}_{\pm}|^{2}}.\nabla\Big)\tilde{\Omega}_{0,2}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})=\mp F_{2}.\nabla(|\tilde{z}_{\pm}|^{-4}):=c_{3}|\tilde{z}_{\pm}|^{-3}\cos(3\tilde{\theta}_{\pm}),

(3.249)

\displaystyle\Big(\Delta+\frac{4\tilde{z}_{\pm}}{|\tilde{z}_{\pm}|^{2}}.\nabla\Big)\tilde{\Omega}_{0,3}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})=-F_{3}.\nabla(|\tilde{z}_{\pm}|^{-4})+\Big(\frac{1}{2}\tilde{z}_{\pm}.\nabla+2\Big)\Omega_{0,1}-F_{1}.\nabla\Omega_{0,1}.

(3.250)

From the identity
\big(\Delta+\frac{4\tilde{z}}{|\tilde{z}|^{2}}.\nabla\big)(f(\tilde{\zeta})\cos(k\tilde{\theta}))=\big(\partial_{\tilde{\zeta}}^{2}f(\tilde{\zeta})+\frac{5}{\tilde{\zeta}}\partial_{\tilde{\zeta}}f(\tilde{\zeta})-\frac{k^{2}}{\tilde{\zeta}^{2}}f(\tilde{\zeta})\big)\cos(k\tilde{\theta})
, we look for
\tilde{\Omega}_{0,1}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})=\frac{c_{0,1}}{|\tilde{z}_{\pm}|^{2}}\cos(2\theta_{\pm})
and
\tilde{\Omega}_{0,2}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})=\frac{c_{0,2}}{|\tilde{z}_{\pm}|}\cos(3\theta_{\pm})
. A direct check yields

\tilde{\Omega}_{0,1}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})=\frac{\cos(2\tilde{\theta}_{\pm})}{8|\tilde{z}_{\pm}|^{2}},\quad\tilde{\Omega}_{0,2}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})=\frac{1}{48}\frac{\cos(3\tilde{\theta}_{\pm})}{|\tilde{z}_{\pm}|}.

(3.251)

As for
\tilde{\Omega}_{0,3}
, we expand the source term in the polar coordinate to get after a simplification

\displaystyle-F_{3}(\tilde{z}_{\pm}).\nabla(|\tilde{z}_{\pm}|^{-4})+\Big(\frac{1}{2}\tilde{z}_{\pm}.\nabla+2\Big)\Omega_{0,1}-F_{1}(\tilde{z}_{\pm}).\nabla\Omega_{0,1}=\frac{\cos(2\tilde{\theta}_{\pm})-\cos(4\tilde{\theta}_{\pm})}{8|\tilde{z}_{\pm}|^{2}}.

A direct check yields

\tilde{\Omega}_{0,3}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})=-\frac{1}{32}\cos(2\tilde{\theta}_{\pm})+\frac{1}{128}\cos(4\tilde{\theta}_{\pm}),

(3.252)

that satisfies the equation
(\Delta+\frac{4\tilde{z}_{\pm}}{|\tilde{z}_{\pm}|^{2}})\tilde{\Omega}_{0,3}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})=\frac{\cos(2\tilde{\theta}_{\pm})-\cos(4\tilde{\theta}_{\pm})}{8|\tilde{z}_{\pm}|^{2}}
.

Using first (
3.246
)-(
3.250
), and then (
3.251
)-(
3.252
) we get for the error generated by the approximate solution

\displaystyle(\mathcal{H}_{\frac{1}{2}}-1)\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{\zeta}_{\pm}^{4}}+\tilde{\Omega}_{0,1}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})\pm\tilde{\Omega}_{0,2}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})+\tilde{\Omega}_{0,3}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=F_{1}(\tilde{z}_{\pm}).\nabla(\tilde{\Omega}_{0,2}+\tilde{\Omega}_{0,3})\pm F_{2}(\tilde{z}_{\pm}).\nabla\sum_{j=1}^{3}\tilde{\Omega}_{0,j}+F_{3}(\tilde{z}_{\pm}).\nabla\sum_{j=1}^{3}\tilde{\Omega}_{0,j}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+F(\tilde{z}_{\pm}).\nabla\left(\frac{1}{\zeta_{\pm}^{4}}+\tilde{\Omega}_{0,1}(z_{\pm})\pm\tilde{\Omega}_{0,2}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})+\tilde{\Omega}_{0,3}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})\right)\ =O(|\tilde{z}_{\pm}|^{-1}).

(3.253)

Introducing
Q_{0,high}=-(\mathcal{H}_{\frac{1}{2}}-1)\sum_{\pm}(\frac{1}{\tilde{\zeta}_{\pm}^{4}}+\tilde{\Omega}_{0,1}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})\pm\tilde{\Omega}_{0,2}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})+\tilde{\Omega}_{0,3}(\tilde{z}_{\pm}))\chi(\tilde{z}\mp a)
and using (
3.253
), we find that the remainder in (
3.245
) solves

(\mathcal{H}_{\frac{1}{2}}-1)\tilde{\omega}_{0,high}=Q_{0,high},\qquad|\nabla^{k}Q_{0,high}|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{|\tilde{z}_{\pm}|}.

(3.254)

With the key estimate
|\nabla^{k}Q_{0,high}|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}|\tilde{z}_{\pm}|^{-1}
at hand, the solution of (
3.254
) can now be found exactly as in the proof of Lemma 4.1 in
[
SSV13
]
, with for some constant
A_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
,

\tilde{\omega}_{0,high}(\tilde{z})=A_{0}+\tilde{\Omega}_{0,high}(\tilde{z}),\qquad|\nabla^{k}\tilde{\Omega}_{0,high}(\tilde{z})|\lesssim|\tilde{z}_{\pm}|^{\sqrt{5}-2-k}\quad\mbox{for }k\in\mathbb{N},

(3.255)

as
z\to\pm a
. The computation of
\tilde{Z}_{0}
that solves the second equation in (
3.237
) can be done similarly, yielding for some constant
\tilde{B}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\tilde{Z}_{0}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})=-\frac{1}{2\tilde{\zeta}^{2}_{\pm}}-\frac{1}{8}\log\tilde{\zeta}_{\pm}-\frac{1}{8}\cos(2\tilde{\theta}_{\pm})+\tilde{B}_{0}+\tilde{Z}_{0,high}(\tilde{z}_{\pm}),

(3.256)

\displaystyle|\nabla^{k}\tilde{Z}_{0,high}(\tilde{z}_{\pm})|\lesssim\tilde{\zeta}_{\pm}^{\sqrt{5}-2-k},\qquad\mbox{for }k\in\mathbb{N}.

(3.257)

The proof is exactly as that of Lemma 4.6 in
[
SSV13
]
. Injecting (
3.245
), (
3.251
), (
3.252
), (
3.255
) and (
3.256
) in (
3.236
) shows the first desired expansion (
3.222
) and estimate (
3.223
). The second desired estimate (
3.227
) for
i=0
follows from the same arguments as in Step 2 for
i=1
.

Step 4
.
Computation of the Poisson field and proof of (
3.225
) and (
3.228
).
We recall the identities

\displaystyle-\Delta(-\frac{1}{4r^{2}})=\frac{1}{r^{4}},\qquad-\Delta(-\frac{1}{2}(\log r)^{2})=\frac{1}{r^{2}},\qquad-\Delta(\frac{1}{4}\cos(2\theta))=\frac{\cos(2\theta)}{r^{2}}

(3.258)

\displaystyle-\Delta(\frac{1}{8}r\cos(3\theta))=\frac{\cos(3\theta)}{r},\qquad-\Delta(-\frac{1}{4}r^{2}\log r+\frac{1}{4}r^{2})=\log r,\qquad-\Delta(-\frac{r^{2}}{4})=1,

(3.259)

\displaystyle-\Delta(-\frac{r^{2}\log r}{4}\cos(2\theta))=\cos(2\theta)\qquad-\Delta(\frac{1}{12}r^{2}\cos(4\theta))=\cos 4\theta.

(3.260)

In view of (
3.222
), we look for a solution of the form

\displaystyle V_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z)=\sum_{\pm}\bar{V}_{i,\pm}^{\textup{ex}}(z_{\pm})\chi(z\mp a)+V_{i,high}^{\textup{ex}}(z),

(3.261)

\displaystyle\bar{V}_{i,\pm}^{\textup{ex}}(z_{\pm})=-\frac{1}{4\zeta_{\pm}^{2}}-\left(\frac{\beta}{4}i-\frac{\beta}{4}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\right)(\log r)^{2}+\frac{\beta}{16}\cos(2\theta)\mp\frac{\beta^{3/2}}{96\sqrt{2}}\zeta_{\pm}\cos(3\theta_{\pm})+(-1)^{i}\frac{\beta^{2}}{16}(\zeta_{\pm}^{2}\log\zeta_{\pm}-\zeta_{\pm}^{2}),

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\beta^{2}\zeta_{\pm}^{2}\left(-\frac{3i}{128}-A_{i}+\left(\frac{2i-1}{16}-\frac{B_{i}}{2}\right)\tilde{\lambda}_{i}+\frac{1-2i+2\tilde{\lambda}_{i}}{32}\log\zeta_{\pm}\cos(2\theta_{\pm})+\frac{1}{384}\cos(4\theta_{\pm})\right)

(3.262)

Injecting the decomposition (
3.261
) in the second equation in (
3.218
), using (
3.258
) and (
3.261
) we find that
V_{i,high}^{\textup{ex}}
solves

\displaystyle-\Delta V_{i,high}^{\textup{ex}}=f_{i,high}

(3.263)

\displaystyle f_{i,high}=\sum_{\pm}\phi_{i,high}^{\textup{ex}}\chi(z\mp a)

(3.264)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+(\chi^{*}-\sum_{\pm}\chi(z\mp a))\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}+2\sum_{\pm}\nabla\bar{V}_{i,\pm}^{\textup{ex}}(z_{\pm}).\nabla\chi(z\mp a)+\sum_{\pm}\bar{V}_{i,\pm}^{\textup{ex}}(z_{\pm})\Delta\chi(z\mp a)

where
f_{i,high}
is compactly supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
and satisfies using (
3.222
),

|\nabla^{k}f_{i,high}(z)|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}|z\pm a|^{\sqrt{5}-2-k}.

(3.265)

We then choose as a solution to (
3.263
)
V_{i,high}^{\textup{ex}}=\Phi_{f_{i,high}}
and claim that as
z_{\pm}\to 0
,

\displaystyle V_{i,high}^{\textup{ex}}=C_{i}\pm(D_{i},0).(z\mp a_{\infty}))+E_{i}(z_{\pm,1}^{2}-z_{\pm,2}^{2})+\tilde{V}_{i,high}^{\textup{ex}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=C_{i}\pm D_{i}\cos(\theta_{\pm})+E_{i}\cos(2\theta_{\pm})+\tilde{V}_{i,high}^{\textup{ex}},

\displaystyle|\nabla^{k}\tilde{V}_{i,high}^{\textup{ex}}(z)|\lesssim\zeta_{\pm}^{\sqrt{5}-k}\mbox{ for }k\in\mathbb{N},

(3.266)

for some constants
C_{i},D_{i},E_{i}
that corresponds to the Taylor expansion of
V_{i,high}
at
a_{\infty}
. Note that the specific form of this Taylor expansion comes from the fact that
V_{i,high}
is invariant by the symmetry
z_{2}\mapsto-z_{2}
and satisfies
\Delta V_{i,high}(a_{\infty})=f_{i,high}(a_{\infty})=0
. We are going to prove the estimate in (
3.266
) first for
k=2
. We have for
j=1,2
,

\displaystyle\nabla\partial_{x_{j}}\tilde{V}_{i,high}^{\textup{ex}}(a_{\infty}+z_{+})

\displaystyle=\nabla\partial_{x_{j}}\tilde{V}_{i,high}^{\textup{ex}}(a_{\infty}+z_{+})-\nabla\partial_{x_{j}}\tilde{V}_{i,high}^{\textup{ex}}(a_{\infty})

\displaystyle=c\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\frac{z-z^{\prime}}{|z-z^{\prime}|^{2}}+\frac{z^{\prime}}{|z^{\prime}|^{2}}\right)\partial_{x_{j}}f_{i,high}(a_{\infty}+z^{\prime})dz^{\prime}

\displaystyle=c\int_{2|z|<|z^{\prime}|}...dz^{\prime}+c\int_{2|z|>|z^{\prime}|}...dz^{\prime}=I+II

Using (
3.265
) and
|\frac{z-z^{\prime}}{|z-z^{\prime}|^{2}}+\frac{z^{\prime}}{|z^{\prime}|^{2}}|\lesssim|z||z^{\prime}|^{-2}
if
2|z|<|z^{\prime}|
we bound
|I|\lesssim\int_{|z|<2|z^{\prime}|\lesssim 1}\frac{|z|}{|z^{\prime}|^{2}}|z^{\prime}|^{\sqrt{5}-3}dz^{\prime}\lesssim|z|^{\sqrt{5}-2}
. We bound
|II|\lesssim\int_{|z^{\prime}|<2|z|}(|z-z^{\prime}|^{-1}+|z^{\prime}|^{-1})|z^{\prime}|^{\sqrt{5}-3}dz^{\prime}\lesssim|z|^{\sqrt{5}-2}
. This shows the estimate in (
3.266
) for
k=2
. Next, since
\partial^{\alpha}\tilde{V}_{i,high}^{\textup{ex}}(a_{\infty})=0
for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
with
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}\leq 2
, and
|\nabla^{2}\tilde{V}_{i,high}^{\textup{ex}}(z)|\lesssim|z\pm a_{\infty}|^{\sqrt{5}-2}
, the estimate in (
3.266
) for
k=0
and
k=1
is a direct consequence of the fundamental Theorem of Calculus. We then show the estimate in (
3.266
) for
k\geq 3
by induction. Assume it is true for some
k\geq 2
. Pick then
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
with
|\alpha|=k
and, for
0<R\leq 1
, let
\tilde{V}_{i,R}^{\alpha}(y)=\partial^{\alpha}\tilde{V}_{i,high}^{\textup{ex}}(a_{\infty}+Ry)
. Then it solves on
\{1/4<|y|<1\}

\tilde{V}_{i,R}^{\alpha}(y)=R^{2}\partial^{\alpha}f_{i,high}(a_{\infty}+Ry)=O(R^{\sqrt{5}-k})

using (
3.265
), where it also satisfies
|\tilde{V}_{i,R}^{\alpha}(y)|\lesssim R^{\sqrt{5}-k-\epsilon}
. Hence by elliptic regularity we have
\nabla\tilde{V}_{i,R}^{\alpha}=O(R^{\sqrt{5}-k})
for
\{1/3<|y|<1/2\}
. This means
|\nabla\partial^{\alpha}\tilde{V}_{i,high}^{\textup{ex}}(a_{\infty}+z_{+})|\lesssim R^{\sqrt{5}-1-k}
for
R/3<|z_{+}|<R/2
. Hence (
3.266
) for
k+1
as
R
is arbitrary.

The first desired expansion (
3.225
) then follows from the decomposition (
3.261
), the estimate (
3.266
), and adding any linear combination of the functions
\sum_{\pm}\frac{z_{1}\mp a_{\infty,1}}{|z\mp a_{\infty}|^{2}}
and
1
and
\sum_{\pm}\log|z\pm a_{\infty}|
which solve the Laplace equation. The second desired estimate (
3.228
) follows from the fact that
V_{i,high}^{\textup{ex}}=\Phi_{f_{i,high}}
with
f_{i,high}
being compactly supported. The value
D_{i,2}^{\textup{ex}}
comes from the Taylor expansion of
z\mapsto\frac{z_{1}+a_{\infty,1}}{|z+a_{\infty}|^{2}}
at
a_{\infty}
.

Step 5
.
Proof of (
3.229
)
. We recall that
|a-a_{\infty}|\lesssim\nu
and
|\tilde{\lambda}_{i}|\lesssim|\ln\nu|^{-1}
. In what follows we take
z\in\cap_{\pm}\{|z\pm a|\gg\nu\}
, and hence
|z\pm a|\approx|z\pm a_{\infty}|
. Injecting (
3.243
) and (
3.256
) in (
3.235
) shows

|\tilde{R}_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z)|\lesssim\frac{1}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}|Z_{i}(z)|\lesssim\frac{1}{|\log\nu|^{2}}\sum_{\pm}\frac{\langle z\rangle^{C}}{|z\pm a_{\infty}|^{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{|\log\nu|^{2}}\sum_{\pm}\frac{\langle z\rangle^{C}}{|z\pm a|^{2}}.

(3.267)

We next estimate
\bar{R}_{i}^{\textup{ex}}
in (
3.221
). For the first term, we have since
|a-a_{\infty}|\lesssim\nu
that

\displaystyle\left|\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+4\sum_{\pm}\frac{z\pm a_{\infty}}{|z\pm a_{\infty}|^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle=4\left|\sum_{\pm}\frac{|z\pm a_{\infty}|^{2}(z\pm a)-(\nu^{2}+|z\pm a|^{2})(z\pm a_{\infty})}{|z\pm a_{\infty}|^{2}(\nu^{2}+|z\pm a|^{2})}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{\nu^{2}+|a-a_{\infty}|}{|z\pm a|^{2}}

so that since
|\nabla\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}|z\pm a_{\infty}|^{-5}\langle z\rangle^{C}
from (
3.222
) we find

\left|\nabla\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}.\left(-\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}-4\sum_{\pm}\frac{z\pm a_{\infty}}{|z\pm a_{\infty}|^{2}}\right)\right|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{\nu^{2}+|a-a_{\infty}|}{|z\pm a|^{7}}\langle z\rangle^{C}.

(3.268)

Next, using
|\nabla^{k}U_{1+2,\nu}|\lesssim\nu^{2}\sum_{\pm}|z\pm a|^{-5}
for
k=0,1
and
|\nabla V_{i}^{\textup{ex}}|\lesssim|\ln\nu|\sum_{\pm}|z\pm a_{\infty}|^{-3}\langle z\rangle^{2}
from (
3.225
) and the assumption
|A_{i}^{\textup{ex}}|,|B_{i}^{\textup{ex}}|\lesssim|\ln\nu|
and
|\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}|z\pm a_{\infty}|^{-4}\langle z\rangle^{C}
from (
3.222
) we find for the second and third terms

\left|-\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla V_{i}^{\textup{ex}}+2U_{1+2,\nu}\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}\right|\lesssim|\ln\nu|\sum_{\pm}\frac{\nu^{2}}{|z\pm a|^{8}}\langle z\rangle^{C}.

(3.269)

Injecting (
3.268
) and (
3.269
) in the definition of
\bar{R}_{i}^{\textup{ex}}
in (
3.221
) we find

|\bar{R}_{i}|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{\nu^{2}}{|z\pm a|^{8}}\langle z\rangle^{C}+\sum_{\pm}\frac{|a-a_{\infty}|}{|z\pm a|^{7}}\langle z\rangle^{C}.

(3.270)

As
R_{i}^{\textup{ex}}=\tilde{R}_{i}^{\textup{ex}}+\bar{R}_{i}^{\textup{ex}}
, combining (
3.267
) and (
3.270
) using
|a-a_{\infty}|\lesssim\nu
shows the desired estimate (
3.229
).

∎

3.3. Matching and computation of the eigenvalues

3.3.1. Matched asymptotic expansions

Lemma 3.13
.

Let
i=0,1
and
\epsilon>0
be given by Proposition
3.4
. Pick

\tilde{\lambda}_{i}=\frac{7-10i+256A_{i}}{16(2i-1)}\frac{1}{|\ln\nu|}\ \frac{1}{1+\frac{1-10i-32B_{i}}{(8i-4)|\ln\nu|}}

(3.271)

and choose the following values of the constants

(A_{i}^{\textup{ex}},B_{i}^{\textup{ex}},C_{i}^{\textup{ex}},L_{i}^{\textup{in}},M_{i}^{\textup{in}})=\left(A_{i}^{\textup{ex}*}+\frac{\alpha^{1/2}}{4}\tilde{L}_{i}^{\textup{in}},B_{i}^{\textup{ex}*},C_{i}^{\textup{ex}*},L_{i}^{\textup{in}*}+\tilde{L}_{i}^{\textup{in}},M_{i}^{\textup{in}*}+\tilde{M}_{i}^{\textup{in}}\right)

(3.272)

where
\tilde{L}_{i}^{\textup{in}},\tilde{M}_{i}^{\textup{in}}\in\mathbb{R}
are free parameters and

\displaystyle A_{i}^{\textup{ex}*}=\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{L_{i}^{\textup{in}*}}{4},

(3.273)

\displaystyle B_{i}^{\textup{ex}*}=\alpha\left(\frac{i-\tilde{\lambda}_{i}}{2}\ln\nu+\frac{2i+1-2\tilde{\lambda}_{i}}{4}\right),

(3.274)

\displaystyle C_{i}^{\textup{ex}*}=\alpha\left(\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{i}-i}{4}\ln^{2}\nu+\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{i}-1-2i}{4}\ln\nu\right),

(3.275)

\displaystyle L_{i}^{\textup{in}*}=\frac{32\alpha^{-\frac{3}{2}}D_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(A_{i}^{\textup{ex}*},B_{i}^{\textup{ex}*})}{1-4i+4\tilde{\lambda}_{i}},

(3.276)

\displaystyle M_{i}^{\textup{in}*}=(2i-1-\tilde{\lambda}_{i})\ln\nu+\frac{5-10i+10\tilde{\lambda}_{i}}{4}-\frac{E_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(A_{i}^{\textup{ex}*},B_{i}^{\textup{ex}*})}{32\alpha^{2}},

(3.277)

Then we have

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{16\nu^{4}}\phi^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}\left(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu}\right)-\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z)=\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{bo}}(z),

(3.278)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{16\nu^{2}}V^{\textup{in}}_{i,\pm}\left(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu}\right)-V_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z)=V_{i,\pm}^{\textup{bo}}(z),

(3.279)

where for
\nu
small enough, for
|a-a_{\infty}|\lesssim\nu
and
|\tilde{L}_{i}^{\textup{in}}|,|\tilde{M}_{i}^{\textup{in}}|\lesssim 1
, the boundary terms satisfy for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle|\nabla^{k}\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{bo}}(z)|\lesssim(\nu^{\epsilon}|z\mp a|^{-4-\epsilon}+|z\mp a|^{\sqrt{5}-2})|z\mp a|^{-k},

(3.280)

\displaystyle|\nabla^{k}V_{i,\pm}^{\textup{bo}}(z)|\lesssim(\nu^{\epsilon}|z-a|^{-2-\epsilon}+|z\mp a|^{\sqrt{5}})|z\mp a|^{-k}+|\tilde{L}_{i}^{\textup{in}}||z\mp a|^{1-k}+|\tilde{M}_{i}^{\textup{in}}||z\mp a|^{2-k},

(3.281)

for all
\nu\ll|z-a|\ll 1
.

Remark 3.14
.

Note that by Proposition
3.12
, the equations (
3.273
)-(
3.277
) corresponds to the following linear system for
A_{i}^{\textup{ex}*},B_{i}^{\textup{ex}*},C_{i}^{\textup{ex}*},L_{i}^{\textup{in}*},M_{i}^{\textup{in}*}
:

\mathfrak{M}(A_{i}^{\textup{ex}*},B_{i}^{\textup{ex}*},C_{i}^{\textup{ex}*},L_{i}^{\textup{in}*},M_{i}^{\textup{in}*})^{\top}=(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4},x_{5})^{\top}

where

\mathfrak{M}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0&0&-\frac{\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}}{4}&0\\
0&1&0&0&0\\
0&0&1&0&0\\
-\frac{32\alpha^{-\frac{3}{2}}D_{i,1}^{\textup{ex}}}{1-4i+4\tilde{\lambda}_{i}}&-\frac{32\alpha^{-\frac{3}{2}}D_{i,2}^{\textup{ex}}}{1-4i+4\tilde{\lambda}_{i}}&0&1&0\\
\frac{\alpha^{-2}E_{i,1}^{\textup{ex}}}{32}&\frac{\alpha^{-2}E_{i,2}^{\textup{ex}}}{32}&0&0&1\end{pmatrix}

and

\displaystyle x_{1}=0,

\displaystyle x_{2}=\alpha\left(\frac{i-\tilde{\lambda}_{i}}{2}\ln\nu+\frac{2i+1-2\tilde{\lambda}_{i})}{4}\right)

\displaystyle x_{3}=\alpha\left(\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{i}-i}{4}\ln^{2}\nu+\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{i}-1-2i}{4}\ln\nu\right),

\displaystyle x_{4}=\frac{32\alpha^{-\frac{3}{2}}D_{i,0}^{\textup{ex}}}{1-4i+4\tilde{\lambda}_{i}},

\displaystyle x_{5}=(2i-1-\tilde{\lambda}_{i})\ln\nu+\frac{5-10i+10\tilde{\lambda}_{i}}{4}-\frac{E_{i,0}^{\textup{ex}}}{32\alpha^{2}}.

We compute using
\alpha^{-1}\beta=1+O(\nu)
that
\textup{Det}(\mathfrak{M})=1-\frac{8\alpha^{-1}D_{i,1}^{\textup{ex}}}{1-4i+4\tilde{\lambda}_{i}}=1+\frac{1}{1-4i+4\tilde{\lambda}_{i}}+O(\nu)\neq 0
so that the system has a unique solution.

Moreover, we have from (
3.273
)-(
3.277
) that

\displaystyle|A_{0}^{\textup{ex}*}|+|B_{0}^{\textup{ex}*}|+|L_{0}^{\textup{in}*}|\lesssim 1\quad\mbox{and}\quad|A_{1}^{\textup{ex}*}|+|B_{1}^{\textup{ex}*}|+|L_{1}^{\textup{in}*}|\lesssim|\ln\nu|,

\displaystyle|C_{i}^{\textup{ex}}|+|M_{i}^{\textup{ex}}|\lesssim|\ln\nu|\qquad\mbox{for }i=0,1.

Proof.

Proof of (
3.280
)
. Recall that
z_{\pm}=z\mp a
in the statement of Proposition
3.4
and
z_{\pm}=z\mp a_{\infty}
in the statement of Proposition
3.12
. In the following proof, we take
z_{\pm}=z\mp a
, introduce
\bar{z}=a-a_{\infty}
and decompose
\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z)=\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z\mp\bar{z})+\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z)-\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z\mp\bar{z})
. We have by these propositions

\displaystyle\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{bo}}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\zeta_{\pm}^{4}}+\frac{\alpha}{\zeta_{\pm}^{2}}\left(\frac{i}{2}+\frac{1}{4}\cos 2\theta_{\pm}\right)\mp\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}\frac{1}{12\sqrt{2}\zeta_{\pm}}\cos(3\theta_{\pm})

\displaystyle\qquad+\alpha^{2}\left(\frac{i}{4}-\frac{7}{64}+\frac{2i-1}{8}\cos(2\theta_{\pm})+\frac{1}{32}\cos(4\theta_{\pm})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\left(-\frac{\alpha}{2\zeta_{\pm}^{2}}+\alpha^{2}\left((\frac{i}{2}-\frac{1}{4})(\ln\zeta_{\pm}-\ln\nu)+\frac{1-10i}{16}-\frac{1}{4}\cos(2\theta_{\pm})\right)\right)+\phi_{i,\pm,\sharp}^{\textup{in},z}(z_{\pm})

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{\zeta_{\pm}^{4}}-\frac{\beta}{\zeta_{\pm}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{2}i+\frac{1}{4}\cos(2\theta_{\pm})-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\right)\pm\frac{\beta^{\frac{3}{2}}}{12\sqrt{2}\zeta_{\pm}}\cos(3\theta_{\pm})-\beta^{2}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\left(\frac{i}{2}-\frac{1}{4}\right)\log\zeta_{\pm}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{\beta^{2}}{32}\left(3i+(8i-4)\cos(2\theta_{\pm})+\cos(4\theta_{\pm})\right)-4\beta^{2}A_{i}-2\beta^{2}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\left(-\frac{1}{8}\cos(2\theta_{\pm})+B_{i}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-\phi_{i,high}^{\textup{ex}}(z\mp\bar{z})+\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z\mp\bar{z})-\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\zeta_{\pm}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{2}i+\frac{1}{4}\cos(2\theta_{\pm})-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\right)\mp\frac{\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}-\beta^{\frac{3}{2}}}{12\sqrt{2}\zeta_{\pm}}\cos(3\theta_{\pm})

(3.282)

\displaystyle\quad+(\alpha^{2}-\beta^{2})\Bigg(\frac{2i-1}{8}\cos(2\theta_{\pm})+\frac{1}{32}\cos(4\theta_{\pm})+\tilde{\lambda}_{i}(\frac{i}{2}-\frac{1}{4})\ln\zeta_{\pm}-\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{i}}{4}\cos(2\theta_{\pm})+\frac{i}{4}-\frac{7}{64}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\left((\frac{1}{4}-\frac{i}{2})\ln\nu+\frac{1-10i}{16}\right)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\beta^{2}\underbrace{\left(\frac{10i-7}{64}-4A_{i}+\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\left(\frac{2i-1}{4}|\ln\nu|+\frac{1-10i}{16}-2B_{i}\right)\right)}_{=0}+\phi_{i,\pm,\sharp}^{\textup{in},z}(z_{\pm})-\phi_{i,high}^{\textup{ex}}(z).

In order to match the constant terms above, we picked

\tilde{\lambda}_{i}=-\frac{\frac{10i-7}{64}-4A_{i}}{\frac{2i-1}{4}|\ln\nu|+\frac{1-10i}{16}-2B_{i}}

which corresponds to (
3.271
). We have
|a-a_{\infty}|\lesssim\nu
so that
|\alpha-\beta|\lesssim\nu
and
\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z\mp\bar{z})-\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z)=\mathcal{O}(\nu\zeta_{\pm}^{-3})=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{\epsilon}\zeta_{\pm}^{-2-\epsilon})
for
\nu\ll\zeta_{\pm}\ll 1
. Using this and
|\tilde{\lambda}_{i}|\lesssim|\ln\nu|^{-1}
we deduce from (
3.282
) using (
3.109
) and (
3.223
) that for
\nu\ll|z-a|\ll 1
,

\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{bo}}(z)=\mathcal{O}(\frac{\nu}{|z-a|^{2}})+\mathcal{O}(\frac{\nu}{|z-a|})+\mathcal{O}(\nu)+\mathcal{O}(\nu^{\epsilon}|z-a|^{-4-\epsilon})+\mathcal{O}(|z-a|^{\sqrt{5}-2}),

which shows (
3.280
) for
k=1
. The estimate propagates for higher order derivative by (
3.109
) and (
3.223
).

Proof of (
3.281
)
. By (
3.110
) and (
3.225
) we compute

\displaystyle V_{i,\pm}^{\textup{bo}}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{4\zeta^{2}_{\pm}}\pm\frac{\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}}{4}\frac{1}{\zeta_{\pm}}\cos(\theta_{\pm})+\alpha\Bigg(\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{i}-i}{4}\ln^{2}\zeta_{\pm}+\left(\frac{i-\tilde{\lambda}_{i}}{2}\ln\nu+\frac{2i+1-2\tilde{\lambda}_{i})}{4}\right)\ln\zeta_{\pm}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{i}-i}{4}\ln^{2}\nu+\frac{2\tilde{\lambda}_{i}-1-2i}{4}\ln\nu+\frac{1}{16}\cos(2\theta_{\pm})\Bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\mp\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}\zeta_{\pm}\left(L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\frac{1-4i+4\tilde{\lambda}_{i}}{32}\cos\theta+\frac{1}{96\sqrt{2}}\cos(3\theta_{\pm})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\alpha^{2}\zeta^{2}_{\pm}\Bigg(\frac{1-2i}{16}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\ln\zeta_{\pm}+\frac{2i-1}{16}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\ln\nu+\frac{2\tilde{\lambda}_{i}+1-2i}{32}\ln\zeta_{\pm}\cos(2\theta_{\pm})+\frac{2i-1-\tilde{\lambda}_{i}}{32}\ln\nu\cos(2\theta_{\pm})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{16}\left(\frac{7}{16}-i+\tilde{\lambda}_{i}(\frac{9}{2}i-\frac{5}{4})\right)+\frac{5-10i+10\tilde{\lambda}_{i}-8M_{i}^{\textup{in}}}{128}\cos(2\theta_{\pm})+\frac{1}{384}\cos(4\theta_{\pm})\Bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad+V^{\textup{in},z}_{i,\pm,\sharp}(z_{\pm})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4\zeta_{\pm}^{2}}+\left(\frac{\beta}{4}i-\frac{\beta}{4}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\right)(\log\zeta_{\pm})^{2}-\frac{\beta}{16}\cos(2\theta_{\pm})\pm\frac{\beta^{3/2}}{96\sqrt{2}}\zeta_{\pm}\cos(3\theta_{\pm})-\beta^{2}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\frac{1-2i}{16}\zeta_{\pm}^{2}\log\zeta_{\pm}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\beta^{2}\zeta_{\pm}^{2}\left(-\frac{3i}{128}-A_{i}+\left(\frac{2i-1}{16}-\frac{B_{i}}{2}\right)\tilde{\lambda}_{i}+\frac{1-2i+2\tilde{\lambda}_{i}}{32}\log\zeta_{\pm}\cos(2\theta_{\pm})+\frac{1}{384}\cos(4\theta_{\pm})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\mp\frac{A_{i}^{\textup{ex}}}{\zeta_{\pm}}\cos(\theta_{\pm})-B_{i}^{\textup{ex}}\log\zeta_{\pm}-C_{i}^{\textup{ex}}\mp D_{i}^{\textup{ex}}\zeta_{\pm}\cos(\theta_{\pm})-E_{i}^{\textup{ex}}\zeta_{\pm}^{2}\cos(2\theta_{\pm})

\displaystyle\qquad-V_{i,high}^{\textup{ex}}(z-\bar{z})+V_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z\mp\bar{z})-V_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z)

\displaystyle=(\alpha-\beta)\left(\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{i}-i}{4}\ln^{2}\zeta_{\pm}+\frac{1}{16}\cos(2\theta_{\pm})\right)\mp\frac{\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}-\beta^{\frac{3}{2}}}{96\sqrt{2}}\zeta_{\pm}\cos(3\theta_{\pm})

\displaystyle\quad+(\alpha^{2}-\beta^{2})\zeta^{2}_{\pm}\Bigg(\frac{1-2i}{16}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\ln\zeta_{\pm}+\frac{2i-1}{16}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\ln\nu+\frac{1}{16}\left(\frac{7}{16}-i+\tilde{\lambda}_{i}(\frac{9}{2}i-\frac{5}{4})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{2\tilde{\lambda}_{i}+1-2i}{32}\ln\zeta_{\pm}\cos(2\theta_{\pm})+\frac{1}{384}\cos(4\theta_{\pm})\Bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\pm\underbrace{\left(\frac{\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}}{4}-A_{i}^{\textup{ex}}\right)}_{=0}\frac{1}{\zeta_{\pm}}\cos(\theta_{\pm})\mp\underbrace{\left(\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\frac{1-4i+4\tilde{\lambda}_{i}}{32}-D_{i}^{\textup{ex}}\right)}_{=2^{-5}[\alpha^{3/2}(1-4i+4\tilde{\lambda}_{i})-8\alpha^{1/2}D_{i,1}^{\textup{ex}}]\tilde{L}_{i}^{\textup{in}}}\zeta_{\pm}\cos(\theta_{\pm})

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\left(\alpha\left(\frac{i-\tilde{\lambda}_{i}}{2}\ln\nu+\frac{2i+1-2\tilde{\lambda}_{i})}{4}\right)-B_{i}^{\textup{ex}}\right)}_{=0}\ln\zeta_{\pm}+\underbrace{\alpha\left(\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{i}-i}{4}\ln^{2}\nu+\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{i}-1-2i}{4}\ln\nu\right)-C_{i}^{\textup{ex}}}_{=0}

\displaystyle\quad+\beta^{2}\zeta_{\pm}^{2}\underbrace{\left(\frac{2i-1}{16}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\ln\nu+\frac{1}{16}\left(\frac{7}{16}-i+\tilde{\lambda}_{i}(\frac{9}{2}i-\frac{5}{4})\right)+\frac{3i}{128}+A_{i}+\left(\frac{1-2i}{16}+\frac{B_{i}}{2}\right)\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\right)}_{=I=0}

\displaystyle+\zeta^{2}_{\pm}\underbrace{\left(\alpha^{2}\left(\frac{2i-1-\tilde{\lambda}_{i}}{32}\ln\nu+\frac{5-10i+10\tilde{\lambda}_{i}-8M_{i}^{\textup{in}}}{128}\right)-E_{i}^{\textup{ex}}\right)}_{=-\frac{\alpha^{2}}{16}\tilde{M}_{i}^{\textup{in}}}\cos(2\theta_{\pm})

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}(\nu^{\epsilon}\zeta_{\pm}^{-2-k-\epsilon})+\mathcal{O}(\zeta_{\pm}^{\sqrt{5}})

(3.283)

where we used (
3.111
) and (
3.226
) to estimate
V^{\textup{in},z}_{1,\pm,\sharp}
and
V_{i,high}^{\textup{ex}}(z)
, as well as
V_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z\mp\bar{z})-V_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z)=\mathcal{O}(\nu\zeta_{\pm}^{-3})=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{\epsilon}\zeta_{\pm}^{-2-\epsilon})
since
|\bar{z}|\lesssim\nu
.

In order to match the
\ln\zeta_{\pm}
term above, we picked
B_{i}^{\textup{ex}}=\alpha\left(\frac{i-\tilde{\lambda}_{i}}{2}\ln\nu+\frac{2i+1-2\tilde{\lambda}_{i})}{4}\right)
which corresponds to (
3.274
). In order to match the constant term, we picked
C_{i}^{\textup{ex}}=\alpha\left(\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{i}-i}{4}\ln^{2}\nu+\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{i}-1-2i}{4}\ln\nu\right)
which corresponds to (
3.275
). We compute that

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{2i-1}{4}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\ln\nu+\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{7}{16}-i+\tilde{\lambda}_{i}(\frac{9}{2}i-\frac{5}{4})\right)+\frac{3i}{32}+4A_{i}+\left(\frac{1-2i}{4}+2B_{i}\right)\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\left(\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\left(\frac{2i-1}{4}\ln\nu+\frac{10i-1}{16}+2B_{i}\right)+\frac{7-10i}{64}+4A_{i}\right)\ =0

by the choice (
3.271
) of
\tilde{\lambda}_{i}
. In order to match the
\zeta_{\pm}^{2}\cos(2\theta_{\pm})
term, we picked
M_{i}^{\textup{in}}=(2i-1-\tilde{\lambda}_{i})\ln\nu+\frac{5-10i+10\tilde{\lambda}_{i}}{4}-\frac{E_{i}^{\textup{ex}}}{32\alpha^{2}}
, which corresponds to (
3.277
).

∎

3.3.2. The full approximate eigenfunctions and proof of Proposition
3.1

We eventually define
\lambda_{i}=2\beta(1-i+\tilde{\lambda}_{i})
where
\tilde{\lambda}_{i}
is given by (
3.13
), and

\phi_{i}(z)=-\frac{1}{16\nu^{4}}\sum_{\pm}\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}\Big(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu}\Big)\chi(\frac{z\mp a}{\eta})+\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z)\big(1-\sum_{\pm}\chi(\frac{z\mp a}{\eta})\big).

(3.284)

The parameters
A_{i}^{\textup{ex}},B_{i}^{\textup{ex}},C_{i}^{\textup{ex}},M_{i}^{\textup{in}},L_{i}^{\textup{in}}
are given by (
3.272
), and the correction parameters
\tilde{L}_{i}^{\textup{in}}
and
\tilde{M}_{i}^{\textup{in}}
are not fixed yet but will be fixed in the first Step of the proof of Proposition
3.1
below in (
3.306
). The function
\chi
is a smooth radial cut-off function with
\chi(x)=1
for
|x|<1
and
\chi(x)=0
for
|x|>2
. We choose
\eta=\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}}
with
\tilde{\epsilon}
small enough so that we can simplify
\mathcal{O}(\nu^{\epsilon}|\zeta_{\pm}|^{-4-\epsilon})=\mathcal{O}(|\zeta_{\pm}|^{\sqrt{5}-2})
and
\mathcal{O}(\nu^{\epsilon}|\zeta_{\pm}|^{-4-\epsilon})=\mathcal{O}(|\zeta_{\pm}|^{\sqrt{5}-2})
for
|\zeta_{\pm}|\approx\eta
in (
3.280
) and (
3.281
). We can now end the proof of Proposition
3.1
.

Proof of Proposition
3.1
.

Step 1
.
Matching the parameters to cancel the Poisson field
. Let
V_{i}(z)=\Phi_{\chi^{*}\phi_{i}}(z)
. We decompose

V_{i}(z)=\sum_{\pm}-\frac{1}{16\nu^{2}}V_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}\Big(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu}\Big)\chi(\frac{z\mp a}{\eta})+V_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z)\big(1-\sum_{\pm}\chi(\frac{z\mp a}{\eta})\big)+\tilde{V}_{i}.

(3.285)

We recall that
-\Delta V_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}=\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}
and
-\Delta V_{i,\pm}^{\textup{ex}}=\chi^{*}\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{ex}}
, so that the equation
-\Delta V_{i}=\chi^{*}\phi_{i}
gives

-\Delta\tilde{V}_{i}=F_{i,+}^{\textup{bo}}+F_{i,-}^{\textup{bo}},\qquad F_{i,\pm}^{\textup{bo}}=\frac{2}{\eta}\nabla\chi(\frac{z\mp a}{\eta}).\nabla V_{i,\pm}^{\textup{bo}}+\frac{1}{\eta 2}\Delta\chi(\frac{z\mp a}{\eta})V_{i,\pm}^{\textup{bo}}.

(3.286)

As
V^{\textup{ex}}
grows sublinearly as
|z|\to\infty
from (
3.228
), by a uniqueness argument for the Poisson equation we have

\tilde{V}_{i}=\tilde{V}_{i,+}+\tilde{V}_{i,-}\qquad\mbox{where}\qquad\tilde{V}_{i,\pm}=\Phi_{F_{i,\pm}^{\textup{bo}}}.

(3.287)

We want to find values for
\tilde{L}_{i}^{\textup{in}}
,
\tilde{M}_{i}^{\textup{in}}
such that

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{1}}\tilde{V}_{i}(a)=\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}\tilde{V}_{i}(a)=0.

(3.288)

In order to achieve (
3.288
), notice that by linearity,

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{x_{1}}\tilde{V}_{i}(a)\\
\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}\tilde{V}_{i}(a)\end{pmatrix}=\mathfrak{M}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{L}_{i}^{\textup{in}}\\
\tilde{M}_{i}^{\textup{in}}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{x_{1}}\tilde{V}_{i}[A_{i}^{\textup{ex}*},B_{i}^{\textup{ex}*},C_{i}^{\textup{ex}},L_{i}^{\textup{ex}*},M_{i}^{\textup{in}*}](a)\\
\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}\tilde{V}_{i}[A_{i}^{\textup{ex}*},B_{i}^{\textup{ex}*},C_{i}^{\textup{ex}*},L_{i}^{\textup{ex}*}M_{i}^{\textup{in}*}](a)\end{pmatrix}

(3.289)

where

\mathfrak{M}=\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{\tilde{L}_{i}^{\textup{in}}}\partial_{x_{1}}\tilde{V}_{i}(a)&\partial_{\tilde{M}_{i}^{\textup{in}}}\partial_{x_{1}}\tilde{V}_{i}(a)\\
\partial_{\tilde{L}_{i}^{\textup{in}}}\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}\tilde{V}_{i}(a)&\partial_{\tilde{M}_{i}^{\textup{in}}}\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}\tilde{V}_{i}(a)\end{pmatrix}.

We compute using the representation formula
\Phi_{f}=-(2\pi)^{-1}\log|\cdot|*f
, and then integrating by parts and using polar coordinates,

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{1}}\tilde{V}_{i,+}(a)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{z_{+,1}}{|z_{+}|^{2}}F_{i,+}^{\textup{bo}}(a+z_{+})dz_{+}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi\eta^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left(-\partial_{rr}\chi(\frac{\zeta_{+}}{\eta})+\frac{\eta}{\zeta_{+}}\partial_{r}\chi(\frac{\zeta_{+}}{\eta})\right)\cos(\theta_{+})V_{i,+}^{\textup{bo}}(a+z_{+})d\zeta_{+}d\theta_{+}.

(3.290)

A direct estimate of the above integral with (
3.281
) yields

\partial_{x_{1}}\tilde{V}_{i,+}[A_{i}^{\textup{ex}*},...,M_{i}^{\textup{in}*}](a)=\mathcal{O}(\eta^{\sqrt{5}-1}).

(3.291)

Next, by (
3.283
) and (
3.272
) we have

\displaystyle\partial_{\tilde{L}_{i}^{\textup{in}}}\tilde{V}_{i,+}^{\textup{bo}}=2^{-5}[\alpha^{3/2}(1-4i+4\tilde{\lambda}_{i})-8\alpha^{1/2}D_{i,1}^{\textup{ex}}]\zeta_{+}\cos(\theta_{+})+O(\zeta_{+}^{\sqrt{5}}),

(3.292)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tilde{M}_{i}^{\textup{in}}}\tilde{V}_{i,+}^{\textup{bo}}=-\frac{\alpha^{2}}{16}\zeta_{+}^{2}\cos(2\theta_{+})+O(\zeta_{+}^{\sqrt{5}}).

(3.293)

Injecting (
3.292
) and (
3.293
) in (
3.290
), using
\int_{0}^{\infty}(-\partial_{rr}\chi(\frac{\zeta_{+}}{\eta})+\frac{\eta}{\zeta_{+}}\partial_{r}\chi(\frac{\zeta_{+}}{\eta}))\zeta_{+}d\zeta_{+}=-2\eta^{2}
and that
\int_{0}^{2\pi}\cos(\theta_{+})\cos(k\theta_{+})d\theta_{+}=\pi\delta_{k=1}
we find

\displaystyle\partial_{\tilde{L}_{i}^{\textup{in}}}\partial_{x_{1}}\tilde{V}_{i,+}(a)=2^{-5}[\alpha^{3/2}(1-4i+4\tilde{\lambda}_{i})-8\alpha^{1/2}D_{i,1}^{\textup{ex}}]\tilde{L}_{i}^{\textup{in}}+\mathcal{O}(\eta^{\sqrt{5}-1}),

(3.294)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tilde{M}_{i}^{\textup{in}}}\partial_{x_{1}}\tilde{V}_{i,+}(a)=\mathcal{O}(\eta^{\sqrt{5}-1}).

(3.295)

Another computation, similar to the one leading to (
3.290
), shows

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}\tilde{V}_{i,+}(a)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{z_{+,1}^{2}-z_{+,2}^{2}}{|z_{+}|^{4}}F_{i,+}^{\textup{bo}}(a+z_{+})dz_{+}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi\eta^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left(-\partial_{rr}\chi(\frac{\zeta_{+}}{\eta})+\frac{\eta}{\zeta_{+}}\partial_{r}\chi(\frac{\zeta_{+}}{\eta})\right)\frac{\cos(2\theta_{+})}{\zeta_{+}}V_{i,+}^{\textup{bo}}(a+z_{+})d\zeta_{+}d\theta_{+}.

(3.296)

Using (
3.281
), (
3.292
) and (
3.293
) and
\int_{0}^{\infty}(-\partial_{rr}\chi(\frac{\zeta_{+}}{\eta})+3\frac{\eta}{\zeta_{+}}\partial_{r}\chi(\frac{\zeta_{+}}{\eta}))\zeta_{+}d\zeta_{+}=-4\eta^{2}
we find

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}\tilde{V}_{i,+}[A_{i}^{\textup{ex}*},...,M_{i}^{\textup{in}*}](a)=\mathcal{O}(\eta^{\sqrt{5}-2}),

(3.297)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tilde{L}_{i}^{\textup{in}}}\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}\tilde{V}_{i,+}(a)=\mathcal{O}(\eta^{\sqrt{5}-2}),

(3.298)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tilde{M}_{i}^{\textup{in}}}\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}\tilde{V}_{i,+}(a)=\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\mathcal{O}(\eta^{\sqrt{5}-2}).

(3.299)

We have for
\eta
small enough, using that
F_{i,-}^{\textup{bo}}
is supported in
\{\eta<|z+a|<2\eta\}
and performing a Taylor expansion,

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{1}}\tilde{V}_{i,-}(a)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{z_{-}-2a}{|z_{-}-2a|^{2}}F_{i,-}^{\textup{bo}}(-a+z_{-})dz_{-}

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{4|a|^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}F_{i,-}^{\textup{bo}}(z)dz+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathcal{O}(|z_{-}|)F_{i,-}^{\textup{bo}}(-a+z_{-})dz_{-}

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{4|a|^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}F_{i,-}^{\textup{bo}}(z)dz+\mathcal{O}(\||z+a|F_{i,-}^{\textup{bo}}\|_{L^{1}})

Direct estimates using (
3.281
), the analogue of (
3.292
) and (
3.293
) for
V_{i,-}^{\textup{bo}}
for
k=1,2
show that
\|F_{i,-}^{\textup{bo}}[A_{i}^{\textup{ex}*},...,M_{i}^{\textup{in}*}]\|_{L^{1}}=\mathcal{O}(\eta^{\sqrt{5}})
,
\||z+a|\partial_{\tilde{L}_{i}^{\textup{in}}}F_{i,-}^{\textup{bo}}\|_{L^{1}}=\mathcal{O}(\eta^{2})
and
\||z+a|\partial_{\tilde{M}_{i}^{\textup{in}}}F_{i,-}^{\textup{bo}}\|_{L^{1}}=\mathcal{O}(\eta^{3})
. Using these estimates and
\int_{0}^{2\pi}\cos(k\theta_{-})d\theta_{-}=0
for
k=1,2
we have

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{1}}\tilde{V}_{i,-}[A_{i}^{\textup{ex}*},...,M_{i}^{\textup{in}*}](a)=\mathcal{O}(\eta^{\sqrt{5}}),

(3.300)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tilde{L}_{i}^{\textup{in}}}\partial_{x_{1}}\tilde{V}_{i,+}(a)=\mathcal{O}(\eta^{2}),

(3.301)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tilde{M}_{i}^{\textup{in}}}\partial_{x_{1}}\tilde{V}_{i,+}(a)=\mathcal{O}(\eta^{\sqrt{5}}).

(3.302)

An analogue computation shows

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}\tilde{V}_{i,-}[A_{i}^{\textup{ex}*},...,M_{i}^{\textup{in}*}](a)=\mathcal{O}(\eta^{\sqrt{5}}),

(3.303)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tilde{L}_{i}^{\textup{in}}}\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}\tilde{V}_{i,+}(a)=\mathcal{O}(\eta^{2}),

(3.304)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tilde{M}_{i}^{\textup{in}}}\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}\tilde{V}_{i,+}(a)=\mathcal{O}(\eta^{\sqrt{5}}).

(3.305)

Combining (
3.291
), (
3.294
), (
3.295
), (
3.297
), (
3.298
), (
3.299
) and (
3.300
)-(
3.305
) and using
D_{i,1}^{\textup{ex}}=-\frac{\beta}{8}
from Proposition
3.12
with
|\alpha-\beta|\lesssim\nu
shows that in the system (
3.289
)

\mathfrak{M}=\begin{pmatrix}2^{-5}\alpha^{3/2}[2-4i+4\tilde{\lambda}_{i}]+\mathcal{O}(\eta^{\sqrt{5}-1})&\mathcal{O}(\eta^{\sqrt{5}-1})\\
\mathcal{O}(\eta^{\sqrt{5}-2})&\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}+\mathcal{O}(\eta^{\sqrt{5}-2})\end{pmatrix}

and

\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{x_{1}}\tilde{V}_{i}[A_{i}^{\textup{ex}*},B_{i}^{\textup{ex}*},...,M_{i}^{\textup{in}*}](a)\\
\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}\tilde{V}_{i}[A_{i}^{\textup{ex}*},B_{i}^{\textup{ex}*},...,M_{i}^{\textup{in}*}](a)\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{O}(\eta^{\sqrt{5}-1})\\
\mathcal{O}(\eta^{\sqrt{5}-2})\end{pmatrix}.

In order to ensure the vanishing condition (
3.288
) for
\tilde{V}_{i}
, we choose

\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{L}_{i}^{\textup{in}}\\
\tilde{M}_{i}^{\textup{in}}\end{pmatrix}=-\mathfrak{M}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{x_{1}}\tilde{V}_{i}[A_{i}^{\textup{ex}*},B_{i}^{\textup{ex}*},...,M_{i}^{\textup{in}*}](a)\\
\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}\tilde{V}_{i}[A_{i}^{\textup{ex}*},B_{i}^{\textup{ex}*},...,M_{i}^{\textup{in}*}](a)\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{O}(\eta^{\sqrt{5}-1})\\
\mathcal{O}(\eta^{\sqrt{5}-2})\end{pmatrix}.

(3.306)

Step 2
.
Estimates for the eigenfunctions
.

Proof of (
3.6
).
We estimate all terms in (
3.284
). First, by (
3.106
), (
3.108
) and (
3.109
) we have

\left|\nabla^{k}\left(\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu})\right)\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{4+k}}

(3.307)

for
|z\mp a|\leq 1
. Second, by (
3.222
) and (
3.223
) we have

\left|\nabla^{k}\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z)\right|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{\langle z\rangle^{C_{k}}}{|z\mp a|^{4+k}}

(3.308)

for
z\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{-a,a\}
. Third, by a direct computation,

\left|\nabla^{k}\left(\chi(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}}})\right)\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{|z\mp a|^{k}}\mathbbm{1}(\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}}\leq|z\mp a|\leq 2\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}})

(3.309)

for all
z\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Injecting (
3.307
), (
3.308
) and (
3.309
) in (
3.284
) shows
|\nabla^{k}\phi_{i}(z)|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{-4-k}\langle z\rangle^{C_{k}}
. This proves the first inequality in (
3.6
). The second inequality in (
3.6
) follows from applying
\nu\partial_{\nu}
to (
3.284
) and performing analogue estimates.

Proof of the first inequality in (
3.8
).
We have by (
3.4
), (
3.5
) and then using
16L_{i}=-\alpha^{1/2}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}
that

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{i}(z)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{16\nu^{4}}\sum_{\pm}\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu})\chi_{\pm a,\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}}}(z)+\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(1-\sum_{\pm}\chi_{\pm a,\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}}}(z))

\displaystyle-\sum_{\pm}\left(\frac{-1}{16\nu^{4}}\Lambda U\pm\frac{L_{i}}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U\right)(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu}))\chi_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}}(z)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{16\nu^{4}}\sum_{\pm}(\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}-\Lambda U\mp\alpha^{1/2}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\partial_{x_{1}}U)(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu})\chi_{\pm a,\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}}}(z)

(3.310)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\pm}\left(\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z)-|z\mp a|^{-4}\right)(\chi_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}}(z)-\chi_{\pm a,\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}}}(z))

\displaystyle-\sum_{\pm}\left(\frac{-1}{16\nu^{4}}(\Lambda U+16|\cdot|^{-4})\pm\frac{L_{i}}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U\right)(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu}))(\chi_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}}(z)-\chi_{\pm a,\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}}}(z)

\displaystyle+\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(1-\sum_{\pm}\chi_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}}(z)).

We now estimate all terms in (
3.310
). Recall that
|\nabla^{k}(\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}-\Lambda U\mp\alpha^{1/2}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)(y)|\lesssim\nu^{2}\langle y\rangle^{-2-k}
by (
3.144
). Hence by (
3.309
) we have

\left|\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\nabla^{k}\left(\sum_{\pm}(\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}-\Lambda U\mp\alpha^{1/2}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\partial_{x_{1}}U)(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu})\chi_{\pm a,\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}}}(z)\right)\right|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{2+k}}.

(3.311)

We have
|\nabla^{k}(\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z)-\sum_{\pm}|z\mp a|^{-4})|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}|z\mp a|^{-2-k}
by (
3.222
) and (
3.223
). By this and (
3.309
) we get

\left|\nabla^{k}\left(\sum_{\pm}\left(\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(z)-|z\mp a|^{-4}\right)(\chi_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}}(z)-\chi_{\pm a,\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}}}(z))\right)\right|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{2+k}}.

(3.312)

We have the tail cancellation
|\nabla^{k}(\Lambda U+16|y|^{-4})|\lesssim|y|^{-6-k}
, and
|\nabla^{k}\partial_{x_{1}}U(y)|\lesssim|y|^{-5-k}
. Hence

\left|\left(\frac{-\nabla^{k}}{16\nu^{4}}(\Lambda U+16|y|^{-4})\pm\frac{L_{i}}{\nu^{3}}\nabla^{k}\partial_{x_{1}}U\right)(y)\right|\lesssim\nu^{-4}|y|^{-6-k}+\nu^{-3}|y|^{-5-k}\lesssim\nu^{-3\tilde{\epsilon}}|y|^{-2-k}

for
\nu^{-1+\tilde{\epsilon}}\lesssim|y|\lesssim\nu^{-1}
. Using that
\chi_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}}-\chi_{\pm a,\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}}}
has support in
\{\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}}\leq|z\mp a|\leq 2\zeta_{*}\}
and (
3.309
) this shows

\left|\nabla^{k}\left(\sum_{\pm}\left(\frac{-1}{16\nu^{4}}(\Lambda U+16|\cdot|^{-4})\pm\frac{L_{i}}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U\right)(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu}))(\chi_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}}(z)-\chi_{\pm a,\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}}}(z)\right)\right|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{\nu^{2-3\tilde{\epsilon}}}{(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{2+k}}.

(3.313)

Finally, as
1-\sum_{\pm}\chi_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}}
is supported in
\cap_{\pm}\{|z\mp a|\geq\zeta_{*}\}
we have

\left|\nabla^{k}\left(\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(1-\sum_{\pm}\chi_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}}(z))\right)\right|\lesssim\langle z\rangle^{C_{k}}.

(3.314)

Injecting (
3.311
), (
3.312
), (
3.313
) and (
3.314
) in (
3.310
) shows
|\nabla^{k}\tilde{\phi}_{i}(z)|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{-2-k}\langle z\rangle^{C_{k}}
. This is the first inequality in (
3.8
).

Proof of the second inequality in (
3.8
).
We compute using (
3.4
), (
3.5
),
16L_{i}=-\alpha^{1/2}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}
and (
3.278
) that

\displaystyle\nu\partial_{\nu}\tilde{\phi}_{i}(z)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{16}\sum_{\pm}\nu\partial_{\nu}\left(\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}(\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}-\Lambda U\mp\alpha^{1/2}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\partial_{x_{1}}U)(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu})\right)\chi_{\pm a,\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}}}(z)

(3.315)

\displaystyle-\sum_{\pm}\nu\partial_{\nu}\left(\frac{-1}{16\nu^{4}}\Lambda U\pm\frac{L_{i}}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu})\right)(\chi_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}}(z)-\chi_{\pm a,\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}}}(z)

\displaystyle+\nu\partial_{\nu}\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(1-\sum_{\pm}\chi_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}}(z))+\sum_{\pm}\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{bo}}(z)\nu\partial_{\nu}(\chi_{\pm a,\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}}}(z))

We now estimate all terms in (
3.315
). We decompose using (
3.33
):

\displaystyle\nu\partial_{\nu}\left(\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\left(\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}-\Lambda U\mp\alpha^{1/2}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\partial_{x_{1}}U\right)(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu})\right)

(3.316)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nu\partial_{\nu}\left(\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}-\Lambda U\right)(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu})\right)+\nu\partial_{\nu}\left(\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\left(\bar{\phi}_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}\mp\alpha^{1/2}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\partial_{x_{1}}U\right)(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu})\right).

For the first term in (
3.316
) we have by (
3.24
)

\displaystyle\nu\partial_{\nu}\left(\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}-\Lambda U\right)(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu})\right)

\displaystyle=\nu\partial_{\nu}\left(\left(\frac{\alpha}{\nu^{2}}(T_{2}^{(i)}+\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\hat{T}_{2})\pm\frac{\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\nu}T_{3}+\alpha^{2}(T_{4}^{(i)}+\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\hat{T}_{4}^{(i)})\right)(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu})\right)

\displaystyle=\left(-\frac{\alpha}{\nu^{2}}(y.\nabla+2)(T_{2}^{(i)}+\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\hat{T}_{2})\mp\frac{\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\nu}(y.\nabla+1)T_{3}+\alpha^{2}y.\nabla(T_{4}^{(i)}+\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\hat{T}_{4}^{(i)})\right)(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu})

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\alpha}{\nu^{2}}(\nu\partial_{\nu}\tilde{\lambda}_{i})\hat{T}_{2}+\alpha^{2}(\nu\partial_{\nu}\tilde{\lambda}_{i})T_{4}^{(i)}.

We have the following tail cancellations by Lemmas
3.5
,
3.6
,
3.7
,
3.8
,
3.9
and
3.10
, and using that
\tilde{\lambda}_{i}=\mathcal{O}(|\ln\nu|^{-1})
and
\nu\partial_{\nu}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}=\mathcal{O}(|\ln\nu|^{-2})
by (
3.271
):

\displaystyle(y.\nabla+2)(T_{2}^{(i)}+\tilde{\lambda}_{i})(y)=\mathcal{O}(\langle y\rangle^{-\sqrt{8}}),

\displaystyle(y.\nabla+1)T_{3}(y)=\mathcal{O}(\langle y\rangle^{2-\sqrt{13}}),

\displaystyle y.\nabla(T_{4}^{(i)}+\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\hat{T}_{4}^{(i)})(y)=\mathcal{O}(\langle y\rangle^{4-\sqrt{20}})+\mathcal{O}(|\ln\nu|^{-1}),

\displaystyle(\nu\partial_{\nu}\tilde{\lambda}_{i})\hat{T}_{2}=\mathcal{O}(|\ln\nu|^{-2}\langle y\rangle^{-2}),

\displaystyle(\nu\partial_{\nu}\tilde{\lambda}_{i})\hat{T}_{4}^{(i)}=\mathcal{O}(|\ln\nu|^{-1}),

for
|y|\lesssim\nu^{-1}
and these estimates propagate to higher order derivatives. Letting
c=\min(\sqrt{8}-2,\sqrt{13}-3,\sqrt{20}-4)
we thus obtain

\displaystyle\left|\nabla^{k}\left(\nu\partial_{\nu}\left(\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}-\Lambda U\right)(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu})\right)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i=0}^{2}\frac{\nu^{c}}{(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{2+c-i+k}}+\frac{|\ln\nu|^{-2}}{(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{2+k}}+\frac{|\ln\nu|^{-1}}{(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{k}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{2+k}}\left(\frac{\nu^{c}}{(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{c}}+|\ln\nu|^{-2}\right)

(3.317)

for
|z\mp a|\lesssim\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}}
. We estimate similarly the second term in (
3.316
) using (
3.34
) and Proposition
3.11
. This is simpler as we do not need to track tail cancellations for this term, and it gives

\left|\nabla^{k}\left(\nu\partial_{\nu}\left(\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\left(\bar{\phi}_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}\mp\alpha^{1/2}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\partial_{x_{1}}U\right)(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu})\right)\right)\right|\lesssim\frac{\nu^{c^{\prime}}}{(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{2+k+c^{\prime}}}.

(3.318)

for some constant
c^{\prime}>0
. Injecting (
3.317
) and (
3.318
) in (
3.316
) we obtain for the first term in (
3.315
)

\left|\nabla^{k}\left(\nu\partial_{\nu}\left(\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\left(\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{in}}-\Lambda U\mp\alpha^{1/2}L_{i}^{\textup{in}}\partial_{x_{1}}U\right)(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu})\right)\right)\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{2+k}}\left(\frac{\nu^{c}}{(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{c}}+|\ln\nu|^{-2}\right)

(3.319)

up to changing the constant
c>0
. Next, for the second term in (
3.315
) we compute

\nu\partial_{\nu}\left(\frac{-1}{16\nu^{4}}\Lambda U\pm\frac{L_{i}}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu})\right)=\left(\frac{1}{16\nu^{4}}(y.\nabla+4)\Lambda U\mp\frac{L_{i}}{\nu^{3}}(y.\nabla+3)\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu})\right)

Using the tail cancellation
(y.\nabla+4)\Lambda U=\mathcal{O}(|y|^{-6})
and
(y.\nabla+3)\partial_{x_{1}}U=\mathcal{O}(|y|^{-5})
, and repeating the same computations as that leading to (
3.313
) shows

\left|\nabla^{k}\left(\nu\partial_{\nu}\left(\frac{-1}{16\nu^{4}}\Lambda U\pm\frac{L_{i}}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu})\right)\right)\right|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{\nu^{2-3\tilde{\epsilon}}}{(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{2+k}}.

(3.320)

For the third term in (
3.315
) we have by (
3.231
) and (
3.271
) that
\nu\partial_{\nu}\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}=\nu\partial_{\nu}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}Z_{i}=\mathcal{O}(|\ln\nu|^{-2})Z_{i}
. Hence by (
3.256
), (
3.243
)and (
3.309
) we infer:

\left|\nabla^{k}\left(\nu\partial_{\nu}\phi_{i}^{\textup{ex}}(1-\sum_{\pm}\chi_{\pm a,\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}}})\right)\right|=\left|\nabla^{k}\left((\nu\partial_{\nu}\tilde{\lambda}_{i})Z_{i}(1-\sum_{\pm}\chi_{\pm a,\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}}})\right)\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{2+k}}.

(3.321)

Finally, for the fourth term in (
3.315
), using (
3.280
), (
3.309
) and the fact that
\nu\partial_{\nu}\chi_{\pm a,\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}}}
has support inside
\{\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}}\leq|z\mp a|\leq 2\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}}\}
we have

\left|\nabla^{k}\left(\phi_{i,\pm}^{\textup{bo}}(z)\nu\partial_{\nu}(\chi_{\pm a,\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}}}(z))\right)\right|\lesssim\frac{\nu^{c^{\prime\prime}}}{(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{k}}

(3.322)

for some
c^{\prime\prime}>0
depending on
\tilde{\epsilon}
. Injecting (
3.319
), (
3.320
), (
3.321
) and (
3.322
) in (
3.315
) shows the second inequality in (
3.8
) as desired.

Proof of (
3.7
).
Let
k\geq 1
. We first estimate
\tilde{V}_{i}
. The two vanishing conditions (
3.288
) imply that

\nabla\tilde{V}_{i}(\pm a)=0\quad\mbox{and}\quad\nabla^{2}\tilde{V}_{i}(\pm a)=0

(3.323)

for both signs
\pm
by symmetry. We first consider the zone
\cup_{\pm}\{|z\mp a|<\frac{1}{2}\eta\}
. We notice by (
3.287
) and (
3.337
) that there since
F_{i,\pm}^{\textup{bo}}
is supported in
\{\eta<|z\mp a|<2\eta\}
using
\nabla^{k}\tilde{V}_{i}=-(2\pi)^{-1}\sum_{\pm}\nabla^{k}(\log|\cdot|)*F_{i,\pm}^{\textup{bo}}
there holds
|\nabla^{2+k}\tilde{V}_{i}|\lesssim\eta^{-2-k}\sum_{\pm}\|F_{i}^{\textup{bo}}\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim\eta^{\sqrt{5}-4-k}
. Therefore, by the vanishing (
3.323
) and a Taylor expansion we have
|\nabla^{k}\tilde{V}_{i}|\lesssim\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}(\sqrt{5}-2)}|z\mp a|^{2}(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{-k}
.

We next consider the zone
\cap_{\pm}\{3\eta<|z-a|\}
where we estimate similarly using that
F_{i,\pm}^{\textup{bo}}
is supported in
\{\eta<|z\mp a|<2\eta\}
that
|\nabla^{k}\tilde{V}_{i}|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{|z\mp a|^{-k}}\sum_{\pm}\|F_{i,\pm}^{\textup{bo}}\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim\eta^{\sqrt{5}}|z\mp a|^{-k}\lesssim\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}(\sqrt{5}-2)}(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{2-k}
.

We finally consider the zone
\cup_{\pm}\{\frac{1}{2}\eta<|z-a|<3\eta\}
. At the boundaries
\cup_{\pm}\{|z-a|=\frac{1}{2}\eta\}
and
\cup_{\pm}\{|z-a|=3\eta\}
we have that
\nabla^{k}\tilde{V}_{i}=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}(\sqrt{5}-2)}\eta^{2-k})
. Since
\tilde{V}_{i}
solves the Poisson equation (
3.286
), with
\nabla^{k}F_{i,\pm}^{\textup{bo}}=\mathcal{O}(\eta^{\sqrt{5}-2-k})=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}(\sqrt{5}-2)}\eta^{-k})
, we deduce by standard elliptic regularity estimates and a scaling argument that
\nabla^{k}\tilde{V}_{i}=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}(\sqrt{5}-2)}\eta^{2-k})
.

Combining the analysis made in the three zones, we have shown that for
k\geq 1
,

|\nabla^{k}\tilde{V}_{i}|\lesssim\nu^{\tilde{\epsilon}(\sqrt{5}-2)}(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{2-k}.

(3.324)

Th estimate (
3.7
) then follows directly from the decomposition (
3.285
) and of the estimates (
3.110
), (
3.225
) and (
3.324
).

4. Coercivity of the linearized operator

In self-similar variables (
2.5
) and around
U_{1+2,\nu}
given by (
2.4
), the linearized operator is

\mathscr{L}^{z}u=\Delta u-\nabla.\Big(u\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+U_{1+2,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{u}\Big)-\frac{1}{2}\Lambda u.

In this section we construct a matched scalar product for which
\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}
, which is the operator
\mathscr{L}^{z}
with a slight truncation of the Poisson field defined by (
2.11
), is coercive on a finite co-dimensional set.

4.1. Matched scalar products

4.1.1. The scalar product for the interior zone

We present in this subsection a scalar product for which the linearized operator
\mathscr{L}^{z}
is to leading order symmetric, for functions that are located around
\pm a
. Namely, consider the bilinear functional

\langle u,v\rangle_{\flat}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}u\mathscr{M}^{z}v\,dz

(4.1)

where we introduce the operator

\mathscr{M}^{z}u=\gamma_{\nu}u-\nu^{2}\Phi_{u},\qquad\gamma_{\nu}(z)=\nu^{2}/U_{1+2,\nu}(z)
