(4.2)

We note that the weight
\gamma_{\nu}
satisfies

\gamma_{\nu}(z)\approx(\nu+|z-a|)^{4}(\nu+|z+a|)^{4}\langle z\rangle^{-4}.

(4.3)

The motivation of introducing (
4.1
) comes from the fact that there is the free energy functional for positive solutions of the problem in original variables introduced in
[
NSY97
]
(see also
[
BDP06
]
,
[
Bil98
]
,
[
GZ98
]
)

\mathcal{F}[u]=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}u\Big(\ln u-\frac{1}{2}\Phi_{u}\Big)dx.

(4.4)

It decreases along the flow for positive solutions with enough regularity and integrability as

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{F}[u]=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}u\Big|\nabla\ln u-\nabla\Phi_{u}\Big|^{2}dx.

The quadratic form (
4.1
) is that associated to the Hessian of this free energy functional at
U_{1+2,\nu}
.

We first show that
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\flat}
is well defined in
L^{2}(\gamma_{\nu}dz)
, where
L^{2}(\gamma_{\nu}dz)
is the weighted
L^{2}
space equipped with the scalar product

\langle u,v\rangle_{\gamma_{\nu}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}uv\gamma_{\nu}dz,\quad\|u\|_{\gamma_{\nu}}=\sqrt{\langle u,u\rangle_{\gamma_{\nu}}}

(4.5)

and it is equivalent to
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\gamma_{\nu}}
on a suitable subspace.

Proposition 4.1

(Scalar product for the interior zone)
.

There exists
C>0
such that the following hold for
u,v\in L^{2}(\gamma_{\nu}dz)
. The functional
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\flat}
is symmetric

\langle u,v\rangle_{\flat}=\langle v,u\rangle_{\flat},

(4.6)

and it is continuous for
|a|>0
and
\nu
small enough,

|\langle u,v\rangle_{\flat}|\leq C\|u\|_{\gamma_{\nu}}\|v\|_{\gamma_{\nu}}.

(4.7)

Moreover, let
\eta>0
small enough and
u
satisfy the orthogonality conditions

\langle u,\mathbbm{1}(|z+\iota a|<\eta)\rangle=\langle u,(\Lambda U)_{i,\nu}\mathbbm{1}(|z+\iota_{i}a|<\eta)\rangle=\langle u,\partial_{j}U_{i,\nu}\mathbbm{1}(|z+\iota_{i}a|<\eta)\rangle=0,

(4.8)

for all
\iota\in\{\pm 1\}
,
i=1,2
,
j=1,2
, where
\iota_{i}=(-1)^{i}
. Then, the quadratic form
\langle u,u\rangle_{\flat}
is positive

\|u\|^{2}_{\gamma_{\nu}}\leq C\langle u,u\rangle_{\flat}.

(4.9)

We then show that
\mathscr{L}^{z}
can be decomposed as the sum of two operators, one which is symmetric and coercive for
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\flat}
, and the other which is negligible for functions that are localized near
\pm a
.

Lemma 4.2

(Coercivity and almost symmetry in the interior zone)
.

The operator
\mathscr{L}^{z}
can be written

\mathscr{L}^{z}q=\nabla.\Big[\gamma_{\nu}^{-1}\nabla\mathscr{M}^{z}q\Big]+\nabla.\big[q\nabla V\big]

(4.10)

where

V=\ln\frac{U_{1+2,\nu}}{U_{12,\nu}}-\frac{1}{4}z^{2}.

(4.11)

For Schwartz functions
u
and
v
, we have

\langle\mathscr{L}^{z}u,v\rangle_{\flat}=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\gamma_{\nu}^{-1}\nabla\mathscr{M}^{z}u\cdot\nabla\mathscr{M}^{z}vdz+\int\nabla.(u\nabla V)\mathscr{M}^{z}vdz.

(4.12)

Moreover, for
|a|\approx 1
, there exists
\delta>0
such that for
\eta^{\prime},\eta>0
small enough, if
u
has support inside
\{|z-a|<\eta^{\prime}\}\cup\{|z+a|<\eta^{\prime}\}
and satisfies the orthogonality conditions

\langle u,(\Lambda U)_{i,\nu}\mathbbm{1}(|z+\iota_{i}a|<\eta)\rangle=\langle u,\partial_{j}U_{i,\nu}\mathbbm{1}(|z+\iota_{i}a|<\eta)\rangle=0,

(4.13)

for all
\iota\in\{\pm 1\}
,
i=1,2
,
j=1,2
, where
\iota_{i}=(-1)^{i}
, there holds

-\langle\mathscr{L}^{z}u,u\rangle_{\flat}\geq\delta\int|\nabla u|^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz.

(4.14)

Remark 4.3
.

If the stationary states are in their equilibrium position
a=(2,0)
, then a direct computation shows that
\nabla V(\pm a)=O(\nu^{2})
. Hence, by (
4.10
) we have that
\mathscr{L}^{z}u
is well approximated by the operator
\nabla.\Big[\gamma_{\nu}^{-1}\nabla\mathscr{M}^{z}u\Big]
, for functions
u
that are localized near
\pm a
. We remark that this operator is symmetric for the scalar product
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\flat}
and that its associated quadratic form is the first term in the right-hand side of (
4.12
). This is what motivated the introduction of the scalar product (
4.1
) to study the coercivity of
\mathscr{L}^{z}
. Note that the coercivity (
4.14
) holds even if
a
is not in the equilibrium position, as the term
\nabla.[q\nabla V]
is negligible with respect to the dissipation effect of the first term in (
4.10
).

We now proceed with the proof of Proposition
4.1
and Lemma
4.2
. In our analysis, we shall rely numerous times on the following inequality.

Lemma 4.4

(Hardy inequality in
L^{2}(\gamma_{\nu}dz)
)
.

For all
|a|\sim 1
, there exists
C>0
such that for all
u\in H^{1}(\gamma_{\nu}dz)
we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{(\nu+|z-a|)^{2}}+\frac{1}{(\nu+|z+a|)^{2}}\right)u^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\nabla u|^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz.

(4.15)

Proof of Lemma
4.4
.

Let
\chi
be a smooth cut-off function with
\chi(z)=1
for
|z|\leq 1
and
\chi(z)=0
for
|z|\geq 2
. For
\eta>0
we decompose

\displaystyle u(z)

\displaystyle=\chi(\frac{z-a}{\eta})u+\chi(\frac{z+a}{\eta})u+(1-\chi(\frac{z-a}{\eta})-\chi(\frac{z-a}{\eta}))u

\displaystyle=u_{1}+u_{2}+u_{3}

Hardy inequality for
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
. We claim that

\int\left(\frac{u_{1}^{2}}{(\nu+|z-a|)^{2}}+\frac{u_{2}^{2}}{(\nu+|z+a|)^{2}}\right)\gamma_{\nu}dz\lesssim\int(|\nabla u_{1}|^{2}+|\nabla u_{2}|^{2})\gamma_{\nu}dz.

(4.16)

We now prove (
4.16
). We estimate for
|z-a|\leq 2\eta
,

\left|\gamma_{\nu}(z)-\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}(z)}\right|=\gamma_{\nu}(z)\frac{U_{2,\nu}(z)}{U_{1,\nu}(z)}\lesssim\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}\eta^{4}

(4.17)

since
|U_{2,\nu}(z)|\lesssim\nu^{2}
and
U_{1+2,\nu}(z)\geq U_{1,\nu}(z)\approx\nu^{-2}\langle\frac{z-a}{\nu}\rangle^{-4}
. As
u_{1}
is supported for
|z-a|\leq 2\eta
, we have by using (
4.17
),

\int\frac{u_{1}^{2}}{(\nu+|z-a|)^{2}}\gamma_{\nu}dz\approx\int\frac{u_{1}^{2}}{(\nu+|z-a|)^{2}}\frac{\nu^{2}dz}{U_{1,\nu}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\int|\nabla u_{1}|^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz\approx\int|\nabla u_{1}|^{2}\frac{\nu^{2}dz}{U_{1,\nu}}.

(4.18)

We now change variables and let
z=a+\nu y
and
u_{1}(z)=v_{1}(y)
so that

\int\frac{u_{1}^{2}}{(\nu+|z-a|)^{2}}\frac{\nu^{2}dz}{U_{1,\nu}}=\nu^{4}\int\frac{v_{1}^{2}}{(1+|y|)^{2}}\frac{dy}{U}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\int|\nabla u_{1}|^{2}\frac{\nu^{2}dz}{U_{1,\nu}}=\nu^{4}\int|\nabla v_{1}|^{2}\frac{dy}{U}.

(4.19)

We recall the Hardy-type inequality
\int v_{1}^{2}\langle y\rangle^{-2}\frac{dy}{U}\lesssim\int|\nabla v_{1}|^{2}\frac{dy}{U}
, see (
C.1
). Combining this inequality with (
4.18
) and (
4.19
) yields

\int\frac{u_{1}^{2}}{(\nu+|z-a|)^{2}}\gamma_{\nu}dz\lesssim\int|\nabla u_{1}|^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz.

The analogue inequality holds for
u_{2}
by symmetry, which proves (
4.16
).

Hardy inequality for
u_{3}
. We claim that

\int\left(\frac{1}{(\nu+|z-a|)^{2}}+\frac{1}{(\nu+|z+a|)^{2}}\right)u^{2}_{3}\gamma_{\nu}dz\lesssim\int|\nabla u_{3}|^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz.

(4.20)

Indeed, we have
\gamma_{\nu}\approx\langle z\rangle^{4}
for
z
in the support of
u_{3}
by (
4.3
). The inequality (
4.20
) then follows from this equivalence and the Hardy-type inequality (
C.1
).

Hardy inequality for
u
. Combining (
4.16
) and (
4.20
) yields

\int\left(\frac{1}{(\nu+|z-a|)^{2}}+\frac{1}{(\nu+|z+a|)^{2}}\right)u^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz\leq C\int(|\nabla u_{1}|^{2}+|\nabla u_{2}|^{2}+|\nabla u_{3}|^{2})\gamma_{\nu}dz.

(4.21)

We decompose

\int|\nabla u|^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz=\int(|\nabla u_{1}|^{2}+|\nabla u_{2}|^{2}+|\nabla u_{3}|^{2})\gamma_{\nu}dz+2\int(\nabla u_{1}+\nabla u_{2}).\nabla u_{3}\gamma_{\nu}dz.

Injecting (
4.21
) in the above identity, we have that for any
\kappa>0
,

\displaystyle\int|\nabla u|^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz

\displaystyle=(1-\kappa)\int|\nabla u|^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz+\kappa\int|\nabla u|^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz

\displaystyle\geq(1-\kappa)\int|\nabla u|^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz+\frac{\kappa}{C}\int\left(\frac{1}{(\nu+|z-a|)^{2}}+\frac{1}{(\nu+|z+a|)^{2}}\right)u^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz

\displaystyle\qquad+2\kappa\int(\nabla u_{1}+\nabla u_{2}).\nabla u_{3}\gamma_{\nu}dz.

(4.22)

We have that
|\nabla u_{i}|\lesssim|\nabla u|+|u|
for
i=1,2,3
. Hence, using the inequality
ab\leq\sqrt{\kappa}\frac{a^{2}}{2}+\frac{b^{2}}{2\sqrt{\kappa}}
we have

2\kappa\int(\nabla u_{1}+\nabla u_{2}).\nabla u_{3}\gamma_{\nu}dz\geq-C\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\int|\nabla u|^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz-C\kappa^{\frac{3}{2}}\int_{\{|z\pm a|\leq 2\eta\}}|u|^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz.

(4.23)

Injecting (
4.23
) in (
4.22
) and taking
\kappa
small enough shows the desired inequality (
4.15
).
∎

The proof of Proposition
4.8
is intimately related to the following spectral and coercivity properties for the operator
\mathscr{M}_{0}
around the stationary state
U
. The following originates from
[
RS14a
]
(see also
[
CGMN22
]
).

Lemma 4.5

(Kernel and coercivity of
\mathscr{M}_{0}
)
.

We have

\mathscr{M}_{0}\Lambda U=-2\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\mathscr{M}_{0}\nabla U=0.

(4.24)

Let
u
be a Schwartz function with
\int u=0
. Then, we have

\langle u,\mathscr{M}_{0}u\rangle\geq 0.

(4.25)

Moreover, there exists a constant
\delta>0
such that for any
R\in[1,\infty]
, if
u
satisfies in addition

\int_{|y|\leq R}u\Lambda Udy=\int_{|y|\leq R}u\partial_{y_{1}}Udy=\int_{|y|\leq R}u\partial_{y_{2}}Udy=0,

(4.26)

where
\{|y|\leq R\}=\mathbb{R}^{2}
if
R=\infty
, then

\langle u,\mathscr{M}_{0}u\rangle\geq\delta\int\frac{|u|^{2}}{U}dy\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}u|^{2}dy\geq\delta\int\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{U}dy.

(4.27)

Proof.

The identity (
4.24
), the inequality (
4.25
) and the first inequality in (
4.27
) for
R=\infty
are proved in
[
RS14a
]
(see Proposition 2.3). We only prove the second inequality in (
4.27
), as the proof of the first inequality for
R\in[1,\infty)
is the same. We recall the subcoercivity estimate from
[
CGMN22
]
(estimate (3.8)),

\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}u|^{2}dy\geq\frac{1}{2}\int|\nabla u|^{2}\frac{dy}{U}-C\int u^{2}.

(4.28)

We now assume by contradiction that (
4.27
) is false. This means that there exist
R_{n}\in[1,\infty]
and
u_{n}
a sequence of Schwartz functions satisfying (
4.26
) with
R=R_{n}
, such that

\int|\nabla u_{n}|^{2}\frac{dy}{U}=1\quad\mbox{for all }n\in\mathbb{N}\qquad\mbox{and}\quad\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}u_{n}|^{2}dy\to 0.

(4.29)

We introduce the space
\dot{H}^{1}_{U^{-1}}
which is the completion of
C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
for the norm
\sqrt{\int|\nabla u|^{2}U^{-1}dy}
. We may assume, up to a subsequence, that
R_{n}\to R_{\infty}\in[1,\infty]
and that there exists
u_{\infty}\in\dot{H}^{1}_{U^{-1}}
such that
u_{n}\rightarrow u_{\infty}
in
L^{2}_{loc}
and
\nabla u_{n}\rightharpoonup\nabla u_{\infty}
in
L^{2}(U^{-1}dy)
. We claim that
u_{\infty}
satisfies the following three properties:

u_{\infty}\neq 0,\qquad\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}u_{\infty}|^{2}dy=0\qquad\mbox{and }\eqref{bd:coercivity-one-bubble-H1-orthogonality}\mbox{ with }R=R_{\infty}.

(4.30)

We now prove (
4.30
). By the Hardy inequality (
C.1
), we have for
\epsilon>0
small that
\int_{|y|\geq\epsilon^{-1}}u^{2}_{n}\lesssim\epsilon^{2}
for all
n
. By (
4.29
) and (
4.29
) we have that
\int u_{n}^{2}\geq c
for all
n
for some
c>0
. Combining, there exists
\epsilon_{0}>0
such that
\int_{|y|\leq\epsilon_{0}^{-1}}u_{n}^{2}\geq c/2
for all
n
. By the compactness of the Sobolev embedding from
H^{1}(|y|\leq\epsilon_{0}^{-1})
into
L^{2}
,
u_{n}
converges strongly in
L^{2}(|y|\leq\epsilon_{0}^{-1})
towards
u_{\infty}
. Thus,
\int u_{\infty}^{2}\geq c/2
which implies the first property in (
4.30
).

Next, by decomposing
-2\pi\nabla\Phi_{u}=-[\chi(y)\log|y|]*\nabla u+[\nabla((1-\chi(y))\log|y|)]*u
, using Cauchy-Schwarz and the Hardy inequality (
C.1
) we bound

|\nabla\Phi_{u}(y)|^{2}\lesssim\langle y\rangle^{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\nabla u|^{2}\frac{1}{U(\tilde{y})}d\tilde{y}.

(4.31)

Using
\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}u=\nabla(u/U)-\nabla\Phi_{u}
, by (
4.31
) and the Hardy inequality (
C.1
), the map
u\mapsto\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}u
is continuous from
\dot{H}^{1}_{U^{-1}}
into
L^{2}(Udy)
. As
u_{n}\rightharpoonup u_{\infty}
in
\dot{H}^{1}_{U^{-1}}
, this implies
\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}u_{n}\rightharpoonup\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}u_{\infty}
in
L^{2}(Udy)
. The second property in (
4.30
) thus follows from (
4.29
) and semi-continuity.

Then, as
\nabla u_{n}\rightharpoonup\nabla u_{\infty}
in
L^{2}_{U^{-1}}
, we have that
u_{n}
converges weakly towards
u_{\infty}
in
L^{2}
by the Hardy inequality (
C.1
). As
\Lambda U,\nabla U\in L^{2}
, we can pass to the limit in the orthogonality (
4.26
) for
u_{n}
, implying the third property in (
4.29
). This concludes the proof of (
4.30
).

To conclude, we have
u_{\infty}\in\dot{H}^{1}(U^{-1}dy)
and
\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}u_{\infty}|^{2}dy=0
by (
4.30
). Hence
\nabla(u_{\infty}/U)=\nabla\Phi_{u_{\infty}}
, which by (
4.31
) implies
|\nabla(u_{\infty}/U)(y)|\lesssim\langle y\rangle^{-1}
. Integrating this inequality and using
|U(y)|\lesssim\langle y\rangle^{-4}
shows
|u_{\infty}(y)|\lesssim\langle y\rangle^{-4}\langle\ln\langle y\rangle\rangle
. This implies that
|\Phi_{u_{\infty}}(y)|\lesssim|\log|y||*|u_{\infty}|\lesssim\langle\ln\langle y\rangle\rangle
. By Lemma 2.1 of
[
RS14a
]
, there holds
u_{\infty}\in\mbox{Span}(\Lambda U,\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{1}}U,\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{2}}U)
. By the third property in (
4.30
), this implies
u_{\infty}=0
. But this contradicts the first property in (
4.30
).

∎

We now prove Proposition
4.1
and Lemma
4.2
.

Proof of Proposition
4.1
.

Step 1
.
Proof of the symmetry identity (
4.6
)
. By (
4.2
) we have

\langle u,v\rangle=\int uv\gamma_{\nu}\,dz-\nu^{2}\int u\Phi_{v}\,dz.

(4.32)

Since
\Delta\Phi_{f}=-f
, the identity (
4.6
) then follows from (
4.32
) and an integration by parts.

Step 2
.
Proof of the continuity estimate (
4.7
)
. We claim that

\int\nu^{2}|\Phi_{u}|^{2}U_{1+2,\nu}dz\lesssim\int u^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz.

(4.33)

The continuity estimate (
4.7
) will then follows from applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and (
4.33
) to (
4.32
). Hence, it remains to show (
4.33
). For that we introduce a small parameter
\eta>0
(we name it
\eta
since our computations will also be used in the proof of (
4.9
)) and decompose:

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle=\mathbbm{1}(|z-a|<\eta)u+\mathbbm{1}(|z+a|<\eta)u+\mathbbm{1}(|z-a|\geq\eta\mbox{ and }|z+a|\geq\eta)u

\displaystyle=u_{1}+u_{2}+u_{3}.

(4.34)

The estimate (
4.33
) will then follow from the estimates (
4.36
), (
4.41
) and (
4.42
) that we prove below for
u_{3}
,
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
respectively.

Estimate for the exterior part
u_{3}
. We notice that for
\nu
small enough
U_{1+2,\nu}\approx\frac{\nu^{2}}{\langle z\rangle^{4}}
if simultaneously
|z-a|\geq\eta
and
|z+a|\geq\eta
, while
U_{1+2,\nu}\lesssim\frac{1}{\nu^{2}}
if either
|z-a|\leq\eta
or
|z+a|\leq\eta
. Hence, the corresponding left-hand and right-hand sides of (
4.33
) satisfy

\int\nu^{2}U_{1+2,\nu}|\Phi_{u_{3}}|^{2}dz\lesssim\int\langle z\rangle^{-4}|\Phi_{u_{3}}|^{2}dz\quad\mbox{and}\quad\int u_{3}^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz\approx\int\langle z\rangle^{4}u_{3}^{2}dz.

Next, we recall the inequality (see the Appendix of
[
CGMN22
]
)

\int\langle y\rangle^{-4}|\Phi_{v}|^{2}dy\lesssim\int\langle y\rangle^{4}v^{2}dy.

(4.35)

Combining the above identity with (
4.35
) yields

\int\nu^{2}U_{1+2,\nu}|\Phi_{u_{3}}|^{2}dz\lesssim\int u_{3}^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz.

(4.36)

Estimate for the inner parts
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
. We first decompose

\int\nu^{2}U_{1+2,\nu}|\Phi_{u_{1}}|^{2}dz\leq\int_{|z+a|>\eta}\nu^{2}U_{1+2,\nu}|\Phi_{u_{1}}|^{2}+\int_{|z+a|<\eta}\nu^{2}U_{1+2,\nu}|\Phi_{u_{1}}|^{2}.

(4.37)

To estimate the first term, we change variables and set
z=a+y\nu
and
v_{1}(y)=u_{1}(z)
. Using
\nu^{2}U_{1+2,\nu}(z)\approx\nu^{2}U_{1,\nu}(z)\approx\langle y\rangle^{-4}
if
|z+a|>\eta
and
\Phi_{v_{1}}(y)=\nu^{-2}\Phi_{u_{1}}(z)
(because
\int u_{1}=0
), we obtain

\int_{|z+a|\geq\eta}\nu^{2}U_{1+2,\nu}|\Phi_{u_{1}}|^{2}dz\approx\nu^{6}\int_{|y+\frac{a}{\nu}|\geq\frac{\eta}{\nu}}|\Phi_{v_{1}}|^{2}\langle y\rangle^{-4}dy\quad\mbox{and}\quad\int u_{1}^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz\approx\nu^{6}\int v_{1}^{2}\langle y\rangle^{4}dy.

Hence, using (
4.35
) we get

\int_{|z+a|\geq\eta}\nu^{2}U_{1+2,\nu}|\Phi_{u_{1}}|^{2}\lesssim\int u_{1}^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz.

(4.38)

To estimate the second term in (
4.37
) we bound
|\Phi_{u_{1}}(z)|\lesssim\int|\log(z-z^{\prime})||u_{1}|(z^{\prime})dz^{\prime}
. As the set
\{|z+a|\leq\eta\}
is for
|\eta|\leq|a|
at distance greater than
|a|
from the support of
u_{1}
this implies
\|\Phi_{u_{1}}\|_{L^{\infty}(|z+a|\leq\eta)}\lesssim\|u_{1}\|_{L^{1}}
. By Hölder we then get

\displaystyle\int_{|z+a|\leq\eta}\nu^{2}U_{1+2,\nu}|\Phi_{u_{1}}|^{2}dz

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Phi_{u_{1}}\|_{L^{\infty}(|z-a|\leq\eta)}^{2}\int\nu^{2}U_{1+2,\nu}dz\lesssim\|u_{1}\|_{L^{1}}^{2}\nu^{2}.

By Cauchy-Schwarz, we have the estimate for
f\in L^{2}(\gamma_{\nu}dz)
,

\|f\|_{L^{1}}^{2}\leq\int\nu^{-2}U_{1+2,\nu}dz\int f^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz\lesssim\nu^{-2}\int f^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz.

(4.39)

Combining the two inequalities above we get

\int_{|z+a|\leq\eta}\nu^{2}U_{1+2,\nu}|\Phi_{u_{1}}|^{2}dz\lesssim\int u_{1}^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz.

(4.40)

Injecting (
4.38
) and (
4.40
) in (
4.37
) shows

\int\nu^{2}U_{1+2,\nu}|\Phi_{u_{1}}|^{2}dz\lesssim\int u_{1}^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz.

(4.41)

By symmetry, we get the analogue estimate for
u_{2}
:

\int\nu^{2}U_{1+2,\nu}|\Phi_{u_{2}}|^{2}dz\lesssim\int u_{2}^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz.

(4.42)

Step 3
.
Proof of the positivity (
4.9
)
. Pick
u\in L^{2}(\gamma_{\nu}dz)
satisfying (
4.8
). We decompose again
u=u_{1}+u_{2}+u_{3}
as in (
4.34
). Injecting this decomposition in (
4.32
), and integrating by parts using
-\Delta\Phi_{f}=f
we get

\displaystyle\langle u,u\rangle_{\flat}

\displaystyle=\int u_{1}^{2}\gamma_{\nu}\,dz-\nu^{2}\int u_{1}\Phi_{u_{1}}dz

\displaystyle+\int u_{2}^{2}\gamma_{\nu}\,dz-\nu^{2}\int u_{2}\Phi_{u_{2}}dz

\displaystyle+\int u^{2}_{3}\gamma_{\nu}dz-2\nu^{2}\int u_{1}\Phi_{u_{2}}dz-2\nu^{2}\int\Phi_{u_{3}}(u_{1}+u_{2})dz-\nu^{2}\int u_{3}\Phi_{u_{3}}dz

\displaystyle=I+II+\int u^{2}_{3}\gamma_{\nu}dz+IV.

The desired estimate (
4.9
) is a consequence of the below estimates (
4.43
) and (
4.47
).

Lower bounds for the first terms
I
and
II
.
We claim that

I\geq\delta\int u_{1}^{2}\gamma_{\nu}\,dz\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad II\geq\delta\int u_{2}^{2}\gamma_{\nu}\,dz

(4.43)

for some universal constant
\delta>0
. To show the first inequality in (
4.43
) we decompose

\displaystyle I=\int u_{1}^{2}\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}\,dz-\nu^{2}\int u_{1}\Phi_{u_{1}}\,dz+\int u_{1}^{2}(\gamma_{\nu}-\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}})\,dz.

(4.44)

We change variables and set
z=a+y\nu
and
v_{1}(y)=u_{1}(z)
. We have
\Phi_{v_{1}}(y)=\nu^{-2}\Phi_{u_{1}}(z)
. The first term in (
4.44
) is

\int u_{1}^{2}\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}\,dz-\nu^{2}\int u_{1}\Phi_{u_{1}}\,dz=\nu^{6}\left(\int v_{1}^{2}\frac{1}{U}\,dy-\int v_{1}\Phi_{v_{1}}\,dy\right)

(4.45)

and the orthogonality conditions (
4.8
) give

\int v_{1}\,dy=\int\Lambda Uv_{1}\,dy=\int\partial_{y_{1}}Uv_{1}\,dy=\int\partial_{y_{2}}Uv_{1}\,dy=0.

Applying the coercivity result of
[
RS14a
]
, we obtain that for some
\delta>0
:

\int v_{1}^{2}\frac{1}{U}\,dy-\int v_{1}\Phi_{v_{1}}\,dy\geq\delta\int v_{1}^{2}\frac{1}{U}\,dy.

Injecting this inequality in (
4.45
), using that
\int v_{1}^{2}\frac{1}{U}\,dy=\nu^{-4}\int u_{1}^{2}\frac{1}{U_{1,\nu}}\,dz
, shows:

\int u_{1}^{2}\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}\,dz-\nu^{2}\int u_{1}\Phi_{u_{1}}\,dz\geq\delta\int u_{1}^{2}\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}\,dz.

(4.46)

Injecting (
4.46
) and (
4.17
) in (
4.44
) shows the first inequality in (
4.43
) for
\eta
small enough. The proof of the second inequality is exactly the same by symmetry.

Upper bound for the fourth term
IV
.
We claim that for
\eta
small enough and then
\nu
small enough,

|IV|\lesssim\eta\int u^{2}\gamma_{\nu}\,dz

(4.47)

We now prove this bound. For the first term in
IV
, by (
4.8
) we have
\int u_{1}=0
, hence:

\nu^{2}\int u_{1}\Phi_{u_{2}}dz=\nu^{2}\int u_{1}(\Phi_{u_{2}}(z)-\Phi_{u_{2}}(a))dz.

Using
|\nabla\Phi_{2}(z)|\lesssim\int|z-z^{\prime}|^{-1}|u_{2}(z^{\prime})|dz^{\prime}
, and that
u_{2}
is supported for
|z+a|<\eta
we obtain for
\eta
small enough that
|\nabla\Phi_{u_{2}}(z)|\lesssim\|u_{2}\|_{L^{1}}
for all
|z-a|\leq\eta
. Hence by the mean value Theorem

\nu^{2}\left|\int u_{1}\Phi_{u_{2}}dz\right|\lesssim\nu^{2}\|u_{1}\|_{L^{1}}\eta\|u_{2}\|_{L^{1}}.

Using the estimate (
4.39
) yields

\nu^{2}\left|\int u_{1}\Phi_{u_{2}}dz\right|\lesssim\eta\int u^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz.

(4.48)

For the second term in
IV
, we estimate that for
z\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,

\displaystyle|\Phi_{u_{3}}(z)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int|\ln|z-z^{\prime}|||u_{3}(z^{\prime})|\lesssim\left(\int|\ln|z-z^{\prime}||^{2}\langle z^{\prime}\rangle^{-4}dz^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int u_{3}^{2}\langle z^{\prime}\rangle^{4}dz^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\eta}\left(\int u^{2}\gamma_{\nu}(z^{\prime})dz^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(4.49)

where we used Cauchy-Schwarz and that
\gamma_{\nu}(z^{\prime})\geq c_{\eta}\langle z^{\prime}\rangle^{4}
for some
c_{\eta}>0
for
z^{\prime}
in the support of
u_{3}
from (
4.3
). Using (
4.49
) and (
4.39
) we obtain

\nu^{2}\left|\int\Phi_{u_{3}}(u_{1}+u_{2})dz\right|\leq\nu^{2}C_{\eta}\left(\int u^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u_{1}+u_{2}\|_{L^{1}}\leq C_{\eta}\nu\int u^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz

(4.50)

For the third term in
IV
, applying Cauchy-Schwarz, (
4.35
) and
\gamma_{\nu}^{-1}\approx\langle z\rangle^{-4}
on the support of
u_{3}
yields

|\nu^{2}\int\Phi_{u_{3}}u_{3}|\lesssim\nu^{2}\left(\int\Phi_{u_{3}}^{2}\langle z\rangle^{-4}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int u_{3}^{2}\langle z\rangle^{4}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\nu^{2}\int u^{2}_{3}\gamma_{\nu}dz.

(4.51)

Combining (
4.48
), (
4.50
) and (
4.51
) shows (
4.47
) as desired.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.2
.

Step 1
.
Proof of the identities (
4.10
) and (
4.12
).
We recall the identity
\nabla U=U\nabla\Phi_{U}
. Hence
\nabla\Phi_{U_{i,\nu}}=\nabla\ln U_{i,\nu}
for
i=1,2
. We thus rewrite

\displaystyle\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}

\displaystyle=\nabla\Phi_{U_{1,\nu}}+\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}=\nabla\ln U_{1,\nu}+\nabla\ln U_{2,\nu}=\nabla\ln U_{12,\nu}

\displaystyle\quad=\nabla\ln U_{1+2,\nu}+\nabla\ln\frac{U_{12,\nu}}{U_{1+2,\nu}}.

Using the above identity, we rewrite

\displaystyle\Delta u+\nabla.(u\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}})

\displaystyle=\nabla.\left(\nabla u-u\nabla\ln U_{1+2,\nu}+u\nabla\ln\frac{U_{1+2,\nu}}{U_{12,\nu}}\right)

\displaystyle=\nabla.\left(U_{1+2,\nu}\nabla\left(\frac{u}{U_{1+2,\nu}}\right)+u\nabla\ln\frac{U_{1+2,\nu}}{U_{12,\nu}}\right).

The first identity (
4.10
) of the lemma follows from injecting the above identity and
\Lambda u=\nabla.(u\nabla(z^{2}/2))
in (
2.8
). The second identity (
4.12
) then follows from (
4.10
) by integrating by parts.

Step 2
.
Proof of the coercivity (
4.14
).
By (
4.12
) we have:

-\langle\mathscr{L}^{z}u,u\rangle_{\flat}=\int\gamma_{\nu}^{-1}|\nabla\mathscr{M}^{z}u|^{2}dz-\int\nabla.(u\nabla V)\mathscr{M}^{z}udz.

(4.52)

The coercivity estimate (
4.14
) then follows from the two estimates (
4.53
) and (
4.59
) for the first and second terms in (
4.52
) respectively, and by taking
\eta^{\prime}
small enough.

Estimate for the first term in (
4.52
).
We claim that

\int\gamma_{\nu}^{-1}|\nabla\mathscr{M}^{z}u|^{2}dz\gtrsim\int|\nabla u|^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz.

(4.53)

We first split

\int\gamma_{\nu}^{-1}|\nabla\mathscr{M}^{z}u|^{2}dz\geq\int_{|z-a|\leq\eta^{\prime}}\nu^{-2}U_{1,\nu}|\nabla\mathscr{M}^{z}u|^{2}dz+\int_{|z+a|\leq\eta^{\prime}}\nu^{-2}U_{2,\nu}|\nabla\mathscr{M}^{z}u|^{2}dz.

(4.54)

We study the first term in (
4.54
). We decompose

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle=\mathbbm{1}(|z-a|\leq\eta^{\prime})u+\mathbbm{1}(|z+a|\leq\eta^{\prime})u

\displaystyle=u_{1}+u_{2}.

Using
\Phi_{u}=\Phi_{u_{1}}+\Phi_{u_{2}}
and
U_{1+2,\nu}^{-1}=U_{1,\nu}^{-1}-U_{2,\nu}U_{1+2,\nu}^{-1}U_{1,\nu}^{-1}
, we decompose for
|z-a|\leq\eta^{\prime}
:

\displaystyle\mathscr{M}^{z}u=\mathscr{M}^{a}_{0}u_{1}-\frac{\nu^{2}U_{2,\nu}}{U_{1+2,\nu}U_{1,\nu}}u-\nu^{2}\Phi_{u_{2}},

where

\mathscr{M}^{a}_{0}u_{1}=\nu^{2}\frac{u_{1}}{U_{1,\nu}}-\Phi_{u_{1}}.

Therefore, using
(a+b+c)^{2}\geq a^{2}-2b^{2}-2c^{2}
we have

\displaystyle\int_{|z-a|\leq\eta^{\prime}}\gamma_{\nu}^{-1}|\nabla\mathscr{M}^{z}u|^{2}dz

\displaystyle\geq\int\nu^{-2}U_{1,\nu}\left(|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}^{a}u_{1}|^{2}-2\left|\nabla\Phi_{u_{2}}\right|^{2}-2\left|\nabla\left(\frac{U_{2,\nu}}{U_{1+2,\nu}U_{1,\nu}}u\right)\right|^{2}\right)dz.

(4.55)

We study the first term in (
4.55
). We change variables
z=a+\nu y
and
v_{1}(y)=u_{1}(z)
. We have
\mathscr{M}^{a}_{0}u_{1}(z)=\nu^{4}\mathscr{M}_{0}v_{1}(y)
, so that

\int\nu^{-2}U_{1,\nu}|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}^{a}u_{1}|^{2}dz=\nu^{4}\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}v_{1}|^{2}dy.

Using the orthogonality conditions (
4.13
), applying Lemma
4.5
shows

\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}v_{1}|^{2}dy\gtrsim\int|\nabla v_{1}|^{2}\frac{dy}{U(y)}.

Combining the two inequalities above, and unwinding the change of variables, we get

\int\nu^{-2}U_{1,\nu}|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}^{z}u_{1}|^{2}dz\gtrsim\int|\nabla u_{1}|^{2}\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}dz.

(4.56)

We turn to the second term in (
4.55
). Using that
|\nabla\Phi_{u_{2}}(z)|\lesssim(|\cdot|^{-1}*|u_{2}|)(z)
and that
u_{2}
is supported in
\{|z+a|\leq\eta^{\prime}\}
we have that
|\nabla\Phi_{u_{2}}(z)|\lesssim\|u_{2}\|_{L^{1}}
for
|z-a|\leq\eta^{\prime}
. As
\int U_{1,\nu}\lesssim 1
we bound

\int_{|z-a|\leq\eta^{\prime}}\nu^{2}U_{1,\nu}\left|\nabla\Phi_{u_{2}}\right|^{2}dz\lesssim\nu^{2}\|u_{2}\|_{L^{1}}^{2}\lesssim\int u^{2}_{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz\lesssim\eta^{{}^{\prime}2}\int|\nabla u|^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz

(4.57)

where we used successively (
4.39
), and (
4.15
) with
\min(\nu+|z+a|)\leq\eta^{\prime}
on the support of
u_{2}
.

We now estimate the third term in (
4.55
). For
|z-a|\leq\eta^{\prime}
one has

\nu^{-4}(U_{1,\nu}(z))^{2}\left(\frac{\nu^{2}U_{2,\nu}}{U_{1+2,\nu}U_{1,\nu}}\right)^{2}=\frac{(U_{2,\nu}(z))^{2}}{(U_{1+2,\nu}(z))^{2}}\lesssim\eta^{{}^{\prime}8},

where we used that
U_{2,\nu}(z)\approx\nu^{2}
and
U_{1+2,\nu}(z)\approx\nu^{2}(\nu+|z-a|)^{-4}
. Similarly,

\nu^{-4}(U_{1,\nu}(z))^{2}\left|\nabla\left(\frac{\nu^{2}U_{2,\nu}}{U_{1+2,\nu}U_{1,\nu}}\right)\right|^{2}\lesssim\eta^{{}^{\prime}6}.

Using the two inequalities above, we have

\nu^{-2}U_{1,\nu}\left|\nabla\left(u\frac{\nu^{2}U_{2,\nu}}{U_{1+2,\nu}U_{1,\nu}}\right)\right|^{2}\lesssim\eta^{{}^{\prime}6}\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}(u^{2}+|\nabla u|^{2}).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\int_{|z-a|\leq\eta^{\prime}}\nu^{-2}U_{1,\nu}\left|\nabla\left(u\frac{\nu^{2}U_{2,\nu}}{U_{1+2,\nu}U_{1,\nu}}\right)\right|^{2}dz

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta^{{}^{\prime}6}\int_{|z-a|\leq\eta^{\prime}}(u^{2}+|\nabla u|^{2})\frac{\nu^{2}dz}{U_{1,\nu}}\lesssim\eta^{{}^{\prime}6}\int|\nabla u|^{2}\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}dz

(4.58)

where we used the Hardy inequality (
4.15
) and (
4.17
).

Injecting (
4.56
), (
4.57
) and (
4.58
) in (
4.55
), we get

\int_{|z-a|\leq\eta^{\prime}}\nu^{-2}U_{1,\nu}|\nabla\mathscr{M}^{z}u|^{2}dz\geq C^{-1}\int|\nabla u_{1}|^{2}\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}dz-C\eta^{{}^{\prime}2}\int|\nabla u|^{2}\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}dz.

Since the second term in (
4.54
) plays a symmetric role, we have similarly

\int_{|z+a|\leq\eta^{\prime}}\nu^{-2}U_{2,\nu}|\nabla\mathscr{M}^{z}u|^{2}dz\geq C^{-1}\int|\nabla u_{2}|^{2}\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}dz-C\eta^{{}^{\prime}2}\int|\nabla u|^{2}\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}dz.

Combining the two estimates above shows the desired identity (
4.53
).

Estimate for the second term in (
4.52
).
We claim that

\left|\int\nabla.(u\nabla V)\mathscr{M}^{z}udz\right|\lesssim\eta^{\prime}\int|\nabla u|^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz.

(4.59)

Using the continuity estimate (
4.7
)

\left|\int\nabla.(u\nabla V)\mathscr{M}^{z}udz\right|\lesssim\left(\int|\nabla.(u\nabla V)|^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int u^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

From (
4.11
), we have that
V(z)=\ln((\nu^{2}+|z-a|^{2})^{2}+(\nu^{2}+|z+a|^{2})^{2})-\ln(8\nu^{2})-\frac{\beta}{2}|z|^{2}
, and
(\nu^{2}+|z-a|^{2})^{2}+(\nu^{2}+|z+a|^{2})^{2}\geq c(a)
for all
z\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
for some
c(a)>0
. Hence,
\nabla V
and
\Delta V
are bounded on compact sets. This implies
|\nabla.(u\nabla V)|\lesssim|u|+|\nabla u|
so that

\int|\nabla.(u\nabla V)|^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz\lesssim\int(u^{2}+|\nabla u|^{2})\gamma_{\nu}.

Using again the support of
u
, and then the Hardy inequality (
4.15
), we have

\int u^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz\lesssim\eta^{{}^{\prime}2}\int\frac{u^{2}}{\min\left((\nu+|z-a|)^{2},(\nu+|z+a|)^{2}\right)}\gamma_{\nu}dz\lesssim\eta^{{}^{\prime}2}\int|\nabla u|^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz.

Combining the three above inequalities yields the desired estimate (
4.59
). This ends the proof of Lemma
4.2
.
∎

4.1.2. The scalar product for the exterior zone

We now present another scalar product, for which the linearized operator is almost symmetric for functions which are localized away from
\pm a
. Namely, consider the weighted space
L^{2}(\omega_{\nu}dz)
with the weight function

\omega_{\nu}(z)=\frac{\nu^{4}}{U_{12,\nu}(z)}e^{-\beta|z|^{2}/2}

(4.60)

and the scalar product
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\omega_{\nu}}
. Note that we have normalized the weight to have

\omega_{\nu}(z)\approx(\nu+|z-a|)^{4}(\nu+|z+a|)^{4}e^{-\beta|z|^{2}/2}.

(4.61)

Functions
u\in L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}
may diverge at infinity in which case their Poisson field
\Phi_{u}
is not well defined. Since in our proof of Theorem
1.1
the contribution from the zone
|z|\gtrsim 10
to the dynamics will be showed to be negligible (up to modulation terms that we will single out), we circumvent this problem by localizing the Poisson field. Let
\chi^{*}(z)
be the cut-off defined by (
2.2
) and define

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u

\displaystyle=\Delta u-\nabla.\Big(u\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}\Big)-\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}+U_{1+2,\nu}u-\beta\Lambda u

(4.62)

\displaystyle=\nabla\cdot\Big(\nabla u-u\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}-U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}+U_{1+2,\nu}u-\beta zu\Big),

so that by (
4.62
) we have

\mathscr{L}^{z}u=\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u-\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla\Phi_{(1-\chi^{*})u}.

Lemma 4.6

(Coercivity and almost symmetry in the exterior zone)
.

We have

\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u=\frac{1}{\omega_{\nu}}\nabla.(\omega_{\nu}\nabla u)+2(U_{1+2,\nu}-\beta)u-\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u},

(4.63)

and, for Schwartz functions
u
and
v
,

\langle\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u,v\rangle_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}=-\int\nabla u.\nabla v\;\omega_{\nu}dz+\int 2(U_{1+2,\nu}-\beta)uv\omega_{\nu}dz-\int\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}v\omega_{\nu}dz.

(4.64)

Moreover, there exists
\delta>0
such that for
|a|\leq 5
and
\eta>0
close to
0
, we have for
\nu>0
small enough that for all
u\in H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}
that is supported in
\{|z-a|\geq\eta\mbox{ and }|z+a|\geq\eta\}
that

-\langle\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u,u\rangle_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\geq\delta\int(\langle z\rangle^{2}u^{2}+|\nabla u|^{2})\omega_{\nu}dz

(4.65)

For
z
away from
\pm a
, we have
\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}=O(\nu^{2})
. Hence, by (
4.63
),
\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u
is well approximated by the operator
\omega_{\nu}^{-1}\nabla.(\omega_{\nu}\nabla u)+2(U_{1+2,\nu}-\beta)u
for functions
u
that are localized away from
\pm a
. This operator is self-adjoint in
L^{2}(\omega_{\nu}dz)
and its associated quadratic form is the sum of the first two terms in the right-hand side of (
4.64
). This is what motivated the introduction of the scalar product with the weight function (
4.60
).

We now proceed with the proof of Lemma
4.6
. We first claim the following inequality which shall be used numerous times in the analysis.

Lemma 4.7

(Hardy-Poincaré inequality in
H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}
)
.

There exists
C>0
such that for all
u\in H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}
, we have for
\nu
small enough and
|a|\leq 5
,

\int\left(\frac{1}{(\nu+|z-a|)^{2}}+\frac{1}{(\nu+|z+a|)^{2}}+|z|^{2}\right)u^{2}\omega_{\nu}dz\leq C\int(u^{2}+|\nabla u|^{2})\omega_{\nu}dz.

(4.66)

Proof.

We decompose
u=u_{1}+u_{2}
where
u_{1}=\chi^{*}u
and
u_{2}=(1-\chi^{*})u
. For
z
in the support of
\chi^{*}
, we have
|z|\leq 20
. By (
4.3
) and (
4.61
) we have

\omega_{\nu}(z)\approx\gamma_{\nu}(z)\qquad\mbox{for }|z|\leq 20.

(4.67)

By the Hardy inequality (
4.15
) and (
4.67
), we have

\int\left(\frac{1}{(\nu+|z-a|)^{2}}+\frac{1}{(\nu+|z+a|)^{2}}\right)u^{2}_{1}\omega_{\nu}dz\leq C\int|\nabla u_{1}|^{2}\omega_{\nu}dz.

(4.68)

For
|z|\geq 10
we have
\omega_{\nu}(z)\approx\langle z\rangle^{4}e^{-|z|^{2}/4}
by (
4.61
). Hence, by the Poincaré inequality (
C.3
),

\int\langle z\rangle^{2}u^{2}_{2}\omega_{\nu}dz\leq C\int(u_{2}^{2}+|\nabla u_{2}|^{2})\omega_{\nu}dz.

(4.69)

Combining (
4.68
) and (
4.69
) yields the desired inequality (
4.66
), since

((\nu+|z-a|)^{-2}+(\nu+|z+a|)^{-2}+|z|^{2})u^{2}\approx((\nu+|z-a|)^{-2}+(\nu+|z+a|)^{-2})u_{1}^{2}+|z|^{2}u_{2}^{2}

and
|\nabla u_{i}|\lesssim|u|+|\nabla u|
for
i=1,2
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.6
.

Step 1
.
Proof of the identities (
4.63
) and (
4.64
).
Using
-\Delta\Phi_{f}=f
we expand (
4.62
) as

\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u=\Delta u-\nabla u.\left(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+\beta z\right)+2(U_{1+2,\nu}-\beta)u-\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}.

Hence, the first identity (
4.63
) of the Lemma holds true provided that the weight
\omega_{\nu}
satisfies

\nabla\ln\omega_{\nu}=-\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}-\beta z.

Since
\nabla\Phi_{U}=\nabla\ln U
, this equation can be solved explicitly, yielding to the formula (
4.60
). Hence (
4.63
) holds true. The second identity (
4.64
) is then obtained from (
4.63
) by integration by parts.

Step 2
.
Proof of the coercivity (
4.65
).
By (
4.64
) we have

-\langle\tilde{\mathscr{L}}u,u\rangle_{\omega_{\nu}}=\int(|\nabla u|^{2}+(1-2U_{1+2,\nu})u^{2})\omega_{\nu}dz+\int\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}u\omega_{\nu}dz.

(4.70)

We study the first term in (
4.70
). Using
U_{i,\nu}(z)=\nu^{2}8(1+|z\pm a|^{2})^{-2}
we have that
|U_{1+2,\nu}|\lesssim\nu^{2}
on the support of
u
. Using this and the Poincaré inequality (
4.66
) we find

\int(|\nabla u|^{2}+(1-2U_{1+2,\nu})u^{2})\omega_{\nu}dz\geq\delta\int(\langle z\rangle^{2}u^{2}+|\nabla u|^{2})\omega_{\nu}dz.

(4.71)

We now turn to the second term in (
4.70
). We make a general estimate for some other Schwartz function
v
that is supported on
\cap_{\pm}\{|z\pm a|\geq\eta\}
. Integrating by parts, we have

\int\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}v\omega_{\nu}dz=-\int\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}\left[(\Delta U_{1+2,\nu}+\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla\ln\omega_{\nu})v+\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla v\right]\omega_{\nu}dz.

(4.72)

Using again that
U_{i,\nu}(z)=\nu^{2}8(1+|z\pm a|^{2})^{-2}
and that
u
and
v
are supported on
\cap_{\pm}\{|z\pm a|\geq\eta\}
we have
\left|(\Delta U_{1+2,\nu}+\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}.\ln\omega_{\nu})v+\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla v\right|\lesssim\nu^{2}(v+|\nabla v|)
for
\nu
small enough. By Cauchy-Schwarz and this inequality, (
4.72
) gives

\left|\int\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}v\omega_{\nu}dz\right|\lesssim\nu^{2}\left(\int_{\min_{\pm}(|z\pm a|\geq\eta}|\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}|^{2}\omega_{\nu}dz\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int(v^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})\omega_{\nu}dz\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(4.73)

where we used that the integrand is supported on
\cap_{\pm}\{|z\pm a|\geq\eta\}
. Using that on the support of
\chi^{*}
we have
\omega_{\nu}\lesssim\langle z\rangle^{-4}
and
\omega_{\nu}\approx_{\eta}1
for
\nu
small enough, we have by (
4.35
) that

\int_{\min_{\pm}(|z\pm a|\geq\eta}|\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}|^{2}\omega_{\nu}dz\lesssim\int\langle z\rangle^{-4}|\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}|^{2}dz\lesssim\int\langle z\rangle^{4}\chi^{*2}u^{2}\lesssim\int u^{2}\omega_{\nu}dz.

(4.74)

Injecting the above inequality in (
4.73
) shows

\left|\int\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}v\omega_{\nu}dz\right|\lesssim\nu^{2}\left(\int u^{2}\omega_{\nu}dz\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int(v^{2}+|\nabla v|^{2})\omega_{\nu}dz\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(4.75)

Injecting (
4.71
) and (
4.75
) with
u=v
in (
4.70
) shows the desired identity (
4.65
) for
\nu
small enough.
∎

4.1.3. Matched scalar product

Recall that we fix
\beta=\frac{1}{2}
for sake of simplicity. We want to match the scalar product for the exterior zone

\langle u,v\rangle_{\omega_{\nu}}=\int uv\omega_{\nu}dz

which is convenient for functions located away from
a
and
-a
, with the scalar product for the interior zone

\langle u,v\rangle_{\flat}=\int uv\gamma_{\nu}\,dz-\nu^{2}\int\Phi_{u}vdz,

(4.76)

which is suitable for functions located near
\pm a
.

Firstly, we compare the weights. Using
U(x)=8(1+|x|^{2})^{-2}
, a Taylor expansion yields

\omega_{\nu}(z)=\gamma_{\nu}(z)\left(2|a|^{4}e^{-|a|^{2}/4}+o(1)\right)

as
\nu\to 0
and
z\to a
or
z\to-a
(see (
4.86
) for the details). Thus, the two weights match up to a factor
c_{*}=2|a|^{4}e^{-|a|^{2}/4}=32e^{-1}+O(|a-a_{\infty}|)
.

Secondly, we consider the second term in (
4.76
). This term does not need to be matched to anything and can be kept as is. The reason is that the contribution from the exterior zone in this term is negligible. Indeed, firstly, the weight function
\gamma_{\nu}
in the first term in (
4.76
) is of order one away from
a
and
-a
, and the weight in the second term in (
4.76
) is of size
\nu^{2}
. Secondly, in the second term in (
4.76
), the interaction between the interior zone and the exterior has a cancellation if
v
satisfies the zero mass condition in the interior zone
\int_{|z\pm a|\leq\eta}v=0
. Indeed, in this case
\int_{|z\pm a|\leq\eta}\nu^{2}\Phi_{u}vdz=\int_{|z\pm a|\leq\eta}\nu^{2}(\Phi_{u}-\Phi_{u}(\pm a))vdz
, so that the contribution from the Poisson field of
u
is lowered.

As the contribution from the exterior zone in the second term in (
4.76
) is negligible, we further localise it. The reason is that the Poisson field
\Phi_{u}
is not well-defined in the space
L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}
due to possible growth at infinity. For that, we will use the cut-off function
\chi^{*}
.

We thus introduce the ”matched scalar product”

\langle u,v\rangle_{*}=\int uv\omega_{\nu}dz-c_{*}\nu^{2}\int\chi^{*}u\,\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}dz,\qquad c_{*}=2|a|^{4}e^{-|a|^{2}/4}.

(4.77)

We first show that
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{*}
is well defined on
L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}
, and it defines a scalar product that is equivalent to
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\omega_{\nu}}
on a suitable subspace. We shall again abuse language and refer to
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{*}
as a scalar product.

Proposition 4.8

(Matched scalar product)
.

There exists
C>0
such that the following hold for
a
close to
a_{\infty}
, for all
u,v\in L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}
. The functional
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{*}
is symmetric

\langle u,v\rangle_{*}=\langle v,u\rangle_{*}

(4.78)

and for
\nu
small enough, it is continuous

|\langle u,v\rangle_{*}|^{2}\leq C\int u^{2}\omega_{\nu}dz\int v^{2}\omega_{\nu}dz.

(4.79)

Moreover, for any
\eta>0
small enough, then for all
\nu
small enough, it is positive for functions satisfying the orthogonality conditions

\langle u,\mathbbm{1}(|z\pm a|<\eta)\rangle=\langle u,\Lambda U_{i,\nu}\mathbbm{1}(|z\pm a|<\eta)\rangle=\langle u,\partial_{j}U_{i,\nu}\mathbbm{1}(|z\pm a|<\eta)\rangle=0

(4.80)

for all
i=1,2
,
j=1,2
and where
\pm
designates
-
or
+
if
i=1
or
i=2
, in the sense that

\int u^{2}\omega_{\nu}dz\leq C\langle u,u\rangle_{*}.

(4.81)

Proof.

Step 1
.
Proof of the symmetry (
4.78
)
. We rewrite (
4.77
) as

\langle u,v\rangle_{*}=\langle u,v\rangle_{\omega_{\nu}}+c_{*}\langle\chi^{*}u,\chi^{*}v\rangle_{\flat}-c_{*}\langle\chi^{*}u,\chi^{*}v\rangle_{\gamma_{\nu}},

(4.82)

where
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\flat}
is introduced in (
4.1
). We then obtain (
4.78
) by using (
4.6
).

Step 2
.
Proof of the continuity (
4.79
)
. From the identity (
4.82
), we have by using (
4.7
) and Cauchy-Schwarz,

|\langle u,v\rangle_{*}|^{2}\lesssim\int(\chi^{*})^{2}u^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz\int(\chi^{*})^{2}v^{2}\gamma_{\nu}dz+\int\omega_{\nu}u^{2}dz\int\omega_{\nu}v^{2}dz.

(4.83)

Combining (
4.83
) and the equivalence
\omega_{\nu}(z)\approx\gamma_{\nu}(z)
for
|z|\leq 20
, one gets (
4.79
).

Step 3
.
Proof of the positivity (
4.81
)
. We pick
u\in L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}
satisfying (
4.80
) and decompose

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle=\mathbbm{1}(|z-a|<\eta)u+\mathbbm{1}(|z+a|<\eta)u+\mathbbm{1}(|z-a|\geq\eta\mbox{ and }|z+a|\geq\eta)u

\displaystyle=u_{1}+u_{2}+u_{3},

so that

\displaystyle\langle u,u\rangle_{*}

\displaystyle=\int\omega_{\nu}u_{1}^{2}\,dz-c_{*}\nu^{2}\int u_{1}\Phi_{u_{1}}dz

\displaystyle+\int\omega_{\nu}u_{2}^{2}\,dz-c_{*}\nu^{2}\int u_{2}\Phi_{u_{2}}dz\ +\int\omega_{\nu}u^{2}_{3}dz

\displaystyle-c_{*}\left(2\nu^{2}\int u_{1}\Phi_{u_{2}}dz-2\nu^{2}\int\Phi_{\chi^{*}u_{3}}(u_{1}+u_{2})dz-\nu^{2}\int\chi^{*}u_{3}\Phi_{\chi_{10}u_{3}}dz\right)

\displaystyle=I+II+\int\omega_{\nu}u^{2}_{3}dz+IV.

The desired inequality (
4.81
) will then follow from the estimates (
4.88
), (
4.89
) and (
4.90
) that we prove below for
I
,
II
and
IV
respectively, by taking
a
close enough to
(2,0)
,
\eta
to
0
and
\nu
small enough.

Lower bounds for
I
and
II
. We compute that for
z
close to
a
:

U_{2,\nu}(z)=\frac{8\nu^{2}}{(\nu^{2}+|z+a|^{2})^{2}}=\frac{\nu^{2}}{2|a|^{4}}+O(\nu^{4}+\nu^{2}|z-a|),

(4.84)

and

\frac{U_{1+2,\nu}(z)}{U_{1,\nu}(z)}=1+\frac{U_{2,\nu}(z)}{U_{1,\nu}(z)}=1+\frac{(\nu^{2}+|z-a|^{2})^{2}}{(\nu^{2}+|z+a|^{2})^{2}}=1+O((\nu+|z-a|)^{4}).

(4.85)

Combining, we get that for
a
close to
(2,0)
:

\displaystyle\frac{\omega_{\nu}(z)}{\gamma_{\nu}(z)}

\displaystyle=\frac{U_{1+2,\nu}(z)}{U_{1,\nu}(z)}\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{2,\nu}(z)}e^{-|z|^{2}/4}=c_{*}+O(\nu^{2}+|z-a|).

(4.86)

Using (
4.84
) and (
4.85
) this estimate propagates to higher order derivatives:

\left|\nabla\left(\frac{\omega_{\nu}}{\gamma_{\nu}}\right)\right|\lesssim 1.

(4.87)

As
u_{1}
is supported inside
\{|z-a|\leq\eta\}
we infer from (
4.86
) that

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle=\int\left(c_{*}\gamma_{\nu}+O\left(\omega_{\nu}(\nu^{2}+|z-a|)\right)\right)u_{1}^{2}\,dz-c_{*}\nu^{2}\int u_{1}\Phi_{u_{1}}dz

\displaystyle=c_{*}\langle u_{1},u_{1}\rangle_{\flat}+O\left((\nu^{2}+\eta)\|u\|_{{\omega_{\nu}}}^{2}\right)

As
u
satisfies (
4.80
), we infer that
u_{1}
satisfies the orthogonality (
4.8
). Applying (
4.9
) from Proposition
4.1
and (
4.86
), we get

I\geq\delta\int\omega_{\nu}u_{1}^{2}dz+O\left((\nu^{2}+\eta)\|u\|_{{\omega_{\nu}}}^{2}\right)

(4.88)

for some
\delta>0
. As
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
play a symmetric role, we have similarly

II\geq\delta\int\omega_{\nu}u_{2}^{2}dz+O\left((\nu^{2}+\eta)\|u\|_{{\omega_{\nu}}}^{2}\right).

(4.89)

Upper bound for
IV
. We recall that this term has already been estimated in (
4.47
) (up to replacing
u
by
\chi^{*}u
in (
4.47
)) so that

|IV|\lesssim\eta\int(\chi^{*})^{2}u^{2}\gamma_{\nu}\,dz\lesssim\eta\int\omega_{\nu}u^{2}dz,

(4.90)

where we used (
4.82
) in the last inequality.
∎

4.2. Coercivity of the linearized operator for the matched scalar product

In this subsection, we first show that the bilinear form associated to the linearized operator with localized Poisson field (
4.62
) for the matched scalar product (
4.77
) is almost symmetric. Then we show it is coercive under suitable orthogonality conditions.

Proposition 4.9
.

There exists
C>0
such that for all
a
close to
a_{\infty}
and
\nu>0
small enough the following holds true. For
u
and
v
Schwartz functions we have

\langle\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u,v\rangle_{*}=\mathcal{Q}(u,v)+\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}(u,v),

(4.91)

where
\mathcal{Q}
is the following symmetric bilinear form:

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}(u,v)

\displaystyle=-\int\nabla u.\nabla v\omega_{\nu}dz+2\int uv(U_{1+2,\nu}-\beta)\omega_{\nu}dz-\int(v\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}+u\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}).\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}\omega_{\nu}dz

\displaystyle+c_{*}\nu^{2}\left(\int\chi^{*}uvdz-\int\chi^{*}\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}U_{1+2,\nu}dz\right)

(4.92)

and where
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}
is a lower order bilinear form (given by (
4.103
)) which satisfies:

\displaystyle|\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}(u,v)|

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu+|a-a_{\infty}|)\sqrt{|\ln\nu|}\min\Big(\nu\sqrt{|\ln\nu|}\|u\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\|v\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\ ,\ \|u\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\|v\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\ ,\ \|u\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\|v\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\Big)

(4.93)

Remark 4.10
.

Thanks to (
4.91
), we have the almost symmetry identity

\langle\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u,v\rangle_{*}=\langle u,\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}v\rangle_{*}+\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}(u,v)-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}(v,u).

(4.94)

The estimate (
4.93
) shows that the lower order part
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}
of the bilinear form
(u,v)\mapsto\langle\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u,v\rangle_{*}
gains one derivative and one factor
\nu+|a-a_{\infty}|
in comparison with the leading order symmetric part
\mathcal{Q}
. This corresponds to two gains, one gain stems from the fact that we have matched at leading order the scalar products of the interior and exterior zones in the definition of
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{*}
, and the other from the fact that there is an extra cancellation if the two stationary states are in their equilibrium positions
a_{\infty}
and
-a_{\infty}
.

Proof.

Step 1
.
Proof of (
4.91
) and computation of
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}
. We first decompose by (
4.77
):

\langle\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u,v\rangle_{*}=\int\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}uv\omega_{\nu}dz-c_{*}\nu^{2}\int\chi^{*}\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}dz.

(4.95)

To compute the first term in (
4.95
), we use the decomposition
\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u=\frac{1}{\omega_{\nu}}\nabla.(\omega_{\nu}\nabla u)+2(U_{1+2,\nu}-\beta)u-\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}
from (
4.63
). Therefore, this term is after integrating by parts

\int\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}uv\omega_{\nu}dz=-\int\nabla u.\nabla v\omega_{\nu}dz+2\int uv(U_{1+2,\nu}-\beta)\omega_{\nu}dz-\int\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}v\omega_{\nu}dz.

(4.96)

To compute the second term in (
4.95
), we use the decomposition

\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u=\Delta u-\nabla.\Big(u(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+\beta z)\Big)-\nabla.(U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u})+(1-\chi^{*})U_{1+2,\nu}u

obtained from (
4.62
) using
-\Delta\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}=\chi^{*}u
. We find

\int\chi^{*}\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}=\int\chi^{*}\left(\Delta u-\nabla.\Big(u(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+\beta z)\Big)-\nabla.(U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u})+(1-\chi^{*})U_{1+2,\nu}u\right)\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}.

(4.97)

Using
\chi^{*}\Delta u=\Delta(\chi^{*}u)-2\nabla\chi^{*}.\nabla u-\Delta\chi^{*}u
, integrating by parts and using
-\Delta\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}=\chi^{*}v
, the first term in the right-hand side of (
4.97
) is

\int\chi^{*}\Delta u\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}=-\int\chi^{*}uv+\int\chi^{*}(1-\chi^{*})uv-\int(2\nabla\chi^{*}+\Delta\chi^{*})u\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}.

(4.98)

Integrating by parts, the second term in the right-hand side of (
4.97
) is

-\int\chi^{*}\nabla.\Big(u\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+\beta z\Big)\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}=\int\chi^{*}u(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+\beta z).\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}+\int\nabla\chi^{*}.(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+\beta z)u\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}.

(4.99)

Integrating by parts, the third term in the right-hand side of (
4.97
) is

-\int\chi^{*}\nabla.(U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u})\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}=\int\chi^{*}U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}+\int U_{1+2,\nu}\nabla\chi^{*}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}.

(4.100)

Injecting (
4.98
), (
4.99
) and (
4.100
) in (
4.97
) we get that the second term in (
4.95
) is

\displaystyle\int\chi^{*}\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}dz

\displaystyle=-\int\chi^{*}uv+\int\chi^{*}u(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+\beta z).\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}+\int\chi^{*}U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}

\displaystyle+\int\chi^{*}(1-\chi^{*})uv-\int(2\nabla\chi^{*}+\Delta\chi^{*})u\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}+\int\nabla\chi^{*}.(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+\beta z)u\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}

\displaystyle+\int U_{1+2,\nu}\nabla\chi^{*}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}+\int\chi^{*}\left((1-\chi^{*})U_{1+2,\nu}u\right)\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}dz.

(4.101)

Injecting (
4.96
) and (
4.102
) in (
4.95
) we obtain

\displaystyle\langle\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u,v\rangle_{*}

\displaystyle=-\int\nabla u.\nabla v\omega_{\nu}dz+2\int uv(U_{1+2,\nu}-\beta)\omega_{\nu}dz-\int\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}v\omega_{\nu}dz

(4.102)

\displaystyle+c_{*}\nu^{2}\left(\int\chi^{*}uv-\int\chi^{*}u(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+\beta z).\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}-\int\chi^{*}U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}\right)

\displaystyle-c_{*}\nu^{2}\Bigg(\int\chi^{*}(1-\chi^{*})uv-\int(2\nabla\chi^{*}+\Delta\chi^{*})u\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}+\int\nabla\chi^{*}.(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+\beta z)u\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\int U_{1+2,\nu}\nabla\chi^{*}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}+\int\chi^{*}(1-\chi^{*})U_{1+2,\nu}u\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}dz\Bigg)

Combining (
4.102
) and (
4.92
), we obtain the desired decomposition (
4.91
) with
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}
given by

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}(u,v)

\displaystyle=\int\chi^{*}u\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}.(\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}\omega_{\nu}-c_{*}\nu^{2}(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+\beta z))

(4.103)

\displaystyle-c_{*}\nu^{2}\Bigg(\int\chi^{*}(1-\chi^{*})uv-\int(2\nabla\chi^{*}+\Delta\chi^{*})u\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}+\int\nabla\chi^{*}.(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+\beta z)u\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\int U_{1+2,\nu}\nabla\chi^{*}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}+\int\chi^{*}(1-\chi^{*})U_{1+2,\nu}u\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}dz\Bigg)

Step 2.

Proof of (
4.93
)
. We decompose (
4.103
) into

\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}(u,v)=\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}(u,v)+\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}(u,v)+\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{3}(u,v)

(4.104)

where

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}(u,v)=\int\chi^{*}u\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}.(\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}\omega_{\nu}-c_{*}\nu^{2}(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+\beta z)),

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}(u,v)=-c_{*}\nu^{2}\Bigg(\int\chi^{*}(1-\chi^{*})uv-\int(2\nabla\chi^{*}+\Delta\chi^{*})u\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}+\int\nabla\chi^{*}.(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+\beta z)u\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\int U_{1+2,\nu}\nabla\chi^{*}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}+\int\chi^{*}(1-\chi^{*})U_{1+2,\nu}u\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}dz\Bigg),

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{3}(u,v)=-c_{*}\nu^{2}\int U_{1+2,\nu}\nabla\chi^{*}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}.

The desired estimate (
4.93
) is then a consequence of the estimate (
4.121
), (
4.126
) and (
4.125
) we prove below for
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
,
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{3}
respectively.

Let
\chi
be the cut-off function defined by (
2.1
). We decompose for
\eta>0
small,

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle=u\chi(\frac{z-a}{\eta})+f\chi(\frac{z+a}{\eta})+u\left(1-\chi(\frac{z-a}{\eta})-\chi(\frac{z+a}{\eta})\right)

\displaystyle=u_{in,a}+u_{in,-a}+u_{ex}

(4.105)

\displaystyle=u_{in}+u_{ex}

and

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle=v\chi(\frac{z-a}{4\eta})+u\chi(\frac{z+a}{4\eta})+v\left(1-\chi(\frac{z-a}{4\eta})-\chi(\frac{z+a}{4\eta})\right)

\displaystyle=v_{in,a}+v_{in,-a}+v_{ex}

(4.106)

\displaystyle=v_{in}+v_{ex}.

Without mentioning it explicitly, we shall use numerous times that for
f=u,v
,

|f_{ex}|+|f_{in,a}|+|f_{in,-a}|+|f_{in}|\lesssim|f|\quad\mbox{and}\quad|\nabla f_{ex}|+|\nabla f_{in,a}|+|\nabla f_{in,-a}|+|\nabla f_{in}|\lesssim|f|+|\nabla f|

and that
\omega_{\nu}\approx_{\eta}1
on the set
\{z\leq 20\}\cap_{\pm}\{|z\pm a|\geq\eta\}
which contains the supports of
\chi^{*}u_{ex}
and
\chi^{*}v_{ex}
.

Estimate for
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}(u,v)
. We decompose:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}(u,v)

\displaystyle=\int(u_{in,a}\nabla\Phi_{v_{in,a}}+u_{in,-a}\nabla\Phi_{v_{in,-a}}+u_{in,a}\nabla\Phi_{v_{in,-a}+\chi^{*}v_{ex}}+u_{in,-a}\nabla\Phi_{v_{in,a}+\chi^{*}v_{ex}}+u_{ex}\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}v})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad.(\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}\omega_{\nu}-c_{*}\nu^{2}\chi^{*}(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+\beta z))dz

(4.107)

We compute that in the above integral

\displaystyle\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}\omega_{\nu}-c_{*}\nu^{2}(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+\beta z)

\displaystyle=\nabla U_{1,\nu}\frac{\nu^{4}e^{-\beta|z|^{2}/2}}{U_{1,\nu}U_{2,\nu}}+\nabla U_{2,\nu}\omega_{\nu}-c_{*}\nu^{2}(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1,\nu}}+\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}+\beta z)

\displaystyle=\nu^{2}\nabla\Phi_{U_{1,\nu}}\left(\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{2,\nu}}e^{-\beta|z|^{2}/2}-c_{*}\right)-c_{*}\nu^{2}(\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}+\beta z).

(4.108)

Let now
z
be a point near
a
. By definition of
c_{*}
, and by definition of
a_{\infty}
, we have

\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{2,\nu}(a)}e^{-\beta|a|^{2}/2}-c_{*}=O(\nu^{2})\quad\mbox{and}\quad\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}(a_{\infty})+\beta z(a_{\infty})=O(\nu^{2}).

(4.109)

Since

\nabla\left(\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{2,\nu}}e^{-\beta|z|^{2}/2}\right)=-\left(\frac{\nabla U_{2,\nu}}{U_{2,\nu}}+\beta z\right)\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{2,\nu}}e^{-\beta|z|^{2}/2}=-\left(\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}+\beta z\right)\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{2,\nu}}e^{-\beta|z|^{2}/2},

this implies

\nabla\left(\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{2,\nu}}e^{-\beta|z|^{2}/2}\right)(a_{\infty})=O(\nu^{2}).

Combining, we perform Taylor expansions and get

\displaystyle\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{2,\nu}(z)}e^{-\beta|z|^{2}/2}-c_{*}\ =\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{2,\nu}(a)}e^{-\beta|a|^{2}/2}-c_{*}+\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{2,\nu}(z)}e^{-\beta|z|^{2}/2}-\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{2,\nu}(a)}e^{-\beta|a|^{2}/2}

\displaystyle=O(\nu^{2})+(z-a).\nabla\left(\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{2,\nu}(\cdot)}e^{-\beta|\cdot|^{2}/2}\right)(a)+O(|z-a|^{2})

\displaystyle=O(\nu^{2})+(z-a).\left(\nabla\left(\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{2,\nu}(\cdot)}e^{-\beta|\cdot|^{2}/2}\right)(a)-\nabla\left(\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{2,\nu}(\cdot)}e^{-\beta|\cdot|^{2}/2}\right)(a_{\infty})\right)+O(|z-a|^{2})

\displaystyle=O(\nu^{2}+|z-a||a-a_{\infty}|+|z-a|^{2})

(4.110)

Injectin (
4.108
) and (
4.109
), using that
\nabla\Phi_{U_{1,\nu}}=O((\nu+|z-a|)^{-1})
and a Taylor expansion, we obtain

\displaystyle\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}(z)\omega_{\nu}(z)-c_{*}\nu^{2}\chi^{*}(z)(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}(z)+\beta z)

\displaystyle=O((\nu^{2}+\nu|a-a_{\infty}|)(\nu+|z-a|)).

(4.111)

For the first term in (
4.107
), we use (
4.111
) and then the estimate (
C.5
) with variable
y=z-a
and
\lambda=\nu
and get

\displaystyle\left|\int u_{in,a}\nabla\Phi_{v_{in,a}}.(\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}\omega_{\nu}-c_{*}\nu^{2}\chi^{*}(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+\beta z)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int O((\nu^{2}+\nu|a-a_{\infty}|)(\nu+|z-a|))|u_{in,a}|)\nabla\Phi_{v_{in,a}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim(\nu^{2}+\nu|a-a_{\infty}|)\left(\int(\nu+|z-a|)^{2}u_{in,a}^{2}dz\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int(\nu+|z-a|)^{2}v_{in,a}^{2}dz\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+(\nu^{2}+\nu|a-a_{\infty}|)\int|u_{in,a}|dz\int|v_{in,a}|dz.

Using the Hardy-Poincaré inequality (
4.66
) and (
4.61
), we bound for
f=u,v
,

\left(\int(\nu+|z-a|)^{2}f_{in,a}^{2}dz\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\min\left(\|f\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\ ,\nu^{-1}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\right),

and, using Cauchy-Schwarz with
\|(\nu+|z-a|)^{-1}\mathbbm{1}(|z-a|<\eta)\|_{L^{2}}\approx\sqrt{|\ln\nu|}
or
\|(\nu+|z-a|)^{-2}\mathbbm{1}(|z-a|<\eta)\|_{L^{2}}\approx\nu^{-1}
and (
4.61
), then (
4.66
), we bound

\displaystyle\|f_{in,a}\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\min\left(\sqrt{|\ln\nu|}\|(\nu+|z-a|)^{-1}f\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\ ,\nu^{-1}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\min\left(\sqrt{|\ln\nu|}\|f\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\ ,\nu^{-1}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\right).

(4.112)

Combining the three inequalities above shows

\displaystyle\left|\int u_{in,a}\nabla\Phi_{v_{in,a}}.(\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}\omega_{\nu}-c_{*}\nu^{2}\chi^{*}(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+\beta z)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim(\nu+|a-a_{\infty}|)|\ln\nu|^{1/2}\min\left(\nu\sqrt{|\ln\nu|}\|u\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\|v\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\ ,\ \|u\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\|v\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\ ,\ \|u\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\|v\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\right).

The second term in (
4.107
) at
-a
can be bounded by the exact same estimates, so that we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\int(u_{in,a}\nabla\Phi_{v_{in,a}}+u_{in,-a}\nabla\Phi_{v_{in,-a}}).(\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}\omega_{\nu}-c_{*}\nu^{2}\chi^{*}(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+\beta z))\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\min\Big(\nu(\nu+|a-a_{\infty}|)|\ln\nu|\|u\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\|v\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\ ,\ (\nu+|a-a_{\infty}|)|\ln\nu|^{1/2}\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\|v\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\ ,

(4.113)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\ (\nu+|a-a_{\infty}|)|\ln\nu|^{1/2}\|u\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\|v\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\Big)

(4.114)

For the third term in (
4.107
) we bound using (
4.111
),

\displaystyle\left|\int u_{in,a}\nabla\Phi_{v_{in,-a}+\chi^{*}v_{ex}}(\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}\omega_{\nu}-c_{*}\nu^{2}\chi^{*}(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+\beta z))\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim(\nu^{2}+\nu|a-a_{\infty}|)\|(\nu+|z-a|))u_{in,a}\|_{L^{1}}\|\nabla\Phi_{v_{in,-a}+\chi^{*}v_{ex}}\|_{L^{\infty}(|z-a|<2\eta)}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\nu^{2}+\nu|a-a_{\infty}|)\|(\nu+|z-a|))u_{in,a}\|_{L^{1}}\|{v_{in,-a}+\chi^{*}v_{ex}}\|_{L^{1}}.

(4.115)

Above, we used that for
z
in the support of
u_{in,a}
and
z^{\prime}
in the support of
v_{in,-a}+\chi^{*}v_{ex}
we have
|z-z^{\prime}|\geq 2\eta
so that
|\nabla\Phi_{v_{in,-a}+\chi^{*}v_{ex}}(z)|\lesssim\int|\ln|z-z^{\prime}|||v_{in,-a}+\chi^{*}v_{ex}|(z^{\prime})dz^{\prime}\lesssim\|v_{in,-a}+\chi^{*}v_{ex}\|_{L^{1}}
. We have by Cauchy-Schwarz, (
4.61
) and (
4.66
):

\displaystyle\|(\nu+|z-a|))u_{in,a}\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\nu+|z-a|))u_{in,a}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|(\nu+|z-a|))^{-1}u_{in,a}\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\min\left(\nu^{-1}\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\ ,\ \|u\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\right).

(4.116)

Also, decomposing
v=v_{in}+v_{ex}
, using (
4.112
) and Cauchy-Schwarz with the fact that
\omega_{\nu}\approx 1
on the support of
v_{ex}
,

\displaystyle\|\chi^{*}v\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim\|v_{in}\|_{L^{1}}+\|\chi^{*}v_{ex}\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim\min\left(\sqrt{|\ln\nu|}\|v\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\ ,\ \nu^{-1}\|v\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\right)

(4.117)

Injecting (
4.116
) and (
4.117
) into (
4.115
), noticing that the fourth term in (
4.107
) can be estimated by a symmetric reasoning, we get

\displaystyle\left|\int(u_{in,a}\nabla\Phi_{v_{in,-a}}+u_{in,-a}\nabla\Phi_{v_{in,a}})(\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}\omega_{\nu}-c_{*}\nu^{2}\chi^{*}(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+\beta z))\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim(\nu+|a-a_{\infty}|)|\ln\nu|^{1/2}\min\left(\nu\|u\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\|v\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\ ,\ \|u\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\|v\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\ ,\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\|v\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\right).

(4.118)

For the fifth and last term in (
4.107
) we decompose
\chi^{*}v=\tilde{v}_{in}+\tilde{v}_{ex}
where
\tilde{v}_{in,a}=v\chi(\frac{4(z-a)}{\eta})+v\chi(\frac{4(z+a)}{\eta})
and
\tilde{v}_{ex}=\chi^{*}v-\tilde{v}_{in}
. Using that
u_{ex}
has support inside
\cap_{\pm}\{|z\pm a|\geq\eta\}
where
|U_{1+2,\nu}|\lesssim\nu^{2}
and
|\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}|\lesssim 1
, we bound

\displaystyle\left|\int u_{ex}\nabla\Phi_{v}(\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}\omega_{\nu}-c_{*}\nu^{2}\chi^{*}(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+\beta z))\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\nu^{2}\|u_{ex}\omega_{\nu}\|_{L^{1}}\|\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{v}_{in}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\cap_{\pm}|z\pm a|>\eta)}+\nu^{2}\|u_{ex}\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\|\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{v}_{ex}}\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\min\left(\nu\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\|v\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\ ,\ \nu^{2}\sqrt{|\ln\nu|}\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\|v\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\right)

(4.119)

where we used that for
z
in the support of
u_{ex}
and
z^{\prime}
in the support of
\tilde{v}_{in}
we have
|z-z^{\prime}|\geq\eta/2
so that
|\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{v}_{in}}(z)|\lesssim\int|z-z^{\prime}|^{-1}|\tilde{v}_{in}(z^{\prime})|dz^{\prime}\lesssim\|\tilde{v}_{in}\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim\min(\sqrt{|\ln\nu|}\|v\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}},\nu^{-1}\|v\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}})
by (
4.117
) and that
\|\tilde{v}_{ex}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\mathbbm{1}(|z|<10)|z|^{-1}\|_{L^{1}}\|\tilde{v}_{ex}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|v\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}
using that
\tilde{v}_{ex}
is supported inside
\{|z|<20\}\cap\{|z\pm a|>\eta/4\}
and Young’s inequality for convolution.

Injecting (
4.113
), (
4.118
) and (
4.119
) in (
4.107
) shows:

|\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}(u,v)|\lesssim(\nu+|a-a_{\infty}|)\sqrt{|\ln\nu|}\min\left(\nu\sqrt{|\ln\nu|}\|u\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\|v\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\ ,\ \|u\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\|v\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\ ,\ \|u\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\|v\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\right)

(4.120)

Estimate for
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}(u,v)
. Using that the functions
\chi^{*}(1-\chi^{*})
,
\nabla\chi^{*}
and
\Delta\chi^{*}
all have their support included in the zone
\{10\leq|z|\leq 20\}
where
\omega_{\nu}\approx 1
,
|\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}|\lesssim 1
and
|U_{1+2,\nu}|\lesssim\nu^{2}
, we bound

\displaystyle\Bigg|\int\chi^{*}(1-\chi^{*})uv+u\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}\left(-(2\nabla\chi^{*}+\Delta\chi^{*})+\nabla\chi^{*}.(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+\beta z)+\chi^{*}(1-\chi^{*})U_{1+2,\nu}\right)dz\Bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\int_{10<|z|<20}|u||v|\omega_{\nu}+\int_{10<|z|<20}|u||\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}|\omega_{\nu}\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\left(\|v\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}+\|\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}\|_{L^{2}(10<|z|<20)}\right).

(4.121)

Let
z\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
with
10<|z|<20
. We have for
z^{\prime}
in the support of
f_{in}
, since this set is supported in
\cup_{\pm}\{|z-a|\leq 2\eta\}\subset\{|z|\leq 6\}
as
|a|<5
and
\eta<1
, that
|z-z^{\prime}|>1
. Hence, using (
4.112
),

|\nabla^{k}\Phi_{f_{in}}(z)|\lesssim\int|\nabla^{k}\ln|z-z^{\prime}|||f_{in}|dz^{\prime}\lesssim\|f_{in}\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim\min\left(\sqrt{|\ln\nu|}\|f\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\ ,\nu^{-1}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\right)

(4.122)

for
k=0,1
. We have
\omega_{\nu}\lesssim\langle z\rangle^{-4}
on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and
\omega_{\nu}\approx_{\eta}1
for
\nu
small enough on the support of
f_{ex}
. Hence by (
4.35
):

\|\Phi_{\chi^{*}f_{ex}}\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}^{2}\lesssim\int\langle z\rangle^{-4}|\Phi_{\chi^{*}f_{ex}}|^{2}dz\lesssim\int\langle z\rangle^{4}\chi^{*2}f_{ex}^{2}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}^{2}

(4.123)

We decompose
\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}=\Phi_{v_{in}}+\Phi_{\chi^{*}v_{ex}}
in (
4.121
), and then use (
4.122
) and (
4.123
) to obtain

\displaystyle\left|\int\chi^{*}(1-\chi^{*})uv+u\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}\left(-(2\nabla\chi^{*}+\Delta\chi^{*})+\nabla\chi^{*}.(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+\beta z)+\chi^{*}(1-\chi^{*})U_{1+2,\nu}\right)dz\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\left(\|v\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}+\|\Phi_{v_{in}}\|_{L^{2}(10<|z|<20)}+\|\Phi_{v_{ex}}\|_{L^{2}(10<|z|<20)}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\min\left(\sqrt{|\ln\nu|}\|v\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\ ,\nu^{-1}\|v\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\right)

This shows

|\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}(u,v)|\lesssim\nu\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\min\left(\nu\sqrt{|\ln\nu|}\|v\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\ ,\|v\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\right)

(4.124)

Estimate for
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{3}(u,v)
. We use that
|U_{1+2,\nu}|\lesssim\nu^{2}
and
\omega_{\nu}\approx 1
on the support of
\nabla\chi^{*}
which is contained in the zone
\{10<|z|<20\}
, and the decompositions
\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}=\nabla\Phi_{u_{in}}+\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u_{ex}}
and
\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}=\Phi_{v_{in}}+\Phi_{v_{ex}}
so that by Hölder

\displaystyle\left|\int U_{1+2,\nu}\nabla\chi^{*}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}\Phi_{\chi^{*}v}\right|\ \lesssim\nu^{2}\int_{10<|z|<20}(|\nabla\Phi_{u_{in}}|+|\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u_{ex}}|)(|\Phi_{v_{in}}|+|\Phi_{\chi^{*}v_{ex}}|)\omega_{\nu}

(4.125)

\displaystyle\lesssim\nu^{2}(\|\nabla\Phi_{u_{in}}\|_{L^{1}(10<|z|<20)}+\|\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u_{ex}}\|_{L^{1}(10<|z|<20)})(\|\Phi_{v_{in}}\|_{L^{\infty}(10<|z|<20)}+\|\Phi_{\chi^{*}v_{ex}}\|_{L^{\infty}(10<|z|<20)}).

By (
4.122
) we have

\|\nabla\Phi_{u_{in}}\|_{L^{1}(10<|z|<20)}\lesssim\nu^{-1}\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\|\Phi_{v_{in}}\|_{L^{\infty}(10<|z|<20)}\lesssim\nu^{-1}\|v\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}

We have using successively Hölder, Sobolev, Lebesgue estimates for the Riesz kernel, the identity
\Delta\Phi_{\chi^{*}u_{ex}}=-\chi^{*}u_{ex}
and Hölder again:

\|\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u_{ex}}\|_{L^{1}(10<|z|<20)})\lesssim\|\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u_{ex}}\|_{L^{6}}\lesssim\|\nabla^{2}\Phi_{\chi^{*}u_{ex}}\|_{L^{3/2}}\lesssim\|\Delta\Phi_{\chi^{*}u_{ex}}\|_{L^{3/2}}\lesssim\|\chi^{*}u_{ex}\|_{L^{3/2}}\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}.

Using Young’s inequality for convolution with that fact that
\chi^{*}v_{ex}
has support inside
\{|z|<20\}
, we have

\|\Phi_{\chi^{*}v_{ex}}\|_{L^{\infty}(10<|z|<20)}\lesssim\|\ln|\cdot|*\chi^{*}v_{ex}\|_{L^{\infty}(10<|z|<20)}\lesssim\|\chi^{*}v_{ex}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|v\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}.

Injecting the three inequalities above in (
4.125
) shows

|\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{3}(u,v)|\lesssim\nu^{2}\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\|v\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}.

(4.126)

∎

Proposition 4.11

(Coercivity of the operator for the matched product)
.

There exists
\delta>0
such that for
\eta>0
small enough, for
a
close to
a_{\infty}
and
\nu
close to
0
, if
u
is a Schwartz function satisfying the orthogonality conditions (
4.13
), then

-\langle\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u,u\rangle_{*}\geq\delta\int|\nabla u|^{2}\omega_{\nu}dz+\delta\int\left(\frac{1}{(\nu+|z-a|)^{2}})+\frac{1}{(\nu+|z+a|)^{2}})+\langle z\rangle^{2}\right)u^{2}\omega_{\nu}dz.

(4.127)

Proof.

Let
\chi
be the cut-off function defined by (
2.1
). We decompose for
\eta^{\prime}>\eta
,

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle=u\left(\chi(\frac{z-a}{\eta^{\prime}})+\chi(\frac{z+a}{\eta^{\prime}})\right)+u\left(1-\chi(\frac{z-a}{\eta^{\prime}})-\chi(\frac{z+a}{\eta^{\prime}})\right)

\displaystyle=u_{in}+u_{ex}.

(4.128)

As
\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u_{in}=\mathscr{L}^{z}u_{in}
we further write

\langle\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u,u\rangle_{*}=\langle\mathscr{L}^{z}u_{in},u_{in}\rangle_{*}+\langle\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u_{ex},u_{ex}\rangle_{*}+\langle\mathscr{L}^{z}u_{in},u_{ex}\rangle_{*}+\langle\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u_{ex},u_{in}\rangle_{*}

(4.129)

The main features of the proof are the following. The first two terms in (
4.129
) will be shown to be coercive thanks to Lemmas
4.6
and
4.2
, see (
4.130
) and (
4.148
) below. Then there are two main contributions in the remaining mixed terms in (
4.129
). The first one consists of the term (
4.150
), which involve the boundary terms due to the cut-off in the decomposition
u=u_{in}+u_{ex}
. It will be showed to be negligible in Step 5 by choosing a cut-off location
\eta^{\prime}=\eta^{\prime}(u)
that depends on the function
u
, for which it is small in the zone
\cup_{\pm}\{\eta^{\prime}<|z\pm a|<2\eta^{\prime}\}
. The second one consists of the term (
4.152
) due to the nonlocal effects of the exterior zone in the interior zone. We crucially use the cancellation that
\mathscr{L}^{z}u_{in}
has zero mean as it can be put in divergence form (due to the mass criticality of the problem at hand) to estimate that contribution in Step 3.

Step 1
.
Estimate for the interior term
. We claim that for
a
close
(0,2)
,
\nu
and
\eta^{\prime}
small enough,

\langle\mathscr{L}^{z}u_{in},u_{in}\rangle_{*}\geq\delta\int|\nabla u_{in}|^{2}\omega_{\nu}dz+\delta\int u_{in}^{2}\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2}}\omega_{\nu}dz,

(4.130)

and we now prove this estimate. We decompose using (
4.77
) and (
4.1
):

-\langle\mathscr{L}^{z}u_{in},u_{in}\rangle_{*}=-c_{*}\langle\mathscr{L}^{z}u_{in},u_{in}\rangle+\int(\mathscr{L}^{z}u_{in})u_{in}\widetilde{\omega_{\nu}}dz,\qquad\widetilde{\omega_{\nu}}=c_{*}\gamma_{\nu}-\omega_{\nu}.

(4.131)

For the first term in (
4.131
), we have that
u_{in}
is supported in
\cup_{\pm}\{|z\pm a|\leq 2\eta^{\prime}\}
and that it satisfies the orthogonality conditions (
4.13
) as
\eta^{\prime}>\eta
. Hence by Lemma
4.2
, the Hardy-type inequality (
4.15
) and the equivalence of the weights (
4.67
):

-\langle\mathscr{L}^{z}u_{in},u_{in}\rangle\geq\delta^{\prime}\int|\nabla u_{in}|^{2}\omega_{\nu}dz+\delta^{\prime}\int u_{in}^{2}\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2}}\omega_{\nu}dz.

(4.132)

The second term in (
4.131
) is

\displaystyle\int(\mathscr{L}^{z}u_{in})u_{in}\widetilde{\omega_{\nu}}dz

\displaystyle=\int\Delta u_{in}u_{in}\widetilde{\omega_{\nu}}dz

(4.133)

\displaystyle-\int\left(\nabla.(u_{in}\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+U_{1+2,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{u_{in}})+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda u_{in}\right)u_{in}\widetilde{\omega_{\nu}}dz.

To estimate the first term in (
4.133
), we integrate by parts

\int\Delta u_{in}u_{in}\widetilde{\omega_{\nu}}dz=-\int|\nabla u_{in}|^{2}\widetilde{\omega_{\nu}}dz-\int u_{in}\nabla u_{in}.\nabla\widetilde{\omega_{\nu}}dz,

By (
4.86
) and (
4.87
) we have that for
i=0,1,
for
|z\pm a|\leq 2\eta^{\prime}
,

\left|\nabla^{i}\widetilde{\omega_{\nu}}\right|=\left|\nabla^{i}\left(\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1+2,\nu}}\left(c_{*}-\frac{\omega_{\nu}}{\gamma_{\nu}}\right)\right)\right|\lesssim\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1+2,\nu}}\frac{\nu^{2}+\eta^{\prime}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{i}}.

(4.134)

Combining the identity and inequality above, and using
|xy|\leq x^{2}+y^{2}
and (
4.67
), we bound

\left|\int\Delta u_{in}u_{in}\widetilde{\omega_{\nu}}dz\right|\lesssim(\nu^{2}+\eta^{\prime})\int\left(|\nabla u_{in}|^{2}+u_{in}^{2}\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2}}\right)\omega_{\nu}dz.

(4.135)

We next turn to the second term in (
4.133
). Using
-\Delta\Phi_{f}=f
and
\nabla\Phi_{f}=\Phi_{\nabla f}
, that
|U_{1,\nu}|\lesssim\nu^{2}(\nu+|z-a|)^{-4}
,
|\nabla U_{1,\nu}|\lesssim\nu^{2}(\nu+|z-a|)^{-5}
and
|\nabla\Phi_{U_{1,\nu}}|\lesssim(\nu+|z-a|)^{-1}
and
\nu(\nu+|z-a|)^{-1}\leq 1
, and the analogue estimates for
U_{2,\nu}
, we have

\left|\nabla.(u_{in}\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+U_{1+2,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{u_{in}})+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda u_{in}\right|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{|\nabla u_{in}|}{\nu+|z\pm a|}+\frac{|u_{in}|}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2}}+\frac{\nu^{2}|\Phi_{\nabla u_{in}}|}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{5}}.

Using this inequality, (
4.134
), and Cauchy-Schwarz,

\displaystyle\left|\int\left(\nabla.(u_{in}\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+U_{1+2,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{u_{in}})+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda u_{in}\right)u_{in}\widetilde{\omega_{\nu}}dz\right|

(4.136)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\nu^{2}+\eta^{\prime})\int\left(|\nabla u_{in}|^{2}+u_{in}^{2}\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2}}+|\Phi_{\nabla u_{in}}|^{2}\sum_{\pm}\frac{\nu^{4}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{8}}\right)\omega_{\nu}dz.

Using successively (
4.61
) and
U_{1+2,\nu}\approx\nu^{2}\sum_{\pm}(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{-4}
we have

\nu^{4}\sum_{\pm}\frac{\nu^{4}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{8}}\omega_{\nu}\lesssim\nu^{4}\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{4}}\approx\nu^{2}U_{1+2,\nu}.

Using this inequality, then (
4.33
) and the equivalence of weights (
4.67
) we have

\int|\Phi_{\nabla u_{in}}|^{2}\sum_{\pm}\frac{\nu^{4}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{8}}\omega_{\nu}dz\lesssim\int\nu^{2}|\Phi_{\nabla u_{in}}|^{2}U_{1+2,\nu}dz\lesssim\int|\nabla u_{in}|^{2}\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1+2,\nu}}dz\approx\int|\nabla u_{in}|^{2}\omega_{\nu}dz

Injecting the above inequality in (
4.136
) shows

\displaystyle\left|\int\left(\nabla.(u_{in}\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+U_{1+2,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{u_{in}})+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda u_{in}\right)u_{in}\widetilde{\omega_{\nu}}dz\right|

(4.137)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\nu^{2}+\eta^{\prime})\int\left(|\nabla u_{in}|^{2}+u_{in}^{2}\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2}}\right)\omega_{\nu}dz.

Injecting (
4.135
) and (
4.137
) in (
4.133
) shows

\left|\int(\mathscr{L}^{z}u_{in})u_{in}\widetilde{\omega_{\nu}}dz\right|\lesssim(\nu^{2}+\eta^{\prime})\int\left(|\nabla u_{in}|^{2}+u_{in}^{2}\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2}}\right)\omega_{\nu}dz.

Injecting this inequality and (
4.132
) in (
4.131
) shows the desired inequality (
4.130
).

Step 2
.
Estimate for the exterior term
. We claim that for any
|a|\leq 5
and
\eta^{\prime}>0
, if
\nu
is small enough:

-\langle\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u_{ex},u_{ex}\rangle_{*}\geq\delta\int|\nabla u_{ex}|^{2}\omega_{\nu}dz+\delta\int u_{ex}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{|z-a|^{2}}+\frac{1}{|a+z|^{2}}+|z|^{2}\right)\omega_{\nu}dz,

(4.138)

We now show (
4.148
). We decompose using (
4.77
):

\langle\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u_{ex},u_{ex}\rangle_{*}=\langle\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u_{ex},u_{ex}\rangle_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}-c^{*}\nu^{2}\int\chi^{*}(\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u_{ex})\Phi_{\chi^{*}u_{ex}}dz.

(4.139)

For the first term, we have by Lemma
4.63
and the Hardy-Poincaré type inequality (
4.66
):

-\langle\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u_{ex},u_{ex}\rangle_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\geq\delta^{\prime}\int\left(|\nabla u_{ex}|^{2}+u_{ex}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{|z-a|^{2}}+\frac{1}{|z+a|^{2}}+|z|^{2}\right)\right)\omega_{\nu}dz.

(4.140)

We rewrite the second term in (
4.139
) using (
4.62
) and
\nabla\Phi_{f}=\Phi_{\nabla f}
:

\displaystyle\int\chi^{*}(\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u_{ex})\Phi_{\chi^{*}u_{ex}}dz

(4.141)

\displaystyle=\int\chi^{*}\left(\Delta u_{ex}-\nabla.(u_{ex}\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}})+U_{1+2,\nu}u_{ex}-\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}\Phi_{\nabla(\chi^{*}u_{ex})}-\beta\Lambda u_{ex}\right)\Phi_{\chi^{*}u_{ex}}dz.

Integrating by parts and using
\nabla\Phi_{f}=\Phi_{\nabla f}
, and then using
|xy|\leq x^{2}+y^{2}
and that
\chi^{*}
is supported in
\{|z|\leq 20\}
, we estimate the first term in (
4.141
) by

\left|\int\chi^{*}\Delta u_{ex}\Phi_{\chi^{*}u_{ex}}dz\right|\lesssim\int_{|z|\leq 20}(|\nabla u_{ex}|^{2}+\Phi_{\chi^{*}u_{ex}}^{2}+|\Phi_{\nabla(\chi^{*}u_{ex})}|^{2})dz.

(4.142)

Since the integral is performed over
\{|z|\leq 20\}
and (
4.35
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{|z|\leq 20}(\Phi_{\chi^{*}u_{ex}}^{2}+|\Phi_{\nabla(\chi^{*}u_{ex})}|^{2})dz\lesssim\int\langle z\rangle^{4}((\chi^{*}u_{ex})^{2}+|\nabla(\chi^{*}u_{ex})|^{2})dz

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\lesssim\int_{|z|\leq 20}(u_{ex}^{2}+|\nabla u_{ex}|^{2})dz\ \leq\ C_{\eta^{\prime}}\int(u_{ex}^{2}+|\nabla u_{ex}|^{2})\omega_{\nu}dz,

(4.143)

where we used that

\omega_{\nu}(z)\geq c_{\eta^{\prime}}>0\qquad\mbox{for }|z|\leq 20\mbox{ and }\min(|z-a|,|z+a|)\geq\eta^{\prime}

(4.144)

from (
4.61
) for the last inequality. Combining (
4.142
), (
4.143
) and (
4.144
) shows

\left|\int\chi^{*}\Delta u_{ex}\Phi_{\chi^{*}u_{ex}}dz\right|\leq C_{\eta^{\prime}}\int(u_{ex}^{2}+|\nabla u_{ex}|^{2})\omega_{\nu}dz.

(4.145)

For the remaining terms in (
4.141
), using that
\Delta f=-f
, and
|\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}(z)|\leq C_{\eta^{\prime}}
and
|U_{1+2,\nu}(z)|+|\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}(z)|\lesssim\nu^{2}
on the support of
u_{ex}
for
\nu
small enough, then
|xy|\leq x^{2}+y^{2}
and the support of
\chi^{*}
, we get

\displaystyle\left|\int\chi^{*}\left(-\nabla.(u_{ex}\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}})+U_{1+2,\nu}u_{ex}-\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}\Phi_{\nabla(\chi^{*}u_{ex})}-\beta\Lambda u_{ex}\right)\Phi_{\chi^{*}u_{ex}}dz\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\eta^{\prime}}\int_{|z|\leq 20}(|\nabla u_{ex}|^{2}+u_{ex}^{2}+(\Phi_{\chi^{*}u_{ex}})^{2}+|\Phi_{\nabla(\chi^{*}u_{ex})}|^{2})dz

\displaystyle\leq C_{\eta^{\prime}}\int(|\nabla u_{ex}|^{2}+u_{ex}^{2})\omega_{\nu}dz,

(4.146)

where we used (
4.143
) and (
4.144
) for the last inequality. Injecting (
4.145
) and (
4.146
) in (
4.141
) shows

\left|\int\chi^{*}(\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u_{ex})\Phi_{\chi^{*}u_{ex}}dz\right|\leq C_{\eta^{\prime}}\int(|\nabla u_{ex}|^{2}+u_{ex}^{2})\omega_{\nu}dz.

(4.147)

In turn, injecting (
4.140
) and (
4.147
) in (
4.139
) and taking
\nu
small enough shows the desired inequality (
4.148
).

Step 3
.
Estimate for the mixed terms
. We claim that for some universal
C>0
,

\displaystyle\left|\langle\mathscr{L}^{z}u_{in},u_{ex}\rangle_{*}\right|+\left|\langle\mathscr{L}^{z}u_{ex},u_{in}\rangle_{*}\right|

(4.148)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\cup_{\pm}\{\eta^{\prime}<|z\pm a|<2\eta^{\prime}\}}\left(|\nabla u|^{2}+\frac{u^{2}}{\eta^{{}^{\prime}2}}\right)\omega_{\nu}dz+C_{\eta^{\prime}}\nu|\ln\nu|\int(|\nabla u|^{2}+u^{2})\omega_{\nu}dz

and we now prove this inequality. Using the almost symmetry identity (
4.94
) and the bound (
4.93
) we have

\langle\mathscr{L}^{z}u_{ex},u_{in}\rangle_{*}=\langle\mathscr{L}^{z}u_{in},u_{ex}\rangle_{*}+\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}(u_{ex},u_{in})=\langle\mathscr{L}^{z}u_{in},u_{ex}\rangle_{*}+O(\nu|\ln\nu|\|u\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}^{2}).

(4.149)

so that it suffices to bound
\langle\mathscr{L}^{z}u_{in},u_{ex}\rangle_{*}
. Using (
4.77
), then (
2.8
) and the divergence form of the operator
\mathscr{L}^{z}f=\nabla.(\nabla f-f\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}-U_{1+2,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{f}-\beta zf)
and
-\Delta\Phi_{f}=f
, we expand

\displaystyle\langle\mathscr{L}^{z}u_{in},u_{ex}\rangle_{*}

\displaystyle=\int(\mathscr{L}^{z}u_{in})u_{ex}\omega_{\nu}dz-\nu^{2}c_{*}\int\chi^{*}(\mathscr{L}^{z}u_{in})\Phi_{\chi^{*}u_{ex}}

\displaystyle=\int(\Delta u_{in}-\nabla u_{in}.\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+2U_{1+2,\nu}u_{in}-\beta\Lambda u_{in})u_{ex}\omega_{\nu}dz

(4.150)

\displaystyle-\int\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla\Phi_{u_{in}}u_{ex}\omega_{\nu}dz

(4.151)

\displaystyle-\nu^{2}c_{*}\int\nabla.(\nabla u_{in}-u_{in}\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}-U_{1+2,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{u_{in}}-\beta zu_{in})\chi_{10}\Phi_{\chi^{*}u_{ex}}.

(4.152)

The estimates (
4.157
), (
4.160
) and (
4.165
) we show below for (
4.150
), (
4.151
) and (
4.152
) respectively, combined with (
4.149
), imply the desired inequality (
4.148
).

Estimate for (
4.150
)
. For the first term in (
4.150
), we integrate by parts and find

\int\Delta u_{in}u_{ex}\omega_{\nu}dz=-\int\nabla u_{in}.\nabla\left(u_{ex}\omega_{\nu}\right).

We have
|\nabla\chi(\frac{z\pm a}{\eta^{\prime}})|\lesssim\eta^{{}^{\prime}-1}\mathbbm{1}(\eta^{\prime}<|z\pm a|<2\eta^{\prime})
. By (
4.128
) and the definition of
\chi
we have

\displaystyle|u_{in}|\leq|u|\mathbbm{1}(\cup_{\pm}\{|z\pm a|<2\eta^{\prime}\}),\quad|\nabla u_{in}|\lesssim(|\nabla u|+\eta^{{}^{\prime}-1}|u|)\mathbbm{1}(\cup_{\pm}\{|z\pm a|<2\eta^{\prime}\}),

(4.153)

\displaystyle|u_{ex}|\leq|u|\mathbbm{1}(\cap_{\pm}\{|z\pm a|>\eta^{\prime}\}),\quad|\nabla u_{ex}|\lesssim(|\nabla u|+\eta^{{}^{\prime}-1}|u|)\mathbbm{1}(\cap_{\pm}\{|z\pm a|>\eta^{\prime}\}).

(4.154)

The integral is located in
\cup_{\pm}\{\eta^{\prime}<|z\pm|<2\eta^{\prime}\}
, where
|\nabla\omega_{\nu}|\lesssim\eta^{{}^{\prime}-1}\omega_{\nu}
from (
4.60
). Using this, (
4.153
) and (
4.154
) and
|xy|\leq x^{2}+y^{2}
,

\left|\int\Delta u_{in}u_{ex}\omega_{\nu}dz\right|\lesssim\int_{\cup_{\pm}\{\eta^{\prime}<|z\pm a|<2\eta^{\prime}\}}\left(|\nabla u|^{2}+\frac{u^{2}}{\eta^{{}^{\prime}2}}\right)\omega_{\nu}dz.

(4.155)

The remaining terms in (
4.150
) are also located in
\cup_{\pm}\{\eta^{\prime}<|z\pm a|<2\eta^{\prime}\}
. Since
|\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{-1}
and
|U_{1+2,\nu}|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\nu^{2}(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{-4}
, we have
|\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}|\lesssim\eta^{{}^{\prime}-1}
and
|U_{1+2,\nu}|\leq C_{\eta^{\prime}}\nu^{2}
on this set. Using this,
|\Lambda u_{in}|\lesssim|\nabla u_{in}|+|u_{in}|
, (
4.153
), (
4.154
) and
|xy|\leq x^{2}+y^{2}
,

\left|\int(-\nabla u_{in}.\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}+2U_{1+2,\nu}u_{in}-\beta\Lambda u_{in})u_{ex}\omega_{\nu}dz\right|\lesssim\int_{\cup_{\pm}\{\eta^{\prime}<|z\pm a|<2\eta^{\prime}\}}\left(|\nabla u|^{2}+\frac{u^{2}}{\eta^{{}^{\prime}2}}\right)\omega_{\nu}dz.

(4.156)

Combining (
4.155
) and (
4.156
) shows

\left|\int(\Delta u_{in}-\nabla u_{in}.\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+2U_{1+2,\nu}u_{in}-\beta\Lambda u_{in})u_{ex}\omega_{\nu}dz\right|\lesssim\int_{\cup_{\pm}\{\eta^{\prime}<|z\pm a|<2\eta^{\prime}\}}\left(|\nabla u|^{2}+\frac{u^{2}}{\eta^{{}^{\prime}2}}\right)\omega_{\nu}dz.

(4.157)

Estimate for (
4.151
)
. We estimate that for
|z|\leq 20
, by Cauchy-Schwarz and as the support of
u_{in}
is included in
\{|z|\leq 20\}
,

\displaystyle|\Phi_{u_{in}}(z)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int|\ln|z-z^{\prime}|||u_{in}(z^{\prime})|dz^{\prime}\lesssim\left(\int_{|z^{\prime}|\leq 20}|\ln|z-z^{\prime}||^{2}\frac{dz^{\prime}}{\omega_{\nu}(z^{\prime})}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int u_{in}^{2}(z^{\prime})\omega_{\nu}(z^{\prime})dz^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\nu^{-1}|\ln\nu|\left(\int u^{2}\omega_{\nu}dz^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(4.158)

where we used that
\omega_{\nu}(z^{\prime})\approx\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1+2,\nu}(z^{\prime})}
for
|z|\leq 20
from (
4.67
) and
