\int|\ln|z-z^{\prime}||U_{1+2,\nu}(z^{\prime})dz^{\prime}\leq|\ln\nu|
and (
4.153
) for the last inequality. Similarly, using also (
4.153
) and (
4.66
),

|\nabla\Phi_{u_{in}}(z)|=|\Phi_{\nabla u_{in}}(z)|\lesssim\nu^{-1}|\ln\nu|\left(\int(|\nabla u|^{2}+u^{2})d\omega_{\nu}dz^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(4.159)

Using that
|\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}|\leq C_{\eta^{\prime}}\nu^{2}
on the support of
u_{ex}
and (
4.159
) we find

\displaystyle\left|\int\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla\Phi_{u_{in}}u_{ex}\omega_{\nu}dz\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\eta^{\prime}}\nu|\ln\nu|\left(\int(|\nabla u|^{2}+u^{2})d\omega_{\nu}dz^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\int|u_{ex}|\omega_{\nu}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\eta^{\prime}}\nu|\ln\nu|\int(|\nabla u|^{2}+u^{2})\omega_{\nu}dz,

(4.160)

where we used Cauchy-Schwarz, (
4.154
) and
\int\omega_{\nu}dz\lesssim 1
for the last inequality.

Estimate for (
4.152
)
. Integrating by parts,

\displaystyle\int\nabla.(\nabla u_{in}-u_{in}\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}-U_{1+2,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{u_{in}}-\beta zu_{in})\chi^{*}\Phi_{\chi^{*}u_{ex}}dz

\displaystyle=\int(\nabla u_{in}-u_{in}\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}-U_{1+2,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{u_{in}}-\beta zu_{in}).\nabla(\chi^{*}\Phi_{\chi^{*}u_{ex}})dz.

We then estimate by using
|\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{-1}
, the support of
u_{in}
and the Hölder inequality,

\displaystyle\left|\int\nabla.(\nabla u_{in}-u_{in}\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}-U_{1+2,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{u_{in}}-\beta zu_{in})\chi^{*}\Phi_{\chi^{*}u_{ex}}dz\right|

(4.161)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\|\nabla u_{in}\|_{L^{1}}+\|u_{in}\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{\nu+|z\pm a|}\|_{L^{1}}+\|\nabla\Phi_{u_{in}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int U_{1+2,\nu}dz\right)\|\nabla(\chi^{*}\Phi_{\chi^{*}u_{ex}})\|_{L^{\infty}}.

We have by (
4.39
) and
\omega_{\nu}\approx\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1+2,\nu}}
on the support of
u_{in}
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{in}\|_{L^{1}}^{2}+\|u_{in}\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{\nu+|z\pm a|}\|_{L^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\nu^{-2}\int(|\nabla u_{in}|^{2}+u_{in}^{2}\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2}})\omega_{\nu}dz

\displaystyle\lesssim\nu^{-2}\int(|\nabla u|^{2}+u^{2})\omega_{\nu}dz,

(4.162)

where we used (
4.153
) and (
4.66
) in the last inequality. We next estimate that for
|z|\leq 20
, using the supports of
\chi^{*}
and Cauchy-Schwarz,

\displaystyle|\Phi_{\chi^{*}u_{ex}}(z)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{|z^{\prime}|\leq 20}|\ln|z-z^{\prime}|||u_{ex}(z^{\prime})|dz^{\prime}\lesssim\left(\int_{|z^{\prime}|\leq 20}|\ln|z-z^{\prime}||^{2}dz^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{|z^{\prime}|\leq 20}u_{ex}^{2}(z^{\prime})dz^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\eta^{\prime}}\left(\int u^{2}\omega_{\nu}dz^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

(4.163)

where we used (
4.154
) and that
\omega_{\nu}\geq c_{\eta^{\prime}}>0
for
z
in the support of
u_{ex}
with
|z|\leq 20
. We obtain similarly using
\nabla\Phi_{f}=\Phi_{\nabla f}
and (
4.154
) that

|\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}u_{ex}}(z)|\leq C_{\eta^{\prime}}\left(\int|\nabla u|^{2}+u^{2})\omega_{\nu}dz^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(4.164)

Injecting (
4.162
), (
4.159
),
\int U_{1+2,\nu}dz\lesssim 1
and (
4.163
) in (
4.161
) shows

\displaystyle\left|\int\nabla.(\nabla u_{in}-u_{in}\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}-U_{1+2,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{u_{in}}-\beta zu_{in})\chi^{*}\Phi_{\chi^{*}u_{ex}}dz\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\leq C_{\eta^{\prime}}|\ln\nu|\nu^{-1}\int(|\nabla u|^{2}+u^{2})\omega_{\nu}dz.

(4.165)

Step 4
.
End of the proof
. Injecting (
4.148
) and (
4.130
) in (
4.129
), we obtain that for some universal constants
C,\delta>0
,

\displaystyle-\langle\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u,u\rangle_{*}

\displaystyle\geq\delta\int\Big[|\nabla u|^{2}+u^{2}\big(\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2}}+|z|^{2}\big)\Big]\omega_{\nu}dz

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-C\int_{\cup_{\pm}\{\eta^{\prime}<|z\pm a|<2\eta^{\prime}\}}\left(|\nabla u|^{2}+\frac{u^{2}}{\eta^{{}^{\prime}2}}\right)\omega_{\nu}dz,

(4.166)

if
a
is close enough to
(2,0)
,
\eta^{\prime}>\eta
are small enough, and
\nu
is small enough, up to possibly reducing the value of
\delta
. Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
be such that
2^{N}\eta\ll 1
. Note that by taking
\eta
small enough, we can ensure that
N
is as large as we want. Performing a dyadic decomposition,

\displaystyle\int\left(|\nabla u|^{2}+u^{2}\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2}}\right)\omega_{\nu}dz\gtrsim\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{\cup_{\pm}\{2^{n}\eta<|z\pm a|<2^{n+1}\eta\}}\left(|\nabla u|^{2}+\frac{u^{2}}{2^{2n}\eta^{2}}\right)\omega_{\nu}dz

\displaystyle\geq N\min_{0\leq n\leq N-1}\int_{\cup_{\pm}\{2^{n}\eta<|z\pm a|<2^{n+1}\eta\}}\left(|\nabla u|^{2}+\frac{u^{2}}{2^{2n}\eta^{{}^{\prime}2}}\right)\omega_{\nu}dz.

Let
0\leq n_{0}\leq N-1
be the integer at which the minimum above is attained, and set
\eta^{\prime}=2^{n_{0}}\eta
. Then

\int_{\cup_{\pm}\{\eta^{\prime}<|z\pm a|<2\eta^{\prime}\}}\left(|\nabla u|^{2}+\frac{u^{2}}{\eta^{{}^{\prime}2}}\right)\omega_{\nu}dz\leq\frac{C}{N}\int\left(|\nabla u|^{2}+u^{2}\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2}}\right)\omega_{\nu}dz.

(4.167)

Injecting (
4.167
) in (
4.166
) and taking
N
large enough shows the desired inequality (
4.129
). ∎

4.3. Coercivity for a second decomposition

We introduce the decomposition

u=\hat{u}+a_{0}\phi_{0},

(4.168)

where the projection
\hat{u}=\hat{u}[\nu,a](u)
and the parameter
a_{0}=a_{0}[\nu,a](u)
are chosen so that

\langle\hat{u},\phi_{0}\rangle_{*}=0.

(4.169)

This projection satisfies the following properties.

Proposition 4.12
.

For all
a
close to
a_{\infty}
and
\nu
small enough, for all
u\in H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}
there exits a unique decomposition (
4.168
) with the property (
4.169
). Furthermore, there exists
C>0
such that if
u
satisfies the orthogonality conditions
\int_{|z\pm a|<\eta}udz=0
, we have the estimates

\langle u,u\rangle_{*}\leq\langle\hat{u},\hat{u}\rangle_{*}

(4.170)

and

|\langle\hat{u},\hat{u}\rangle_{*}|\leq C\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}^{2}

(4.171)

and

\left|\nu\partial_{\nu}(\langle\hat{u},\hat{u}\rangle_{*})\right|+\left|\nu\partial_{a}(\langle\hat{u},\hat{u}\rangle_{*})\right|\leq C\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}^{2},

(4.172)

as well as

|a_{0}|\leq C\min\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{|\ln\nu|}}\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}},\frac{1}{|\ln\nu|}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\right).

(4.173)

If
u
satisfies the orthogonality conditions (
4.80
) then one has
\langle\hat{u},\hat{u}\rangle_{*}\geq 0
and the equivalence

\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}^{2}\approx\langle u,u\rangle_{*}\approx\langle\hat{u},\hat{u}\rangle_{*}.

(4.174)

Above, the implicit constants in the
\lesssim
are independent of
a
and
\nu
.

Before we prove Proposition
4.12
, we show the following intermediate result.

Lemma 4.13
.

There exists
C_{0}(a)
that is positive for
a
close to
a_{\infty}
, such that for all
a
close to
a_{\infty}
and
\nu
small enough:

\displaystyle\langle\phi_{0},\phi_{0}\rangle_{*}=-C_{0}|\ln\nu|+O(1),

(4.175)

\displaystyle|\nu\partial_{\nu}\langle\phi_{0},\phi_{0}\rangle_{*}|+|\ln\nu|^{-1}|\partial_{a}\langle\phi_{0},\phi_{0}\rangle_{*}|\lesssim 1.

(4.176)

Proof.

Step 1
.
A preliminary identity
. Pick
\eta>0
small. We claim that

\langle\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{z-a}{\nu}),f\rangle_{*}=-2c_{*}\int_{|z-a|<\eta}f+O\left(\min\Big(\sqrt{|\ln\nu|}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\ ,\ \|\frac{f}{\sqrt{\nu+|z-a|}}\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\Big)\right).

(4.177)

To prove it, we decompose using (
4.77
)

\displaystyle\langle\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{z-a}{\nu}),f\rangle_{*}=\int\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{z-a}{\nu})f\omega_{\nu}dz-c_{*}\nu^{2}\int\chi^{*}f\Phi_{\chi^{*}\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})}dz

\displaystyle=c_{*}\int\chi_{a,\eta}f\left(\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{z-a}{\nu})\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}-\nu^{2}\Phi_{\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})}\right)+c_{*}\nu^{2}\int(\chi_{a,\eta}-\chi^{*})f\Phi_{\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int f\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{z-a}{\nu})\chi_{a,\eta}\left(\omega_{\nu}-\frac{c_{*}\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}\right)+c_{*}\nu^{2}\int\chi^{*}f\Phi_{(1-\chi^{*})\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int f\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{z-a}{\nu})\omega_{\nu}(1-\chi_{a,\eta})

(4.178)

We have
\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}-\nu^{2}\Phi_{\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})}=\mathscr{M}_{0}(\Lambda U)(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})=-2
by (
4.24
), hence

\int\chi_{a,\eta}f\left(\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{z-a}{\nu})\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}-\nu^{2}\Phi_{\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})}\right)=-2\int\chi_{a,\eta}f

(4.179)

We have that
\nu^{2}\Phi_{\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})}=(z-a).\nabla\Phi_{\frac{1}{\nu^{2}}U(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})}+\frac{1}{2\pi}\int U=O(\nu^{2})
for
\zeta_{*}<|z-a|\lesssim 1
so that

\nu^{2}\int(\chi_{a,\eta}-\chi^{*})f\Phi_{\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})}=O(\nu^{2}\|\chi^{*}f\|_{L^{1}})=O(\nu\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}})

(4.180)

where we used (
4.39
), (
4.3
) and (
4.61
). We have for
a
and
z
close to
a_{\infty}
by (
4.77
) that

\omega_{\nu}-\frac{c_{*}\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}=\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}\left(\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{2,\nu}}e^{-|z|^{4}/4}-c_{*}\right)=\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}O(\nu^{2}+|z-a|).

(4.181)

Hence by Cauchy-Schwarz, using
\|\chi_{a,\eta}\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{z-a}{\nu})(\nu^{2}+|z-a|)\|_{L^{2}_{\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}}}\lesssim\sqrt{|\ln\nu|}
:

\left|\int f\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{z-a}{\nu})\chi_{a,\eta}\left(\omega_{\nu}-\frac{c_{*}\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}\right)dz\right|\lesssim\sqrt{|\ln\nu|}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}.

(4.182)

By Cauchy-Schwarz again
\int f\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{z-a}{\nu})\chi_{a,\eta}\left(\omega_{\nu}-\frac{c_{*}\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}\right)dz=O(\|(\nu+|z-a|)^{-1/2}f\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}})
using
\|\sqrt{\nu+|z-a|}\chi_{a,\eta}\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{z-a}{\nu})(\nu^{2}+|z-a|)\|_{L^{2}_{\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}}}\lesssim 1
. Combining this inequality with (
4.182
) shows

\left|\int f\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{z-a}{\nu})\chi_{a,\eta}\left(\omega_{\nu}-\frac{c_{*}\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}\right)dz\right|\lesssim\min\left(\sqrt{|\ln\nu|}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\ ,\ \|\frac{f}{\sqrt{\nu+|z-a|}}\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\right).

(4.183)

We have that
\Phi_{(1-\chi_{*})\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})}
is bounded as
|(1-\chi_{*})\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})|\lesssim\langle z\rangle^{-4}
, so that

|\nu^{2}\int\chi^{*}f\Phi_{(1-\chi^{*})\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})}|\lesssim\nu^{2}\|\chi^{*}f\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim\nu\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}

(4.184)

by (
4.39
), (
4.3
) and (
4.61
). Using that
|\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{z-a}{\nu})|\lesssim|z-a|^{-4}\lesssim 1
for
|z-a|\gtrsim\eta
we have by Cauchy-Schwarz

\int f\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{z-a}{\nu})\omega_{\nu}(1-\chi_{a,\eta})=\mathcal{O}(\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}).

(4.185)

Injecting (
4.179
) and (
4.180
) using
\omega_{\nu}\approx 1
for
|z\pm a|\approx\eta
, and (
4.181
), (
4.183
), (
4.184
) and (
4.185
) in (
4.178
) shows (
4.177
).

Step 2
.
A secondary identity
. We claim that

\langle\frac{1}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{z-a}{\nu}),f\rangle_{*}=\nu\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}

(4.186)

The proof is similar to Step 1. We decompose using (
4.77
)

\displaystyle\langle\frac{1}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{z-a}{\nu}),f\rangle_{*}

\displaystyle=c_{*}\int\chi_{a,\eta}f\left(\frac{1}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{z-a}{\nu})\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}-\nu^{2}\Phi_{\frac{1}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})}\right)+c_{*}\nu^{2}\int(\chi_{a,\eta}-\chi^{*})f\Phi_{\frac{1}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int f\frac{1}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{z-a}{\nu})\chi_{a,\eta}\left(\omega_{\nu}-\frac{c_{*}\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}\right)+c_{*}\nu^{2}\int\chi^{*}f\Phi_{(1-\chi^{*})\frac{1}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int f\frac{1}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{z-a}{\nu})\omega_{\nu}(1-\chi_{a,\eta})

(4.187)

We have
\frac{1}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}-\nu^{2}\Phi_{\frac{1}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})}=\nu\mathscr{M}_{0}(\partial_{x_{1}}U)(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})=0
by (
4.24
), hence

\int\chi_{a,\eta}f\left(\frac{1}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{z-a}{\nu})\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}-\nu^{2}\Phi_{\frac{1}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})}\right)=0

(4.188)

The remaining terms in (
4.187
) are estimated exactly as (
4.180
) using
\nu^{2}\Phi_{\frac{1}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})}=\nu^{2}\partial_{x_{1}}\Phi_{U}(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})=O(\nu^{2})
for
\zeta_{*}<|z-a|\lesssim 1
, as (
4.185
) using
\|\chi_{a,\eta}\frac{1}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{z-a}{\nu})(\nu+|z-a|)\|_{L^{2}_{\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}}}\lesssim\nu
, as (
4.184
) using
\Phi_{(1-\chi_{*})\frac{1}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})}=O(\nu)
for
|z|\lesssim 1
and as (
4.182
) using
|\frac{1}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{z-a}{\nu})|\lesssim\nu^{2}
for
|z-a|>\eta
, yielding

\displaystyle|\nu^{2}\int(\chi_{a,\eta}-\chi^{*})f\Phi_{\frac{1}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})}|\lesssim\nu\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}

(4.189)

\displaystyle\left|\int f\frac{1}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{z-a}{\nu})\chi_{a,\eta}\left(\omega_{\nu}-\frac{c_{*}\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}\right)dz\right|\lesssim\nu\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}},

(4.190)

\displaystyle|\nu^{2}\int\chi^{*}f\Phi_{(1-\chi^{*})\frac{1}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})}|\lesssim\nu^{2}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}},

(4.191)

\displaystyle\left|\int f\frac{1}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{z-a}{\nu})\omega_{\nu}(1-\chi_{a,\eta})\right|\lesssim\nu^{2}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}.

(4.192)

Injecting (
4.188
), (
4.189
), (
4.190
), (
4.191
) and (
4.192
) in (
4.187
) shows the desired identity (
4.186
).

Step 3
.
Proof of the Lemma
. Injecting the expansions (
3.106
), (
3.108
) and (
3.222
) in (
3.4
) we decompose

\phi_{0}(z)=-\frac{1}{16}\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{z\pm a}{\nu})+\sum_{\pm}\frac{\pm L_{0}}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{z\pm a}{\nu})+\check{\phi}_{0}(z),

(4.193)

with
\check{\phi}_{0}=\check{\phi}_{0}^{1}+\check{\phi}_{0}^{2}
where

\check{\phi}_{0}^{1}(z)=\sum_{\pm}\frac{\cos(2\theta_{\pm})}{8|z\pm a|^{2}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\check{\phi}_{0}^{2}(z)=\sum_{\pm}O\left(\frac{1}{|z\pm a|^{2}}(\frac{1}{|\ln\nu|}+\frac{\nu^{2}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2}})+\frac{\langle z\rangle^{c}}{|z\pm a|}\right).

(4.194)

We then decompose:

\displaystyle\langle\phi_{0},\phi_{0}\rangle_{*}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{256}\langle\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{\cdot\pm a}{\nu}),\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{\cdot\pm a}{\nu})\rangle_{*}

\displaystyle+\langle-\frac{1}{8}\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{\cdot\pm a}{\nu})+\sum_{\pm}\frac{\pm L_{0}}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{z\pm a}{\nu}),\sum_{\pm}\frac{\pm L_{0}}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{z\pm a}{\nu})\rangle_{*}

\displaystyle+2\langle-\frac{1}{16}\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{\cdot\pm a}{\nu})\pm\frac{L_{0}}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{z\pm a}{\nu}),\check{\phi}_{0}\rangle_{*}+O(1)

(4.195)

where we used
|\langle\check{\phi}_{0},\check{\phi}_{0}\rangle_{*}|\lesssim\|\check{\phi}_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}^{2}\lesssim 1
which follows from (
4.79
) and (
4.194
). Note that the analogue estimate of (
4.177
) holds for
\Lambda U(\frac{z+a}{\nu})
and
\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{z+a}{\nu})
by symmetry. Using (
4.177
), (
4.186
), (
4.194
) and
|L_{0}|\lesssim 1
shows:

2\langle-\frac{1}{16}\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{\cdot\pm a}{\nu})\pm\frac{L_{0}}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{z\pm a}{\nu}),\check{\phi}_{0}\rangle_{*}=O(1).

(4.196)

Using (
4.186
) and
|L_{0}|\lesssim 1
shows

\displaystyle\left|\langle-\frac{1}{8}\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{\cdot\pm a}{\nu})+\sum_{\pm}\frac{\pm L_{i}}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{z\pm a}{\nu}),\sum_{\pm}\frac{\pm L_{i}}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{z\pm a}{\nu})\rangle_{*}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\nu\left\|\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{\cdot\pm a}{\nu})\right\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}+\sum_{\pm}\nu\left\|\frac{1}{\nu^{3}}\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{z\pm a}{\nu})\right\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\lesssim 1+\nu.

(4.197)

Next, combining (
4.178
), (
4.179
), (
4.180
), (
4.181
) and (
4.184
), with the fact that
\int\chi_{a,\eta}\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})dz=O(1)
and
\int(\chi^{*}-\chi_{a,\eta})\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})dz=O(1)
as
\int\Lambda U=0
, and
\|\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\lesssim\nu^{-1}
:

\langle\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{\cdot\pm a}{\nu}),\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{\cdot\pm a}{\nu})\rangle_{*}=\sum_{\pm}\int\frac{1}{\nu^{8}}|\Lambda U(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu})|^{2}\chi_{\pm a,\eta}\left(\omega_{\nu}-\frac{c_{*}\nu^{2}}{U_{i_{\pm},\nu}}\right)+O(1)

(4.198)

where
i_{+}=1
and
i_{-}=2
. We perform a Taylor expansion and get

\displaystyle\omega_{\nu}-c_{*}\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}\left(\frac{(\nu^{2}+|z+a|^{2})^{2}}{8}e^{-|z|^{2}/4}-\frac{|2a|^{4}}{8}e^{-|2a|^{2}/4}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}e^{-|a|^{2}/4}\left(\frac{(|z+a|^{2})^{2}}{8}e^{(|a|^{2}-|z|^{2})/4}-\frac{|2a|^{4}}{8}+O(\nu^{2})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}\left(c_{1}(1,0).(z-a)+c_{2}|z-a|^{2}+c_{3}|(1,0).(z-a)|^{2}+O(|z-a|^{3})\right)

with
e^{|a|^{2}/4}c_{1}=32|a|^{3}-8|a|^{5}
,
e^{|a|^{2}/4}c_{2}=8|a|^{2}-4|a|^{4}
and
e^{|a|^{2}/4}c_{3}=16|a|^{2}-2|a|^{6}-16|a|^{4}
. Therefore, after changing variables
z=a+\nu y
:

\int\frac{1}{\nu^{8}}|\Lambda U(\frac{z-a}{\nu})|^{2}\chi_{a,\eta}\left(\omega_{\nu}-\frac{c_{*}\nu^{2}}{U_{1,\nu}}\right)=\int\chi(\frac{\nu y}{\eta})\frac{(\Lambda U)^{2}}{U}(c_{2}|y|^{2}+c_{3}y_{1}^{2})dy+O(1).

(4.199)

where we used that both
\Lambda U
and
U
are radial so that the first term cancels. Using that
\frac{(\Lambda U)^{2}}{U^{2}}=\frac{32}{|y|^{4}}+O(|y|^{-6})
as
|y|\to\infty
we compute

\int\chi(\frac{\nu y}{\eta})\frac{(\Lambda U)^{2}}{U}(c_{2}|y|^{2}+c_{3}y_{1}^{2})dy=(64\pi c_{2}+32\pi c_{3})|\ln\nu|+O(1).

(4.200)

Injecting (
4.199
) and (
4.200
) in (
4.198
) shows

\langle\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{\cdot\pm a}{\nu}),\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{\cdot\pm a}{\nu})\rangle_{*}=(128\pi c_{2}+64\pi c_{3})|\ln\nu|+O(1).

(4.201)

Finally, injecting (
4.196
), (
4.197
) and (
4.201
) in (
4.195
) shows the desired identity (
4.175
). The sign of the constants follows from the explicit computations
c_{2}(a_{\infty})=-32e^{-1}
and
c_{3}(a_{\infty})=-320e^{-1}
. The proof of (
4.176
) follows from the exact same computations, by differentiating all expressions with respect to
\nu
and
a
; we omit it.

∎

Proof of Proposition
4.12
.

By the orthogonality (
4.169
) the projection is given by

a_{0}=\frac{\langle u,\phi_{0}\rangle_{*}}{\langle\phi_{0},\phi_{0}\rangle_{*}},

(4.202)

the denominator being nonzero by (
4.175
).

Proof of (
4.170
)
. We have by the orthogonality (
4.169
)

\langle u,u\rangle_{*}=\langle\hat{u},\hat{u}\rangle_{*}+a_{0}^{2}\langle\phi_{0},\phi_{0}\rangle_{*}.

(4.203)

This implies (
4.170
) since
\langle\phi_{0},\phi_{0}\rangle_{*}<0
by (
4.175
).

Proof of (
4.174
)
. Injecting (
4.202
) in (
4.203
) gives

\langle\hat{u},\hat{u}\rangle_{*}=\langle u,u\rangle_{*}-\frac{\langle u,\phi_{0}\rangle_{*}^{2}}{\langle\phi_{0},\phi_{0}\rangle_{*}}.

(4.204)

We already know
|\langle u,u\rangle_{*}|\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}^{2}
from (
4.79
) and
|\langle\phi_{0},\phi_{0}\rangle_{*}|\approx|\ln\nu|
from (
4.175
) so it suffices to prove

|\langle u,\phi_{0}\rangle_{*}|\lesssim\min(\sqrt{|\ln\nu|}\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}},\|u\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}).

(4.205)

Using the decomposition (
4.193
) we have
\langle u,\phi_{0}\rangle_{*}=\langle u,\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{z\pm a}{\nu})+\check{\phi}_{0}\rangle_{*}
. We have by (
4.177
), since
\int_{|z\pm a|<\eta}udz=0
, that
\langle u,\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\Lambda U(\frac{z\pm a}{\nu})\rangle_{*}=O(\min(\sqrt{|\ln\nu|}\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}},\|u\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}))
. We have by (
4.79
), since
\|\check{\phi}_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\lesssim 1
by (
4.194
), that
\langle u,\check{\phi}_{0}\rangle_{*}=O(\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}})
. This shows (
4.205
) as desired.

Proof of (
4.172
)
. We differentiate the first term in (
4.204
):

\displaystyle\nu\partial_{\nu}\langle u,u\rangle_{*}=\int u^{2}\nu\partial_{\nu}\omega_{\nu}dz-2c_{*}\nu^{2}\int\chi^{*}u\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}dz,

\displaystyle\nu\partial_{a}\langle u,u\rangle_{*}=\int u^{2}\nu\partial_{a}\omega_{\nu}dz-2\partial_{a}(c_{*})\nu^{3}\int\chi^{*}u\Phi_{\chi^{*}u}dz.

Using
|\nu\partial_{\nu}\omega_{\nu}|+|\nu\partial_{a}\omega_{\nu}|\lesssim\omega_{\nu}
from (
4.60
),
\partial_{a}c_{*}=O(1)
,(
4.33
), (
4.61
) and (
4.3
) we get:

|\nu\partial_{\nu}\langle u,u\rangle_{*}|+|\nu\partial_{a}\langle u,u\rangle_{*}|\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}^{2}.

The derivative with respect to
a
and
\nu
of the second term in (
4.204
) are estimated similarly, injecting (
4.176
) and performing the same estimates as in the proof of (
4.174
).

Proof of (
4.173
)
. It follows from injecting (
4.205
) and (
4.175
) in (
4.202
).

Proof of (
4.174
).
It follows from (
4.170
), (
4.174
) and the coercivity (
4.81
).

Proposition 4.14

(Coercivity of the operator for the second decomposition)
.

There exists
\delta>0
such that for
\eta>0
small enough, for
\nu
close to
0
and
|a-a_{\infty}|\lesssim\nu
, if
u
is a Schwartz function satisfying the orthogonality conditions (
4.13
), then

-\left\langle\widehat{\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u},\hat{u}\right\rangle_{*}\geq\delta\int|\nabla u|^{2}\omega_{\nu}dz+\delta\int\left(\frac{1}{(\nu+|z-a|)^{2}})+\frac{1}{(\nu+|z+a|)^{2}})+\langle z\rangle^{2}\right)u^{2}\omega_{\nu}dz.

(4.206)

Proof.

Using successively the orthogonality (
4.169
), the decomposition
u=\hat{u}+a_{0}\phi_{0}
, the almost symmetry identity (
4.94
) and the almost eigenfunction identity (
3.13
) we have

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u},\hat{u}\rangle_{*}

\displaystyle=\langle\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u,\hat{u}\rangle_{*}

(4.207)

\displaystyle=\langle\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u,u\rangle_{*}-a_{0}\langle\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u,\phi_{0}\rangle_{*}

\displaystyle=\langle\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u,u\rangle_{*}-a_{0}(\langle u,\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}\phi_{0}\rangle_{*}+\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}(u,\phi_{0})-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}(\phi_{0},u))

\displaystyle=\langle\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u,u\rangle_{*}-a_{0}\left(2\beta\Big(1+\frac{\gamma_{0}}{\ln\nu}\Big)\langle u,\phi_{0}\rangle_{*}+\langle u,R_{0}\rangle_{*}+\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}(u,\phi_{0})-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}(\phi_{0},u)\right)

By (
4.173
) and (
4.175
) the second term in the right-hand side above is

|a_{0}2\beta\Big(1+\frac{\gamma_{0}}{\ln\nu}\Big)\langle\phi_{0},\phi_{0}\rangle_{*}|\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}.

(4.208)

We have for the third term

\langle u,R_{0}\rangle_{*}\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\|R_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\lesssim\frac{1}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}

(4.209)

by (
4.79
), (
3.14
) and a direct estimate. We have by (
4.93
) and (
3.6
) for the fourth and fifth terms in (
4.207
)

\displaystyle|\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}(u,\phi_{0})|+|\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}(\phi_{0},u)|

\displaystyle\lesssim(\nu+|a-a_{\infty}|)\sqrt{|\ln\nu|}\|u\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\|\phi_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{|\ln\nu|}(1+\nu^{-1}|a-a_{\infty}|)\|u\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}.

(4.210)

Injecting (
4.173
), (
4.208
), (
4.209
) and (
4.210
) in (
4.207
) shows:

\langle\widehat{\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u},\hat{u}\rangle_{*}=\langle\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}u,u\rangle_{*}+O\left(\frac{1+\nu^{-1}|a-a_{\infty}|}{\sqrt{|\ln\nu|}}\|u\|_{H^{1}_{\omega_{\nu}}}^{2}\right)

The desired inequality (
4.206
) then follows from the above identity and Proposition
4.11
.

∎

5. Corrected multisoliton in parabolic variables

In this section we consider the evolution term of the Keller-Segel equations in self-similar parabolic variables

\Delta w-\nabla.(w\nabla\Phi_{w})-\beta\Lambda w.

(5.1)

By the scaling renormalization
w(z)=2\beta\tilde{w}(\sqrt{2\beta}z)
it will suffice to consider the case
\beta=\frac{1}{2}
:

\Delta w-\nabla.(w\nabla\Phi_{w})-\frac{1}{2}\Lambda w.

(5.2)

We apply the terminology ”soliton” in analogy with wave equations, and call the sum of two stationary states a multisoliton. We look for a solution that is to leading order a multisoliton. We aim at relating the evolution term (
5.1
) it generates to the eigenfunctions of Proposition
3.1
and to the modulation directions (that is to say, the parameter derivatives) of the multisoliton.

5.1. Derivation of the corrected stationary equation

5.1.1. The equilibrium position
\overline{a}
of the stationary states

We first look for an approximate stationary solution, i.e. that makes (
5.2
) vanish to leading order, of the form

U_{1+2,\nu}=U_{1,\nu}+U_{2,\nu},

where

U_{1,\nu}(z)=\frac{1}{\nu^{2}}U(\frac{z-a}{\nu})\quad\mbox{and}\quad U_{2,\nu}(z)=\frac{1}{\nu^{2}}U(\frac{z+a}{\nu})

for
\nu>0
small. We compute the evolution term (
5.2
)

\displaystyle\Delta U_{1+2,\nu}-\nabla.(U_{1+2,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}})-\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U_{1+2,\nu}

\displaystyle=-\left(\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}+\frac{z}{2}\right).\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\left(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1,\nu}}+\frac{z}{2}\right).\nabla U_{2,\nu}

\displaystyle\qquad-U_{1+2,\nu}+2U_{1,\nu}U_{2,\nu}

The most singular term being on the first line, the above is minimised when the vector field vanish at the positions of the stationary states, i.e. when

\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}(a)+\frac{a}{2}=0.

Since
\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}(z)=-\frac{4(z+a)}{\nu^{2}+|z+a|^{2}}
the above leads in the limit
\nu\to 0
to solving for
a_{\infty}
in the equation
-\frac{2a_{\infty}}{|a_{\infty}|^{2}}+\frac{a_{\infty}}{2}=0
. The solution is
a_{\infty}=(2,0)
given our symmetry assumptions. For equation (
5.1
) the equilibrium position is thus

a_{\infty}=\left(\sqrt{\frac{2}{\beta}},0\right).

(5.3)

5.1.2. Projection of the error on the eigenfunctions and the modulation directions

We now aim at a refined decomposition of the evolution term (
5.1
) generated by
U_{1+2,\nu}
as an approximate solution to (
5.1
), when the stationnary states are near their equilibrium position (
5.3
). To do so, we expand

\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}(z)=-\frac{8a}{\nu^{2}+4|a|^{2}}+(\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}(z)-\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}(a)),

which leads to

\displaystyle\Delta U_{1+2,\nu}-\nabla.(U_{1+2,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}})-\beta\Lambda U_{1+2,\nu}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=-\frac{\beta}{\nu^{2}}\Lambda U\left(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu}\right)-\frac{\beta}{\nu^{2}}\Lambda U\left(\frac{\cdot+a}{\nu}\right)+\left(\frac{8a}{\nu^{2}+4|a|^{2}}-\beta a\right).\left(\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\nabla U_{2,\nu}\right)

(5.4)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad-\left(\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}(z)-\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}(a)\right).\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\left(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1,\nu}}(z)-\nabla\Phi_{U_{1,\nu}}(-a)\right).\nabla U_{2,\nu}+2U_{1,\nu}U_{2,\nu}.

Our aim is to link the right-hand side, which is the time derivative term in the Keller-Segel equation in parabolic variables generated by the multisoliton, to the eigenfunctions constructed in Proposition
3.1
, and to the modulation directions (directional derivatives) of the multisoliton. We notice from the expansion of the eigenfunctions that
-\frac{\beta}{\nu^{2}}\Lambda U\left(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu}\right)-\frac{\beta}{\nu^{2}}\Lambda U\left(\frac{\cdot+a}{\nu}\right)
is the leading order term in the expansion of
16\beta\nu^{2}\phi_{0}
near
\pm a
. The second term
\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\nabla U_{2,\nu}
is the directional derivative of the multisoliton with respect to the position
a
of the stationary states. The effect of second line will be of lower order.

Therefore, the main problem in this section will be to construct a small enough function
\Xi
such that the corrected multisoliton

\mathfrak{U}=U_{1+2,\nu}+\Xi,

(5.5)

satisfies the corrected stationary equation

\displaystyle E_{0}=

\displaystyle\Delta\mathfrak{U}-\nabla.(\mathfrak{U}\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}+\chi^{*}\Xi})-\beta\Lambda\mathfrak{U}

(5.6)

\displaystyle=8\lambda_{0}\nu^{2}\phi_{0}+\mu^{\prime}.\left(\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\nabla U_{2,\nu}\right)+\Psi_{0}

with
\mu=\mu[a,\nu]\in\mathbb{R}
and
\mu^{\prime}=\mu^{\prime}[a,\nu]\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
some parameters to be found and
\Psi_{0}
suitably small, where
\chi^{*}
is defined in (
2.1
). Since
\Xi
grows at infinity that makes
\Phi_{\Xi}
is not well-defined, the localization
\Phi_{\chi^{*}\Xi}
is technically necessary. This localization will nonetheless be harmless since the contribution of the far away zone
|z|\gg 1
will be of lower order.

5.2. The corrected multisoliton

Proposition 5.1

(Improved stationary solution in parabolic variables)
.

There exists a smooth function
\Xi[\nu,a]
defined for all
\nu
small enough and
|a-\bar{a}|\lesssim\frac{\nu}{|\ln\nu|}
that satisfies the following for

\mu^{\prime}=\frac{8a}{\nu^{2}+4|a|^{2}}-\beta a+\tilde{\mu}^{\prime},

(5.7)

where
\tilde{\mu}^{\prime}(a)=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{2})
.

i)

(Pointwise estimates)
For
z\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
j\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle|\nabla^{k}\Xi(z)|+|\nabla^{j}\nu\partial_{\nu}\Xi(z)|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{\nu^{2}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2+k}}\langle z\rangle^{C_{k}},

(5.8)

\displaystyle\quad|\nabla^{k}\partial_{a}\Xi(z)|\leq\sum_{\pm}\frac{\nu^{2}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{3+k}}\langle z\rangle^{C_{k}},

(5.9)

and

\sum_{\pm}\Big|\int_{|z\pm a|\leq\zeta_{*}}\nu\partial_{\nu}\Xi(z)dz\Big|\lesssim\nu^{2}.

(5.10)

ii)

(Estimate of the Poisson field)

|\nabla^{k}\Phi_{\chi^{*}\Xi}|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{\nu^{2}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{k}}\langle z\rangle^{2}\langle\ln\langle z\rangle\rangle\qquad\mbox{for }k\geq 1,

(5.11)

and in particular

|\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}\Xi}(a)|\lesssim\nu.

(5.12)

iii)

(Generated error)
The function
\mathfrak{U}
solves the corrected stationary equation (
5.6
) with

\displaystyle|\Psi_{0}(z)|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{\langle z\rangle^{C}}{|\log\nu|^{2}(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2}},

(5.13)

so that we have in particular

\displaystyle\|\Psi_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\lesssim\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\log\nu|^{2}},

(5.14)

\displaystyle\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim 1}\Psi_{0}\lesssim\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\log\nu|}.

(5.15)

iv)

(Parameter derivative)
The function
\mathfrak{U}
satisfies the parameter derivative identity

\displaystyle\nu\partial_{\nu}\mathfrak{U}

\displaystyle=16\phi_{0}-\nu^{2}(L_{0},0).(\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\nabla U_{2,\nu})+\tilde{\mathfrak{U}},

(5.16)

\displaystyle|\nabla^{k}\tilde{\mathfrak{U}}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{\nu^{2}}{(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{2+k}}\left(\frac{\nu^{\delta_{0}}}{(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{\delta_{0}}}+\frac{1}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\right).

(5.17)

We postpone the proof of Proposition
5.1
to the end of this section.

5.3. The first ansatz for the leading order correction

Injecting the decomposition (
5.5
) and (
5.4
) in (
5.6
) we have

\displaystyle\Psi_{0}

\displaystyle=\Delta\mathfrak{U}-\nabla.(\mathfrak{U}\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}+\chi^{*}\Xi})-\beta\Lambda\mathfrak{U}-8\lambda_{0}\nu^{2}\phi_{0}-\mu^{\prime}.\left(\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\nabla U_{2,\nu}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\beta}{\nu^{2}}\Lambda U\left(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu}\right)-\frac{\beta}{\nu^{2}}\Lambda U\left(\frac{\cdot+a}{\nu}\right)+\left(\frac{8a}{\nu^{2}+4|a|^{2}}-\beta a\right).\left(\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\nabla U_{2,\nu}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}(z)-\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}(a)\right).\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\left(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1,\nu}}(z)-\nabla\Phi_{U_{1,\nu}}(-a)\right).\nabla U_{2,\nu}+2U_{1,\nu}U_{2,\nu}

\displaystyle\quad+\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}(\Xi)-\nabla.(\Xi\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}\Xi})-\mu\nu^{2}\phi_{0}-\mu^{\prime}.\left(\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\nabla U_{2,\nu}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\beta}{\nu^{2}}\Lambda U\left(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu}\right)-\frac{\beta}{\nu^{2}}\Lambda U\left(\frac{\cdot+a}{\nu}\right)-8\lambda_{0}\nu^{2}\phi_{0}+\left(\frac{8a}{\nu^{2}+4|a|^{2}}-\beta a-\mu^{\prime}\right).\left(\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\nabla U_{2,\nu}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}(z)-\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}(a)\right).\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\left(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1,\nu}}(z)-\nabla\Phi_{U_{1,\nu}}(-a)\right).\nabla U_{2,\nu}+2U_{1,\nu}U_{2,\nu}

\displaystyle\quad+\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}(\Xi)-\nabla.(\Xi\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}\Xi})

(5.18)

Our first ansatz is to consider the decomposition

\displaystyle\Xi=8\left[\nu^{2}\phi_{0}+(\frac{1}{16}\Lambda U-L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu}+(\frac{1}{16}\Lambda U+L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}\right]+\tilde{\Xi},

(5.19)

\displaystyle\Phi_{\chi^{*}\Xi}=8\left[\nu^{2}\Phi_{\chi^{*}\phi_{0}}+\Phi_{(\frac{1}{16}\Lambda U-L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu}}+\Phi_{(\frac{1}{16}\Lambda U+L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}}\right]+\chi^{*}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}+\bar{\mathfrak{V}}

(5.20)

where for
f:\mathbb{R}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
we introduce the notation

f_{1,\nu}=\frac{1}{\nu^{2}}f\left(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu}\right)\quad\mbox{and}\quad f_{2,\nu}=\frac{1}{\nu^{2}}f\left(\frac{\cdot+a}{\nu}\right)

and where
-\Delta\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}=\tilde{\Xi}
. Recalling that
\Phi_{f}
is the unique solution modulo constant to
-\Delta\Phi_{f}=f
that grows at most logarithmically, we choose in view of the identity (
5.20
)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{V}}=\bar{\mathfrak{V}}_{1}+\bar{\mathfrak{V}}_{2},

(5.21)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{V}_{1}}=\Phi_{\left((\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U-8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu}+(\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U+8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}\right)(\chi^{*}-1)},\qquad\bar{\mathfrak{V}}_{2}=2\Phi_{\nabla\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}.\nabla\chi^{*}}+\Phi_{\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}\Delta\chi^{*}}.

(5.22)

We then have

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}(\Xi)

\displaystyle=8\nu^{2}\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}\phi_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\mathscr{L}^{z}((\Lambda U)_{1,\nu})-\nu 8L_{0}\mathscr{L}^{z}((\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu})+\frac{1}{2}\mathscr{L}^{z}((\Lambda U)_{2,\nu})+8\nu L_{0}\mathscr{L}^{z}((\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu})

\displaystyle\quad+\mathscr{L}^{z}(\tilde{\Xi},\tilde{\mathfrak{V}})-\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}}(a).(\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\nabla U_{2,\nu})

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}}-\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}}(a)+\nabla((\chi^{*}-1)\tilde{\mathcal{V}})\right).\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\left(\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}}-\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}}(-a)+\nabla((\chi^{*}-1)\tilde{\mathcal{V}})\right).\nabla U_{2,\nu}.

We compute for
j=1,2
, using the cancellations
\Delta(\Lambda U)_{j,\nu}-\nabla.((\Lambda U)_{j,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{U_{j,\nu}})-\nabla.(U_{j,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{(\Lambda U)_{j,\nu}})=0
and
\Delta(\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{j,\nu}-\nabla.((\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{j,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{U_{j,\nu}})-\nabla.(U_{j,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{(\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{j,\nu}})=0
, that

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}^{z}\left((\Lambda U)_{1,\nu}\right)=-\nabla U_{2,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{(\Lambda U)_{1,\nu}}+2U_{2,\nu}(\Lambda U)_{1,\nu}-\nabla(\Lambda U)_{1,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}-\beta(\Lambda^{2}U)_{1,\nu}-\beta a.\nabla(\Lambda U)_{1,\nu},

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}^{z}\left((\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu}\right)=-\nabla U_{2,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{(\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu}}+2U_{2,\nu}(\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu}-\nabla(\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}-\beta(\Lambda\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu}-\beta a.\nabla(\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu},

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}^{z}\left((\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}\right)=-\nabla U_{1,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}}+2U_{1,\nu}(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}-\nabla(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{U_{1,\nu}}-\beta(\Lambda^{2}U)_{2,\nu}+\beta a.\nabla(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu},

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}^{z}\left((\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}\right)=-\nabla U_{1,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{(\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}}+2U_{1,\nu}(\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}-\nabla(\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{U_{1,\nu}}-\beta(\Lambda\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}+\beta a.\nabla(\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu},

Injecting (
3.1
) and the above identities in (
5.18
), the error becomes

\displaystyle\Psi_{0}

\displaystyle=-\beta(\Lambda U)_{1,\nu}-\beta(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}-8\lambda_{0}\nu^{2}\phi_{0}+\left(\frac{8a}{\nu^{2}+4|a|^{2}}-\beta a-\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}}(a)-\mu^{\prime}\right).\left(\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\nabla U_{2,\nu}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}(z)-\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}(a)\right).\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\left(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1,\nu}}(z)-\nabla\Phi_{U_{1,\nu}}(-a)\right).\nabla U_{2,\nu}+2U_{1,\nu}U_{2,\nu}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\left(-\nabla U_{2,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{(\Lambda U)_{1,\nu}}+2U_{2,\nu}(\Lambda U)_{1,\nu}-\nabla(\Lambda U)_{1,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}-\beta(\Lambda^{2}U)_{1,\nu}-\beta a.\nabla(\Lambda U)_{1,\nu}\right),

\displaystyle\quad-8L_{0}\nu\left(-\nabla U_{2,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{(\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu}}+2U_{2,\nu}(\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu}-\nabla(\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}-\beta(\Lambda\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu}-\beta a.\nabla(\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\left(-\nabla U_{1,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}}+2U_{1,\nu}(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}-\nabla(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{U_{1,\nu}}-\beta(\Lambda^{2}U)_{2,\nu}+\beta a.\nabla(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+8\nu L_{0}\left(-\nabla U_{1,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{(\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}}+2U_{1,\nu}(\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}-\nabla(\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{U_{1,\nu}}-\beta(\Lambda\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}+\beta a.\nabla(\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}}-\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}}(a)+\nabla((\chi^{*}-1)\tilde{\mathcal{V}})\right).\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\left(\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}}-\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}}(-a)+\nabla((\chi^{*}-1)\tilde{\mathcal{V}})\right).\nabla U_{2,\nu}

\displaystyle\quad+8\lambda_{0}\nu^{2}\phi_{0}+8\nu^{2}R_{0}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathscr{L}^{z}(\tilde{\Xi},\tilde{\mathfrak{V}})-\nabla.(\Xi\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}\Xi})

\displaystyle=-\beta[\Lambda U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda^{2}U-8\nu L_{0}\Lambda\partial_{x_{1}}U]_{1,\nu}-\beta[\Lambda U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda^{2}U+8L_{0}\nu\Lambda\partial_{x_{1}}U]_{2,\nu}

(5.23)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{8a}{\nu^{2}+4|a|^{2}}-\beta a-\nabla\Phi_{(\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U+8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}}(a)-\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}}(a)-\mu^{\prime}\right).\left(\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\nabla U_{2,\nu}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}(z)-\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}(a)\right).\nabla(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U-8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu}-\left(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1,\nu}}(z)-\nabla\Phi_{U_{1,\nu}}(-a)\right).\nabla(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U+8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(-\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}(a)-\beta a\right).\nabla(\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U-8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}})_{1,\nu}+\left(\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}(a)+\beta a\right).\nabla(\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U+8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}+2U_{1,\nu}U_{2,\nu}

\displaystyle\quad-\nabla U_{2,\nu}.(\nabla\Phi_{(\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U-\nu 8L_{0}\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu}}-\nabla\Phi_{(\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U+8\nu L_{0}\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu}}(-a))+2U_{2,\nu}(\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U-8\nu L_{0}\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu}

\displaystyle\quad-\nabla U_{1,\nu}.(\nabla\Phi_{(\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U+8\nu L_{0}\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}}-\nabla\Phi_{(\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U+8\nu L_{0}\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}}(a))+2U_{1,\nu}(\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U+8\nu L_{0}\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}}-\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}}(a)+\nabla((\chi^{*}-1)\tilde{\mathcal{V}})\right).\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\left(\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}}-\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}}(-a)+\nabla((\chi^{*}-1)\tilde{\mathcal{V}})\right).\nabla U_{2,\nu}

\displaystyle\quad+8\nu^{2}R_{0}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathscr{L}^{z}(\tilde{\Xi},\tilde{\mathfrak{V}})-\nabla.(\Xi\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}\Xi})

All terms in the right-hand side above (except at this stage those involving
\tilde{\Xi}
,
\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}
and
\bar{\mathfrak{V}}
that have not been yet defined) will be shown to be suitably small away from the stationary states.

5.4. The second ansatz for higher order corrections

5.4.1. Decomposition of the error terms generated by the first ansatz

There remains to introduce further corrections that are localized near the stationary states. To do so we perform Taylor expansions to get the leading order terms in the above expression (
5.23
). Thanks to Lemma
3.3
we have

\displaystyle\left(\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}(z)-\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}(a)\right).\nabla(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U-8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{3}\alpha^{\frac{i+1}{2}}\nu^{i-3}(G_{i,+}.\nabla(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U-8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U))(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})-\frac{1}{\nu}(\gamma_{1},0).\nabla(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U-8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U))(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{\nu^{3}}[\nabla\Phi_{2,\nu}(a)+\alpha a-\nu^{2}(\gamma_{1},0)].\nabla(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U-8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})+\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}(\tilde{G}_{+}.\nabla(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U-8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu}).

(5.24)

where
\gamma_{1}=\frac{\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}}{4\sqrt{2}}
. Next,

\frac{1}{2}(-\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}(a)-\beta a).\nabla(\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U-8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu}=\frac{1}{\nu^{2}}(\gamma_{2},0).\nabla\Lambda U(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})-\frac{1}{\nu}(16L_{0}\gamma_{2},0).\nabla\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})

(5.25)

where
(\gamma_{2},0)=-\frac{\beta a+\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}(a)}{2\nu}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
satisfies
|\gamma_{2}|\lesssim 1
since
|a-a_{\infty}|\lesssim\nu
. Then,

\displaystyle 2U_{1,\nu}U_{2,\nu}+U_{2,\nu}(\Lambda U)_{1,\nu}+U_{1,\nu}(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{3}\Lambda U(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})+\gamma_{3}\Lambda U(\frac{\cdot+a}{\nu})

(5.26)

\displaystyle\quad+2U_{1,\nu}U_{2,\nu}+U_{2,\nu}(\Lambda U_{1,\nu}+U_{1,\nu}(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}-U_{2,\nu}(a)(\Lambda U)_{1,\nu}-U_{1,\nu}(-a)(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}

where
\gamma_{3}=\frac{U_{2,\nu}(a)}{\nu^{2}}
satisfies
|\gamma_{3}|\lesssim 1
and the last line satisfies

\displaystyle 2U_{1,\nu}U_{2,\nu}+U_{2,\nu}(\Lambda U_{1,\nu}+U_{1,\nu}(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}-U_{2,\nu}(a)(\Lambda U)_{1,\nu}-U_{1,\nu}(-a)(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}

(5.27)

\displaystyle=(2U+\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}(a)U_{1,\nu}+(2U+\Lambda U)_{1,\nu}(-a)U_{2,\nu}

\displaystyle\quad+2(U_{1,\nu}-U_{1,\nu}(-a))(U_{2,\nu}-U_{2,\nu}(a))+(U_{2,\nu}-U_{2,\nu}(a))((\Lambda U)_{1,\nu}-(\Lambda U)_{1,\nu}(-a))

\displaystyle\quad+(U_{1,\nu}-U_{1,\nu}(-a))((\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}-(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}(a))

\displaystyle\quad-2U_{1,\nu}(-a)U_{2,\nu}(a)-U_{2,\nu}(a)(\Lambda U)_{1,\nu}(-a)-U_{1,\nu}(-a)(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}(a).

All terms in (
5.27
) will be shown to be suitably small later on. We expand

\displaystyle-\nabla U_{1,\nu}.(\nabla\Phi_{(\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U+8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}}(z)-\nabla\Phi_{(\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U+8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}}(a))

(5.28)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha^{2}}{16}(\nabla U.(-3y_{1},y_{2}))(\frac{z-a}{\nu})

\displaystyle\quad-\nabla U_{1,\nu}.\Bigg(\nabla\Phi_{(\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U+8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}}(z)-\frac{1}{2}\nabla\Phi_{(\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U+8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}}(a)+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{16}\nu^{2}(-3(z_{1}-|a|),z_{2})\Bigg)

We compute for future use that

\nabla\Phi_{\Lambda U}(y)=\nabla(y.\nabla\Phi_{U})(y)=-\frac{8y}{(1+|y|^{2})^{2}}.

This implies

\partial_{y_{1}}^{2}\Phi_{\Lambda U}(y)=\frac{32y_{1}^{2}-8-8|y|^{2}}{(1+|y|^{2})^{3}},\quad\partial_{y_{2}}^{2}\Phi_{\Lambda U}(y)=\frac{32y_{2}^{2}-8-8|y|^{2}}{(1+|y|^{2})^{3}},\quad\partial_{y_{1}}\partial_{y_{2}}\Phi_{\Lambda U}(y)=\frac{32y_{1}y_{2}}{(1+|y|^{2})^{3}}.

Hence

\partial_{z_{1}}^{2}\Phi_{(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}}(a)=\nu^{2}\frac{96|a|^{2}-8\nu^{2}}{(4|a|^{2}+\nu^{2})^{3}},\quad\partial_{z_{2}}^{2}\Phi_{(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}}(a)=\nu^{2}\frac{-32|a|^{2}-8\nu^{2}}{(4|a|^{2}+\nu^{2})^{3}},\quad\partial_{z_{1}}\partial_{z_{2}}\Phi_{(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}}(a)=0.

Therefore, recalling that
\alpha=\frac{2}{|a|^{2}}
,

(\nabla^{2}\Phi_{(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}}(a))(z-a)=\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}\nu^{2}(3(z_{1}-|a|),-z_{2})+O(\nu^{4}|z-a|).

Hence by a Taylor expansion, we have the following estimate

\left|\nabla\Phi_{(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}}(z)-\nabla\Phi_{(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}}(a)+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}\nu^{2}(-3(z_{1}-|a|),z_{2})\right|\lesssim\frac{\nu^{2}(\nu+|z-a|)^{2}}{(\nu+|z+a|)^{3}}\langle z\rangle^{2}.

Using
\Phi_{(\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}}(z)=\partial_{x_{1}}\Phi(\frac{z+a}{\nu})
and
|\nabla^{k}\partial_{x_{1}}\Phi_{U}(y)|\lesssim\langle y\rangle^{-1-k}
we have by Taylor expansion

|\nabla\Phi_{(\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}}(z)-\nabla\Phi_{(\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}}(a)|\lesssim\frac{\nu^{2}(\nu+|z-a|)}{(\nu+|z+a|)^{3}}\langle z\rangle^{2}.

Combining, we have the following estimate for the remainder term appearing in (
5.28
),

\displaystyle\left|\nabla\Phi_{(\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U+8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}}(z)-\nabla\Phi_{(\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U+8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}}(a)+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{16}\nu^{2}(-3(z_{1}-|a|),z_{2})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\nu^{2}(\nu+|z-a|)^{2}}{(\nu+|z+a|)^{3}}\langle z\rangle^{2}

(5.29)

which will be used to show that it is suitably small everywhere later on. Next, we have
\nabla^{2}\bar{V}_{1}(a)(z-a)=\partial_{z_{1}}^{2}\bar{V}_{1}(a)(z_{1}-|a|,-z_{2})
because
\bar{V}_{1}
is even in
z_{2}
and because it satisfies
-\Delta\bar{V}_{1}(a)=0
. We introduce
\gamma_{4}=\nu^{-2}\nabla^{2}\bar{V}_{1}(a)
and expand accordingly

\nabla\bar{V}_{1}(z)=\nabla\bar{V}_{1}(a)+\gamma_{4}\nu^{2}(z_{1}-|a|,-z_{2})+\left(\nabla\bar{V}_{1}(z)-\nabla\bar{V}_{1}(a)-\nabla^{2}\bar{V}_{1}(a)(z-a)\right).

(5.30)

By the formula (
5.22
) and elliptic regularity, using
|\nabla^{k}U(y)|\lesssim\langle y\rangle^{-4-k}
and that
\chi^{*}-1
is supported for
|z|\geq 10
, we have
|\gamma_{4}|\lesssim 1
and

|\nabla\bar{V}_{1}(z)-\nabla\bar{V}_{1}(a)-\nabla^{2}\bar{V}_{1}(a)(z-a)|\lesssim\nu^{2}|z-a|^{2}.

(5.31)

Injecting (
5.24
), (
5.25
), (
5.26
), (
5.28
) and (
5.30
) in (
5.23
) gives the decomposition

\displaystyle\Psi_{0}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{2}\sum_{\pm}\nu^{i-2}F_{i+2,\pm}\left(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu}\right)+\mathscr{L}^{z}(\tilde{\Xi},\tilde{\mathfrak{V}})+\bar{\Psi}_{0}^{(0)}

(5.32)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(\frac{8a}{\nu^{2}+4|a|^{2}}-\beta a-\nabla\Phi_{(\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U+8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}}(a)-\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}}(a)-\mu^{\prime}\right).\left(\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\nabla U_{2,\nu}\right)-\nabla.(\Xi\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}\Xi})

where

\displaystyle F_{2,\pm}=-\beta(\Lambda U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda^{2}U)-\alpha G_{1,+}.\nabla(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U)\pm(\gamma_{2},0).\nabla\Lambda U,

(5.33)

\displaystyle F_{3,\pm}=\mp(\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}G_{2,+}-(\gamma_{1},0)).\nabla(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U)\pm 8L_{0}\beta\Lambda\partial_{x_{1}}U\pm 8L_{0}\alpha G_{1,+}.\nabla\partial_{x_{1}}U-(16L_{0}\gamma_{2},0).\nabla\partial_{x_{1}}U,

(5.34)

\displaystyle F_{4,\pm}=-\alpha^{2}G_{3,+}.\nabla(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U)+\gamma_{3}\Lambda U+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{16}\nabla U.(-3y_{1},y_{2})+8L_{0}\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}G_{2,+}.\nabla\partial_{x_{1}}U

(5.35)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-8L_{0}(\gamma_{1},0).\nabla\partial_{x_{1}}U-\gamma_{4}(y_{1},-y_{2}).\nabla U,

and

\displaystyle\bar{\Psi}_{0}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}(\tilde{G}_{+}.\nabla(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U-8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})-\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}(\tilde{G}_{-}.\nabla(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U+8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)(\frac{\cdot+a}{\nu})

(5.36)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{\nu^{3}}[\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}(a)+\alpha a-\nu^{2}(\gamma_{1},0)].\left(\nabla(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U-8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})-\nabla(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U+8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)(\frac{\cdot+a}{\nu})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+8L_{0}\alpha^{2}\nu(G_{3,+}.\nabla\partial_{x_{1}}U)(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})-8L_{0}\alpha^{2}\nu(G_{3,+}.\nabla\partial_{x_{1}}U)(\frac{\cdot+a}{\nu})

\displaystyle\quad+2U_{1,\nu}U_{2,\nu}+U_{2,\nu}(\Lambda U_{1,\nu}+U_{1,\nu}(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}-U_{2,\nu}(a)(\Lambda U)_{1,\nu}-U_{1,\nu}(-a)(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}

\displaystyle\quad-\nabla U_{1,\nu}.\left(\nabla\Phi_{(\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U+8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}}(z)-\frac{1}{2}\nabla\Phi_{(\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U+8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}}(a)+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{16}\nu^{2}(-3(z_{1}-|a|),z_{2})\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\nabla U_{2,\nu}.\left(\nabla\Phi_{(\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U-8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu}}(z)-\frac{1}{2}\nabla\Phi_{(\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U-8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu}}(a)+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{16}\nu^{2}(-3(z_{1}+|a|),z_{2})\right)

\displaystyle\quad-16L_{0}\nu U_{2,\nu}(\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu}+16L_{0}\nu U_{1,\nu}(\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}}(z)-\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}}(a)-\nabla^{2}\bar{\mathfrak{V}}_{1}(a)(z-a)+\nabla((\chi^{*}-1)\tilde{\mathcal{V}})\right).\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\left(\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}}(z)-\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}}(-a)-\nabla^{2}\bar{\mathfrak{V}}_{1}(-a)(z+a)+\nabla((\chi^{*}-1)\tilde{\mathcal{V}})\right).\nabla U_{2,\nu}

\displaystyle\quad+8\nu^{2}R_{0},

5.4.2. The second ansatz

We introduce
\iota_{+}=1
and
\iota_{-}=2
. We compute for two functions
\mathfrak{T}
and
\mathfrak{V}
such that
-\Delta\mathfrak{V}=\mathfrak{T}
,

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}^{z}\left(\mathfrak{T}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu}),\nu^{2}\mathfrak{V}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\nu^{2}}\mathscr{L}_{0}(\mathfrak{T},\mathfrak{V})(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})-\nabla.\left(\mathfrak{T}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})\nabla\Phi_{U_{\iota_{\mp}},\nu}\right)-\nu\nabla.\left(U_{\iota_{\mp},\nu}\nabla\mathfrak{V}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})\right)-\beta\Lambda(\mathfrak{T}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu}))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\nu^{2}}\mathscr{L}_{0}(\mathfrak{T},\mathfrak{V})(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})-\frac{1}{\nu^{2}}\nabla\mathfrak{T}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu}).\nabla\Phi_{U}(\frac{\cdot\pm a}{\nu})-\frac{1}{\nu^{2}}\nabla U(\frac{\cdot\pm a}{\nu}).\left(\nabla\mathfrak{V}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})-\nabla\mathfrak{V}(\frac{\mp 2a}{\nu})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2}{\nu^{2}}\mathfrak{T}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})U(\frac{\cdot\pm a}{\nu})-\beta\Lambda\mathfrak{T}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})\mp\frac{\beta a}{\nu}.\nabla\mathfrak{T}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})-\nu\nabla\mathfrak{V}(\frac{\mp 2a}{\nu}).\nabla U_{\iota_{\mp},\nu}

We introduce for
0\leq i\leq 3
,

\bar{G}_{>i,\pm}(z)=\frac{1}{\nu}\left(\sum_{j=i+1}^{3}\alpha^{\frac{j+1}{2}}\nu^{j+1}G_{i,\pm}+\tilde{G}_{\pm}\right)(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu})

so that from Lemma
3.3
we have
\nabla\Phi_{U}(y\pm\frac{2a}{\nu})=\mp\alpha a\nu+\sum_{j=1}^{i}\alpha^{\frac{j+1}{2}}\nu^{j+1}G_{j,\pm}+\nu\tilde{G}_{>i,\pm}(\pm a+\nu y)
for
0\leq i\leq 3
. This implies that

\frac{1}{\nu}\nabla\Phi_{U}(\frac{z\pm a}{\nu})=\mp\alpha a+\sum_{j=1}^{i}\alpha^{\frac{j+1}{2}}\nu^{j}G_{j,\pm}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})+\bar{G}_{>i,\pm}(z).

By Lemma
3.3
we have for
|z\mp a|\ll 1
that
|\bar{G}_{>i,\pm}(z)|\lesssim(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{i+1}
. Using
|\nabla\Phi_{U}(y)|\lesssim\langle y\rangle^{-1}
, (
3.18
), (
3.19
) and (
3.20
) we have for
|z\mp a|\gtrsim 1
that
|\bar{G}_{>i,\pm}(z)|\lesssim(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{-1}+(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{i}
. Combining, we have for
i=0,1,2,3
,

|\bar{G}_{>i,\pm}(y)|\lesssim(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{i+1}(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{-1}

(5.37)

for
z\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, which will be used later on. This leads to the decomposition

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}^{z}\left(\mathfrak{T}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu}),\nu^{2}\mathfrak{V}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\nu^{2}}\mathscr{L}_{0}(\mathfrak{T},\mathfrak{V})(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})-\nu\nabla\mathfrak{V}(\frac{\mp 2a}{\nu}).\nabla U_{\iota_{\pm},\nu}\pm\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\nu}a.\nabla\mathfrak{T}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})-\alpha(G_{1,\pm}.\nabla\mathfrak{T})(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})-\beta\Lambda\mathfrak{T}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{\nu}\nabla\mathfrak{T}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu}).\bar{G}_{>1,\pm}(z)-\frac{1}{\nu^{2}}\nabla U(\frac{\cdot\pm a}{\nu}).\left(\nabla\mathfrak{V}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})-\nabla\mathfrak{V}(\frac{\mp 2a}{\nu})\right)+\frac{2}{\nu^{2}}\mathfrak{T}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})U(\frac{\cdot\pm a}{\nu})

and for
j=1,2
to the decomposition

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}^{z}\left(\nu^{j}\mathfrak{T}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu}),\nu^{j+2}\mathfrak{V}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})\right)

\displaystyle=\nu^{j-2}\mathscr{L}_{0}(\mathfrak{T},\mathfrak{V})(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})-\nu^{j+1}\nabla\mathfrak{V}(\frac{\mp 2a}{\nu}).\nabla U_{\iota_{\pm},\nu}\pm\nu^{j}\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\nu}a.\nabla\mathfrak{T}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})-\nu^{j}\beta\Lambda\mathfrak{T}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})

\displaystyle\quad-\nu^{j-1}\nabla\mathfrak{T}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu}).\bar{G}_{>0,\pm}(z)-\nu^{j-2}\nabla U(\frac{\cdot\pm a}{\nu}).\left(\nabla\mathfrak{V}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})-\nabla\mathfrak{V}(\frac{\mp 2a}{\nu})\right)+2\nu^{j-2}\mathfrak{T}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})U(\frac{\cdot\pm a}{\nu}).

Our second ansatz is the refined decomposition

(\tilde{\Xi},\tilde{\mathfrak{V}})=\sum_{\pm}\sum_{i=0}^{2}(\nu^{i}\mathfrak{T}_{i+2,\pm},\nu^{i+2}\mathfrak{V}_{i+2,\pm})(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu})

(5.38)

Then, assuming that
\nabla\mathfrak{V}_{i+2,+}(\frac{-2a}{\nu})=-\nabla\mathfrak{V}_{i+2,-}(\frac{2a}{\nu})
for
i=0,1,2
which will be ensured by symmetry properties, we get

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}^{z}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{2}(\nu^{i}\mathfrak{T}_{i+2,\pm},\nu^{i+2}\mathfrak{V}_{i+2,\pm})(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{2}\sum_{\pm}\nu^{i-2}\mathscr{L}_{0}(\mathfrak{T}_{i+2,\pm},\mathfrak{V}_{i+2,\pm})(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})-\left(\sum_{i=0}^{2}\nu^{i+1}\nabla\mathfrak{V}_{i+2,-}(\frac{2a}{\nu})\right).(\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\nabla U_{2,\nu})

\displaystyle+\sum_{\pm}\left(\pm\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\nu}a.\nabla\mathfrak{T}_{2,\pm}-\beta\Lambda\mathfrak{T}_{2,\pm}-\alpha(G_{1,\pm}.\nabla\mathfrak{T}_{2,\pm})\right)(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{\nu}\nabla\mathfrak{T}_{2,\pm}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu}).\bar{G}_{>1,\pm}(z)+\sum_{\pm}\sum_{i=1,2}\left(\pm\nu^{i}\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\nu}a.\nabla\mathfrak{T}_{i+2,\pm}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})-\nu^{i}\beta\Lambda\mathfrak{T}_{i+2,\pm}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu}\right)

\displaystyle-\sum_{\pm}\sum_{i=1,2}\nu^{i-1}\nabla\mathfrak{T}_{i+2,\pm}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu}).\bar{G}_{>0,\pm}(z)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\pm}\sum_{i=0}^{2}\left(-\nu^{i-2}\nabla U(\frac{\cdot\pm a}{\nu}).\left(\nabla\mathfrak{V}_{i+2,\pm}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})-\nabla\mathfrak{V}_{i+2,\pm}(\frac{\mp 2a}{\nu})\right)+2\nu^{i-2}\mathfrak{T}_{i+2}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})U(\frac{\cdot\pm a}{\nu})\right).

We furthermore choose

\mu^{\prime}=\frac{8a}{\nu^{2}+4|a|^{2}}-\beta a-\nabla\Phi_{(\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U+8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}}(a)-\bar{\mathfrak{V}}(a)-\sum_{i=0}^{2}\nu^{i+1}\nabla\mathfrak{V}_{i+2,-}(\frac{2a}{\nu})+\nu^{2}(\mu^{\prime\prime},0).

(5.39)

The identity (
5.32
) becomes

\displaystyle\Psi_{0}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\pm}\nu^{-2}\left(F_{2,\pm}+\mathscr{L}_{0}(\mathfrak{T}_{2,\pm},\mathfrak{V}_{2,\pm})\right)\left(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu}\right)

(5.40)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\pm}\nu^{-1}\left(F_{3,\pm}\mp\mu^{\prime\prime}\partial_{x_{1}}U+\mathscr{L}_{0}(\mathfrak{T}_{3,\pm},\mathfrak{V}_{3,\pm})\right)\left(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\pm}\left(F_{4,\pm}+\mathscr{L}_{0}(\mathfrak{T}_{4,\pm},\mathfrak{V}_{4,\pm})\pm\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\nu}a.\nabla\mathfrak{T}_{2,\pm}-\beta\Lambda\mathfrak{T}_{2,\pm}-\alpha(G_{1,\pm}.\nabla\mathfrak{T}_{2,\pm})\right)\left(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\bar{\Psi}_{0}^{(0)}+\bar{\Psi}_{0}^{(1)}-\nabla.(\Xi\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}\Xi})

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\pm}\sum_{i=0}^{2}\left(-\nu^{i-2}\nabla U(\frac{\cdot\pm a}{\nu}).\left(\nabla\mathfrak{V}_{i+2,\pm}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})-\nabla\mathfrak{V}_{i+2,\pm}(\frac{\mp 2a}{\nu})\right)+2\nu^{i-2}\mathfrak{T}_{i+2}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})U(\frac{\cdot\pm a}{\nu})\right).

5.4.3. Decomposition of the nonlinear terms

We have

\displaystyle\Xi=8\nu^{2}\phi_{0}+\frac{1}{2}(\Lambda U)_{1,\nu}+\frac{1}{2}(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}-8L_{0}\nu(\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu}+8L_{0}\nu(\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}+\sum_{\pm}\sum_{i=0}^{2}\nu^{i}\mathfrak{T}_{i+2,\pm}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu}),

(5.42)

\displaystyle\Phi_{\chi^{*}\Xi}=8\nu^{2}\Phi_{\chi^{*}\phi_{0}}+\frac{1}{2}\Phi_{(\Lambda U)_{1,\nu}}+\frac{1}{2}\Phi_{(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}}-8L_{0}\nu\Phi_{(\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu}}+8L_{0}\nu\Phi_{(\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}}+\chi^{*}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}+\bar{\mathfrak{V}}.

(5.43)

We introduce

\displaystyle(\tilde{T}_{0,\pm},\tilde{V}_{0,\pm})=(\Lambda U,\Phi_{\Lambda U}),

\displaystyle(\tilde{T}_{1,\pm},\tilde{V}_{1,\pm})=\mp 16L_{0}(\partial_{x_{1}}U,\partial_{x_{1}}\Phi_{U}),

\displaystyle(\tilde{T}_{2,\pm},\tilde{V}_{2,\pm})=\alpha(T_{2}^{(0)},V_{2}^{(0)})+\alpha\tilde{\lambda_{0}}(\hat{T}_{2},\hat{V}_{2})\pm\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\nu}(\mathcal{S}_{1,1},\mathcal{W}_{1,1}).

We let

\displaystyle(\tilde{\phi}_{0,>2},\tilde{V}_{0,>2})=(\phi_{0},\Phi_{\chi^{*}V_{0}})-\frac{1}{16}\sum_{\pm}\sum_{j=0}^{2}(\nu^{j-4}\tilde{T}_{j,\pm},\nu^{j-2}\tilde{V}_{j,\pm})(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu}),

so that

\displaystyle\phi_{0}=-\frac{1}{16}\sum_{\pm}\sum_{j=0}^{2}\nu^{j-4}\tilde{T}_{j,\pm}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})+\tilde{\phi}_{0,>2},

\displaystyle\Phi_{\chi^{*}\phi_{0}}=-\frac{1}{16}\sum_{\pm}\sum_{j=0}^{2}\nu^{j-4}\tilde{V}_{j,\pm}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})+\tilde{V}_{0,>2},

where by
mettre ref

\displaystyle|\tilde{\phi}_{0,>2}(z)|\lesssim\frac{1}{(\nu+|z\mp a|)(\nu+|z\pm a|)}\langle z\rangle^{C},

(5.44)

\displaystyle|\nabla\tilde{V}_{0,>2}(z)|\lesssim|\ln\nu|.

(5.45)

We define

\displaystyle(\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,\pm},\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,\pm})=(\mathfrak{T}_{2,\pm},\mathfrak{V}_{2,\pm})-\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{T}_{2,\pm},\tilde{V}_{2,\pm}),

(5.46)

\displaystyle\tilde{\Xi}_{>2}=8\nu^{2}\tilde{\phi}_{0,>2}+\sum_{\pm}\sum_{j=3}^{4}\nu^{j-2}\mathfrak{T}_{j,\pm}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu}),

(5.47)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{>2}=8\nu^{2}\tilde{V}_{0,>2}+\chi^{*}\sum_{\pm}\sum_{j=3}^{4}\nu^{j}\mathfrak{V}_{j,\pm}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})+(\chi^{*}-1)\sum_{\pm}\nu^{2}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,\pm}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu}),

(5.48)

so that

\displaystyle\Xi=\sum_{\pm}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,\pm}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})+\tilde{\Xi}_{>2},

(5.49)

\displaystyle\Phi_{\chi^{*}\Xi}=\sum_{\pm}\nu^{2}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,\pm}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})+\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{>2}+\bar{\mathfrak{V}}.

We decompose the nonlinear term accordingly,

\displaystyle\nabla.(\Xi\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}\Xi})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\pm}\nabla.(\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,\pm}\nabla\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,\pm})(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})

(5.50)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\pm}\nabla.(\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,\pm}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})(\nu\nabla\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,\mp}(\frac{\cdot\pm a}{\nu})+\nabla\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{>2}+\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}}))

\displaystyle\quad+\nabla.((\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,\mp}(\frac{\cdot\pm a}{\nu})+\tilde{\Xi}_{>2})(\nu\nabla\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,\pm}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu}))\quad+\nabla.(\tilde{\Xi}_{>2}(\nabla\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{>2}+\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}})).

Injecting (
5.50
) in (
5.40
), the error can be written as

\displaystyle\Psi_{0}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\pm}\nu^{-2}\left(F_{2,\pm}+\mathscr{L}_{0}(\mathfrak{T}_{2,\pm},\mathfrak{V}_{2,\pm})\right)\left(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu}\right)

(5.51)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\pm}\nu^{-1}\left(F_{3,\pm}\mp\mu^{\prime\prime}\partial_{x_{1}}U+\mathscr{L}_{0}(\mathfrak{T}_{3,\pm},\mathfrak{V}_{3,\pm})\right)\left(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\pm}\left(F_{4,\pm}+\mathscr{L}_{0}(\mathfrak{T}_{4,\pm},\mathfrak{V}_{4,\pm})\pm\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\nu}a.\nabla\mathfrak{T}_{2,\pm}-\beta\Lambda\mathfrak{T}_{2,\pm}-\alpha(G_{1,\pm}.\nabla\mathfrak{T}_{2,\pm})-\nabla.(\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,\pm}\nabla\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,\pm})\right)\left(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\bar{\Psi}_{0}^{(0)}+\bar{\Psi}_{0}^{(1)}+\bar{\Psi}_{0}^{(2)}

where

\displaystyle\bar{\Psi}_{0}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\pm}\nabla.(\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,\pm}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})(\nu\nabla\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,\mp}(\frac{\cdot\pm a}{\nu})+\nabla\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{>2}+\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}}))+\nabla.((\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,\mp}(\frac{\cdot\pm a}{\nu})+\tilde{\Xi}_{>2})(\nu\nabla\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,\pm}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu}))

\displaystyle\quad-\nabla.(\tilde{\Xi}_{>2}(\nabla\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{>2}+\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}})),

(5.52)

\displaystyle\Psi_{0}

5.4.5. Elliptic equations in polar coordinates

We use polar coordinates to solve (
5.4.4
), (
5.4.4
) and (
5.4.4
). We recall the formula

\nabla(f(r)\cos(m\theta)).\nabla(g(r)\cos(k\theta))=\partial_{r}f\partial_{r}g\cos(m\theta)\cos(k\theta)+\frac{fg}{r}mk\sin(m\theta)\sin(k\theta)

as well as the trigonometric identities

2\cos(m\theta)\cos(k\theta)=\cos((m+k)\theta)+\cos((m-k)\theta),\quad 2\sin(m\theta)\sin(k\theta)=-\cos((m+k)\theta)+\cos((m-k)\theta).

Using (
3.18
) we compute

\displaystyle G_{1,+}.\nabla(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U)=\frac{1}{2}r\partial_{r}(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U)\cos(2\theta),

\displaystyle(\gamma_{2},0).\nabla\Lambda U=\gamma_{2}\partial_{r}\Lambda U\cos(\theta),

which, injected in (
5.33
) gives

\displaystyle F_{2,\pm}

\displaystyle=-\beta(\Lambda U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda^{2}U)\pm\gamma_{2}\partial_{r}\Lambda U\cos\theta-\frac{\alpha}{2}r\partial_{r}(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U)\cos(2\theta)

\displaystyle=:-\mathfrak{S}_{2,0}(r)\mp\mathfrak{S}_{2,1}(r)\cos(\theta)-\mathfrak{S}_{2,2}(r)\cos(2\theta).

(5.60)

In order to solve (
5.4.4
) we thus decompose

(\mathfrak{T}_{2,\pm},\mathfrak{V}_{2,\pm})=\sum_{k=0}^{2}(\pm 1)^{k}(\mathfrak{T}_{2,k}(r)\cos(k\theta),\mathfrak{V}_{2,k}(r)\cos(k\theta))

(5.61)

and we require that for
k=0,1,2
the profiles
(\mathfrak{T}_{2,k},\mathfrak{V}_{2,k})
solve the systems

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}\mathscr{L}_{0,k}(\mathfrak{T}_{2,k},\mathfrak{V}_{2,k})=\mathfrak{S}_{2,k},\\
-\Delta_{k}\mathfrak{V}_{2,k}=\mathfrak{T}_{2,k}.\end{array}\right.

(5.62)

Next, we have using (
3.19
) the cancellation

\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}G_{2,+}-(\gamma_{1},0)=\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}(-\frac{1}{12\sqrt{2}}\nabla(r^{3}\cos(3\theta))+\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}(1,0))-(\frac{\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}}{4\sqrt{2}},0)=-\frac{\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}}{12\sqrt{2}}\nabla(r^{3}\cos(3\theta))\\

so that the first term in the expression (
5.34
) is

\mp(\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}G_{2,+}-(\gamma_{1},0)).\nabla(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U)=\pm\frac{\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}}{4\sqrt{2}}r^{2}\partial_{r}(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U)\cos(3\theta).

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\Lambda\partial_{x_{1}}U=\Lambda\partial_{r}U\cos\theta,

\displaystyle G_{1,+}.\nabla\partial_{x_{1}}U=(\frac{1}{4}r\partial_{r}^{2}U+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}U)\cos\theta+(\frac{1}{4}r\partial_{r}^{2}U-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}U)\cos(3\theta),

\displaystyle(1,0).\nabla\partial_{x_{1}}U=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}^{2}U+\frac{1}{2r}\partial_{r}U+(\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}^{2}U-\frac{1}{2r}\partial_{r}U)\cos(2\theta),

and the identity (
5.34
) then implies

\displaystyle F_{3,\pm}\mp\mu^{\prime\prime}\partial_{x_{1}}U

\displaystyle=-8L_{0}\gamma_{2}(\partial_{r}^{2}U+\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}U)\pm\left(8L_{0}(\frac{\alpha}{4}+\beta)\Lambda\partial_{r}U-\mu^{\prime\prime}\partial_{r}U\right)\cos(\theta)

(5.63)

\displaystyle-8L_{0}\gamma_{2}(\partial_{r}^{2}U-\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}U)\cos(2\theta)\pm\left(\frac{\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}}{4\sqrt{2}}r^{2}\partial_{r}(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U)+8L_{0}\alpha(\frac{1}{4}r\partial_{r}^{2}U-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}U)\right)\cos(3\theta)

\displaystyle:=-\mathfrak{S}_{3,0}(r)\mp\mathfrak{S}_{3,1}(r)\cos(\theta)-\mathfrak{S}_{3,2}(r)\cos(2\theta)\mp\mathfrak{S}_{3,3}(r)\cos(3\theta).

To solve the system (
5.4.4
) we thus take

(\mathfrak{T}_{3,\pm},\mathfrak{V}_{3,\pm})=\sum_{k=0}^{3}(\pm 1)^{k}(\mathfrak{T}_{3,k}(r)\cos(k\theta),\mathfrak{V}_{3,k}(r)\cos(k\theta))

(5.64)

where for

the profiles

-\Delta_{k}\mathfrak{V}_{3,k}=\mathfrak{T}_{3,k}.\end{array}\right.

(5.65)

Finally, we compute using (
3.20
) the cancellation

\displaystyle-\alpha^{2}G_{3,+}.\nabla(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U)+\gamma_{3}\Lambda U+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{16}\nabla U.(-3y_{1},y_{2})

\displaystyle=-\alpha^{2}\left(\frac{1}{64}\nabla(r^{4}\cos(4\theta))+\frac{1}{16}(-3y_{1},y_{2})\right).\nabla(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U)+\gamma_{3}\Lambda U+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{16}\nabla U.(-3y_{1},y_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha^{2}}{32}r\partial_{r}\Lambda U+\gamma_{3}\Lambda U+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{16}r\partial_{r}\Lambda U\cos(2\theta)-\frac{\alpha^{2}}{16}r^{3}\partial_{r}(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U)\cos(4\theta),

and using
\gamma_{1}=\frac{\alpha^{3/2}}{4\sqrt{2}}
and (

\displaystyle 8L_{0}\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}G_{2,+}.\nabla\partial_{x_{1}}U-8L_{0}(\gamma_{1},0).\nabla\partial_{x_{1}}U

\displaystyle=L_{0}\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}(-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}r^{2}\partial_{r}^{2}U-\frac{1}{3\sqrt{2}}r\partial_{r}U)\cos(2\theta)+L_{0}\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}(-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}r^{2}\partial_{r}^{2}U+\frac{1}{3\sqrt{2}}r\partial_{r}U)\cos(4\theta)

\displaystyle F_{4,\pm}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha^{2}}{32}r\partial_{r}\Lambda U+\gamma_{3}\Lambda U+\left((\frac{\alpha^{2}}{16}-\gamma_{4})r\partial_{r}\Lambda U+L_{0}\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}(-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}r^{2}\partial_{r}^{2}U-\frac{1}{3\sqrt{2}}r\partial_{r}U)\right)\cos(2\theta)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(-\frac{\alpha^{2}}{16}r^{3}\partial_{r}(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U)+L_{0}\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}(-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}r^{2}\partial_{r}^{2}U+\frac{1}{3\sqrt{2}}r\partial_{r}U)\right)\cos(4\theta).

We have by (
5.61

\displaystyle a.\nabla\mathfrak{T}_{2,\pm}=\pm\frac{|a|}{2}[\partial_{r}\mathfrak{T}_{2,1}+\frac{\mathfrak{T}_{2,1}}{r}]+|a|(\partial_{r}\mathfrak{T}_{2,0}+\frac{\partial_{r}\mathfrak{T}_{2,2}}{2}+\frac{\mathfrak{T}_{2,2}}{2r})\cos(\theta)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\pm\frac{a}{2}[\partial_{r}\mathfrak{T}_{2,1}-\frac{\mathfrak{T}_{2,1}}{r}]\cos(2\theta)+\frac{|a|}{2}(\partial_{r}\mathfrak{T}_{2,2}-\frac{\mathfrak{T}_{2,2}}{r})\cos(3\theta),

\displaystyle\beta\Lambda\mathfrak{T}_{2,\pm}=\beta\Lambda\mathfrak{T}_{2,0}\pm\beta\Lambda\mathfrak{T}_{2,1}\cos(\theta)+\beta\Lambda\mathfrak{T}_{2,2}\cos(2\theta),

\displaystyle G_{1,\pm}.\nabla\mathfrak{T}_{2,\pm}=\frac{r}{4}\partial_{r}\mathfrak{T}_{2,2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{T}_{2,2}\pm(\frac{r}{4}\partial_{r}\mathfrak{T}_{2,1}+\frac{\mathfrak{T}_{2,1}}{4})\cos(\theta)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\qquad+\frac{r}{2}\partial_{r}\mathfrak{T}_{2,0}\cos(2\theta)\pm(\frac{r}{4}\partial_{r}\mathfrak{T}_{2,1}-\frac{\mathfrak{T}_{2,1}}{4})\cos(3\theta)+(\frac{r}{4}\partial_{r}\mathfrak{T}_{2,2}-\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{T}_{2,2})\cos(4\theta).

We introduce

\displaystyle(\tilde{T}_{2,0},\tilde{V}_{2,0})=\alpha(T_{2,0}^{(0)},V_{2,0}^{(0)})+\alpha\tilde{\lambda_{0}}(\hat{T}_{2},\hat{V}_{2}),

(5.66)

\displaystyle(\tilde{T}_{2,1},\tilde{V}_{2,1})=\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\nu}(\mathcal{S}_{1,1,1},\mathcal{W}_{1,1,1}),

\displaystyle(\tilde{T}_{2,2},\tilde{V}_{2,2})=\alpha(T_{2,2},V_{2,2})

and, for

so that we have the decomposition
