The nonlinear term is

-\nabla.(\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,\pm}\nabla\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,\pm})=\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,\pm}^{2}-\nabla.\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,\pm}.\nabla\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,\pm}.

We compute each term above:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,\pm}^{2}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,0}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}^{2}\pm(2\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,0}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}+\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2})\cos(\theta)

\displaystyle+(\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}^{2}+2\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,0}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2})\cos(2\theta)\pm\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2})\cos(3\theta)+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}^{2}\cos(4\theta)

and

\displaystyle\nabla.\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,\pm}.\nabla\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,\pm}

\displaystyle=\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,0}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,0}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,1}+\frac{1}{2r^{2}}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,1}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,2}+\frac{2}{r^{2}}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,2}

\displaystyle\pm\left(\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,0}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,1}+\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,0}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,1}+\frac{1}{2r^{2}}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,1}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,2}+\frac{1}{2r^{2}}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,2}\right)\cos(\theta)

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,1}-\frac{1}{2r^{2}}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,1}+\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,0}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,2}+\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,0}\right)\cos(2\theta)

\displaystyle\pm\left(\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,1}-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,1}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,2}-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,2}\right)\cos(3\theta)

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,2}-\frac{2}{r^{2}}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,2}\right)\cos(4\theta),

and so the nonlinear term can be decomposed as

\displaystyle\nabla.(\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,\pm}\nabla\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,\pm})

\displaystyle=-\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,0}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}^{2}+\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,0}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,0}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,1}+\frac{1}{2r^{2}}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,1}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,2}+\frac{2}{r^{2}}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,2}

\displaystyle\pm\Bigg(-2\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,0}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}-\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}+\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,0}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,1}+\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,0}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,1}+\frac{1}{2r^{2}}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,2}+\frac{1}{2r^{2}}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,2}\Bigg)\cos(\theta)

\displaystyle+\left(-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}^{2}-2\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,0}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,1}-\frac{1}{2r^{2}}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,1}+\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,0}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,2}+\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,0}\right)\cos(2\theta)

\displaystyle\pm\left(-\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,1}-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,1}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,2}-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,2}\right)\cos(3\theta)

\displaystyle+\left(-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,2}-\frac{2}{r^{2}}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,2}\right)\cos(4\theta)

Therefore, by (
5.35
),

\displaystyle-F_{4,\pm}\mp\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\nu}a.\nabla\mathfrak{T}_{2,\pm}+\Lambda\mathfrak{T}_{2,\pm}+\alpha(G_{1,\pm}.\nabla\mathfrak{T}_{2,\pm})+\nabla.(\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,\pm}\nabla\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,\pm})=\sum_{k=0}^{4}(\pm 1)^{k}\mathfrak{S}_{4,k}\cos(4\theta)

with

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}_{4,0}=-\frac{\alpha^{2}}{32}r\partial_{r}\Lambda U-\gamma_{3}\Lambda U-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\nu}\frac{|a|}{2}(\partial_{r}\mathfrak{T}_{2,1}+\frac{\mathfrak{T}_{2,1}}{r})+\beta\Lambda\mathfrak{T}_{2,0}+\frac{\alpha}{4}\Lambda\mathfrak{T}_{2,2}-\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,0}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}^{2}

(5.67)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}^{2}+\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,0}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,0}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,1}+\frac{1}{2r^{2}}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,1}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,2}+\frac{2}{r^{2}}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,2},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}_{4,1}=-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\nu}|a|(\partial_{r}\mathfrak{T}_{2,0}+\frac{\partial_{r}\mathfrak{T}_{2,2}}{2}+\frac{\mathfrak{T}_{2,2}}{2r})+\beta\Lambda\mathfrak{T}_{2,1}+\alpha(\frac{r}{4}\partial_{r}\mathfrak{T}_{2,1}+\frac{\mathfrak{T}_{2,1}}{4})

(5.68)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-2\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,0}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}-\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}+\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,0}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,1}+\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,0}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,1}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{1}{2r^{2}}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,1}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,2}+\frac{1}{2r^{2}}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,2},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}_{4,2}=(\gamma_{4}-\frac{\alpha^{2}}{16})r\partial_{r}\Lambda U-L_{0}\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}(-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}r^{2}\partial_{r}^{2}U-\frac{1}{3\sqrt{2}}r\partial_{r}U)-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\nu}\frac{|a|}{2}(\partial_{r}\mathfrak{T}_{2,1}-\frac{\mathfrak{T}_{2,1}}{r})+\beta\Lambda\mathfrak{T}_{2,2}+\frac{\alpha}{2}r\partial_{r}\mathfrak{T}_{2,0},

(5.69)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}^{2}-2\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,0}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,1}-\frac{1}{2r^{2}}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,1}+\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,0}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,2}+\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,0},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}_{4,3}=-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\nu}\frac{|a|}{2}(\partial_{r}\mathfrak{T}_{2,2}-\frac{\mathfrak{T}_{2,2}}{r})+\alpha(\frac{r}{4}\partial_{r}\mathfrak{T}_{2,1}-\frac{\mathfrak{T}_{2,1}}{4})

(5.70)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,1}-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,1}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,2}-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,1}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,2}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}_{4,4}=\frac{\alpha^{2}}{16}r^{3}\partial_{r}(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U)-L_{0}\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}(-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}r^{2}\partial_{r}^{2}U+\frac{1}{3\sqrt{2}}r\partial_{r}U)+\frac{\alpha}{4}r\partial_{r}\mathfrak{T}_{2,2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}\mathfrak{T}_{2,2}

(5.71)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}\partial_{r}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,2}-\frac{2}{r^{2}}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,2}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,2}.

In order to solve the system (
5.4.4
) we decompose accordingly

(\mathfrak{T}_{4,\pm},\mathfrak{V}_{4,\pm})=\sum_{k=0}^{4}(-1)^{k}(\mathfrak{T}_{2,k}(r)\cos(k\theta),\mathfrak{V}_{2,k}(r)\cos(k\theta))

(5.72)

where the profiles
(\mathfrak{T}_{4,k},\mathfrak{V}_{4,k})
for
k=1,...,4
solve the systems

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}\mathscr{L}_{0,k}(\mathfrak{T}_{4,k},\mathfrak{V}_{4,k})=\mathfrak{S}_{4,k},\\
-\Delta_{k}\mathfrak{V}_{4,k}=\mathfrak{T}_{4,k}.\end{array}\right.

(5.73)

In the lemma below we make an abuse of language and say that
(\mathfrak{T}_{i,k},\mathfrak{V}_{i,k})
is smooth if the function
y\mapsto(\mathfrak{T}_{i,k}(r)\cos(\theta),\mathfrak{V}_{i,k}(r)\cos(\theta))
is smooth on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
\mu^{\prime\prime}=-8L_{0}(\frac{\alpha}{4}+\beta)
. There exist smooth solutions to (
5.62
), (
5.65
) and (
5.73
) such that as
r\to\infty
, for
k\in\mathbb{N}
:

\displaystyle|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathfrak{T}_{2,0}(r)|\lesssim r^{-4-k}\ln r,\qquad|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathfrak{V}_{2,0}(r)|\lesssim r^{-2-k}\ln r,

(5.74)

\displaystyle|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathfrak{T}_{2,1}(r)|\lesssim r^{-3-k},\qquad|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathfrak{V}_{2,1}(r)|\lesssim r^{-1-k}\ln r,

(5.75)

\displaystyle|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathfrak{T}_{2,2}(r)|\lesssim r^{-4-k},\qquad|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathfrak{V}_{2,2}(r)|\lesssim r^{-2-k}\ln r,

(5.76)

\displaystyle|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathfrak{T}_{3,0}(r)|\lesssim r^{-4-k}\ln r,,\qquad|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathfrak{V}_{3,0}(r)|\lesssim r^{-2-k}\ln r,

(5.77)

\displaystyle|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathfrak{T}_{3,1}(r)|\lesssim r^{-3-k},\qquad|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathfrak{V}_{3,1}(r)|\lesssim r^{-1-k}\ln r,

(5.78)

\displaystyle|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathfrak{T}_{3,2}(r)|\lesssim r^{-4-k},\qquad|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathfrak{V}_{3,2}(r)|\lesssim r^{-2-k}\ln r,

(5.79)

\displaystyle|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathfrak{T}_{3,3}(r)|\lesssim r^{-3-k},\qquad|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathfrak{V}_{3,3}(r)|\lesssim r^{-1-k},

(5.80)

\displaystyle|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathfrak{T}_{4,0}(r)|\lesssim r^{-2-k}\ln^{2}r,\qquad|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathfrak{V}_{4,0}(r)|\lesssim r^{-k}\ln^{3}r,

(5.81)

\displaystyle|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathfrak{T}_{4,1}(r)|\lesssim r^{-1-k}\ln r,\qquad|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathfrak{V}_{4,1}(r)|\lesssim r^{1-k}\ln r,

(5.82)

\displaystyle|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathfrak{T}_{4,2}(r)|\lesssim r^{-2-k}\ln r,\qquad|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathfrak{V}_{4,2}(r)|\lesssim r^{-k}\ln r,

(5.83)

\displaystyle|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathfrak{T}_{4,3}(r)|\lesssim r^{-1-k},\qquad|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathfrak{V}_{4,3}(r)|\lesssim r^{1-k},

(5.84)

\displaystyle|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathfrak{T}_{4,4}(r)|\lesssim r^{-2-k},\qquad|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathfrak{V}_{4,4}(r)|\lesssim r^{-k}.

(5.85)

Thanks to the above Lemma, injecting (
5.74
)-(
5.85
) in (
5.61
), (
5.64
) and (
5.72
) shows

\displaystyle|\nabla^{k}\mathfrak{T}_{2}(y)|\lesssim\langle y\rangle^{-3-k},\qquad|\nabla^{k}\mathfrak{V}_{2}(y)|\lesssim\langle y\rangle^{-1-k}\langle\ln\langle y\rangle\rangle,

(5.86)

\displaystyle|\nabla^{k}\mathfrak{T}_{3}(y)|\lesssim\langle y\rangle^{-3-k},\qquad|\nabla^{k}\mathfrak{V}_{3}(y)|\lesssim\langle y\rangle^{-1-k},

(5.87)

\displaystyle|\nabla^{k}\mathfrak{T}_{4}(y)|\lesssim\langle y\rangle^{-1-k},\qquad|\nabla^{k}\mathfrak{V}_{4}(y)|\lesssim\langle y\rangle^{1-k}\langle\ln\langle y\rangle\rangle,

(5.88)

Proof.

As we will see, most of the equations in (
5.62
), (
5.65
) and (
5.73
) involve source terms with the same particular cancellations and estimates as for the equations (
3.1.5
)-(
3.1.5
) that were solved in Proposition
3.11
. Therefore, we will refer to the proof of Proposition
3.11
when the computations will be the same, and will give details for the equations that are different.

The smoothness of these profiles (smoothness seen as the radial component of the corresponding spherical harmonics) follows from the choice of the integration constants in the formulas below and from the behaviour near
0
of the solutions to the homogeneous systems in Lemma
A.2
. We use the notation
f(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(g(r))
if
|\partial_{r}^{k}f(r)|\lesssim r^{-k}g(r)
for
r\geq 1
.

Computation of
(\mathfrak{T}_{2,0},\mathfrak{V}_{2,0})
. We move to the partial mass setting. We first notice that the source term
\mathfrak{S}_{2,0}
enjoys three cancellations. Namely we have the mass cancellation
\int\Lambda U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda^{2}U=0
so that
\int_{0}^{\infty}r\mathfrak{S}_{2,0}(r)dr=0
and hence
m_{\mathfrak{S}_{2,0}}(r)=\int_{0}^{r}\zeta\mathfrak{S}_{2,0}(\zeta)d\zeta=-\int_{r}^{\infty}\zeta\mathfrak{S}_{2,0}(\zeta)d\zeta
. This, and the tail cancellation
U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U=\mathcal{O}(\langle y\rangle^{-6})
that implies
\mathfrak{S}_{2,0}(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-6})
, give

m_{\mathfrak{S}_{2,0}}(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-4}).

(5.89)

Using
\Lambda f=r^{-1}\partial_{r}(r^{2}f)
we have the identity
m_{\mathfrak{S}_{2,0}}(r)=r^{2}U(r)+\frac{1}{2}r\partial_{r}(r^{2}U)
. This implies the following third cancellation for the partial mass

\int_{0}^{\infty}rm_{\mathfrak{S}_{2,0}}(r)dr=\int_{0}^{\infty}(r^{3}U(r)+\frac{1}{2}r^{2}\partial_{r}(r^{2}U))dr=0.

(5.90)

We solve (
5.62
) for
k=0
using Lemma (
A.1
) and obtain

\displaystyle\mathfrak{T}_{2,0}(r)=\frac{\partial_{r}m_{\mathfrak{T}_{2,0}}}{r},\qquad\Phi_{\mathfrak{T}_{2,0}}=C-\int_{1}^{r}\frac{m_{\mathfrak{T}_{2,0}}(\zeta)}{\zeta}d\zeta,

(5.91)

\displaystyle m_{\mathfrak{T}_{2,0}}(r)=\frac{1}{2}\psi_{0}\int_{r}^{1}\frac{\zeta^{4}+4\zeta^{2}\ln\zeta-1}{\zeta}m_{\mathfrak{S}_{2,0}}(\zeta)d\zeta+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\psi}_{0}\int_{0}^{r}\zeta m_{\mathfrak{S}_{2,0}}(\zeta)d\zeta,

(5.92)

for
C\in\mathbb{R}
any integration constant. Injecting (
5.89
), (
5.90
) and (
A.3
) in (
5.92
) shows
m_{\mathfrak{T}_{2,0}}(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-2}\ln r)
. In turn, injecting this in (
5.91
) shows the desired estimates (
5.74
).

Computation of
(\mathfrak{T}_{2,1},\mathfrak{V}_{2,1})
. By (
5.60
), the system (
5.62
) for
k=1
is the same as (
3.1.5
) up to a constant factor. We thus take
(\mathfrak{T}_{2,1},\mathfrak{V}_{2,1})=(\mathcal{S}_{1,1,1},\mathcal{W}_{1,1,1})
. The estimates (
5.75
) then follow from (
3.194
).

Computation of
(\mathfrak{T}_{2,2},\mathfrak{V}_{2,2})
. Injecting the tail cancellation
U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U=\mathcal{O}(\langle y\rangle^{-6})
in (
5.60
) gives

\mathfrak{S}_{2,2}(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-6}).

(5.93)

We solve the system (
5.62
) by applying Lemma
A.3
and obtain

(\mathfrak{T}_{2,2},\mathfrak{V}_{2,2})=\sum_{k=0}^{4}\gamma_{2,2,k}(r)(h_{2,k},g_{2,k})

where we choose the integration constants so that

\displaystyle\gamma_{2,2,1}(r)=-\int_{r}^{\infty}\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{2,2}W_{2,1}}{W_{2}}ds=\int_{r}^{\infty}\mathcal{O}_{s\to\infty}(s^{-5})ds=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-4}),

\displaystyle\gamma_{2,2,2}(r)=-\int_{0}^{r}\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{2,2}W_{2,2}}{W_{2}}ds=\int_{0}^{r}\mathcal{O}_{s\to\infty}(s^{-1})ds=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(\ln r),

\displaystyle\gamma_{2,2,3}(r)=-\int_{r}^{\infty}\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{2,2}W_{2,3}}{W_{2}}ds=\int_{r}^{\infty}\mathcal{O}_{s\to\infty}(s^{-3-\sqrt{8}})ds=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-2-\sqrt{8}}),

\displaystyle\gamma_{2,2,4}(r)=-\int_{0}^{r}\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{2,2}W_{2,4}}{W_{2}}ds=\int_{0}^{r}\mathcal{O}_{s\to\infty}(s^{-3+\sqrt{8}})ds=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-2+\sqrt{8}})

where the estimates of the above integrals follow from (
5.93
) and the estimates of Lemma
A.3
. Combining these estimates with the asymptotic behaviours of
(h_{2,k},g_{2,k})
of Lemma
A.2
shows the desired estimates (
5.76
).

Computation of
(\mathfrak{T}_{3,0},\mathfrak{V}_{3,0})
. We have by (
5.63
) the cancellation

\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathfrak{S}_{3,0}=8L_{0}\gamma_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}(r\partial_{r}^{2}U+\partial_{r}U)dr=0

and the asymptotic behaviour
\mathfrak{S}_{3,0}(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-6})
. Hence
\mathfrak{S}_{3,0}
satisfies the same cancellation and estimate as the source term
\mathfrak{S}_{2,0}
in the previous computation of
(\mathfrak{T}_{2,0},\mathfrak{V}_{2,0})
. The same arguments then yield (
5.77
).

Computation of
(\mathfrak{T}_{3,1},\mathfrak{V}_{3,1})
. The idea is to use the parameter
\mu^{\prime\prime}
to enforce an orthogonality condition. Using the commutator relation
\partial_{r}\Lambda-\Lambda\partial_{r}=\partial_{r}
and the identity
\partial_{r}\Lambda U=4\sqrt{2}\Sigma_{3,1}
, and choosing
\mu^{\prime\prime}=-8L_{0}(\frac{\alpha}{4}+\beta)
we get by (
5.63
) that
\mathfrak{S}_{3,1}=-32\sqrt{2}L_{0}(\frac{\alpha}{4}+\beta)\Sigma_{3,1}
. In view of Lemma
3.7
, we thus choose
(\mathfrak{T}_{3,1},\mathfrak{V}_{3,1})=-32\sqrt{2}L_{0}(\frac{\alpha}{4}+\beta)(T_{3,1},V_{3,1})
and obtain the estimates (
5.78
).

Computation of
(\mathfrak{T}_{3,2},\mathfrak{V}_{3,2})
. We have by (
5.63
) that
\mathfrak{S}_{3,2}(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-6})
. This is the same estimate as (
5.93
) for
\mathfrak{S}_{2,2}
. The same arguments used in the computation of
(\mathfrak{T}_{2,2},\mathfrak{V}_{2,2})
then show (
5.79
).

Computation of
(\mathfrak{T}_{3,3},\mathfrak{V}_{3,3})
. We inject the tail cancellation
U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U=\mathcal{O}(\langle y\rangle^{-6})
in (
5.63
) and obtain

\mathfrak{S}_{3,3}=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-5}).

We notice that this asymptotic behaviour as
r\to\infty
is exactly as that of
\Sigma_{3,0,3}
in Step 4 of the proof of Proposition
3.11
, see (
3.216
). By reproducing exactly the same argument as that which was made to compute
(\mathcal{S}_{3,0,3},\mathcal{W}_{3,0,3})
, we get the same estimates (
3.199
) for
(\mathfrak{T}_{3,3},\mathfrak{V}_{3,3})
. This shows (
5.80
).

Computation of
(\mathfrak{T}_{4,0},\mathfrak{V}_{4,0})
. We first notice using (
5.67
) the mass cancellation

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}r\mathfrak{S}_{4,0}dr

\displaystyle=-\frac{\alpha^{2}}{32}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{2}\partial_{r}\Lambda Udr-\gamma_{3}\int_{0}^{\infty}r\Lambda Udr-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\nu}\frac{|a|}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}(r\partial_{r}\mathfrak{T}_{2,1}+\mathfrak{T}_{2,1})dr

\displaystyle\qquad+\beta\int_{0}^{\infty}r\Lambda\mathfrak{T}_{2,0}dr+\frac{\alpha}{4}\int_{0}^{\infty}r\Lambda\mathfrak{T}_{2,2}dr+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla.(\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,+}\nabla\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,+})dy\ =0.

(5.94)

Indeed, all terms in the right-hand side above vanish, because of integration by parts that are legitimate from the asymptotic behaviours (
5.74
), (
5.75
) and (
5.76
) that have already been obtained previously in the proof. Moreover, these same asymptotic behaviours imply
\mathfrak{S}_{4,0}(r)=\mathcal{O}(r^{-4}\ln r)
. We notice that this estimate and the cancellation (
5.94
) are exactly as that for
\Sigma_{2,2,0}
in Step 2 of the proof of Proposition
3.11
, see (
3.210
) and (
3.211
), up to a
\ln r
factor for the estimate. The same argument as that of Step 2 in the proof of Proposition
3.11
can be applied, yielding for
\mathfrak{S}_{4,0}
the same estimates (
3.197
) as for
(\mathcal{S}_{2,2,0},\mathcal{W}_{2,2,0})
, with an additional
\log r
factor. This is (
5.81
).

Computation of
(\mathfrak{T}_{4,1},\mathfrak{V}_{4,1})
. Injecting (
5.74
), (
5.75
) and (
5.76
) in (
5.68
) we find

\mathfrak{S}_{4,1}(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-3}).

This estimate is exactly as the estimate (
3.209
) for
\Sigma_{3,1,1}
in Step 1 of the proof of Proposition
3.11
. The exact same argument yield that
(\mathfrak{T}_{4,1},\mathfrak{V}_{4,1})
satisfiies the same estimates as (
3.200
) for
(\mathcal{S}_{3,1,1},\mathcal{W}_{3,1,1})
, yielding (
5.82
).

Computation of
(\mathfrak{T}_{4,2},\mathfrak{V}_{4,2})
. By (
5.74
), (
5.75
) and (
5.76
) and (
5.69
) we have

\mathfrak{S}_{4,2}(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-4}\ln r).

This is the same estimate as for
\Sigma_{2,2,2}
in Step 3 of the proof of Proposition
3.11
, up to a
\log r
factor. Reproducing the same arguments shows that
(\mathfrak{T}_{4,2},\mathfrak{V}_{4,2})
enjoys the same estimates as (
3.198
) for
(\mathcal{S}_{2,2,2},\mathcal{W}_{2,2,2})
with an additional
\log r
factor. This shows (
5.83
).

Computation of
(\mathfrak{T}_{4,3},\mathfrak{V}_{4,3})
. Injecting (
5.75
) and (
5.76
) in (
5.70
) we get

\mathfrak{S}_{4,3}(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-3}).

We recognise the same estimate as that for
\Sigma_{3,1,3}
in Step 4 of the proof of Proposition
3.11
. The exact same reasoning shows for
(\mathfrak{T}_{4,3},\mathfrak{V}_{4,3})
the same estimates as (
3.201
) for
(\mathcal{S}_{3,1,3},\mathcal{W}_{3,1,3})
. This implies (
5.84
).

Computation of
(\mathfrak{T}_{4,4},\mathfrak{V}_{4,4})
. The tail cancellation
U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U=\mathcal{O}(\langle y\rangle^{-6})
and (
5.76
) imply using (
5.71
) that

\mathfrak{S}_{4,4}(r)=\mathcal{O}_{r\to\infty}(r^{-4}).

This estimate being the same as the estimate (
3.217
) for
\Sigma_{4,0,4}
in Step 5 of the proof of Proposition
3.11
, we obtain the same estimates (
3.204
) for
(\mathfrak{T}_{4,4},\mathfrak{V}_{4,4})
. This is precisely (
5.85
).

∎

5.5. End of the construction of the corrected multisoliton

Proof of Proposition
5.1
.

Step 1
.
Proof of the pointwise estimates (
5.8
), (
5.9
) and (
5.11
)
. We estimate the right-hand sides of (
5.19
) and (
5.20
). We have by (
3.5
) that

\nu^{2}\phi_{0}+(\frac{1}{16}\Lambda U-L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu}+(\frac{1}{16}\Lambda U+L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}=\sum_{\pm}(1-\chi_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}})\nu^{-2}(\frac{1}{16}\Lambda U-L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})+\nu^{2}\tilde{\phi}_{0}.

Using that
(1-\chi_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}})
is supported for
|z\mp a|\geq\zeta_{*}
and
|\nabla^{k}U(y)|\lesssim\langle y\rangle^{-4-k}
we have

|\sum_{\pm}(1-\chi_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}})\nu^{-2}(\frac{1}{16}\Lambda U-L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})|\lesssim\nu^{2}\langle z\rangle^{C}.

Similarly, since
\Phi_{f}=-(2\pi)^{-1}\log|\cdot|*f
we have

|\Phi_{\sum_{\pm}(\chi^{*}-\chi_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}})\nu^{-2}(\frac{1}{16}\Lambda U-L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})}|\lesssim\nu^{2}\langle\ln\langle z\rangle\rangle.

Combining with (
3.8
) and (
3.9
) this shows

\left|\nu^{2}\phi_{0}+(\frac{1}{16}\Lambda U-L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu}+(\frac{1}{16}\Lambda U+L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}\right|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{\nu^{2}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2}}\langle z\rangle^{C}

(5.95)

and

\left|\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}\nu^{2}\phi_{0}+(\frac{1}{16}\Lambda U-L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu}+(\frac{1}{16}\Lambda U+L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}}\right|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{\nu^{2}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)}\langle z\rangle^{C}.

(5.96)

We have by injecting the estimates of Lemma
5.2
in (
5.38
) using
\ln\langle y\rangle\lesssim\langle y\rangle^{\epsilon}
that for any
0<\epsilon<1
arbitrarily small that

|\tilde{\Xi}|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\sum_{i=0}^{2}\nu^{i}|\mathfrak{T}_{i+2,\pm}(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu})|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\sum_{i=0}^{2}\frac{\nu^{3-\epsilon}}{(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{3-\epsilon-i}}\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{\nu^{3-\epsilon}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{3-\epsilon}}\langle z\rangle^{C}.

(5.97)

and similarly

|\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{\nu^{3-\epsilon}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{1-\epsilon}}\langle z\rangle^{C}.

(5.98)

Injecting (
5.98
) and
|U|\lesssim\langle y\rangle^{-4}
in (
5.21
), then using
\Phi_{f}=-(2\pi)^{-1}\log|\cdot|*f
we find

\bar{\mathfrak{V}}=\Phi_{\left((\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U-8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu}+(\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U+8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}\right)(\chi^{*}-1)}+2\Phi_{\nabla\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}.\nabla\chi^{*}}+\Phi_{\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}\Delta\chi^{*}}=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{2}\langle z\rangle)

(5.99)

Injecting (
5.95
), (
5.96
), (
5.97
), (
5.98
) and (
5.99
) in (
5.19
) and (
5.20
) shows the first inequality in (

) and (

) for
k=0
. The estimates for general

follow similarly as all estimates above adapt to higher order derivatives. The
\nu\partial_{\nu}
and
\partial_{a}
also follow similarly. This shows (
5.8
), (
5.9
) and (

).

Step 2
.
Proof of (
5.7
)
. We define
\mu^{\prime}
by (
5.39
) with
\mu^{\prime\prime}=-8L_{0}(\frac{\alpha}{4}+\beta)
as in Lemma
5.2

\Phi_{(\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U+8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}}(a)=(\nabla(\frac{1}{2}y.\nabla\Phi_{U}+8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}\Phi_{U})(\frac{2a}{\nu})=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{2}).

We have

by (

), and using the estimates of Lemma

with the bound

for any

arbitrarily small we have

\sum_{i=0}^{2}\nu^{i+1}\nabla\mathfrak{V}_{i+2,-}(\frac{2a}{\nu})=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{3-\epsilon}).

Injecting the above estimates in (
5.39
), we obtain that
\mu^{\prime}=\frac{8a}{\nu^{2}+4|a|^{2}}-\beta a+\mathcal{O}(\nu^{2})
as desired.

Step 3
.
Proof of (
5.10
)
. We have by the decomposition (
5.49
):

\displaystyle\int_{|z\mp a|\leq\zeta_{*}}\nu\partial_{\nu}\Xi(z)dz

\displaystyle=\nu\partial_{\nu}\int_{|z\mp a|\leq\zeta_{*}}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,\pm}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu})+\nu\partial_{\nu}\int_{|z\mp a|\leq\zeta_{*}}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,\mp}(\frac{\cdot\pm a}{\nu})+\nu\partial_{\nu}\int_{|z\mp a|\leq\zeta_{*}}\tilde{\Xi}

(5.100)

Using the decomposition (
5.46
) and decomposing onto spherical harmonics, and then performing an explicit computation by changing variables

and using (

), (

), (

), (

) and

Using the decomposition (
5.46
) we find that

\int_{|z\mp a|\leq\zeta_{*}}\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,\mp}(\frac{\cdot\pm a}{\nu})=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{2}).

Using (

Injecting the above estimates in (

5.10
) as desired.

Step 4
.
Estimate for the error
\bar{\Psi}_{0}
generated by the first terms in the correction
. We estimate all terms in the right-hand side of (
5.36
). For the first line we use that for
|y|\ll\nu^{-1}
there hold
|\tilde{G}_{+}|\lesssim\nu^{5}\langle y\rangle^{4}
from Lemma
3.3
and
U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U-8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U=\mathcal{O}(\langle y\rangle^{-6})
to infer that for
z
close to
a
,

\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}(\tilde{G}_{+}.\nabla(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U-8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})=\mathcal{O}(\frac{\nu^{4}}{(\nu+|z-a|)^{3}}).

For
|z-a|\gtrsim 1
we have
\nabla(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U-8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{7}|z-a|^{-6})=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{7}\langle z\rangle^{-6})
, and
\tilde{G}_{+}(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})=\mathcal{O}(\frac{\nu}{\nu+|z+a|})+\mathcal{O}(\nu|z-a|^{3})=\mathcal{O}(\frac{\nu}{\nu+|z+a|})+\mathcal{O}(\nu\langle z\rangle^{3})
by (
3.17
) and
\nabla\Phi_{U}(y)=\mathcal{O}(\langle y\rangle^{-1})
. Hence

\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}(\tilde{G}_{+}.\nabla(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U)(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})=\mathcal{O}(\frac{\nu^{8}\langle z\rangle^{-6}}{\nu+|z+a|})+\mathcal{O}(\nu^{4}\langle z\rangle^{-3})=\mathcal{O}(\frac{\nu^{4}\langle z\rangle^{-2}}{\nu+|z+a|}).

Combining, we obtain

\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}(\tilde{G}_{+}.\nabla(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U-8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})=\mathcal{O}(\frac{\nu^{4}\langle z\rangle}{(\nu+|z+a|)(\nu+|z-a|)^{3}})

(5.101)

For the second line, we use Lemma
3.3
and estimate

\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}(a)+\alpha a-\nu^{2}(\gamma_{1},0)=\frac{1}{\nu}(-\alpha a\nu+\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}\nu^{3}(\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}},0)+\mathcal{O}(\nu^{5}))+\alpha a-\nu^{2}(\frac{\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}}{4\sqrt{2}},0)=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{4}).

Using
\nabla(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U-8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)(y)=\mathcal{O}(\langle y\rangle^{-7})+\mathcal{O}(\nu\langle y\rangle^{-6})
this implies

\frac{1}{\nu^{3}}[\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}(a)+\alpha a-\nu^{2}(\gamma_{1},0)].\nabla(U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U-8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})=\mathcal{O}(\frac{\nu^{8}\langle z\rangle}{\nu^{7}+|z-a|^{7}}).

(5.102)

For the third line, we use
G_{3,+}=\mathcal{O}(\langle y\rangle^{3})
from Lemma
3.3
and
\nabla\partial_{x_{1}}U=\mathcal{O}(\langle y\rangle^{-6})
so that

8L_{0}\alpha^{2}\nu G_{3,+}.\nabla\partial_{x_{1}}U(\frac{\cdot-a}{\nu})=\mathcal{O}(\frac{\nu^{4}}{(\nu+|z-a|)^{3}}).

(5.103)

For the fourth line, we recall the decomposition (
5.27
) and estimate all terms in the right-hand side of this decomposition. For the first line, using
2U+\Lambda U=\mathcal{O}(\langle y\rangle^{-6})
we have
(2U+\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}(a)=(2U+\Lambda U)_{1,\nu}(-a)=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{4})
so that since
U=\mathcal{O}(\langle y\rangle^{-4})
we have

(2U+\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}(a)U_{1,\nu}+(2U+\Lambda U)_{1,\nu}(-a)U_{2,\nu}=\mathcal{O}(\frac{\nu^{6}}{\nu^{4}+|z-a|^{4}})+\mathcal{O}(\frac{\nu^{6}}{\nu^{4}+|z+a|^{4}}).

(5.104)

For the second and third line, we have for
z
close to
-a
that
(U_{1,\nu}-U_{1,\nu}(-a))=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{2}|z+a|)
by Taylor expansion, and for
z
away from
-a
that
(U_{1,\nu}-U_{1,\nu}(-a))=\mathcal{O}(\frac{\nu^{2}}{(\nu+|z-a|)^{4}}\langle z\rangle^{4})
. This implies
(U_{1,\nu}-U_{1,\nu}(-a))=\mathcal{O}(\frac{\nu^{2}|z+a|}{(\nu+|z-a|)^{4}}\langle z\rangle^{3})
for
z\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Analogue estimates can be showed for
(U_{2,\nu}-U_{2,\nu}(a))
,
(\Lambda U)_{1,\nu}-(\Lambda U)_{1,\nu}(-a)
and
(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}-(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}(a)
, producing:

\displaystyle 2(U_{1,\nu}-U_{1,\nu}(-a))(U_{2,\nu}-U_{2,\nu}(a))+(U_{2,\nu}-U_{2,\nu}(a))((\Lambda U)_{1,\nu}-(\Lambda U)_{1,\nu}(-a))

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+(U_{1,\nu}-U_{1,\nu}(-a))((\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}-(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}(a))\ =\mathcal{O}(\frac{\nu^{4}\langle z\rangle^{6}}{(\nu+|z-a|)^{3}(\nu+|z+a|)^{3}}).

(5.105)

Finally, since
U_{1,\nu}(-a),U_{2,\nu}(a),(\Lambda U)_{1,\nu}(-a),(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}(a)=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{2})
we have

-2U_{1,\nu}(-a)U_{2,\nu}(a)-U_{2,\nu}(a)(\Lambda U)_{1,\nu}(-a)-U_{1,\nu}(-a)(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}(a)=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{4}).

(5.106)

Injecting (
5.104
), (
5.105
) and (
5.106
) in (
5.27
) shows

\displaystyle 2U_{1,\nu}U_{2,\nu}+U_{2,\nu}(\Lambda U_{1,\nu}+U_{1,\nu}(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}-U_{2,\nu}(a)(\Lambda U)_{1,\nu}-U_{1,\nu}(-a)(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}

(5.107)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(\frac{\nu^{4}\langle z\rangle^{6}}{(\nu+|z-a|)^{3}(\nu+|z+a|)^{3}}).

For the fifth line, we use (
5.29
) and
\nabla U_{1,\nu}=\mathcal{O}(\frac{\nu^{2}}{(\nu+|z-a|)^{5}})
to get

\displaystyle\nabla U_{1,\nu}.\left(\nabla\Phi_{(\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U+8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}}(z)-\nabla\Phi_{(\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U+8L_{0}\nu\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{2,\nu}}(a)+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{8}\nu^{2}(-3(z_{1}-|a|),z_{2})\right)

(5.108)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad=\mathcal{O}(\frac{\nu^{4}}{(\nu+|z+a|)^{3}(\nu+|z-a|)^{3}}\langle z\rangle^{2}).

For the seventh line, using
U_{2,\nu}(z)=\mathcal{O}(\frac{\nu^{2}}{(\nu+|z+a|)^{4}})
and

we have

\displaystyle 16L_{0}\nu U_{2,\nu}(\partial_{x_{1}}U)_{1,\nu}=\mathcal{O}(\frac{\nu^{6}}{(\nu+|z-a|)^{5}(\nu+|z+a|)^{4}}).

(5.109)

For the eighth line we have that
|\nabla^{k}\bar{\mathfrak{V}}_{2}(z)|\lesssim\nu^{3-\epsilon}
for
z
close to
a
because of (
5.22
), (
5.98
) and elliptic regularity. Hence
|\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}}_{2}(z)-\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}}_{2}(a)|\lesssim\nu^{3-\epsilon}|z-a|
. Combining with (

) and (

\left|\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}}(z)-\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}}(a)-\nabla^{2}\bar{V}_{1}(a)(z-a)+\nabla((\chi^{*}-1)\tilde{\mathcal{V}})\right|\lesssim\nu^{2}(\nu+|z-a|)^{2-\epsilon}.

Using that
|\nabla U_{1,\nu}|\lesssim\nu^{2}(\nu+|z-a|)^{-5}
this implies

\displaystyle\left|\left(\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}}(z)-\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}}(a)-\nabla^{2}\bar{V}_{1}(a)(z-a)+\nabla((\chi^{*}-1)\tilde{\mathcal{V}})\right).\nabla U_{1,\nu}\right|\lesssim\frac{\nu^{4}}{(\nu+|z-a|)^{3-\epsilon}}.

(5.110)

Injecting (
5.101
), (
5.102
), (
5.103
), (
5.107
), (
5.108
) and (

) and their analogue estimates for the symmetric terms, and (

), in (

) shows

|\bar{\Psi}_{0}^{(0)}|\lesssim\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2+k}}\langle z\rangle^{C}

(5.111)

Step 5
.

\displaystyle\lesssim\nu^{i-2}\langle\frac{z\mp a}{\nu}\rangle^{-5}\frac{\nu^{2-i-\epsilon}}{(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{2-i-\epsilon}}|z\pm a|\langle z\rangle^{1-i}+\nu^{i-2}\langle\frac{z\mp a}{\nu}\rangle^{-3+i}\langle\frac{z\pm a}{\nu}\rangle^{-4}

) and (

(5.116)

Step 6
.
Estimate for the error term
\bar{\Psi}_{0}^{(2)}

(5.117)

\displaystyle|\nabla^{k}\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,\pm}(y)|\lesssim|\nabla^{k}\mathfrak{V}_{2,\pm}(y)|+|\nabla^{k}\tilde{V}_{2,\pm}(y)|\lesssim\langle y\rangle^{-3-k}+\langle y\rangle^{-k}\langle\ln\langle y\rangle\rangle\lesssim\langle y\rangle^{\epsilon-k},

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{\nu^{2}\langle z\rangle^{C_{k}}}{(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{1+k}}+\sum_{\pm}\sum_{j=3}^{4}\frac{\nu^{3-\epsilon}}{(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{5-\epsilon+k-j}}\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{\nu^{2}\langle z\rangle^{C_{k}}}{(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{1+k}}.

(5.119)

By (

with

) we have for

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{\nu^{2}|\ln\nu|}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{k-1}}+\sum_{\pm}\sum_{j=3}^{4}\frac{\nu^{3-\epsilon}}{(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{3-\epsilon+k-j}}+\nu^{2-\epsilon}\ \lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{\nu^{2-\epsilon}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{k-1}}.

(5.120)

Using (
5.117
), (
5.118
), (
5.120
) and (
5.99
) we infer that for the first term in (
5.52
):

\displaystyle|\frac{1}{\nu}\nabla\tilde{\mathfrak{T}}_{2,\pm}(\frac{\cdot\mp a}{\nu}).(\nu\nabla\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{2,\mp}(\frac{\cdot\pm a}{\nu})+\nabla\tilde{\mathfrak{V}}_{>2}+\nabla\bar{\mathfrak{V}})|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\frac{\nu^{2}}{(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{3}}\left(\frac{\nu^{2-\epsilon}}{\nu+|z\pm a|}+\nu^{2-\epsilon}+\nu^{2}\right)\lesssim\frac{\nu^{4-\epsilon}}{(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{3}(\nu+|z\pm a|)}

(5.121)

All other terms in (
5.52
) can be estimated by similar computations to the one establishing (
5.121
), using (
5.117
), (
5.118
), (
5.119
), (
5.120
) and (
5.99
), producing eventually the following estimate:

\displaystyle|\bar{\Psi}_{0}^{(2)}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{\nu^{4-\epsilon}\langle z\rangle^{C}}{(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{3}}.

(5.122)

Step 7
.
Proof of (
5.13
), (
5.14
) and (
5.15
)
. The bound (
5.13
) follows directly from injecting (
5.111
), (
5.116
) and (
5.122
) in (
5.59
). Then, the bounds (
5.14
) and (
5.15
) follow directly from (
5.13
).

Step 8
.
Proof of (
5.16
)
. We have by (
5.19
) and (
3.5
):

\mathfrak{U}=U_{1+2,\nu}+8\left(\nu^{2}\tilde{\phi}_{0}(z)+\sum_{\pm}\left(\frac{1}{16\nu^{2}}\Lambda U\mp\frac{L_{0}}{\nu}\partial_{x_{1}}U\right)(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu})(1-\chi_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}}(z))\right)+\tilde{\Xi}.

Hence we have the identity

\displaystyle\nu\partial_{\nu}\mathfrak{U}

\displaystyle=-(\Lambda U)_{1,\nu}-(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}+16\nu^{2}\tilde{\phi}_{0}

\displaystyle+\frac{8}{\nu^{2}}\left(\sum_{\pm}\left(\frac{-1}{16}\Lambda^{2}U\pm L_{0}\nu(y.\nabla+1)(\partial_{x_{1}}U)\right)(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu})(1-\chi_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}}(z))\right)+8\nu^{3}\partial_{\nu}\tilde{\phi}_{0}(z)+\nu\partial_{\nu}\tilde{\Xi}.

We have by (
3.5
) that

\displaystyle-(\Lambda U)_{1,\nu}-(\Lambda U)_{2,\nu}+16\nu^{2}\tilde{\phi}_{0}

\displaystyle=16\phi_{0}-\nu^{2}(L_{0},0).(\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\nabla U_{2,\nu})+\sum_{\pm}\left(\frac{1}{\nu^{2}}\Lambda U\mp\frac{16L_{0}}{\nu}\partial_{x_{1}}U\right)(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu})(\chi_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}}-1).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\nu\partial_{\nu}\mathfrak{U}

\displaystyle=16\phi_{0}-\nu^{2}(L_{0},0).(\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\nabla U_{2,\nu})+8\nu^{3}\partial_{\nu}\tilde{\phi}_{0}(z)+\nu\partial_{\nu}\tilde{\Xi}

(5.123)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\pm}\left(\frac{1}{\nu^{2}}(\Lambda^{2}U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda^{2}U)\mp\frac{8L_{0}}{\nu}(y.\nabla+3)(\partial_{x_{1}}U)\right)(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu})(\chi_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}}(z)-1).

We have using (
5.38
) and Lemma
5.2
with
\langle\log\langle y\rangle\rangle\lesssim\langle y\rangle^{\epsilon}
for any
0<\epsilon\ll 1
arbitrarily small that

\displaystyle|\nabla^{k}\nu\partial_{\nu}\tilde{\Xi}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\sum_{i=0}^{2}\nu^{i}\left|\nabla^{k}\left((i\mathfrak{T}_{i+2,\pm}-y.\nabla\mathfrak{T}_{i+2,\pm})(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu})\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{\nu^{3-\epsilon}}{(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{3-\epsilon-i+k}}\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{\nu^{3-\epsilon}\langle z\rangle^{C_{k}}}{(\nu+|z\mp a|)^{3-\epsilon+k}}.

(5.124)

We have using the tail cancellation
\Lambda U(y)+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda U(y)=\mathcal{O}(\langle y\rangle^{-6})
, that
\partial_{x_{1}}U(y)=\mathcal{O}(\langle y\rangle^{-5})
and that
\chi_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}}-1
is supported inside
\{|z\mp a|\geq\zeta_{*}\}
that

\left|\nabla^{k}\left(\sum_{\pm}\left(\frac{1}{\nu^{2}}(\Lambda^{2}U+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda^{2}U)\mp\frac{8L_{0}}{\nu}(y.\nabla+3)(\partial_{x_{1}}U)\right)(\frac{z\mp a}{\nu})(\chi_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}}(z)-1)\right)\right|\lesssim\nu^{4}\langle z\rangle^{-5-k}.

(5.125)

Injecting the second inequality in (
3.8
), (
5.124
) and (
5.125
) in (
5.123
) shows the desired identity (
5.16
).

∎

6. Approximate blowup solution and bootstrap regime

6.1. Full approximate blowup solution

We now define the approximate solution to the self-similar equation (
1.14
) in the sense that the generated error term is sufficiently small in
L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}
after extracting the leading order terms. It happens that the leading order terms are linked to the approximate eigenfunctions constructed in Proposition
3.1
, from which we can derive appropriate modulation equations driving the law of blowup.

Proposition 6.1

(Approximate blowup solution)
.

Assume that
(\nu,a,\alpha)
are
\mathcal{C}^{1}
maps

(\nu,a,\alpha):[\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\mapsto(0,\nu^{*}]\times\big[2-a^{*},2+a^{*}\big]\times(0,\alpha^{*}],

for
0<\nu^{*},a^{*},\alpha^{*}\ll 1
and the a priori bounds:

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}\Big|\lesssim\frac{1}{|\ln\nu|},\quad\quad|a_{\tau}|\lesssim\nu^{2},\quad|\alpha|\lesssim\nu^{2},\quad|\alpha_{\tau}|\lesssim\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|}.

(6.1)

We define an approximate solution to (
1.14
) by

W[\nu,a,\alpha]=\mathfrak{U}[\nu,a]+\alpha\Big[\phi_{1}-\phi_{0}-\frac{L_{1}-L_{0}}{\nu}\big(\partial_{1}U_{1,\nu}\chi_{a,\zeta_{*},a}-\partial_{1}U_{2,\nu}\chi_{-a,\zeta_{*}}\big)\Big]=U_{1+2,\nu}+\tilde{W},

(6.2)

where
\mathfrak{U}=U_{1+2,\nu}+\Xi
is the improved stationary solution introduced in (
5.5
),
\phi_{0},\phi_{1}
are the two approximate eigenfunctions described in Proposition
3.1
and

\tilde{W}=\Xi+\alpha H,\quad H=\phi_{1}-\phi_{0}-\frac{L_{1}-L_{0}}{\nu}\big(\partial_{1}U_{1,\nu}\chi_{a,\zeta_{*}}-\partial_{1}U_{2,\nu}\chi_{-a,\zeta_{*}}\big).

(6.3)

The error generated by
W
to (
1.14
) is defined by

\displaystyle E=-\partial_{\tau}W+\Delta W-\nabla\cdot(W\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}+\tilde{W}\chi^{*}})-\beta\Lambda W,

(6.4)

that can be decomposed as

\displaystyle E(z,\tau)

\displaystyle=\textup{Mod}_{0}(\tau)\;\phi_{0}(z)+\textup{Mod}_{1}(\tau)\;\phi_{1}(z)+\textup{Mod}_{a}(\tau)\cdot\big(\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\nabla U_{2,\nu}\big)+\Psi(z,\tau),

(6.5)

where

\displaystyle\textup{Mod}_{0}

\displaystyle=-16\nu^{2}\Big(\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}-\frac{\lambda_{0}}{2}\Big)+\alpha_{\tau}-\lambda_{0}\alpha,

(6.6)

\displaystyle\textup{Mod}_{1}

\displaystyle=-\alpha_{\tau}+\lambda_{1}\alpha,\qquad\nu\textup{Mod}_{a}=a_{\tau}+\frac{8a}{\nu^{2}+4|a|^{2}}-\beta a+\hat{\mu},

(6.7)

where
\lambda_{0}
and
\lambda_{1}
are the two approximate eigenvalues given in
3.1
,
L_{0},L_{1}
in (
3.5
) and
\hat{\mu}=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{2})
, and
\Psi
is the remaining small term satisfying

\displaystyle\sum\int_{|z\pm a|\leq\zeta_{*}}\Psi\lesssim\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|},\qquad\|\Psi\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\Big\|\big(\nu+\sum|z\pm a|\big)\nabla\Psi\Big\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\lesssim\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}.

(6.8)

Proof.

We first derive the expression of the generated error
\Psi
by plugging
W=U_{1+2,\nu}+\Xi+H=\mathfrak{U}+H
into the expression (
6.4
) of
E
and using the definition (
5.6
) of
E_{0}
and the definition (
2.11
) of
\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}
to write

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle=-\partial_{\tau}W+\nabla.(\nabla W-W\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}+\tilde{W}\chi^{*}})-\beta\Lambda W

\displaystyle=-\partial_{\tau}\big(\mathfrak{U}+\alpha H\big)+E_{0}+\alpha\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}H-\nabla.\Big(\alpha H\nabla\Phi_{(\Xi+\alpha H)\chi^{*}}+(\Xi+\alpha H))\nabla\Phi_{\alpha H\chi^{*}}\Big).

We recall from (
5.16
) the identity

\displaystyle-\partial_{\tau}\mathfrak{U}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}\nu\partial_{\nu}\mathfrak{U}-a_{\tau}\partial_{a}\mathfrak{U}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}\Big[16\nu^{2}\phi_{0}-\nu^{2}(L_{0},0)\big(\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\nabla U_{2,\nu}\big)+\tilde{\mathfrak{U}}\Big]+\frac{a_{\tau}}{\nu}.\big(\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\nabla U_{2,\nu}\big)-a_{\tau}\partial_{a}\Xi.

and from the expansion (
3.5
) of
\phi_{i}
the relation
H=\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0}
and the identity

\displaystyle-\partial_{\tau}(\alpha H)

\displaystyle=-\alpha_{\tau}H-\alpha\partial_{\tau}H

\displaystyle=-\alpha_{\tau}\Big[\phi_{1}-\phi_{0}-\frac{L_{1}-L_{0}}{\nu}\big(\partial_{1}U_{1,\nu}\chi_{a,\zeta_{*}}-\partial_{1}U_{2,\nu}\chi_{-a,\zeta_{*}}\big)\Big]-\alpha\Big(\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}\nu\partial_{\nu}+a_{\tau}\partial_{a}\Big)(\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0}).

From the expansion (
5.6
) of
E_{0}
and the approximate eigenproblem (
3.1
), we write

\displaystyle E_{0}+\alpha\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}H

\displaystyle=8\lambda_{0}\nu^{2}\phi_{0}+\mu^{\prime}.(\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\nabla U_{2,\nu})+\Psi_{0}+\alpha\big(\lambda_{1}\phi_{1}-\lambda_{0}\phi_{0}+R_{1}-R_{0}\big)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\alpha}{\nu}(L_{1}-L_{0})\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}\big(\partial_{1}U_{1,\nu}\chi_{a,\zeta_{*}}-\partial_{1}U_{2,\nu}\chi_{-a,\zeta_{*}}\big).

Plugging all these identities together with
H=\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0}
, we then obtain

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle=(\alpha_{\tau}-16\nu_{\tau}\nu+8\lambda_{0}\nu^{2}-\alpha\lambda_{0})\phi_{0}-(\alpha_{\tau}-\alpha\lambda_{1})\phi_{1}

\displaystyle\quad+\Big(\frac{a_{\tau}}{\nu}+\nu_{\tau}\nu(L_{0},0)+\frac{\alpha_{\tau}}{\nu}(L_{1}-L_{0},0)+\mu^{\prime}\Big).\big(\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\nabla U_{2,\nu}\big)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}\tilde{\mathfrak{U}}-a_{\tau}\partial_{a}\Xi-\alpha\Big(\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}\nu\partial_{\nu}+a_{\tau}\partial_{a}\Big)(\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0})+\Psi_{0}+\alpha(R_{1}-R_{0})

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\alpha_{\tau}}{\nu}(L_{1}-L_{0})\big(\partial_{1}U_{1,\nu}(1-\chi_{a,\zeta_{*}})-\partial_{1}U_{2,\nu}(1-\chi_{-a,\zeta_{*}})\big)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\alpha}{\nu}(L_{1}-L_{0})\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}\big(\partial_{1}U_{1,\nu}\chi_{a,\zeta_{*}}-\partial_{1}U_{2,\nu}\chi_{-a,\zeta_{*}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad-\nabla.\Big(\alpha(\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0})\nabla\Phi_{(\Xi+\alpha(\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0}))\chi^{*}}+(\Xi+\alpha(\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0}))\nabla\Phi_{\alpha(\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0})\chi^{*}}\Big)

\displaystyle=\textup{Mod}_{0}\phi_{0}+\textup{Mod}_{1}\phi_{1}+\textup{Mod}_{a}.\big(\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\nabla U_{2,\nu}\big)+\Psi.

Here,
\textup{Mod}_{0}
,
\textup{Mod}_{1}
and
\textup{Mod}_{a}
are given by (
6.6
) and (
6.7
) with the precise definition thanks to (
5.7
) and the estimates
|L_{0}|\lesssim 1,|L_{1}|\lesssim|\ln\nu|
(see Proposition
3.1
)

\hat{\mu}=\tilde{\mu}^{\prime}+\nu_{\tau}\nu(L_{0},0)+\frac{\alpha_{\tau}}{\nu}(L_{1}-L_{0},0)=\mathcal{O}(\nu^{2}),

and

\displaystyle\Psi

\displaystyle=-\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}\tilde{\mathfrak{U}}-a_{\tau}\partial_{a}\Xi-\alpha\Big(\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}\nu\partial_{\nu}+a_{\tau}\partial_{a}\Big)(\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0})+\Psi_{0}+\alpha(R_{1}-R_{0})

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\alpha_{\tau}}{\nu}(L_{1}-L_{0})\big(\partial_{1}U_{1,\nu}(1-\chi_{a,\zeta_{*}})-\partial_{1}U_{2,\nu}(1-\chi_{-a,\zeta_{*}})\big)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\alpha}{\nu}(L_{1}-L_{0})\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}\big(\partial_{1}U_{1,\nu}\chi_{a,\zeta_{*}}-\partial_{1}U_{2,\nu}\chi_{-a,\zeta_{*}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad-\nabla.\Big(\alpha(\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0})\nabla\Phi_{(\Xi+\alpha(\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0}))\chi^{*}}+(\Xi+\alpha(\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0}))\nabla\Phi_{\alpha(\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0})\chi^{*}}\Big).

We now estimate all terms involved in the expression of
\Psi
. From the pointwise estimate (
5.17
) and the a prior estimate
|\nu_{\tau}/\nu|\lesssim 1/|\ln\nu|
, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}\tilde{\mathfrak{U}}\Big|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{\nu^{2}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2}|\ln\nu|}\Big(\frac{\nu^{\delta_{0}}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{\delta_{0}}}+\frac{1}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\Big),

from which we estimate

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{|z\pm a|\leq\zeta_{*}}\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}\tilde{\mathfrak{U}}(z)dz\Big|\lesssim\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|}\int_{|y|\leq\zeta_{*}/\nu}\frac{1}{\langle y\rangle^{2}}\Big(\frac{1}{\langle y\rangle^{\delta_{0}}}+\frac{1}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\Big)dy\lesssim\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|},

and

\displaystyle\Big\|\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}\tilde{\mathfrak{U}}\Big\|_{\omega_{\nu}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\int\sum_{\pm}\frac{\nu^{4}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{4}}\Big(\frac{\nu^{2\delta_{0}}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2\delta_{0}}}+\frac{1}{|\ln\nu|^{4}}\Big)(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{4}e^{-\beta|z|^{2}}dz

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\nu^{6}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\int\Big(\frac{1}{\langle y\rangle^{2\delta_{0}}}+\frac{1}{|\ln\nu|^{4}}e^{-\beta\nu^{2}|y|^{2}}\Big)dy\lesssim\frac{\nu^{6}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\big(\nu^{-2+2\delta_{0}}+\frac{\nu^{-2}}{|\ln\nu|^{4}}\big)\lesssim\frac{\nu^{4}}{|\ln\nu|^{6}}.

From (
5.9
) and the a prior estimate
|a_{\tau}|\lesssim\nu^{2}
, we have

\big|a_{\tau}\partial_{a}\Xi\big|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{\nu^{4}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{3}}\langle z\rangle^{C_{0}},

which gives

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{|z\pm a|\leq\zeta_{*}}a_{\tau}\partial_{a}\Xi dz\Big|\lesssim\nu^{3}\int_{|y|\leq\frac{\zeta_{*}}{\nu}}\frac{1}{\langle y\rangle^{3}}dy\lesssim\nu^{3},

and

\displaystyle\Big\|a_{\tau}\partial_{a}\Xi\Big\|^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\nu^{4}\int\sum_{\pm}\frac{\nu^{4}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{6}}\langle z\rangle^{2C_{0}}(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{4}e^{-\beta|z|^{2}}dz

\displaystyle\lesssim\nu^{8}\int\langle y\rangle^{-2}\langle\nu y\rangle^{2C_{0}}e^{-\beta\nu^{2}|y|^{2}}dy\lesssim\nu^{8}|\ln\nu|.

From the pointwise estimates (
3.8
) and (
3.10
), and the a prior bounds (
6.1
), we have

\displaystyle\Big|\alpha\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}\nu\partial_{\nu}(\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0})\Big|\lesssim\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|}\sum_{\pm}\frac{\langle z\rangle^{C_{0}}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2}}\Big(\frac{\nu^{\delta_{0}}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{\delta_{0}}}+\frac{1}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\Big),

which is the same pointwise estimate as for
\nu_{\tau}/\nu\tilde{\mathfrak{U}}
, hence,

\Big|\int_{|z\pm a|\leq\zeta_{*}}\alpha\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}\nu\partial_{\nu}(\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0})dz\Big|\lesssim\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|},\quad\big\|\alpha\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}\nu\partial_{\nu}(\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0})\big\|^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}\lesssim\frac{\nu^{4}}{|\ln\nu|^{6}}.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\alpha a_{\tau}\partial_{a}(\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0})\Big|\lesssim\nu^{4}\sum_{\pm}\frac{\langle z\rangle^{C_{0}}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{3}},

which is the same pointwise estimate as
a_{\tau}\partial_{a}\Xi
, hence,

\Big|\int_{|z\pm a|\leq\zeta_{*}}\alpha a_{\tau}\partial_{a}(\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0})dz\Big|\lesssim\nu^{3},\quad\big\|\alpha a_{\tau}\partial_{a}(\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0})\big\|^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}\lesssim\nu^{8}|\ln\nu|.

From (
5.14
) and (
5.15
), we have already obtained the estimates

\Big|\int_{|z\pm a|\leq\zeta_{*}}\Psi_{0}dz\Big|\lesssim\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|},\quad\|\Psi_{0}\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\lesssim\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}.

From (
3.14
) and
|\alpha|\lesssim\nu^{2}
, we have already obtained the partial mass estimate

\Big|\int\alpha(R_{1}-R_{0})\Big|\lesssim\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|}.

Using the pointwise estimate (
3.14
), we estimate

\|\alpha(R_{1}-R_{0})\|^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}\lesssim\frac{\nu^{4}}{|\ln\nu|^{4}}\int\sum_{\pm}\frac{\langle z\rangle^{2C_{0}}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{4}}(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{4}e^{-\beta|z|^{2}}dz\lesssim\frac{\nu^{4}}{|\ln\nu|^{4}}.

Using the pointwise estimates (
5.8
), (
3.8
), (
3.9
), and the a priori bound
|\alpha|\lesssim\nu^{2}
, we get

\displaystyle\|-\nabla.\Big(\alpha(\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0})\nabla\Phi_{(\Xi+\alpha(\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0}))\chi^{*}}+(\Xi+\alpha(\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0}))\nabla\Phi_{\alpha(\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0})\chi^{*}}\Big)\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\nu^{4}\left\|\sum\frac{\langle z\rangle^{C}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{4}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}+\nu^{3}\left\|\sum\frac{\langle z\rangle^{C}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{3}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\lesssim\nu^{3}+\nu^{3}\sqrt{|\ln\nu|}.

Using the divergence structure and the pointwise estimates (
3.8
), (
3.9
), (
5.8
),

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}-\nabla.\Big(\alpha(\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0})\nabla\Phi_{(\Xi+\alpha(\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0}))\chi^{*}}+(\Xi+\alpha(\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0}))\nabla\Phi_{\alpha(\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0})\chi^{*}}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{|z\pm a|\sim\zeta_{*}}\Big|\alpha(\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0})\nabla\Phi_{(\Xi+\alpha(\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0}))\chi^{*}}+(\Xi+\alpha(\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0}))\nabla\Phi_{\alpha(\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{0})\chi^{*}}\Big|\lesssim\nu^{4}|\ln\nu|.

Collecting all the above estimates yields the desired estimates (
6.8
). This ends the proof of Proposition
6.1
.
∎

6.2. Formulation of the problem and bootstrap regime

We formulate the problem of constructing solutions to (
1.1
) that blow up in finite time by a collision of two single-solitons. In terms of the self-similarity variables (
1.14
), we introduce the first linearization

\displaystyle w(z,\tau)=U_{1+2,\nu}(z)+\tilde{\varepsilon}(z,\tau),

(6.9)

where
\tilde{\varepsilon}
solves

\partial_{\tau}\tilde{\varepsilon}=\mathscr{L}^{z}\tilde{\varepsilon}-\nabla\cdot(\tilde{\varepsilon}\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{\varepsilon}})+\tilde{E},

(6.10)

with

\tilde{E}=-(\partial_{\tau}+\beta\Lambda)U_{1+2,\nu}-\nabla\cdot(U_{1,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{U_{2,\nu}}+U_{2,\nu}\nabla\Phi_{U_{1,\nu}}).

(6.11)

We use this equation to estimate the outer part
\tilde{\varepsilon}(1-\chi^{*})
and the contribution of the Poision field
\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{\varepsilon}(1-\chi^{*})}
, namely we decompose

\tilde{\varepsilon}=\tilde{\varepsilon}\chi^{*}+\varepsilon(1-\chi^{*}),\quad\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{\varepsilon}}=\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{\varepsilon}\chi^{*}}+\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{\varepsilon}(1-\chi^{*})}.

(6.12)

For the inner part, we use the refined approximate solution
W
introduced in Proposition
6.1
to introduce the second linearization

w(z,\tau)=W[\nu,a,\alpha](z)+\varepsilon(z,\tau)=U_{1+2,\nu}(z)+\tilde{W}[\nu,a,\alpha](z)+\varepsilon(z,\tau),

(6.13)

where
\nu,a,\alpha
are parameter functions to be determined,
W
and
\tilde{W}
are defined by (
6.2
) and (
6.3
). We thus have the relation

\tilde{\varepsilon}=\tilde{W}+\varepsilon,

where
\varepsilon
solves the linearized problem

\partial_{\tau}\varepsilon=\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}\varepsilon-L(\varepsilon)-NL(\varepsilon)+E,

(6.14)

where
\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}
is the linearized operator around
U_{1+2,\nu}
with a cut-off of the Poison field introduced in (
4.62
),
L(\varepsilon)
is the small linear term

L\varepsilon=\nabla\cdot\Big(\varepsilon\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{W}\chi^{*}}+\tilde{W}\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon\chi^{*}}\Big)+\nabla\cdot\big(\tilde{W}\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{\varepsilon}(1-\chi^{*})}\big),

(6.15)

and
NL(\varepsilon)
is the nonlinear term

NL(\varepsilon)=\nabla\cdot\big(\varepsilon\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon\chi^{*}}+\varepsilon\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{\varepsilon}(1-\chi^{*})}\big),

(6.16)

and
E
is defined in (
6.4
).

Our aim is to construct a global in time solution
\varepsilon
to (
6.14
) though a robust energy estimate technique. Recall from Proposition
4.11
that the localized linear operator
\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}
is coercive under the adapted scalar product
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\ast}
introduced in (
1.18
). It’s natural to control the remainder through the adapted norm

\|\varepsilon\|_{\ast}^{2}=\langle\varepsilon,\varepsilon\rangle_{\ast}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\omega_{\nu}dz-c_{\ast}\nu^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\chi^{*}\varepsilon\Phi_{\chi^{*}\varepsilon}dz,\quad c_{\ast}=32e^{-1}.

(6.17)

It is proved to be coercive once we impose the orthogonality conditions:

\langle\varepsilon,1\rangle_{L^{2}_{loc,\pm}}=\langle\varepsilon,\Lambda U_{i,\nu}\rangle_{L^{2}_{loc,\pm}}=\langle\varepsilon,\partial_{j}U_{i,\nu}\rangle_{L^{2}_{loc,\pm}}=0,\quad i,j=1,2,

(6.18)

where we use the notation

\langle f,g\rangle_{L^{2}_{loc,\pm}}=\int_{|z\mp a|<\eta}fgdz,\quad\textup{for some fixed constant $0<\eta\ll 1$.}

The orthogonality condition (
6.18
) ensures the coercivity estimate (see Proposition
4.8
)

\|\varepsilon\|_{\ast}^{2}=\langle\varepsilon,\varepsilon\rangle_{\ast}\gtrsim\|\varepsilon\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}.

(6.19)

In order to close the modulation equations for the parameters
\nu,a,\alpha
and to close the nonlinear analysis, we need an estimate of
\nabla\varepsilon
as well. To do so, we split the control of
\varepsilon
into three parts:

- the inner zone
B_{\zeta_{*}}(a)\cup B_{\zeta_{*}}(-a)
with a fixed constant
0<\zeta_{*}\ll 1
: we introduce

q^{\textup{in}}_{\pm}(y_{\pm},\tau)=\nu^{2}\varepsilon(z,\tau)\chi_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}}(z),\qquad y_{\pm}=\frac{z\mp a}{\nu},

(6.20)

and the
H^{1}
inner norm

\|q(\tau)\|^{2}_{H^{1}_{\textup{in}}}=\sum_{\pm}\Big(\langle-\mathscr{L}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}_{\pm},\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}_{\pm}\rangle+\frac{\nu^{2}}{\zeta_{*}^{2}}\langle q^{\textup{in}}_{\pm},\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}_{\pm}\rangle\Big)\gtrsim\sum_{\pm}\Big(\int\frac{|\nabla q^{\textup{in}}_{\pm}|^{2}}{U(y_{\pm})}+\frac{\nu^{2}}{\zeta_{*}^{2}}\int\frac{|q^{\textup{in}}_{\pm}|^{2}}{U(y_{\pm})}\Big),

(6.21)

where the coercivity is archived by the orthogonality condition (
6.18
) (see Lemma
4.5
). We also need a round bound of
L^{\infty}
, for which we introduce

q_{2,\pm}^{\textup{in}}=\mathscr{L}_{0}q_{\pm}^{\textup{in}},

(6.22)

and further decompose

q_{2,\pm}^{\textup{in}}(y_{\pm},\tau)=c_{1}\Lambda U(y_{\pm})+c_{2,1}\partial_{1}U(y_{\pm})+c_{2,2}\partial_{2}U(y_{\pm})+\tilde{q}_{2,\pm}^{\textup{in}}(y_{\pm}),

(6.23)

where
c_{1}
and
c_{2,1}
,
c_{2,2}
are chosen so that

\tilde{q}_{2,\pm}^{\textup{in}}\perp_{L^{2}}\{\Lambda U(y_{\pm}),\nabla U(y_{\pm})\},

to ensure the coercivity (see Lemma
4.5
)

\|\tilde{q}(\tau)\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{\textup{in}}}=\sum_{\pm}\langle\tilde{q}_{2,\pm}^{\textup{in}},\mathscr{M}_{0}\tilde{q}_{2,\pm}^{\textup{in}}\rangle\gtrsim\sum_{\pm}\int\frac{|\tilde{q}_{2,\pm}^{\textup{in}}|^{2}}{U(y_{\pm})}dy_{\pm}.

(6.24)

The coefficients

|c_{1}|+|c_{2,1}|+|c_{2,2}|\lesssim\|q(\tau)\|_{H^{1}_{\textup{in}}},

(6.25)

together with
H^{2}_{\textup{in}}
-norm and Sobolev inequality yield a round
L^{\infty}
bound of
q^{\textup{in}}_{\pm}
which is enough for the nonlinear analysis.

- the intermediate zone
B_{\zeta^{*}}\setminus\{B_{\zeta_{*}/2}(a)\cup B_{\zeta_{*}/2}(-a)\}
with a fixed constant
\zeta^{*}\geq 10
: we use the parabolic regularity to control

\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{bd}=\sum_{\pm}\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{H^{2}(B_{\zeta^{*}}\setminus B_{\zeta_{*}/2}(\pm a))}.

(6.26)

- for the far away zone
\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus B_{\zeta^{*}}
, we simply use the maximum principle to obtain the bound

\|\tilde{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{\textup{ex}}=\big\|\tilde{\varepsilon}(1-\chi_{\zeta^{*}})|z|^{\frac{3}{2}}\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

(6.27)

Definition 6.2

(Bootstrap regime)
.

Let
\tau_{0}\gg 1
,
\bar{K}\gg 1
,
K\gg 1
,
0<\zeta_{*},1/\zeta^{*}\ll 1
. For
\tau\geq\tau_{0}
, we define the set
\mathcal{S}(\tau)
of all functions
\varepsilon
for which the following holds:

i)

(Modulation parameters) There exist
\mathcal{C}^{1}
functions
\nu(\tau),a(\tau)
and
\alpha(\tau)
satisfying

|\alpha-8\nu^{2}|\leq\bar{K}\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|}.

(6.28)

\left|\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}-\frac{\beta\gamma_{1}}{\ln\nu}\right|\leq\frac{\bar{K}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}},

(6.29)

\Big|a-(2,0)\Big|\leq\bar{K}\nu,

(6.30)

ii)

(Control of the remainder)

\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\leq K\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}},

(6.31)

\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{bd}\leq K^{2}\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}.

(6.32)

\|q(\tau)\|_{H^{1}_{\textup{in}}}\leq K^{3}\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}.

(6.33)

\|\tilde{q}(\tau)\|_{H^{2}_{\textup{in}}}\leq K^{4}\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}.

(6.34)

\|\tilde{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{\textup{ex}}\leq K^{5}\nu^{2}.

(6.35)

6.3. Modulation equations

Lemma 6.3

(Modulation equation)
.

We have

\big|\textup{Mod}_{0}\big|\lesssim\|q(\tau)\|_{H^{1}_{\textup{in}}}+\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}+\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}},

(6.36)

\big|\textup{Mod}_{1}\big|\lesssim\frac{1}{|\ln\nu|}\Big(\|q(\tau)\|_{H^{1}_{\textup{in}}}+\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}+\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|}\Big),

(6.37)

\big|\nu\textup{Mod}_{a}\big|\lesssim\|q(\tau)\|_{H^{1}_{\textup{in}}}+\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}+\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}.

(6.38)

Proof.

\textup{Mod}_{1}
equation:
We first derive (
6.37
) by writing from the local orthogonality condition (
6.18
) and equation (
6.14
) of
\varepsilon
,

\displaystyle 0=\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}\partial_{\tau}\varepsilon dz=\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}\big(\tilde{\mathscr{L}}\varepsilon-L(\varepsilon)-NL(\varepsilon)+E\big)dz.

Using the divergent structure of
\tilde{\mathscr{L}}(\varepsilon),L(\varepsilon),NL(\varepsilon)
defined in (
4.62
), (
6.15
), (
6.16
) to get the estimate

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}\tilde{\mathscr{L}}\varepsilon-L(\varepsilon)-NL(\varepsilon)\Big|\lesssim\int_{|z\pm a|\sim\zeta_{*}}\Big|\nabla\varepsilon-\varepsilon\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}-U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}\varepsilon}+U_{1+2,\nu}\varepsilon-\beta z\varepsilon\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{|z\pm a|\sim\zeta_{*}}\Big|\varepsilon\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{W}\chi^{*}}+\tilde{W}\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon\chi^{*}}+\tilde{W}\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{\varepsilon}(1-\chi^{*})}\Big|+\int_{|z\pm a|\sim\zeta_{*}}\Big|\varepsilon\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon\chi^{*}}+\varepsilon\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{\varepsilon}(1-\chi^{*})}\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{|z\pm a|\sim\zeta_{*}}|\nabla\varepsilon|+\|U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}\varepsilon}+\tilde{W}\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon\chi^{*}}\|_{L^{\infty}(|z\pm a|\sim\zeta_{*})}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\Big(\|\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+U_{1+2,\nu}+\beta z+\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{W}\chi^{*}}+\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon\chi^{*}}+\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{\varepsilon}(1-\chi^{*})}\|_{L^{\infty}(|z\pm a|\sim\zeta_{*})}\Big)\int_{|z\pm a|\sim\zeta_{*}}|\varepsilon|.

Using the rough estimates (
7.8
), (
7.9
), (
7.11
),
|U_{1+2,\nu}(z)|\lesssim\nu^{2}
and
|\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}|\lesssim 1
for
|z\pm a|\sim\zeta_{*}
and the estimate

|\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}\varepsilon}(z)|\lesssim\int|\ln|z-z^{\prime}|\;\nabla(\varepsilon\chi^{*})|\lesssim\frac{|\ln\nu|}{\nu}\big(\|q(\tau)\|_{H^{1}_{\textup{in}}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{bd}\big)\lesssim K^{3}\nu|\ln\nu|.

to get

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}\tilde{\mathscr{L}}\varepsilon-L(\varepsilon)-NL(\varepsilon)\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{|z\pm a|\sim\zeta_{*}}\big(|\nabla\varepsilon|+|\varepsilon|\big)+\nu^{2}\|\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon\chi^{*}}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|q(\tau)\|_{H^{1}_{\textup{in}}}+\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}+K^{3}\nu^{3}|\ln\nu|.

(6.39)

For the error term, we use the decomposition (
6.5
) and (
6.8
) to get

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}Edz\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}\Big(\textup{Mod}_{0}\phi_{0}+\textup{Mod}_{1}\phi_{1}\Big)dz\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\mathcal{O}\Big(\big|\textup{Mod}_{a}\big|\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{|z\pm a|\sim\zeta_{*}}\big(|U_{i,\nu}|+|\Xi|\big)dz+\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|}\Big)

\displaystyle=\Big|\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}\Big(\textup{Mod}_{0}\phi_{0}+\textup{Mod}_{1}\phi_{1}\Big)dz\Big|+\mathcal{O}\Big(\nu^{2}\big|\textup{Mod}_{a}\big|+\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|}\Big).

Using
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Lambda U=0
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\partial_{1}U=0
, the decomposition (
4.193
) and
(
4.194
), we obtain the bound

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}\phi_{0}(z)dz\Big|\lesssim\Big|\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}\Big[\frac{1}{\nu^{2}}\Lambda U_{\nu}(z\pm a)\pm\frac{L_{0}}{\nu}\partial_{1}U_{\nu}(z\pm a)\Big]dz\Big|+\Big|\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}\check{\phi}_{0}(z)dz\Big|\lesssim 1.

From (
3.106
) and the fact that the term
T_{2}^{(1)}
consists of a radial component
T_{2,0}^{(1)}
which behaves like
\frac{1}{|y_{\pm}|^{2}}
for
|y_{\pm}|\gg 1
, combined with the cancellation
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Lambda U=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\partial_{1}U=0
, we end up with the estimate

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}\phi_{1}(z)dz\Big|=\Big|\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}\frac{1}{\nu^{2}}T_{2,0}^{(i)}\big(\frac{z\pm a}{\nu}\big)dz\Big|+\mathcal{O}(1)\sim|\ln\nu|.

Collecting all the estimates yields

\big|\textup{Mod}_{1}\big|\lesssim\frac{1}{|\ln\nu|}\Big(\|q(\tau)\|_{H^{1}_{\textup{in}}}+\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}+\big|\textup{Mod}_{0}\big|+\nu^{2}\big|\textup{Mod}_{a}\big|+\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|}\Big),

(6.40)

which concludes the proof of (
6.37
).

\textup{Mod}_{0}
equation:
The derivation of (
6.36
) is proceeded similarly by writing from the local orthogonality (
6.18
) and equation (
6.14
) of
\varepsilon
,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{d\tau}\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}\varepsilon\Lambda U_{\nu}(z\pm a)dz=\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}\big(\tilde{\mathscr{L}}\varepsilon-L(\varepsilon)-NL(\varepsilon)+E\big)\Lambda U_{\nu}(z\pm a)dz

\displaystyle+\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}\varepsilon\nu\partial_{\nu}\Lambda U_{\nu}(z\pm a)dz\pm a_{\tau}\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}\varepsilon\nabla_{a}\Lambda U_{\nu}(z\pm a)dz.

For the main linear term, small linear and nonlinear terms, we integrate by parts and estimate similarly as in (
6.39
), then use Cauchy-Schwarz,

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}\big(\tilde{\mathscr{L}}\varepsilon-L(\varepsilon)-NL(\varepsilon)\big)\Lambda U_{\nu}(z\pm a)dz\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\int_{|z\pm a|\sim\zeta_{*}}\big(|\nabla\varepsilon|+|\varepsilon|+\nu^{2}\|\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon\chi^{*}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big)|\Lambda U_{\nu}(z\pm a)|dz

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}\big(|\nabla\varepsilon|+|\varepsilon|+\nu^{2}\|\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon\chi^{*}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big)|\nabla_{a}\Lambda U_{\nu}(z\pm a)|dz

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\nu^{2}\Big(\|q(\tau)\|_{H^{1}_{\textup{in}}}+\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}+K^{3}\nu^{3}|\ln\nu|\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\nu^{-4}\Big(\|q(\tau)\|_{H^{1}_{\textup{in}}}+\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}+K^{3}\nu^{3}|\ln\nu|\Big),

where we used
\omega_{\nu}(z)\sim\sum_{\pm}(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{4}
for
|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}
and the estimate

\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}|\nabla\Lambda U_{\nu}(z\pm a)|^{2}\omega_{\nu}^{-1}dz\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}\frac{\nu^{4}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{10}}\frac{1}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{4}}dz\lesssim\nu^{-8}.

The time derivative terms are estimated by Cauchy-Schwarz,

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}\varepsilon\nu\partial_{\nu}\Lambda U_{\nu}(z\pm a)dz\pm a_{\tau}\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}\varepsilon\nabla_{z}\Lambda U_{\nu}(z\pm a)dz\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big|\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}\Big|\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\Big(\sum_{\pm}\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}|\Lambda^{2}U_{\nu}|^{2}\omega_{\nu}^{-1}dz\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}+|a_{\tau}|\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\Big(\sum_{\pm}\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}|\nabla_{a}\Lambda U_{\nu}|^{2}\omega_{\nu}^{-1}dz\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\Big(\Big|\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}\Big|\nu^{-3}+|a_{\tau}|\nu^{-4}\Big).

For the error term, we recall from (
3.5
) the estimate for
|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}
,

\phi_{i}(z)\sim\sum\frac{1}{\nu^{2}}\Lambda U_{\nu}(z\pm a)+\sum\frac{1}{\nu}\partial_{1}U_{\nu}(z\pm a)\sim\sum\frac{1}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{4}}+\sum\frac{\nu^{2}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{5}}.

We then estimate

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}E\Lambda U_{\nu}(z\pm a)dz\Big|=\sum_{i=0}^{1}\Big|\textup{Mod}_{i}\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}\phi_{i}\Lambda U_{\nu}(z\pm a)dz\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathcal{O}\Big(\big|\textup{Mod}_{a}\big|\sum_{i=0}^{1}\sum_{\pm}\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}\big(|U_{i,\nu}|+|\Xi|\big)|\nabla\Lambda U_{\nu}(z\pm a)|dz+\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}|\Psi||\Lambda U_{\nu}(z\pm a)|dz\Big)

\displaystyle\sim\sum_{i=0}^{1}\big|\textup{Mod}_{i}\big|\Big(\sum_{\pm}\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}\frac{\nu^{2}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{8}}dz+\sum_{\pm}\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}\frac{\nu^{4}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{9}}dz\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathcal{O}\Big(\big|\textup{Mod}_{a}\big|\sum_{\pm}\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}\frac{\nu^{4}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{9}}dz+\|\Psi\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\Big(\sum_{\pm}\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}\frac{\nu^{4}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{12}}dz\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\sim\nu^{-4}\sum_{i=0}^{1}\big|\textup{Mod}_{i}\big|+\mathcal{O}\Big(\nu^{-3}\big|\textup{Mod}_{a}\big|+\nu^{-3}\|\Psi\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\Big).

A collection of the above estimates and using the bounds (
6.8
),
|\nu_{\tau}/\nu|\lesssim 1/|\ln\nu|,|a_{\tau}|\lesssim\nu
yield

\sum_{i=0}^{1}\big|\textup{Mod}_{i}\big|\lesssim\|q(\tau)\|_{H^{1}_{\textup{in}}}+\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}+\nu\big|\textup{Mod}_{a}\big|+K^{3}\nu^{3}|\ln\nu|.

(6.41)

\textup{Mod}_{a}
equation:
We now prove the equation (
6.38
). The derivation of (
6.38
) is entirely similar as for (
6.41
) and (
6.40
) by using the orthogonality (
6.18
) and equation (
6.14
), integration by parts and Cauchy-Schwarz. We omit the detail here and only write the outcome estimate

\nu\big|\textup{Mod}_{a}\big|\lesssim\|q(\tau)\|_{H^{1}_{\textup{in}}}+\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}+\sum_{i=0}^{1}\big|\textup{Mod}_{i}\big|+\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}.

(6.42)

Putting together the three estimates (
6.40
), (
6.41
) and (
6.42
) yields desired equations and concludes the proof of Lemma
6.3
.
∎

7. Analysis in the bootstrap regime

7.1. Matched-scalar product based energy estimate

7.1.1. Further decomposition and energy identity

The orthogonality condition (
6.18
) also allows us to derive a dynamical system driving the law of blowup as in Lemma
6.3
below. However, such a local condition (
6.18
) is not enough, at the linear level, to control
\varepsilon
using an energy estimate due to the modulation terms which do not satisfy a suitable estimate (the Mod terms appearing in (
6.5
)). To overcome this issue, we use the projection (
4.168
) and decompose

\varepsilon=\hat{\varepsilon}+a_{0}\phi_{0},

(7.1)

where
\hat{\varepsilon}
satisfies the orthogonality in the self-similar zone

\langle\hat{\varepsilon},\phi_{0}\rangle_{\ast}=0.

(7.2)

The uniqueness and the properties of this decomposition can be found in Proposition
4.12
, and imply the estimates

|a_{0}|\lesssim\min\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\ln\nu}}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}},\frac{1}{|\ln\nu|}\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\right),

(7.3)

and the equivalence

\|\varepsilon\|^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}\approx\langle\varepsilon,\varepsilon\rangle_{\ast}\approx\langle\hat{\varepsilon},\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}.

(7.4)

We compute

\frac{d}{d\tau}\langle\hat{\varepsilon},\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{*}=\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}\nu\partial_{\nu}(\langle\hat{\varepsilon},\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{*})+\frac{a_{\tau}}{\nu}\nu\partial_{a}(\langle\hat{\varepsilon},\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{*})+2\langle\widehat{\partial_{\tau}\varepsilon},\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{*},

(we remark that
\widehat{\partial_{\tau}\varepsilon}
is the projection of
\partial_{\tau}\varepsilon
, so
\widehat{\partial_{\tau}\varepsilon}\neq\partial_{\tau}\hat{\varepsilon}
), and use the estimate (
4.172
) to bound the first term by

\left|\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}\nu\partial_{\nu}(\langle\hat{\varepsilon},\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{*})+\frac{a_{\tau}}{\nu}\nu\partial_{a}(\langle\hat{\varepsilon},\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{*})\right|\lesssim\frac{|\nu_{\tau}|+|a_{\tau}|}{\nu}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}^{2},

and the orthogonality (
4.169
) to obtain that
\langle\widehat{\partial_{\tau}\varepsilon},\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{*}=\langle\partial_{\tau}\varepsilon,\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{*}
. Combining and injecting (
6.16
) and (
4.174
), we arrive at the following energy identity for the adapted norm of the projection:

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{d\tau}\langle\hat{\varepsilon},\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{*}+O\left(\frac{|\nu_{\tau}|+|a_{\tau}|}{\nu}\langle\hat{\varepsilon},\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{*}\right)=\langle\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}\varepsilon-L(\varepsilon)-NL(\varepsilon)+E,\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{*}.

(7.5)

Thanks to the global orthogonality (
7.2
), we can eliminate the Mod terms in
E
once performing a standard energy estimate. Once the energy estimate for
\hat{\varepsilon}
is established, we can directly derive a similar estimate for
\varepsilon
thanks to Proposition
4.12
.

7.1.2.
L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}
energy estimate

This section is devoted to the main energy estimate in
L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}
, where we crucially rely on the equivalence (
7.4
), the global coercivity of the linearized operator for the adapted scalar product (
4.127
) to gain the dissipation, and the global orthogonality (
7.2
) to cancel out the Mod terms in
E
. We claim the following.

Lemma 7.1

(
L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}
-estimate)
.

Let
\varepsilon
be a solution to (
6.14
) and satisfy the bootstrap bounds in Definition
6.2
for
\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]
. Then, we have the following estimate for
\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]
:

\frac{d}{d\tau}\langle\hat{\varepsilon},\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}\leq-\delta_{0}\langle\hat{\varepsilon},\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}+C\Big(|\textup{Mod}_{1}|+\nu^{2}|\textup{Mod}_{a}|+\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\Big)^{2}.

(7.6)

for some
\delta_{0}>0
, and
C=C(\zeta_{*},\zeta^{*})>0
.

Proof.

We estimate all the terms in the right hand side of (
7.5
). As for the main linear term, we recall from Proposition
4.14
and the fact that
\langle\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}\varepsilon,\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}=\langle\widehat{\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}\varepsilon},\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}
the coercivity estimate

\displaystyle\langle\tilde{\mathscr{L}}^{z}\varepsilon,\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}

\displaystyle\leq-\delta\left[\langle\hat{\varepsilon},\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}+\int\big(\sum_{\pm}\frac{|\varepsilon|^{2}}{\nu^{2}+|z\pm a|^{2}}+\langle z\rangle^{2}\varepsilon^{2}+|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}\big)\omega_{\nu}\right]\quad\textup{for some $\delta>0$}.

(7.7)

We now use this coercivity estimate and the equivalence of the norm (
7.4
) to control all the remaining terms in (
7.5
).

The small linear term
:

From the pointwise estimate (
5.8
) of
\Xi
, the improved pointwise estimate (
3.11
) and the relation
|\alpha|\sim\nu^{2}
, we have the rough bound

k\in\mathbb{N},\quad\big|\nabla^{k}\tilde{W}(z)\big|\lesssim\big|\nabla^{k}\big(\Xi(z)+\alpha(\phi_{1}-\phi_{0})\big)\big|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{\nu^{2}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2+k}},\quad|\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{W}\chi^{*}}|\lesssim\nu|\ln\nu|.

(7.8)

Let us write from the decomposition (
7.1
),

\displaystyle\langle L\varepsilon,\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}

\displaystyle=\langle L\varepsilon,\varepsilon\rangle_{\ast}+a_{0}\langle L\varepsilon,\phi_{0}\rangle_{\ast},

where we recall
\langle,\rangle_{\ast}
the adapted scalar product defined by (
1.18
), and the definition of
L
from (
6.15
) the expression

\displaystyle L(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\nabla.\Big[\varepsilon\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{W}\chi^{*}}+\tilde{W}\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon\chi^{*}}+\tilde{W}\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{\varepsilon}(1-\chi^{*})}\Big]

\displaystyle=\nabla\varepsilon.\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{W}\chi^{*}}-2\chi^{*}\tilde{W}\varepsilon+\nabla\tilde{W}.\big(\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon\chi^{*}}+\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{\varepsilon}(1-\chi^{*})}\big)-\tilde{W}\tilde{\varepsilon}(1-\chi^{*}).

Since estimates concerning
\tilde{W}
already has a gain of
\nu^{2}
factor, so the estimate for
\langle L\varepsilon,\varepsilon\rangle_{\ast}
is simply absorbed by the dissipation. From the continuity estimate (
4.79
) and the bound (
7.8
), we get

\displaystyle|\langle\nabla\varepsilon.\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{W}\chi^{*}},\varepsilon\rangle_{\ast}|\lesssim\|\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{W}\chi^{*}}\|_{\infty}\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\lesssim\nu|\ln\nu|\;\|\big(\nabla\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}^{2}+\|\varepsilon\|^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}\big).

We estimate from the convolution formula and Cauchy-Schwarz,

|\Phi_{\chi^{*}\varepsilon}(z)|\lesssim\int\big|\ln|z-z^{\prime}|\varepsilon(z^{\prime})\big|\chi^{*}dz^{\prime}\lesssim\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\Big(\int_{|z^{\prime}|\leq 2\zeta^{*}}\ln^{2}|z-z^{\prime}|\omega_{\nu}^{-1}dz^{\prime}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\frac{|\ln\nu|}{\nu}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}},

(7.9)

and

|\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}\varepsilon}(z)|\lesssim\int_{|z^{\prime}|\leq 2\zeta^{*}}\big|\ln|z-z^{\prime}|\big(\nabla\varepsilon(z^{\prime})+\varepsilon(z^{\prime})\nabla\chi^{*}(z^{\prime})\big)\big|dz^{\prime}\lesssim\frac{|\ln\nu|}{\nu}\big(\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\big).

(7.10)

We have from Lemma
B.1
with
\gamma=3/2
and
\sigma=1/8
and

|\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{\varepsilon}(1-\chi^{*})}(z)|\lesssim\|\tilde{\varepsilon}(1-\chi^{*})|z|^{\frac{3}{2}}\|_{\infty}\Big(|z|^{-\sigma}+\langle z\rangle^{-3/2}|z|\Big)\lesssim\|\varepsilon\|_{\textup{ex}}\Big(|z|^{-1/8}+\langle z\rangle^{-3/2}|z|\Big).

(7.11)

Using the estimate (
7.8
), (
7.9
), the definition of
\langle,\rangle_{\ast}
and Cauchy-Schwarz, we obtain

\displaystyle|\langle\chi^{*}\tilde{W}\varepsilon,\varepsilon\rangle_{\ast}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\nu^{2}\int\sum_{\pm}\frac{|\varepsilon|^{2}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2}}\omega_{\nu}dz+\int\chi^{*}\sum_{\pm}\frac{\nu^{2}|\varepsilon|}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2}}|\Phi_{\varepsilon\chi^{*}}|dz

\displaystyle\lesssim\nu^{2}\int\sum_{\pm}\frac{|\varepsilon|^{2}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2}}\omega_{\nu}dz

\displaystyle\quad+\nu|\ln\nu|\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\left(\int\sum_{\pm}\frac{|\varepsilon|^{2}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2}}\omega_{\nu}dz\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{|z|\leq 2\zeta^{*}}\frac{dz}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\nu^{2}\int\sum_{\pm}\frac{|\varepsilon|^{2}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2}}\omega_{\nu}dz+\nu|\ln\nu|^{\frac{3}{2}}\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\left(\int\sum_{\pm}\frac{|\varepsilon|^{2}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2}}\omega_{\nu}dz\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\nu|\ln\nu|^{\frac{3}{2}}\Big(\int\sum_{\pm}\frac{|\varepsilon|^{2}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2}}\omega_{\nu}dz+\|\varepsilon\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\Big).

For the estimate of the Poisson field, we use the continuity (
4.79
), (
7.8
), (
7.10
), (
7.11
) to obtain

\displaystyle\big|\langle\nabla\tilde{W}(\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon\chi^{*}}+\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{\varepsilon}(1-\chi^{*})}),\varepsilon\rangle_{\ast}\big|\lesssim\|\nabla\tilde{W}.(\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon\chi^{*}}+\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{\varepsilon}(1-\chi^{*})})\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\nu|\ln\nu|\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}(\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}})\sum_{\pm}\left(\int\frac{\langle z\rangle^{C}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{6}}\omega_{\nu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\nu^{2}\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\|\varepsilon\|_{\textup{ex}}\sum_{\pm}\left(\int\frac{\langle z\rangle^{C}|z|^{-1/4}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{6}}\omega_{\nu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\nu|\ln\nu|^{\frac{3}{2}}\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}(\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}})+\nu^{2-1/8}|\ln\nu|\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\|\varepsilon\|_{\textup{ex}}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\nu|\ln\nu|^{\frac{3}{2}}\big(\|\nabla\varepsilon\|^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}+\|\varepsilon\|^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}\big)+K^{4}\frac{\nu^{6-1/8}}{|\ln\nu|},

where we used the bootstrap bounds (
6.31
) and (
6.35
) int the last step.

The estimate of the small outer term is obtained from the continuity (
4.79
) and (
7.8
),

\displaystyle\big|\langle\tilde{W}\tilde{\varepsilon}(1-\chi^{*}),\varepsilon\rangle_{\ast}\big|\lesssim\|\tilde{W}(1-\chi^{*})\|_{\infty}\|\varepsilon\|^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}\lesssim\nu^{2}\|\varepsilon\|^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}.

A collection of all the above estimates and taking
\tau_{0}
large so that
\nu(\tau_{0})
small yield

|\langle L\varepsilon,\varepsilon\rangle_{\ast}|\lesssim\nu|\ln\nu|^{\frac{3}{2}}\Big(\|\nabla\varepsilon\|^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}+\|\varepsilon\|^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}+\sum_{\pm}\Big\|\frac{\varepsilon}{\nu+|z\pm a|}\Big\|_{\omega_{\nu}}^{2}\Big)+\frac{\nu^{4}}{|\ln\nu|^{4}}.

(7.12)

We now estimate
\langle L\varepsilon,\phi_{0}\rangle_{\ast}
, we shall use the divergence structure of
L\varepsilon
, the adapted scalar product (
1.18
) and the cancellation
\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}\Lambda U=0
to gain a smallness for this estimate. We recall from (
3.5
) and (
3.8
),

\phi_{0}(z)=\sum_{\pm}\Big(-\frac{1}{16\nu^{2}}\Lambda U_{\nu}(z\pm a)\pm\frac{L_{0}}{\nu}\partial_{1}U_{\nu}(z\pm a)\Big)\chi_{{}_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}}}(z)+\tilde{\phi}_{0},\quad|\nabla^{k}\tilde{\phi}_{0}(z)|\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\frac{\langle z\rangle^{C_{k}}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2+k}}.

(7.13)

We first handle the leading term
\frac{1}{\nu^{2}}\Lambda U_{\nu}(z\pm a)\chi_{{}_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}}}
since the estimate concerning
\tilde{\phi}_{0}
is straightforward without using the cancellation
\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}\Lambda U=0
. We remark that a direct estimate by using (
4.79
) applied to the leading term is not sufficient since
\|\nu^{-2}\Lambda U_{\nu}(z\pm a)\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\lesssim\nu^{-1}
, this is the reason we do need to use the algebraic cancellation
\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}\Lambda U=0
to improve the estimate concerning the leading term of
\phi_{0}
. We have from the identity (
4.177
) and the divergence of
L\varepsilon
,

\displaystyle\Big|\nu^{-2}\sum_{\pm}\langle L\varepsilon,\Lambda U_{\nu}(z\pm a)\chi_{{}_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}}}\rangle_{\ast}\Big|=\sum_{\pm}\Big|-2c_{*}\int_{|z\pm a|\leq\zeta_{*}}L\varepsilon\Big|+\mathcal{O}\Big(\sqrt{\ln\nu}\|L\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\int_{|z\pm a|\sim\zeta_{*}}\Big|\varepsilon\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{W}\chi^{*}}+\tilde{W}\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon\chi^{*}}+\tilde{W}\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{\varepsilon}(1-\chi^{*})}\Big|+\mathcal{O}\Big(\sqrt{\ln\nu}\|L\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\Big).

We use (
7.8
), (
7.10
) and (
7.11
) to estimate
