\displaystyle\sum_{\pm}\int_{|z\pm a|\sim\zeta_{*}}\Big|\varepsilon\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{W}\chi^{*}}+\tilde{W}\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon\chi^{*}}+\tilde{W}\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{\varepsilon}(1-\chi^{*})}\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\|\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{W}\chi^{*}}\|_{L^{\infty}(|z\pm a|\sim\zeta_{*})}\int_{|z\pm a|\sim\zeta_{*}}|\varepsilon|+\sum_{\pm}\|\tilde{W}\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon\chi^{*}}+\tilde{W}\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{\varepsilon}(1-\chi^{*})}\|_{L^{\infty}(|z\pm a|\sim\zeta_{*})}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\nu|\ln\nu|\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\nu^{2}\frac{|\ln\nu|}{\nu}\Big(\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\Big)+\nu^{2}\|\varepsilon\|_{\textup{ex}},

and from (
7.8
) the bounds
\|\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{W}\chi^{*}}\|\lesssim\nu|\ln\nu|
,
\|\chi^{*}\tilde{W}\|\lesssim 1
,
\|\nabla\tilde{W}\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\lesssim\nu^{2}\sqrt{|\ln\nu|}
and
\|\tilde{W}\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\lesssim\nu^{2}
,

\displaystyle\|L\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}

\displaystyle=\big\|\nabla\varepsilon.\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{W}\chi^{*}}-2\chi^{*}\tilde{W}\varepsilon+\nabla\tilde{W}.\big(\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon\chi^{*}}+\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{\varepsilon}(1-\chi^{*})}\big)-\tilde{W}\tilde{\varepsilon}(1-\chi^{*})\big\|_{\omega_{\nu}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{W}\chi^{*}}\|_{\infty}\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\|\chi^{*}\tilde{W}\|_{\infty}\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\|\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}\varepsilon}\|_{\infty}\|\nabla\tilde{W}\|_{\omega_{\nu}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\|\varepsilon\|_{\textup{ex}}\|\nabla\tilde{W}(|z|^{-1/8}+\langle z\rangle^{-3/2}|z|)\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{\textup{ex}}\|\tilde{W}\|_{\omega_{\nu}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\nu|\ln\nu|\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\nu|\ln\nu|^{\frac{3}{2}}\big(\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\big)+\|\varepsilon\|_{\textup{ex}}\nu^{2-1/8}+\nu^{2}\|\varepsilon\|_{\textup{ex}}.

Using
\|\tilde{\phi}_{0}\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\lesssim 1
, the estimate (
4.187
)
|\langle\nu^{-1}\partial_{1}U_{\nu}(z\pm a),L\varepsilon\rangle_{\ast}|\lesssim\nu\|L\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}
, the continuity estimate (
4.79
), the smallness estimate (
7.3
), the bootstrap bound (
6.35
) and gathering all the obtained estimates yield

\displaystyle\big|a_{0}\langle L\varepsilon,\phi_{0}\rangle_{\ast}\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim|a_{0}|\Big\{\Big|\nu^{-2}\sum_{\pm}\langle L\varepsilon,\Lambda U_{\nu}(z\pm a)\chi_{{}_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}}}\rangle_{\ast}\Big|+\nu\|L\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\|\tilde{\phi}_{0}\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{|\ln\nu|}\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\Big\{\nu|\ln\nu|^{2}\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\nu^{2-1/8}\sqrt{|\ln\nu|}\|\varepsilon\|_{\textup{ex}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\nu|\ln\nu|\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}^{2}+\frac{1}{|\ln\nu|}\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\frac{\nu^{4}}{|\ln\nu|^{4}}.

We combine this estimate and (
7.12
) to complete the control of the small linear term as

|\langle L\varepsilon,\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle|\lesssim\frac{1}{|\ln\nu|}\Big(\|\nabla\varepsilon\|^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}+\|\varepsilon\|^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}+\sum_{\pm}\Big\|\frac{\varepsilon}{\nu+|z\pm a|}\Big\|_{\omega_{\nu}}^{2}\Big)+\frac{\nu^{4}}{|\ln\nu|^{4}}.

(7.14)

The nonlinear term
:

From the definitions (
6.16
) and (
7.1
), the continuity estimate (
4.79
), we write

\displaystyle\big|\langle NL(\varepsilon),\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}\big|=\big|\langle NL(\varepsilon),\varepsilon\rangle_{\ast}+a_{0}\langle NL(\varepsilon),\phi_{0}\rangle_{\ast}\big|\lesssim\|NL(\varepsilon)\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+|a_{0}||\langle NL(\varepsilon),\phi_{0}\rangle_{\ast}|.

For the estimate of
\|NL(\varepsilon)\|_{\omega_{\nu}}
, we expand
NL(\varepsilon)
and use (
7.10
), (
7.11
),

\displaystyle\|NL(\varepsilon)\|_{\omega_{\nu}}

\displaystyle=\|\nabla\varepsilon\cdot\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon\chi^{*}}+\nabla\varepsilon\cdot\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{\varepsilon}(1-\chi^{*})}\big)-\varepsilon^{2}\chi^{*}-\varepsilon\tilde{\varepsilon}(1-\chi^{*})\|_{\omega_{\nu}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\|\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon\chi^{*}}\|_{\infty}+\|\nabla\varepsilon(|z|^{-1/8}+\langle z\rangle^{-3/2}|z|)\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\|\varepsilon\|_{\textup{ex}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\big(\|\varepsilon\chi^{*}\|_{\infty}+\|\varepsilon\|_{\textup{ex}}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\big(\nu|\ln\nu|+K^{3}\nu^{2-1/8}\big)+\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\big(\frac{K^{3}}{|\ln\nu|}+K^{3}\nu^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\nu|\ln\nu|\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\frac{K^{3}}{|\ln\nu|}\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}.

where we used the bootstrap bound (
6.35
) and the
L^{\infty}
estimate from Sobolev and the bootstrap bounds (
6.32
), (
6.33
), (
6.34
) and (
6.25
),

\displaystyle\|\varepsilon\chi^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq\sum_{\pm}\nu^{-2}\|q^{\textup{in}}_{\pm}\mathbf{1}_{\{|y_{\pm}|\lesssim\zeta^{*}/\nu}\}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\nu^{-2}\big(\|\tilde{q}\|_{H^{2}_{\textup{in}}}+\|q\|_{H^{1}_{\textup{in}}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{bd}\big)\lesssim\frac{K^{3}}{|\ln\nu|}.

The estimate for
|\langle NL(\varepsilon),\phi_{0}\rangle_{\ast}|
is proceeded as for the small linear term. We decompose
\phi_{0}
as in (
7.13
) to extract the leading term of
\phi_{0}
for which we use the identity (
4.177
) and the divergent form of
NL(\varepsilon)
to get the estimate

\displaystyle|\langle NL(\varepsilon),\phi_{0}\rangle_{\ast}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\sum_{\pm}\langle NL(\varepsilon),\nu^{-2}\Lambda U_{\nu}(z\pm a)\chi_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}}\rangle_{\ast}\right|+\nu\|NL(\varepsilon)\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\|NL(\varepsilon)\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\|\tilde{\phi}_{0}\|_{\omega_{\nu}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\left|-2c_{*}\int_{|z\pm a|\leq\zeta_{*}}NL(\varepsilon)+4c_{*}\int_{|z\pm a|\leq\zeta_{*}}NL(\varepsilon)\right|+\|NL(\varepsilon)\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\big(\sqrt{\ln\nu}+\nu+\|\tilde{\phi}_{0}\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\int_{|z\pm a|\sim\zeta_{*}}\big|\varepsilon\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon\chi^{*}}+\varepsilon\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{\varepsilon}(1-\chi^{*})}\big|+\|NL(\varepsilon)\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\big(\sqrt{\ln\nu}+1\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\big(\|\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon\chi^{*}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|z\pm a|\sim\zeta_{*}\}}\|_{\infty}+\|\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{\varepsilon}(1-\chi^{*})}\mathbf{1}_{\{|z\pm a|\sim\zeta_{*}\}}\|_{\infty}\big)+\|NL(\varepsilon)\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\sqrt{\ln\nu}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\big(\frac{|\ln\nu|}{\nu}(\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}})+\|\varepsilon\|_{\textup{ex}}\big)+\nu|\ln\nu|^{3/2}\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\frac{K^{3}}{\sqrt{|\ln\nu|}}\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{K^{5}\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{5/2}}.

A collection of the above estimates and using the estimate (
7.3
) yield

\big|\langle NL(\varepsilon),\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}\big|\lesssim\Big(\nu|\ln\nu|\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\frac{K^{3}}{|\ln\nu|}\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\Big)\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\frac{K^{5}\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{5/2}}\frac{1}{|\ln\nu|}\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\lesssim\frac{K^{5}\nu^{4}}{|\ln\nu|^{9/2}}.

(7.15)

The error term
:

We use the decomposition (
6.5
), the orthogonality (
7.2
) to write

\displaystyle|\langle E,\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}|\leq|\textup{Mod}_{1}||\langle\phi_{1}-\phi_{0},\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}|+|\textup{Mod}_{a}||\langle\nabla U_{1,\nu}+\chi_{-a,\zeta_{*}}\nabla\Xi-\nabla U_{2,\nu}-\chi_{a,\zeta_{*}}\nabla\Xi,\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}|+|\langle\Psi,\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}|.

We remark that canceling the
\textup{Mod}_{0}
term in
E
is crucial to avoid the loop in the analysis, the
\textup{Mod}_{1}
already gains a
\frac{1}{|\ln\nu|}
and
\textup{Mod}_{a}
is small enough. Let recall from (
7.1
) the decomposition
\hat{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon+a_{0}\phi_{0}
, and from (
3.5
)
\phi_{1}-\phi_{0}=\sum_{\pm}\frac{L_{1}-L_{0}}{\nu}\partial_{1}U_{\nu}(z\pm a)+\mathcal{O}\Big(\sum_{\pm}\frac{\langle z\rangle^{C}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2}}\Big)
, then we use the cancellation
\mathscr{M}(\partial_{1}U)=0
and the identity (
4.79
) to estimate

\displaystyle|\langle\phi_{1}-\phi_{0},\varepsilon\rangle_{\ast}|\lesssim\Big\|\sum_{\pm}\frac{\langle z\rangle^{C}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{2}}\Big\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\lesssim\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}.

For the term
a_{0}\phi_{0}
, we proceed as before for the linear small term by using (
7.13
), the identity (
4.177
), the estimate (
4.187
) and the continuity estimate (
4.79
) to obtain

\displaystyle|\langle\phi_{1}-\phi_{0},\phi_{0}\rangle_{\ast}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big|\nu^{-2}\sum_{\pm}\langle\phi_{1}-\phi_{0},\Lambda U_{\nu}(z\pm a)\chi_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}}\rangle_{\ast}\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\Big|\sum\langle\phi_{1}-\phi_{0},\nu^{-1}\partial_{1}U_{\nu}(z\pm a)\rangle_{\ast}\Big|+|\langle\phi_{1}-\phi_{0},\tilde{\phi}_{0}\rangle_{\ast}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\pm}\Big|\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}\phi_{1}-\phi_{0}\Big|+\sum_{\pm}\Big\|\frac{\phi_{1}-\phi_{0}}{\sqrt{\nu+|z\pm a|}}\Big\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{0}\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\big(\nu+\|\tilde{\phi}_{0}\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim|\ln\nu|.

Summing up the above bounds and using (
7.3
) yields,

|\textup{Mod}_{1}||\langle\phi_{1}-\phi_{0},\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}|\lesssim|\textup{Mod}_{1}|\big(\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+|a_{0}||\ln\nu|\big)\lesssim|\textup{Mod}_{1}|\big(\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\big).

(7.16)

For the estimate of the
\textup{Mod}_{a}
term, we recall the cancellation
\mathscr{M}_{0}\nabla U=0
, from which and the definition of the inner produce
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\ast}
to write

\displaystyle|\langle\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\nabla U_{2,\nu},\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}|\lesssim|\langle\nabla U_{1,\nu}(1-\chi_{a,\zeta_{*}})-\nabla U_{2,\nu}(1-\chi_{-a,\zeta_{*}}),\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}|\equiv\big|\langle G,\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}\big|,

where we have a rough pointwise bound from
|\nabla U_{\nu}(z\pm a)|\lesssim\frac{\nu^{2}}{(\nu+|z\pm a|)^{5}}
,

\displaystyle|G|

\displaystyle=|\nabla U_{1,\nu}(1-\chi_{a,\zeta_{*}})-\nabla U_{2,\nu}(1-\chi_{-a,\zeta_{*}})|\lesssim\nu^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\{|z\pm a|\gtrsim\zeta_{*}\}}.

We use this estimate and the decompositions (
7.1
), (
7.13
), the continuity estimate (
4.79
), the identity (
4.177
) and (
7.3
) to obtain

\displaystyle\big|\langle G,\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\big|\langle G,\varepsilon\rangle_{\ast}\big|+|a_{0}|\big|\langle G,\nu^{-2}\sum_{\pm}\Lambda U_{\nu}(a\pm a)\chi_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}}\rangle_{\ast}\big|+|a_{0}|\big|\langle G,\tilde{\phi}_{0}\rangle_{\ast}\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|G\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+|a_{0}|\left\{\sum_{\pm}\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}|G|+\Big\|\frac{G}{\sqrt{\nu+|z\pm a|}}\Big\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\|G\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\|\tilde{\phi}_{0}\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\nu^{2}\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\frac{1}{|\ln\nu|}\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\nu^{2}\lesssim\nu^{2}\big(\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\big).

We then arrive at the estimate

|\textup{Mod}_{a}||\langle\nabla U_{1,\nu}-\nabla U_{2,\nu},\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}|\lesssim\nu^{2}|\textup{Mod}_{a}|\big(\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\big).

(7.17)

It remains to estimate the scalar with
\Psi
term. Similarly, we use (
7.1
), (
7.13
), (
4.79
), (
4.177
), (
5.14
) and (
7.3
) to write

\displaystyle|\langle\Psi,\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}|

\displaystyle\leq|\langle\Psi,\varepsilon\rangle_{\ast}|+|a_{0}||\langle\Psi,\phi_{0}\rangle_{\ast}|

\displaystyle\leq|\langle\Psi,\varepsilon\rangle_{\ast}|+|a_{0}|\Big(\sum_{\pm}|\langle\Psi,\nu^{-2}\Lambda U_{\nu}(z\pm a)\chi_{\pm a,\zeta_{*}}\rangle_{\ast}|+|\langle\Psi,\tilde{\phi}_{0}\rangle_{\ast}|\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Psi\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+|a_{0}|\Big(\Big|\sum_{\pm}\int_{|z\pm a|\lesssim\zeta_{*}}\Psi\Big|+\sqrt{|\ln\nu|}\|\Psi\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\|\Psi\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\|\tilde{\phi}_{0}\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\frac{1}{|\ln\nu|}\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\Big(\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|}+\frac{\nu^{2}}{\sqrt{|\ln\nu|}}+\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\big(\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\big).

(7.18)

Summing up (
7.16
), (
7.17
), (
7.18
) yields

|\langle E,\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}|\lesssim\big(\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\big)\Big(|\textup{Mod}_{1}|+\nu^{2}|\textup{Mod}_{a}|+\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\Big).

(7.19)

Plugging all the estimates (
7.14
), (
7.15
), (
7.19
), (
7.7
) into (
7.5
) and using Cauchy-Schwarz with
\epsilon
and the equivalence (
7.4
) yield

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{d\tau}\langle\hat{\varepsilon},\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(-\delta+\mathcal{O}\big(\frac{|\nu_{\tau}|+|a_{\tau}|}{\nu}\big)\Big)\langle\hat{\varepsilon},\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}-\delta\Big(\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}^{2}+\|\langle z\rangle\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}^{2}+\sum_{\pm}\big\|\frac{\varepsilon}{\nu+|z\pm a|}\big\|^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{C}{|\ln\nu|}\Big(\|\nabla\varepsilon\|^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}+\|\varepsilon\|^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}+\sum_{\pm}\Big\|\frac{\varepsilon}{\nu+|z\pm a|}\Big\|_{\omega_{\nu}}^{2}\Big)+C\frac{\nu^{4}}{|\ln\nu|^{4}}+\frac{CK^{5}\nu^{4}}{|\ln\nu|^{9/2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+C\big(\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\big)\Big(|\textup{Mod}_{1}|+\nu^{2}|\textup{Mod}_{a}|+\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\Big(-\frac{\delta}{2}+\mathcal{O}\big(\frac{|\nu_{\tau}|+|a_{\tau}|}{\nu}\big)\Big)\langle\hat{\varepsilon},\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}-\frac{\delta}{2}\Big(\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}^{2}+\|\langle z\rangle\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}^{2}+\sum_{\pm}\big\|\frac{\varepsilon}{\nu+|z\pm a|}\big\|^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad+C\Big(|\textup{Mod}_{1}|+\nu^{2}|\textup{Mod}_{a}|+\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\delta}{4}\langle\hat{\varepsilon},\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}+C\Big(|\textup{Mod}_{1}|+\nu^{2}|\textup{Mod}_{a}|+\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\Big)^{2},

which is the desired estimate (
7.6
). This concludes the proof of Lemma
7.1
.
∎

7.2.
H^{1}
estimate in the inner region

We derive in this section a monotonicity formula to control the remainder
\varepsilon(z,\tau)
in the inner region
|z\pm a|\ll 1
. In particular, we aim at improving the bootstrap bound (
6.33
) thanks to the following lemma.

Lemma 7.2

(
H^{1}
-estimate)
.

Let
\varepsilon
be a solution to (
6.14
) and satisfy the bootstrap bounds in Definition
6.2
for
\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]
. Then, we have the following estimate for
\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]
:

\frac{d}{d\tau}\|q(\tau)\|^{2}_{H^{1}_{\textup{in}}}\leq-\frac{1}{\zeta_{*}}\|q(\tau)\|^{2}_{H^{1}_{\textup{in}}}+C\Big(\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|^{2}_{bd}+\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}+\frac{\nu^{4}}{|\ln\nu|^{4}}\Big).

(7.20)

for some
C=C(\zeta_{*},\zeta^{*})>0
.

Proof.

To ease the notation, we drop
\pm
in the definition (
6.20
) of
q^{\textup{in}}_{\pm}(y_{\pm},s)
in this proof and write

q(y,s)=\nu^{2}\varepsilon(z,\tau),\quad q^{\textup{in}}(y,s)=q(y,s)\chi_{*}(y),\quad y=\frac{z-a}{\nu},\quad\frac{ds}{d\tau}=\frac{1}{\nu^{2}},

where
\chi_{*}
is introduced in (
2.2
). The case
y=\frac{z+a}{\nu}
is handled in the same way.

Energy identities:

From (
6.14
), we write the equation for
q(y,s)
,

\partial_{s}q=\mathscr{L}_{0}q+L_{1}q-NL(q)+\nu^{4}E,

(7.21)

where
\mathscr{L}_{0}
is defined in (
2.9
) and
L_{1}
is a small linear term

\displaystyle L_{1}(q)

\displaystyle=\nu^{2}\Big(\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}-\beta\Big)\Lambda q+\Big(\nu a_{\tau}-\beta\nu a-\nabla\Phi_{U}(y+2a/\nu)\Big).\nabla q

\displaystyle\quad-\nabla.\big(q\nabla\Phi_{\nu^{2}\tilde{W}\chi^{*}}+\nu^{2}\tilde{W}\nabla\Phi_{q\chi^{*}}\big)-\nabla\big(\nu^{2}\tilde{W}\nabla_{\tilde{q}(1-\chi^{*})}\big),

(7.22)

and
NL(q)
is the nonlinear term

NL(q)=\nabla\cdot\big(q\nabla_{q\chi^{*}}+q\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{q}(1-\chi^{*})}\big),\quad\tilde{q}(y,s)=\nu^{2}\tilde{\varepsilon}(z,\tau).

(7.23)

We multiply the equation of
q
by
\chi_{*}
, take a scalar with
\frac{\nu^{2}}{\zeta_{*}^{2}}\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}
and
-\mathscr{M}_{0}\mathscr{L}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}
respectively, and use the fact that
\mathscr{M}_{0}
is symmetric to get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{ds}

\displaystyle\left\{-\int q_{2}^{\textup{in}}\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}+\frac{\nu^{2}}{\zeta_{*}^{2}}\int q^{\textup{in}}\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}\right\}=-\int q_{2}^{\textup{in}}\mathscr{M}_{0}q_{2}^{\textup{in}}+\frac{\nu^{2}}{\zeta_{*}^{2}}\int q_{2}^{\textup{in}}\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}\qquad\big(q_{2}^{\textup{in}}=\mathscr{L}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-\int q_{2}^{\textup{in}}\mathscr{M}_{0}\big[\big(L_{1}(q)-NL(q)+\nu^{4}E\big)\chi_{*}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{\nu^{2}}{\zeta_{*}^{2}}\int q^{\textup{in}}\mathscr{M}_{0}\big[\big(L_{1}(q)-NL(q)+\nu^{4}E\big)\chi_{*}\big]-\frac{\nu^{4}}{\zeta_{*}^{2}}\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}\int q^{\textup{in}}\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}.

(7.24)

The local orthogonality condition (
6.18
) and Lemma
4.5
yields the coercivity

-\int q_{2}^{\textup{in}}\mathscr{M}_{0}(q^{\textup{in}})=\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}(q^{\textup{in}})|^{2}dy\gtrsim\int\frac{|\nabla(q^{\textup{in}})|^{2}}{U}dy,

(7.25)

and recall from (
4.27
),

\int q^{\textup{in}}\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}dy\gtrsim\int\frac{|q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}}{U}dy.

(7.26)

We have by Hardy inequality and the support of
q^{\textup{in}}
,

-\int\frac{|\nabla q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}}{U}dy\lesssim-\int\frac{|q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}}{(1+|y|^{2})U}dy\lesssim-\frac{\nu^{2}}{\zeta_{*}^{2}}\int\frac{|q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}}{U}.

(7.27)

We recall the fact that
\langle f,\mathscr{M}_{0}f\rangle_{L^{2}}\geq 0
if
\int f=0
(see again Proposition 2.3 in
[
RS14a
]
). The divergence form of
\mathscr{L}_{0}
already yields the orthogonality
\langle q_{2}^{\textup{in}},1\rangle_{L^{2}}=0
, hence,

\int q_{2}^{\textup{in}}\mathscr{M}_{0}q_{2}^{\textup{in}}dy\geq 0.

Collecting these inequalities and using the a priori bound
|\nu_{\tau}/\nu|\lesssim 1/|\ln\nu|\ll 1
for
\nu(\tau_{0})\ll 1
, we rewrite the energy identity (
7.24
) as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{ds}

\displaystyle\left\{\int U\big|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}\big|^{2}+\frac{\nu^{2}}{\zeta_{*}^{2}}\int q^{\textup{in}}\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}\right\}\leq-\delta\Big(\frac{\nu^{2}}{\zeta_{*}^{2}}\int\frac{|\nabla q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}}{U}+\frac{\nu^{4}}{\zeta_{*}^{2}}\int\frac{|q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}}{U}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\left|\int q_{2}^{\textup{in}}\mathscr{M}_{0}\big[\big(L_{1}(q)-NL(q)+\nu^{4}E\big)\chi_{*}\big]\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{\nu^{2}}{\zeta_{*}^{2}}\left|\int\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}\big[\big(L_{1}(q)-NL(q)+\nu^{4}E\big)\chi_{*}\big]\right|.

(7.28)

Thanks to the dissipation, we now estimate all the remaining terms by using integration by parts, Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the bootstrap bounds in Definition
6.2
. We emphasize that the algebraic identities
\mathscr{M}_{0}\Lambda U=-2
and
\mathscr{M}_{0}\nabla U=0
and the local orthogonality (
6.18
)
\langle q^{\textup{in}},1\rangle_{L^{2}_{loc}}=0
are crucial in getting a better estimate once the generated error term
E\chi_{*}
is concerned.

The scaling linear term
\Lambda q
:

We first write

\displaystyle\mathscr{M}_{0}(\Lambda q\chi_{*})

\displaystyle=\mathscr{M}_{0}(\Lambda q^{\textup{in}}-y.\nabla\chi_{*}q)

\displaystyle=y.\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}+2\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}+\frac{q^{\textup{in}}}{U}y.\nabla\Phi_{U}-y.\nabla\Phi_{q^{\textup{in}}}-\Phi_{y.\nabla q^{\textup{in}}}-\mathscr{M}_{0}(y.\nabla\chi_{*}q).

We then estimate the highest order term by using the divergence form
q_{2}^{\textup{in}}=\nabla.(U\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}})
and integration by parts

\displaystyle\left|\int q_{2}^{\textup{in}}y.\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}+2\int q_{2}^{\textup{in}}\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\int U\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}.\nabla(y.\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}})\right|+2\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}+\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}\Big|\frac{\nabla.(yU)}{U}\Big|\lesssim\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}.

Using the round bound
|y.\nabla\Phi_{U}|\lesssim 1
, Cauchy-Schwarz and Hardy inequality, we estimate

\displaystyle\left|\int q_{2}^{\textup{in}}\frac{q^{\textup{in}}}{U}y.\nabla\Phi_{U}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int U\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}.\nabla\big[(\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}+\Phi_{q^{\textup{in}}})y.\nabla\Phi_{U}\big]\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}+\int U|\nabla\Phi_{q^{\textup{in}}}|^{2}+\int U|\Phi_{q^{\textup{in}}}|^{2}|\nabla(y.\nabla\Phi_{U})|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}.

Similarly, we estimate for lower order terms by Cauchy-Schwarz and Hardy inequality,

\displaystyle\left|\int q_{2}^{\textup{in}}\big(y.\nabla\Phi_{q^{\textup{in}}}+\Phi_{y.\nabla q^{\textup{in}}}\big)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}.

As for the boundary term, we use the
H^{2}
-bound (
6.32
) to estimate

\displaystyle\left|\int q_{2}^{\textup{in}}\mathscr{M}_{0}(y.\nabla\chi_{*}q)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}+\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}(y.\nabla\chi_{*}q)|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}+\int_{\nu|y|\sim\zeta_{*}}\frac{|\nabla q|^{2}}{U}+\frac{|q|^{2}}{|y|^{2}U}+U|\nabla\Phi_{y.\nabla\chi_{*}q}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}+\frac{\zeta_{*}^{4}}{\nu^{4}}\|\nabla q\|^{2}_{L^{2}(|\nu y|\sim\zeta_{*})}+\frac{\zeta_{*}^{2}}{\nu^{2}}\|q\|^{2}_{L^{2}(|\nu y|\sim\zeta_{*})}+\frac{\nu^{4}}{\zeta_{*}^{4}}\|\nabla\Phi_{y.\nabla\chi_{*}q}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\nu|y|\sim\zeta_{*})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}+C(\zeta_{*})\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|^{2}_{bd}.

A collection of all the estimates and recall that
|\nu_{\tau}/\nu|\lesssim 1/|\ln\nu|
, we derive

\displaystyle\left|\nu^{2}\Big(\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}-\beta\Big)\int q_{2}^{\textup{in}}\mathscr{M}_{0}(\Lambda q\chi_{*})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\nu^{2}\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}+C(\zeta_{*})\nu^{2}\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|^{2}_{bd}.

(7.29)

The estimate for
\int q^{\textup{in}}\mathscr{M}_{0}(\Lambda q\chi_{*})
is proceeded in the same manner by integration by parts and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, where we end up with

\displaystyle\frac{\nu^{2}}{\zeta_{*}^{2}}\left|\nu^{2}\big(\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}-\beta\big)\int\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}\Lambda q\chi_{*}\right|\lesssim\frac{\nu^{4}}{\zeta^{2}_{*}}\left(\int\frac{|q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}}{U}+C(\zeta_{*})\nu^{-2}\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{bd}^{2}\right).

(7.30)

The linear gradient term
\nabla q
:

We write

\mathscr{M}_{0}(\nabla q\chi_{*})=\mathscr{M}_{0}(\nabla q^{\textup{in}}-y\nabla\chi_{*}q)=\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}+\frac{q^{\textup{in}}}{U}\nabla\Phi_{U}+\nabla\Phi_{q^{\textup{in}}}-\mathscr{M}_{0}(\nabla\chi_{*}q),

and from the definition of
\textup{Mod}_{a}
and Taylor expansion of
\nabla\Phi_{U}(y+2a/\nu)=\nabla\Phi_{U}(2a/\nu)+\nu^{2}F_{1}(y)
with
|F_{1}(y)|+|y\nabla.F_{1}(y)|\lesssim 1+|y|
for
|y|\leq\frac{2\zeta_{*}}{\nu}
,

\displaystyle\nu a_{\tau}-\beta a\nu-\nabla\Phi_{U}(y+2a/\nu)=\nu\textup{Mod}_{a}+\nu^{2}F_{1}(y),

where we recall from (
6.38
) and (
5.12
) the bounds

|\nu\textup{Mod}_{a}|\lesssim\|q(\tau)\|_{\textup{in}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}.

We estimate by integration by parts

\displaystyle\left|\int q_{2}^{\textup{in}}F_{1}(y).\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\int\nabla.(U\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}})F_{1}(y).\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}\right|\lesssim\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}\frac{|\nabla.(F_{1}U)|}{U}\lesssim\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}.

The lower order terms and the boundary term are estimated by using integration by parts, Hardy inequality and
H^{2}
-bound (
6.32
) as for the scaling term,

\displaystyle\left|\int q_{2}^{\textup{in}}F_{1}.\big(\frac{q^{\textup{in}}}{U}\nabla\Phi_{U}+\nabla\Phi_{q^{\textup{in}}}-\mathscr{M}_{0}(\nabla\chi_{*}q\big)\right|\lesssim\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}+C(\zeta_{*})\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{bd}^{2}.

Collecting all the bounds yields

\displaystyle\left|\int q_{2}^{\textup{in}}\mathscr{M}_{0}\big[(\nu a_{\tau}-\beta a\nu-\nabla\Phi_{U}(y+2a/\nu)).\nabla q\chi_{*}\big]\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\lesssim\big(\|q(\tau)\|_{\textup{in}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\big)\Big(\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}+C(\zeta_{*})\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|^{2}_{bd}\Big).

(7.31)

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\nu^{2}}{\zeta_{*}^{2}}\left|\int\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}\big[(\nu a_{\tau}-\beta a\nu-\nabla\Phi_{U}(y+2a/\nu)).\nabla q\chi_{*}\big]\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\lesssim\frac{\nu^{2}}{\zeta_{*}^{2}}\big(\|q(\tau)\|_{\textup{in}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{\omega_{\nu}}+\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\big)\Big(\int\frac{|q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}}{U}+C(\zeta_{*})\nu^{-2}\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|^{2}_{bd}\Big).

(7.32)

The small linear term:

From the definition (
6.3
), the pointwise estimate (
5.8
) of
\Xi
, the improved pointwise estimate (
3.11
), and the bound
|\alpha|\lesssim\nu^{2}
, we have the round bound

\nu^{2}\big((y.\nabla)^{2}\tilde{W}(\nu y)+|y.\nabla\tilde{W}(\nu y)|+|\tilde{W}(\nu y)|\big)\lesssim\frac{\nu^{2}}{1+|y|^{2}},\quad\nu^{2}|\nabla\Phi_{\chi^{*}\tilde{W}(\nu y)}|\lesssim\nu^{3}|\ln\nu|.

We write

\displaystyle\big[\nabla.(q\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{W}\chi^{*}})+\nabla.(\tilde{W}\nabla\Phi_{q\chi^{*}})\big]\chi_{*}=\nabla q^{\textup{in}}.\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{W}\chi^{*}}-q\nabla\chi_{*}.\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{W}\chi^{*}}-2q^{\textup{in}}\tilde{W}+\nabla\tilde{W}.\nabla\Phi_{q\chi^{*}}\chi_{{}_{*}}.

The highest order term
\nabla q^{\textup{in}}.\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{W}\chi^{*}}
is simply estimated by integration by parts,

\displaystyle\left|\nu^{2}\int q_{2}^{\textup{in}}\mathscr{M}_{0}(\nabla q^{\textup{in}}.\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{W}\chi^{*}})\right|+\frac{\nu^{4}}{\zeta_{*}^{2}}\left|\int\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}\nabla q^{\textup{in}}.\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{W}\chi^{*}}\right|\lesssim\nu^{2}\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}+\frac{\nu^{4}}{\zeta_{*}^{2}}\int\frac{|q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}}{U}.

The lower and boundary terms are estimated by integration by parts,
H^{2}
-bound (
6.32
) and Hardy inequality in the same manner as for the scaling term,

\displaystyle\left|\nu^{2}\int q_{2}^{\textup{in}}\mathscr{M}_{0}\big[-q\nabla\chi_{*}.\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{W}\chi^{*}}-2q^{\textup{in}}\tilde{W}+\nabla\tilde{W}.\nabla\Phi_{q\chi^{*}}\chi_{*}\big]\right|

\displaystyle\qquad+\left|\frac{\nu^{4}}{\zeta_{*}^{2}}\int\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}\big[-q\nabla\chi_{*}.\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{W}\chi^{*}}-2q^{\textup{in}}\tilde{W}+\nabla\tilde{W}.\nabla\Phi_{q\chi^{*}}\chi_{*}\big]\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\lesssim\nu^{2}\left(\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}+C(\zeta_{*})\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{bd}^{2}\right)+\frac{\nu^{4}}{\zeta_{*}^{2}}\left(\int\frac{|q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}}{U}+C(\zeta_{*})\nu^{-2}\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{bd}^{2}\right).

For the Poison term in the outer region, we estimate using (
6.32
), (
6.35
) and Lemma
B.1
with
\gamma=3/2
and
\sigma=1/4

|\chi_{{}_{*}}\nabla_{y}\Phi_{\tilde{q}(1-\chi_{{}_{*}})}|\lesssim\nu|\chi_{\zeta_{*},a}\nabla_{z}\Phi_{\varepsilon(1-\chi_{\zeta_{*},a})}|\lesssim\nu^{\frac{3}{4}}\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{\textup{ex}}(|y|^{-1/4}),

(7.33)

From this and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we get

\displaystyle\left|\nu^{2}\int q_{2}^{\textup{in}}\mathscr{M}_{0}\big(\nabla\tilde{W}.\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{q}(1-\chi_{*})}\chi_{*}\big)\right|\lesssim\nu^{2}\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}+\nu^{3}\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{\textup{ex}}^{2}.

and

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\nu^{4}}{\zeta_{*}^{2}}\int\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}\nabla\tilde{W}.\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{q}(1-\chi_{*})}\chi_{*}\right|\lesssim\frac{\nu^{4}}{\zeta_{*}^{2}}\int\frac{|q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}}{U}+C(\zeta_{*})\nu^{3}\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{\textup{ex}}^{2}.

Collecting all these estimates yields the estimate for the small linear term

\displaystyle\left|\nu^{2}\int q_{2}^{\textup{in}}\mathscr{M}_{0}\Big(\big[(\nabla.(q\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{W}\chi_{*}})+\nabla.(\tilde{W}\Phi_{q\chi_{*}})+\nabla.(\tilde{W}\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{q}(1-\chi^{*})})\big]\chi_{*}\Big)\right|

\displaystyle\qquad+\left|\frac{\nu^{4}}{\zeta_{*}^{2}}\int\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}\big[(\nabla.(q\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{W}\chi_{*}})+\nabla.(\tilde{W}\Phi_{q\chi_{*}})+\nabla.(\tilde{W}\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{q}(1-\chi^{*})})\big]\chi_{*}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\lesssim\nu^{2}\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}+\frac{\nu^{4}}{\zeta_{*}^{2}}\int\frac{|q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}}{U}+C(\zeta_{*})\big(\nu^{2}\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{bd}+\nu^{3}\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{\textup{ex}}\big).

(7.34)

The generated error term
\nu^{4}E
:

By using the algebraic identities
\mathscr{M}_{0}\Lambda U=-2,\mathscr{M}_{0}\nabla U=0
and the local orthogonality
\langle q,1\rangle_{L^{2}_{loc}}=0
, we cancel out the leading order term
\Lambda U
and
\nabla U
of
\nu^{4}E
that crucially gains the factor
\nu^{2}
. We recall from the definition (
3.5
) of
\phi_{0}
and
\phi_{1}
, and
\chi_{*}(y)=\chi_{\zeta_{*}/\nu}(y)=\chi_{a,\zeta_{*}}(z)
,

\nu^{4}\phi_{i}(z)\chi_{*}=\Big(-\frac{1}{16}\Lambda U(y)\pm\nu L_{i}\partial_{1}U(y)\Big)\chi_{*}(y)+\nu^{4}\tilde{\phi}_{i}(z)\chi_{*}(y),

where we have by (
3.10
)

k\in\mathbb{N},\quad|\nu^{4}\nabla^{k}_{y}\tilde{\phi}_{i}(z)|=|\nu^{4+k}\nabla_{z}\tilde{\phi}_{i}(z)|\lesssim\frac{\nu^{2}}{1+|y|^{2+k}},\quad\quad|y|\leq\frac{2\zeta_{*}}{\nu}.

(7.35)

From the cancellation
\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}\Lambda U=0
and
\mathscr{M}_{0}\nabla U=0
, integration by parts and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we estimate

\displaystyle\left|\int q_{2}^{\textup{in}}\mathscr{M}_{0}(\nu^{4}E\chi_{*})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i=0}^{1}|\textup{Mod}_{i}|\int\left|U\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}\right|\big|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}\big(\Lambda U(1-\chi_{*})+\nu\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}\big(\partial_{1}U(1-\chi_{*})+\nu^{4}\tilde{\phi}_{i}\chi_{*}\big)\big|

\displaystyle\quad+|\textup{Mod}_{a}|\int\big|U\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}\big|\big|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}\big(\nu\nabla U(y)(1-\chi_{*})-\nu\nabla U(y+2a/\nu)\chi_{*}+\nu^{5}\nabla\Xi\chi_{*}\big)\big|

\displaystyle\qquad\int\big|U\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}\big|\big|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}(\nu^{4}\Psi\chi_{*})\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\nu^{2}\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}+\sum_{i=0}^{1}\nu^{-2}|\textup{Mod}_{i}|^{2}\int U\Big|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}\big(\Lambda U(1-\chi_{*})+\nu\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}\big(\partial_{1}U(1-\chi_{*})+\nu^{4}\tilde{\phi}_{i}\chi_{*}\big)\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+|\textup{Mod}_{a}|\int U\Big|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}\big(\nabla U(y)(1-\chi_{*})-\nabla U(y+2a/\nu)\chi_{*}+\nu^{4}\nabla\Xi\chi_{*}\big)\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int U\big|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}(\nu^{3}\Psi\chi_{*})\big|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\nu^{2}\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}+\nu^{2}\sum_{i=0}^{1}|\textup{Mod}_{i}|^{2}+\nu^{3}|\textup{Mod}_{a}|^{2}+\frac{\nu^{6}}{|\ln\nu|^{4}},

where we used in the last line the decays
|\Lambda U(y)|\lesssim(1+|y|)^{-4}
and
|\nabla U(y)|\lesssim(1+|y|)^{-5}
, the pointwise estimate (
5.8
)
|\nabla_{y}\Xi(z)\chi_{*}|=\nu|\nabla_{z}\Xi(z)\chi_{a,\zeta_{*}}(z)|\lesssim(1+|y|)^{-3}
and (
6.8
).
From Lemma
6.3
, we thus derive

\left|\int q_{2}^{\textup{in}}\mathscr{M}_{0}(\nu^{4}E\chi_{*})\right|\lesssim\nu^{2}\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}+\nu^{2}\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}+\nu^{2}\|q(\tau)\|^{2}_{H^{1}_{\textup{in}}}+\frac{\nu^{6}}{|\ln\nu|^{4}}.

(7.36)

The estimate for
\frac{\nu^{2}}{\zeta_{*}^{2}}\int q^{\textup{in}}\mathscr{M}_{0}(\nu^{4}E)
is to use the cancellation
\mathscr{M}_{0}\Lambda U=-2
,
\mathscr{M}_{0}\nabla U=0
together with the local orthogonality
\langle q^{\textup{in}},1\rangle_{L^{2}}=0
to cancel out the leading order terms in
\nu^{4}E
. The remaining terms are estimated similarly and we omit it here.

The nonlinear term:

We write from (
7.23
) and use
\chi_{*}\chi^{*}=\chi_{*}
,
\chi_{*}(1-\chi^{*})=0
,

\displaystyle NL(q)\chi_{*}

\displaystyle=\nabla\cdot\big[q\nabla\big(\Phi_{q\chi^{*}}+\Phi_{\tilde{q}(1-\chi^{*})}\big)\big]\chi_{*}

\displaystyle=\nabla q^{\textup{in}}.\nabla\big(\Phi_{q\chi^{*}}+\Phi_{\tilde{q}(1-\chi^{*})}\big)-q\nabla\chi_{*}.\nabla\big(\Phi_{q\chi^{*}}+\Phi_{\tilde{q}(1-\chi^{*})}\big)-q^{\textup{in}}q.

We first get the bound from the definition of
\nabla\Phi_{f}
, Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the
H^{2}
-bound (
6.32
),

\displaystyle|\nabla\Phi_{q\chi^{*}}(y)|

\displaystyle\lesssim|\nabla\Phi_{q\chi_{*}}|+\nu|\nabla_{z}\Phi_{\varepsilon(1-\chi_{*})\chi^{*}}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int|\ln|x-y|\;|\nabla q^{\textup{in}}(x)|dx+\nu\int|\ln|x-z|\;|\nabla_{x}\varepsilon(1-\chi_{*})\chi^{*}|dx

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int\frac{|\nabla q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}}{U}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+C(\zeta_{*},\zeta^{*})\nu\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{bd}\lesssim\|q(\tau)\|_{H^{1}_{\textup{in}}}+C(\zeta_{*},\zeta^{*},K)\frac{\nu^{3}}{|\ln\nu|}.

Using this bound, the definition of
\mathscr{M}_{0}
, Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, the estimate
\|\Phi_{\nabla q^{\textup{in}}}\|_{\infty}^{2}\lesssim\int\frac{|\nabla q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}}{U}
, the bootstrap bounds (
6.33
), (
6.34
) and (
6.25
) to obtain

\displaystyle\left|\int q_{2}^{\textup{in}}\mathscr{M}_{0}(\nabla q^{\textup{in}}.\nabla\Phi_{q\chi^{*}})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\int\frac{q_{2}^{\textup{in}}\nabla q^{\textup{in}}.\nabla\Phi_{q\chi^{*}}}{U}\right|+\left|\int q_{2}^{\textup{in}}\Phi_{\nabla q^{\textup{in}}.\nabla\Phi_{q\chi^{*}}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\Phi_{q\chi^{*}}\|_{\infty}\left(\int\frac{|q_{2}^{\textup{in}}|^{2}}{U}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left[\left(\int\frac{|\nabla q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}}{U}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\|\Phi_{|\nabla q^{\textup{in}}|}\|_{\infty}\left(\int U\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\Phi_{q\chi^{*}}\|_{\infty}\left(\int\frac{|q_{2}^{\textup{in}}|^{2}}{U}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int\frac{|\nabla q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}}{U}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\frac{C(\zeta_{*},\zeta^{*},K)\nu^{6}}{|\ln\nu|^{5}}.

Using the bound (
7.33
), we similarly estimate

\displaystyle\left|\int q_{2}^{\textup{in}}\mathscr{M}_{0}(\nabla q^{\textup{in}}.\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{q}(1-\chi^{*})})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\nu^{\frac{3}{4}}\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{\textup{ex}}\left(\int\frac{|q_{2}^{\textup{in}}|^{2}}{U}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int\frac{|\nabla q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}}{U}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\frac{C(\zeta_{*},\zeta^{*},K)\nu^{6+3/4}}{|\ln\nu|^{4}}.

and the cut-off term

\displaystyle\left|\int q_{2}^{\textup{in}}\mathscr{M}_{0}\big(q\nabla\chi_{*}.(\nabla\Phi_{q\chi^{*}}+\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{q}(1-\chi^{*})}\big)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\Phi_{q\chi^{*}}+\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{q}(1-\chi^{*})}\|_{\infty}\left(\int\frac{|q_{2}^{\textup{in}}|^{2}}{U}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\times

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left[\left(\int\frac{|qy.\nabla\chi_{*}|^{2}}{y^{2}U}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\|\Phi_{q\nabla\chi_{*}}\|_{\infty}\left(\int U\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{C(\zeta_{*},\zeta^{*},K)\nu^{6}}{|\ln\nu|^{5}},

and the quadratic term by using the
\textup{supp}(\chi_{*})=\{|y|\leq\frac{2\zeta_{*}}{\nu}\}
, the
L^{\infty}
bound from Sobolev, (
6.23
) and (
6.25
),
\|q(s)\|_{L^{\infty}(|y|\leq 2\zeta_{*}/\nu)}\lesssim\|\tilde{q}(\tau)\|_{H^{2}_{\textup{in}}}+\|q(\tau)\|_{H^{1}_{\textup{in}}}+\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{bd}
,

\displaystyle\left|\int q_{2}^{\textup{in}}\mathscr{M}_{0}(q^{\textup{in}}q)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}|\left(\frac{|\nabla q^{\textup{in}}q|}{U}+\frac{q^{\textup{in}}\nabla q}{U}-\frac{|q^{\textup{in}}q|}{U}|\nabla\Phi_{U}|+|\nabla\Phi_{q^{\textup{in}}q}|\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left[\|q\|_{L^{\infty}(|y|\leq\frac{2\zeta_{*}}{\nu})}\left(\int\frac{|\nabla q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}}{U}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\|q^{\textup{in}}\|_{\infty}\left(\int_{|y|\leq\frac{2\zeta_{*}}{\nu}}\frac{|\nabla q|^{2}}{U}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdots\left.+\left(\int\frac{|q^{\textup{in}}|^{2}}{U}|\nabla\Phi_{U}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\|q\|_{L^{\infty}(|y|\leq\frac{2\zeta_{*}}{\nu})}\|\nabla\Phi_{q^{\textup{in}}}\|_{\infty}\left(\int U\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\|q(\tau)\|_{H^{1}_{\textup{in}}}^{2}\big(\|\tilde{q}(\tau)\|_{H^{2}_{\textup{in}}}+\|q(\tau)\|_{H^{1}_{\textup{in}}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{bd}\big)\lesssim\frac{C(\zeta_{*},\zeta^{*},K)\nu^{6}}{|\ln\nu|^{5}}.

The estimate for
\frac{\nu^{2}}{\zeta_{*}^{2}}\int\mathscr{M}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}NL(q)\chi_{*}
is simpler, so we omit it. Collecting all the estimates to the energy identity (
7.28
) and taking
\zeta_{*}\ll 1
,
\tau_{0}\gg 1
such that
\frac{1}{\zeta_{*}}\gg 1
and
\frac{1}{|\ln\nu|}\ll 1
and changing the available
\frac{d\tau}{ds}=\nu^{2}
yield the desired estimate (
7.37
). This concludes the proof of Lemma
7.2
.
∎

7.3.
H^{2}
energy estimate in the inner region

We perform a similar energy estimate to the precedent subsection. The extra condition we use here is the coercivity (
6.24
). Note by scaling that the typical size of
\|q(\tau)\|_{H^{2}_{\textup{in}}}
is of size
\mathcal{O}(\nu^{3}/|\ln\nu|)
, but we don’t need to go to this order as an
H^{2}_{\textup{in}}
estimate of size
\mathcal{O}(\nu^{2}/|\ln\nu|)
is enough to get a round
L^{\infty}
bound by Sobolev for
q^{\textup{in}}
of size
\mathcal{O}(\nu^{2}/|\ln\nu|)
in order to close the nonlinear estimate. We claim the following.

Lemma 7.3

(
H^{2}
-estimate)
.

Let
\varepsilon
be a solution to (
6.14
) and satisfy the bootstrap bounds in Definition
6.2
for
\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]
. Then, we have the following estimate for
\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]
:

\frac{d}{d\tau}\|\tilde{q}(\tau)\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{\textup{in}}}\leq-\delta_{2}\|\tilde{q}(\tau)\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{\textup{in}}}+C\Big(\|q(\tau)\|^{2}_{H^{1}_{\textup{in}}}+\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|^{2}_{bd}+\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}+\frac{\nu^{4}}{|\ln\nu|^{4}}\Big).

(7.37)

for some
\delta_{2}>0
and
C=C(\zeta_{*},\zeta^{*})>0
.

Proof.

We write from (
7.21
)mthe equation for
q_{2}^{\textup{in}}=\mathscr{L}_{0}q^{\textup{in}}
with
q^{\textup{in}}=q\chi_{*}
,

\partial_{s}q_{2}^{\textup{in}}=\mathscr{L}_{0}q_{2}^{\textup{in}}+\mathscr{L}_{0}\Big[[\chi_{*},\mathscr{L}_{0}]q+\big(L_{1}q-NL(q)+\nu^{4}E\big)\chi_{*}\Big],

(7.38)

where the commutator
[\chi_{*},\mathscr{L}_{0}]q=\chi_{*}\mathscr{L}_{0}q-\mathscr{L}_{0}(q\chi_{*})
. We then write the energy identity for
\tilde{q}_{2}^{\textup{in}}
introduced by (
6.23
) by using
\mathscr{M}_{0}\Lambda U=-2
,
\mathscr{M}_{0}\nabla U=0
,
\int q_{2}^{\textup{in}}=0
, Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the divergence form of
\mathscr{L}_{0}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{ds}\int\tilde{q}_{2}^{\textup{in}}\mathscr{M}_{0}\mathscr{L}_{0}\tilde{q}_{2}^{\textup{in}}

\displaystyle=-\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}\tilde{q}^{\textup{in}}_{2}|^{2}+\int\mathscr{M}_{0}\tilde{q}_{2}^{\textup{in}}\mathscr{L}_{0}\Big[[\chi_{*},\mathscr{L}_{0}]q+\big(L_{1}q-NL(q)+\nu^{4}E\big)\chi_{*}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim-\frac{1}{2}\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}\tilde{q}_{2}^{\textup{in}}|^{2}+\int U\Big|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}\Big([\chi_{*},\mathscr{L}_{0}]q+\big(L_{1}q-NL(q)+\nu^{4}E\big)\chi_{*}\Big)\Big|^{2}.

We recall from Lemma
4.5
the coercivity estimate
-\int U|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}\tilde{q}_{2}^{\textup{in}}|\leq-\delta\int\frac{|\nabla\tilde{q}_{2}^{\textup{in}}|^{2}}{U}
. We have by Hardy inequality and the support of
\chi_{*}
the estimate
-\int\frac{|\nabla\tilde{q}_{2}^{\textup{in}}|^{2}}{U}\lesssim-\frac{\nu^{2}}{\zeta_{*}^{2}}\int\frac{|\tilde{q}_{2}^{\textup{in}}|^{2}}{U}
which shows the dissipation to control all lower order terms. The estimate of all the terms in
\int U\big|\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}\big[\big(L_{1}q-NL(q)+\nu^{4}E\big)\chi_{*}\big]\big|^{2}
follows the same lines as for Lemma
7.2
, where we emphasize again the crucial algebraic identities
\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}\Lambda U=\nabla\mathscr{M}_{0}\nabla U=0
to gain a factor
\nu^{2}
once the generated error term
\nu^{4}E
is concerned. The small linear term is estimated in the same way, except that there is one more derivative in comparison with the proof of Lemma
7.2
that we use in addition
\|q(\tau)\|_{H^{1}_{\textup{in}}}
bound. The nonlinear term is estimated by
\frac{C(\zeta_{*},\zeta^{*},K)\nu^{6}}{|\ln\nu|^{5}}
thanks to the round
L^{\infty}
bound
\|q^{\textup{in}}(\tau)\|_{\infty}\lesssim C(K)\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}
, hence, we always gain an extra small factor
\frac{1}{|\ln\nu|}
. The extra commutator term is located in the support of
\nabla\chi_{*}
and is bounded by
C(\zeta_{*})\nu^{2}\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|^{2}_{bd}
thanks to the decay of
U(y)\lesssim(1+|y|^{4})^{-1}
. This concludes the proof of Lemma
7.3
.
∎

7.4. Control in the middle and outer regions

We recall the two fixed positive constants

\zeta^{*}\gg 1,\quad\zeta_{*}\ll 1,

and claim the following.

Lemma 7.4

(
H^{2}
control of
\varepsilon
in the middle zone)
.

Let
\varepsilon
be a solution to (
6.14
) and satisfy the bootstrap bounds in Definition
6.2
for
\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]
. Then, we have the following estimate for
\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]
:

\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{H^{2}(B_{\zeta^{*}}\setminus\cup_{\pm}B_{\zeta_{*}}(\pm a))}\leq C(\zeta^{*},\zeta_{*})K\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}.

(7.39)

Proof.

We note from the bound (
6.31
) and the definition of the weight function
\omega_{\nu}
which is of size
\omega_{\nu}(z)\sim 1
for
|z\pm a|\sim 1
, the
L^{2}
estimate

\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{L^{2}(Q_{4})}\leq C(\zeta_{*},\zeta^{*})K\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}},\quad Q_{4}=\big\{z\in B_{4\zeta^{*}}\setminus\cup_{\pm}B_{\zeta_{*}/4}(\pm a)\big\}.

(7.40)

From this
L^{2}
bound and a standard parabolic regularity, see for example Lemma 4.7 in
[
CGMN21
]
, we obtain the desired estimate.
∎

As for the control in the outer region, we use the maximum principle for a sake of simplicity even through other techniques such as standard-energy estimates gives a similar result. We claim the following.

Lemma 7.5

(Weighted
L^{\infty}
control of
\tilde{\varepsilon}
in the outer zone)
.

Let
\tilde{\varepsilon}
be a solution to (
6.10
) and satisfy the bootstrap bounds in Definition
6.2
for
\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]
and
|\tilde{\varepsilon}(z,\tau_{0})|\leq\nu^{2}(\tau_{0})\langle z\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}}
for
z\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Then, we have the following estimate for
\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]
and
|z|\geq\zeta^{*}\gg 1
:

\big|\tilde{\varepsilon}(z,\tau)\big|\leq\frac{1}{2}K^{3}\nu^{2}\,|z|^{-\frac{3}{2}}.

(7.41)

Proof.

We rewrite equation (
6.10
) as

\tilde{\varepsilon}_{\tau}=\Delta\tilde{\varepsilon}-\big(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+\frac{1}{2}z\big).\nabla\tilde{\varepsilon}-\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{\varepsilon}}-(1-2U_{1+2,\nu})\tilde{\varepsilon}+\tilde{E}(z,\tau).

(7.42)

We note from the definition of
U_{1+2,\nu}(z)
and the bootstrap bound
|\nu_{\tau}/\nu|\leq K/|\ln\nu|
, the estimates for
|z|\geq\zeta^{*}\gg 1
,

|U_{1+2,\nu}(z)|\lesssim\frac{\nu^{2}}{|z|^{4}},\quad|\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}(z)|\lesssim\frac{\nu^{2}}{|z|^{5}},\quad|\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}(z)|\lesssim\frac{1}{|z|},\quad|E(z,\tau)|\lesssim K\frac{\nu^{2}}{|z|^{4}}.

(7.43)

We note that there is
-\tilde{\varepsilon}
term in (
7.42
) that gives a decay estimate, we simply consider the functions

\tilde{h}^{*}(z,\tau)=\frac{1}{2}K^{3}\nu^{2}|z|^{-\frac{3}{2}},\quad\tilde{h}_{*}(z,\tau)=-\frac{1}{2}K^{3}\nu^{2}|z|^{-\frac{3}{2}},

that serves as a sub and super solutions to (
7.42
). The information we need to justify this fact is a pointwise estimate of the Poison field
\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{h}^{*}}
and the boundary data
\tilde{\varepsilon}(z,\tau)
at
|z|=\zeta^{*}
that can be retrieved from the a priori bootstrap bounds given in Definition
6.2
. As for the estimate of
\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{h}^{*}}
, we have from Lemma
B.1
with
\gamma=\frac{3}{2}
the estimate

\forall|z|\geq\zeta^{*},\quad|\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{h}^{*}}(z)|\lesssim\big\|\langle z\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}\tilde{h}^{*}(z)\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}|z|^{-\sigma}\lesssim K^{3}\nu^{2}|z|^{-\sigma},\quad\sigma\in(0,\frac{1}{2}).

(7.44)

From (
7.43
) and (
7.44
), we estimate for
|z|\geq\zeta^{*}
,

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\tilde{h}^{*}}\Big[\tilde{h}^{*}_{\tau}-\Delta\tilde{h}^{*}+\big(\nabla\Phi_{U_{1+2,\nu}}+\frac{1}{2}z\big).\nabla\tilde{h}^{*}+\nabla U_{1+2,\nu}.\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{h}^{*}}+(1-2U_{1+2,\nu})\tilde{h}^{*}-\tilde{E}(z,\tau)\Big]

\displaystyle\geq-\Big|\frac{2\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}\Big|-\frac{9}{4|z|^{2}}-\frac{C}{|z|^{2}}-\frac{3}{4}-\frac{C\nu^{2}}{|z|^{7/2}}\frac{1}{|z|^{\sigma}}+1-\frac{C\nu^{2}}{|z|^{4}}-\frac{C}{K^{3}|z|^{5/2}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4}-\frac{C}{|\ln\nu|}-\frac{C}{(\zeta^{*})^{2}}>0,

for
\tau_{0}
and
\zeta^{*}
large enough.
We check the boundary
|z|=\zeta^{*}
from the decomposition (
6.9
),
\tilde{\varepsilon}=\tilde{W}+\varepsilon
, the bootstrap
H^{2}
-bound (
6.32
), and the pointwise estimate on
\tilde{W}=\Xi+\alpha(\phi_{1}-\phi_{0})
for
|z|=\zeta^{*}
,

\forall|z|=\zeta^{*},\quad|\tilde{\varepsilon}(z,\tau)|=|\tilde{W}(z,\tau)|+|\varepsilon(z,\tau)|\leq\frac{C\nu^{2}}{|z|^{2}}+K^{3}\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|}<\frac{K^{3}\nu^{2}}{2|z|^{\frac{3}{2}}}.

Similar estimates hold for
\tilde{h}_{*}
, and the desired estimate follows from a comparison principle. ∎

7.5. Proof of the main theorem

The existence proof of the main theorem
1.1
follows once we show that the residue
\tilde{\varepsilon}
belongs to the bootstrap regime
6.2
for all time
\tau\geq\tau_{0}
provided that the initial data
\varepsilon(z,\tau_{0})
is well prepared and belongs to the bootstrap regime
6.2
as well. We assume by contradiction that

\tau^{*}=\sup\{\tau_{1}\geq\tau_{0}\;\textup{such that the solution belongs to \ref{def:bootstrap} on}\;[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]\}<+\infty.

We will show that this is impossible by solving the modulation equations obtained in Lemma
6.3
, the various monotone energy estimates in Lemmas
7.1
,
7.2
,
7.3
,
7.4
and
7.5
.

-
Improved estimates for modulation parameter functions:
We write from Lemma
6.3
, the bootstrap definition (
6.2
), the eigenvalues
\lambda_{0}=1+\frac{\gamma_{0}}{\ln\nu}+\mathcal{O}(|\ln\nu|^{-2})
and
\lambda_{1}=\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\ln\nu}+\mathcal{O}(|\ln\nu|^{-2})
from Proposition
3.1
and the fixed
\beta=\frac{1}{2}
, the system for the three parameter functions
\nu(\tau),\alpha(\tau)
and
a(\tau)
,

\displaystyle-16\nu^{2}\Big(\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{2\ln\nu}\Big)+\alpha_{\tau}-\Big(1+\frac{\gamma_{0}}{\ln\nu})\alpha=\mathcal{O}\Big(\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\Big),

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha_{\tau}}{\alpha}-\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\ln\nu}=\mathcal{O}\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\Big),\qquad\qquad a_{\tau}=\frac{a}{2}-\frac{8a}{\nu^{2}+4|a|^{2}}+\mathcal{O}\Big(\frac{\nu}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\Big).

The first equation can be rearranged as

\Big(8\nu^{2}-\alpha\Big)\Big(1+\frac{\gamma_{0}}{\ln\nu}\Big)+(\alpha-8\nu^{2})_{\tau}=\mathcal{O}\Big(\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\Big).

We note that the choice
\alpha=8\nu^{2}
makes the left-hand side vanish and the relation
\frac{\alpha_{\tau}}{\alpha}=2\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}
. Thus, the second equation for
\alpha
gives

\Big|\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}-\frac{\gamma_{1}}{2\ln\nu}\Big|\leq\frac{C}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}.

(7.45)

where
C>0
is independent of the bootstrap constant
K
. Hence, we take
\bar{K}
large for which the bootstrap estimate (
6.29
) holds with a strict inequality. The solution to (
7.45
) can be explicitly obtained by integrating in time,

\ln^{2}\nu(\tau)=\gamma_{1}\tau\Big(1+\mathcal{O}\big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\tau}}\big)\Big),\quad\nu(\tau)=e^{-\sqrt{\gamma_{1}\tau}+\mathcal{O}(1)}.

In terms of the variable
t
, we recall that
\tau=-\ln(T-t)
, we then obtain the final blowup law as

\lambda(t)=\sqrt{T-t}\nu(\tau)=C_{0}\sqrt{T-t}e^{-\sqrt{\gamma_{1}|\ln(T-t)|}}\quad\textup{as}\;\;t\to T.

We then introduce the linearization

\alpha=8\nu^{2}+\tilde{\alpha},\quad a=\bar{a}+\tilde{a},\quad\bar{a}=(2,0),

where
\bar{a}
are the exact solutions to

\frac{2\bar{a}}{|a|^{2}}=\frac{\bar{a}}{2}.

The linearized equations for
\tilde{\alpha}
and
\tilde{a}
satisfy

\displaystyle\tilde{\alpha}_{\tau}=\tilde{\alpha}\Big(1+\frac{\gamma_{0}}{\ln\nu}\Big)+\mathcal{O}\Big(\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\Big),

(7.46)

\displaystyle\tilde{a}_{\tau}=\frac{\bar{a}}{2}\bar{a}.\tilde{a}+\mathcal{O}\Big(|\tilde{a}|^{2}+\frac{\nu}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\Big).

(7.47)

These two ODEs do not have a decaying solution for arbitrary initial data, and we shall solve them by a Brouwer fixed point argument later.

-
Improved energy bounds:
From Lemma
7.1
,
6.3
and the bootstrap bounds in
6.2
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\tau}\langle\hat{\varepsilon},\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}

\displaystyle\leq-\delta_{0}\langle\hat{\varepsilon},\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}+\frac{C}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\Big(\|q(\tau)\|_{H^{1}_{\textup{in}}}^{2}+\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{\omega_{\nu}}^{2}\Big)+\frac{C\nu^{4}}{|\ln\nu|^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq-\delta_{0}\langle\hat{\varepsilon},\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}+\frac{C\nu^{4}}{|\ln\nu|^{4}}\leq-\delta_{0}\langle\hat{\varepsilon},\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}+CK\frac{e^{-4\sqrt{\gamma_{1}\tau}}}{\tau^{2}},

where we used the bootstrap
|\nu(\tau)|\leq\bar{K}e^{-\sqrt{\gamma_{1}\tau}}
and
|\ln\nu(\tau)|\sim\sqrt{\tau}
. For a fixed
\tau_{0}\gg 1
, consider
\tau_{1}\in[\tau_{0},2\tau_{0}]
and integrate in time the above inequality, we get

\displaystyle\langle\hat{\varepsilon},\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\delta_{0}(\tau_{1}-\tau_{0})}+CKe^{-\delta_{0}\tau_{1}}\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}e^{\delta_{0}\tau}\frac{e^{-4\sqrt{\gamma_{1}\tau}}}{\tau^{2}}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq CK\frac{e^{-4\sqrt{\gamma_{1}\tau_{1}}}}{\tau_{1}^{2}}\leq\frac{K^{2}}{4}\frac{\nu(\tau_{1})^{4}}{|\ln\nu(\tau_{1})|^{4}},

where we used integration by parts and
1/\tau_{1}\sim 1/\tau\sim 1/\tau_{0}
for
\tau,\tau_{1}\in[\tau_{0},2\tau_{0}]
and
\tau_{0}\gg 1
. We recall from (
4.174
) the equivalence
\hat{\varepsilon},\hat{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\ast}\sim\|\varepsilon\|^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}
, hence, we get the improved bound

\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{\omega_{\nu}}\leq\frac{K}{2}\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}},\quad\forall\tau\in[\tau_{0},2\tau_{0}].

From Lemmas
7.4
and (
7.5
), we already obtain the improved bounds

\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{bd}\leq CK\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\leq\frac{K^{2}}{2}\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}},\quad\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{\textup{ex}}\leq\frac{K^{5}}{2}\nu^{2}.

For the improvement of
H^{1}
bound, we have from the energy estimate obtained in Lemma
7.2
and the bootstrap definition
6.2
,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\tau}\|q(\tau)\|_{H^{1}_{\textup{in}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{\zeta_{*}}\|q(\tau)\|_{H^{1}_{\textup{in}}}^{2}+CK^{4}\frac{\nu^{4}}{|\ln\nu|^{4}},

from which we integrate in time to get the estimate

\|q(\tau)\|_{H^{1}_{\textup{in}}}^{2}\leq CK^{4}\frac{\nu^{4}}{|\ln\nu|^{4}}\leq\frac{K^{6}}{4}\frac{\nu^{4}}{|\ln\nu|^{4}}.

Similarly, we write from the energy estimate obtained in Lemma
7.3
and the bootstrap definition
6.2
,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\tau}\|\tilde{q}(\tau)\|_{H^{2}_{\textup{in}}}^{2}\leq-\delta_{2}\|\tilde{q}(\tau)\|_{H^{2}_{\textup{in}}}^{2}+CK^{6}\frac{\nu^{4}}{|\ln\nu|^{4}},

thus, the improved bound

\|\tilde{q}(\tau)\|_{H^{2}_{\textup{in}}}^{2}\leq CK^{6}\frac{\nu^{4}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}}\leq\frac{K^{8}}{4}\frac{\nu^{4}}{|\ln\nu|^{4}}.

We have shown that all the bootstrap estimates in Definition
6.2
are improved, except for the growing modes
\tilde{\alpha}
and
\tilde{a}
. In fact, at the exit time
\tau=\tau^{*}
, we compute from the two odes satisfied by
\tilde{\alpha}
and
\tilde{a}
,

\frac{d}{d\tau}\big(|\tilde{\alpha}|^{2}\big)\Big|_{\tau=\tau^{*}}>0,\quad\frac{d}{d\tau}\big(|\tilde{a}|^{2}\big)\Big|_{\tau=\tau^{*}}>0.

By a Brouwer fixed-point argument, there exists initial data
\tilde{\alpha}(\tau_{0})
and
\tilde{a}(\tau_{0})
such that
|\tilde{\alpha}(\tau)|\leq\frac{\bar{K}\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|}
and
|\tilde{a}(\tau)|\leq\bar{K}\nu^{2}
for all
\tau\geq\tau_{0}
. Hence, the solution
\varepsilon(z,\tau)
is trapped in the bootstrap regime
6.2
for all
\tau\geq\tau_{0}
.

-
Positivity and
16\pi
mass concentration at the origin of the constructed solution:
In terms of the self-similar variable (
2.7
) and the decomposition (
6.13
), we have constructed initial data
w(\tau_{0})
for (
2.7
) of the form

w(z,\tau_{0})=U_{\nu_{0}}(z-a_{0})+U_{\nu_{0}}(z+a_{0})+\tilde{W}[\nu_{0},a_{0},\alpha_{0}](z)+\varepsilon_{0}(z),

(7.48)

where
(\varepsilon_{0},\nu_{0},a_{0},\alpha_{0})
satisfy estimates given in the bootstrap regime
6.2
, from which the solution
w(z,\tau)
to (
2.7
) is decomposed as

w(z,\tau)=U_{\nu}(z-a)+U_{\nu}(z+a)+\tilde{W}[\nu,a,\alpha](z)+\varepsilon(z,\tau),\quad\forall\tau\geq\tau_{0},

(7.49)

where
(\varepsilon(\tau),\nu(\tau),a(\tau),\alpha(\tau))
is trapped in the bootstrap regime
6.2
for all time
\tau\geq\tau_{0}
with a specific choice of initial data
\tilde{a}(\tau_{0})
and
\tilde{\alpha}(\tau_{0})
. We first claim that the initial data (
7.48
) is nonnegative, so is our constructed solution
w(z,\tau)
. We fix
0<\eta_{0}\ll 1
and split the domain into
|z\pm a_{0}|\leq\eta_{0}
and
|z\pm a_{0}|\leq\eta_{0}
. In the region
|z\pm a_{0}|\geq\eta_{0}
, by the construction of the approximate eigenfunctions (see Proposition
3.1
) and the corrected steady state (
5.5
) (see Proposition
5.1
) and
\alpha_{0}\sim\nu_{0}^{2}
, we have

|\tilde{W}[\nu_{0},a_{0},\alpha_{0}](z)|=\big|\alpha_{0}\big(\phi_{1}[\nu_{0},a_{0}](z)-\phi_{0}[\nu_{0},a_{0}](z)\big)+\Xi[\nu_{0},a_{0}](z)\big|\lesssim\frac{\nu_{0}^{2}}{\nu_{0}^{2}+|z\pm a_{0}|^{2}}\lesssim\frac{\nu_{0}^{2}}{\eta_{0}^{2}},

and since
\varepsilon_{0}
satisfies the bootstrap estimates which implies that
|\varepsilon_{0}(z)|\leq C(K)\nu_{0}^{2}
, and by the definition of
U_{\nu_{0}}(z\pm a_{0})
, we have for
|z\pm a_{0}|\geq\eta_{0}
,

U_{\nu_{0}}(z\pm a_{0})=\frac{8\nu_{0}^{2}}{(\nu_{0}+|z\pm a_{0}|)^{4}}\geq\frac{8\nu_{0}^{2}}{\eta_{0}^{4}}\gg\frac{\nu_{0}^{2}}{\eta_{0}^{2}}+C(K)\nu_{0}^{2},

from which we get
w(z,\tau_{0})>0
for
|z\pm a_{0}|\geq\eta_{0}
. In the region
|z\pm a|\leq\eta_{0}
, we compute from the expansion of the inner approximate eigenfunction (see Proposition
3.4
), we write in the variable
y_{\pm}=\frac{z\mp a_{0}}{\nu_{0}}
,

U_{\nu_{0}}(z\pm a)+\tilde{W}(\nu_{0},a_{0},\alpha_{0})(z)+\varepsilon_{0}(z)=\frac{1}{\nu_{0}^{2}}\Big[U(y_{\pm})+\mathcal{O}(\sum_{k=2}^{4}\nu_{0}^{k}\langle y_{\pm}\rangle^{k-4})+\|q_{0}\mathbf{1}_{\{|y_{\pm}|\leq\frac{\eta_{0}}{\nu_{0}}\}}\|_{\infty}\Big],

where
q_{0}(y_{\pm})=\nu_{0}^{2}\varepsilon_{0}(z)
and we have from the bootstrap definition
6.2
the bound
\|q_{0}\mathbf{1}_{\{|y_{\pm}|\leq\frac{\eta_{0}}{\nu_{0}}\}}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\frac{\nu_{0}^{2}}{|\ln\nu_{0}|^{2}}
, which implies that
w(z,\tau_{0})>0
in the region
\langle y_{\pm}\rangle\leq\frac{\eta_{0}}{\nu_{0}}
. This proves the positivity of the initial data
w(z,\tau_{0})
, so is
w(z,\tau)
.

We also compute the total mass concentrating at the origin by estimating from the bootstrap definition
6.2
,

\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{L^{1}(|z|\lesssim 1)}\lesssim\|\varepsilon(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}}}\lesssim\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{2}},\quad\|\tilde{W}\|_{L^{1}(|z|\lesssim 1)}\lesssim\nu^{2}|\ln\nu|,

hence, for any
A\gg 1
fixed constant,

\int_{|x|\leq A\sqrt{T-t}}u(x,t)dx=\int_{|z|\leq A}w(z,\tau)dz=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}U_{1+2,\nu}(z)dz+\mathcal{O}(\nu^{2}|\ln\nu|)=16\pi+\mathcal{O}(\nu^{2}|\ln\nu|).

This fact shows that our constructed solution has a
16\pi
mass concentrating at the origin. This concludes the proof of the existence part of Theorem
1.1
.

Appendix A
Inhomogenous linearized system around a stationary state

In this subsection we solve for
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
the system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}\mathscr{L}_{0}(w,\phi_{w})=f(r)\cos(\ell\theta),\\
-\Delta\phi_{w}=w,\end{array}\right.

following to a larger extent
[
Vel02
,
SSV13
]
.

A.1. The radial case

For
\ell=0
, we introduce the partial mass variable:

m_{w}(r)=\int_{0}^{r}w(r^{\prime})r^{\prime}dr^{\prime},\quad w=\frac{\partial_{r}m_{w}}{r},\quad\partial_{r}\Phi_{w}=-\frac{m_{w}}{r},

where
m_{w}
solves the equation

\mathscr{A}_{0}m_{w}=m_{f}\quad\textup{with}\quad\mathscr{A}_{0}=\partial_{r}^{2}-\frac{\partial_{r}}{r}+\frac{Q}{r}\partial_{r}+\frac{\partial_{r}Q}{r},\quad Q=\frac{4r^{2}}{1+r^{2}}.

(A.1)

Lemma A.1

(Inversion of
\mathscr{A}_{0}
)
.

For any
g\in\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{R}^{+},\mathbb{R})
, a solution to
\mathscr{A}_{0}u=g
is given by

\mathscr{A}_{0}^{-1}g=\frac{1}{2}\psi_{0}\int_{r}^{1}\frac{\zeta^{4}+4\zeta^{2}\ln\zeta-1}{\zeta}g(\zeta)d\zeta+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\psi}_{0}\int_{0}^{r}\zeta g(\zeta)d\zeta,

(A.2)

where
\psi_{0}
and
\tilde{\psi}_{0}
are the two fundamental solutions to
\mathscr{A}_{0}\phi=0
given by

\psi_{0}=\frac{r^{2}}{(1+r^{2})^{2}},\quad\tilde{\psi}_{0}=\frac{r^{4}+4r^{2}\ln r-1}{(1+r^{2})^{2}}.

(A.3)

A.2. The non radial case

For
\ell\geq 1
, we look for the solution of the form

w=\psi(r)\cos(\ell\theta),\quad\phi_{w}=\omega(r)\cos(\ell\theta),

where
(\psi,\omega)
satisfies the system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{r}^{2}\psi+\dfrac{\partial_{r}\psi}{r}-\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{r^{2}}\psi+\dfrac{4r}{1+r^{2}}\partial_{r}\psi+\dfrac{32r}{(1+r^{2})^{3}}\partial_{r}\omega+\dfrac{16\psi}{(1+r^{2})^{2}}=f(r),\\
\partial_{r}^{2}\omega+\dfrac{\partial_{r}\omega}{r}-\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{r^{2}}\omega+\psi=0.\end{array}\right.

(A.4)

System (
A.4
) can be solved in the closed form thanks to the following result.

Lemma A.2

(Solution of the homogeneous system (
A.4
))
.

For
\ell\geq 1,
there exist four linearly independent solutions
(\psi,\omega)
of the homogegenous system (
A.4
) with
f\equiv 0
:

\displaystyle(h_{\ell,1},g_{\ell,1})

\displaystyle=[(\ell-1)r^{2}+\ell+1]\left(\frac{8r^{\ell}}{(r^{2}+1)^{3}},\frac{r^{\ell}}{r^{2}+1}\right),

\displaystyle(h_{\ell,2},g_{\ell,2})

\displaystyle=\Big(r^{4}+4r^{2}\ln r-1\Big)\left(\frac{8}{r(r^{2}+1)^{3}},\frac{1}{r(r^{2}+1)}\right)\quad\textup{for $\ell=1$},

\displaystyle(h_{\ell,2},g_{\ell,2})

\displaystyle=[(\ell+1)r^{2}+\ell-1]\left(\frac{8}{r^{\ell}(r^{2}+1)^{3}},\frac{1}{r^{\ell}(r^{2}+1)}\right)\quad\textup{for $\ell\geq 2$},

\displaystyle(h_{\ell,3},g_{\ell,3})

\displaystyle=(8r^{\ell}+O(r^{\ell+2}),-r^{\ell}+O(r^{\ell+2}))\qquad\textup{as}\;\;r\to 0,

\displaystyle(h_{\ell,3},g_{\ell,3})

\displaystyle=K_{\ell}r^{\sqrt{\ell^{2}+4}}\left(16r^{-2}+O(r^{-4}),-4+O(r^{\ell-\sqrt{\ell^{2}+4}})\right)\quad\textup{as}\;\;r\to+\infty,

\displaystyle(h_{\ell,4},g_{\ell,4})

\displaystyle=(c_{1,4}r^{-1},c_{1,4}^{\prime}r^{-1})+O(r)\qquad\text{as}\;\;r\to 0,\quad(c_{1,4},c_{1,4}^{\prime})\neq(0,0),\quad\textup{for $\ell=1$},

\displaystyle(h_{\ell,4},g_{\ell,4})

\displaystyle=\left(r^{-\sqrt{5}-2}+O(r^{-\sqrt{5}-4}),\frac{-1}{4}r^{-\sqrt{5}}+O(r^{-\sqrt{5}-2})\right)\quad\textup{as}\;\;r\to+\infty\quad\textup{for $\ell=1$},

\displaystyle(h_{\ell,4},g_{\ell,4})

\displaystyle=(c_{\ell,4}r^{-\ell}+O(r^{-\ell+2}),c_{\ell,4}^{\prime}r^{-\ell}+O(r^{-\ell+2}))\qquad\text{as}\;\;r\to 0,\quad(c_{\ell,4},c_{\ell,4}^{\prime})\neq(0,0)\quad\textup{for $\ell\geq 2$},

\displaystyle(h_{\ell,4},g_{\ell,4})

\displaystyle=C_{\ell}\left(16r^{-\sqrt{\ell^{2}+4}-2}+O(r^{-\ell-4}),-4r^{-{\sqrt{\ell^{2}+4}}}+o(r^{-\ell-2})\right)\quad\textup{as}\;\;r\to+\infty\quad\textup{for $\ell\geq 2$},

for some constants
K_{\ell},C_{\ell}>0
and
\kappa_{\ell}\geq 0
. These estimates propagate for higher order derivatives. Moreover, we have for
\ell\geq 2
,

C_{\ell}K_{\ell}=\frac{\ell}{\sqrt{\ell^{2}+4}}.

(A.5)

and we have for
\ell\geq 1
,

W_{\ell}:=\left|\begin{matrix}h_{\ell,1}&h_{\ell,2}&h_{\ell,3}&h_{\ell,4}\\
g_{\ell,1}&g_{\ell,2}&g_{\ell,3}&g_{\ell,4}\\
h_{\ell,1}^{\prime}&h_{\ell,2}^{\prime}&h_{\ell,3}^{\prime}&h_{\ell,4}^{\prime}\\
g_{\ell,1}^{\prime}&g_{\ell,2}^{\prime}&g_{\ell,3}^{\prime}&g_{\ell,4}^{\prime}\end{matrix}\right|=\frac{E_{\ell}}{r^{2}(1+r^{2})^{2}}.

(A.6)

with
E_{1}=-128\sqrt{5}K_{1}
and
E_{\ell}=-2^{10}(\ell+1)(\ell-1)\ell^{2}
for
\ell\geq 2
.

Proof.

Step 1
.
The solutions
. The functions
(h_{\ell,k},g_{\ell,k})
are obtained for
\ell\geq 2
and
k=1,2,3
in Theorem 3.2 in
[
SSV13
]
in Theorem 4.3 in
[
Vel02
]
, and for
\ell=1
and
k=1,2,3
in Theorem 4.2 in
[
Vel02
]
. The main term in the asymptotic behaviours as
r\to 0
and
r\to\infty
for
h_{\ell,3}
and
g_{\ell,3}
is obtained in these references. The remainders in the
O()^{\prime}s
for
h_{\ell,3}
and
g_{\ell,3}
are easily obtained from the proofs of Theorems 4.2 and 4.3 in
[
Vel02
]
.

The only difference between these references and the present lemma is the behaviour as
r\to\infty
of
(h_{\ell,4},g_{\ell,4})
. First, for
\ell\geq 2
, in
[
SSV13
]
a solution
(\tilde{h}_{\ell,4},\tilde{g}_{\ell,4})
is obtained such that

\displaystyle(\tilde{h}_{\ell,4},\tilde{g}_{\ell,4})

\displaystyle\sim(8r^{-\ell},-r^{-\ell})\qquad\text{as}\;\;r\to 0,\quad\textup{for $\ell\geq 2$},

\displaystyle(\tilde{h}_{\ell,4},\tilde{g}_{\ell,4})

\displaystyle=C_{\ell}\left(16r^{-\sqrt{\ell^{2}+4}-2}+O(r^{-\ell-4}),\kappa_{\ell}r^{-\ell}-4r^{-{\sqrt{\ell^{2}+4}}}+o(r^{-\ell-2})\right)\;\textup{as}\;r\to+\infty\;\;\textup{for $\ell\geq 2$}.

where
\kappa_{\ell}\in\mathbb{R}
and
C_{\ell}>0
. We define
(h_{\ell,4},g_{\ell,4})=(\tilde{h}_{\ell,4},\tilde{g}_{\ell,4})-\frac{C_{\ell}\kappa_{\ell}}{\ell+1}(h_{\ell,2},g_{\ell,2})
which is a solution that satisfies the desired behaviour of the Lemma.

For
\ell=1
we actually need to compute
(g_{1,4},h_{1,4})
. We choose one that has an asymptotic behaviour that is different from an analogue solution obtained in Theorem 4.2 in
[
Vel02
]
because there is an error there in the computation of a constant called
B_{1}
on page 1598.

We recall from page 1596 in
[
Vel02
]
first that for
\ell=1
a function
(\psi,\omega)
solves (
A.4
) if and only if it can be decomposed as

(\psi,\omega)=\left(\frac{8}{r(r^{2}+1)^{3}}(F+G),\frac{1}{r(r^{2}+1)}(F-G)\right)

where
(F,G)
solves

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}F-\left(\frac{1}{r}+\frac{4r}{r^{2}+1}\right)\partial_{r}F+\frac{8}{r^{2}+1}F=\frac{4}{r^{2}+1}(r\partial_{r}G-3G),

(A.7)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}G-\left(\frac{1}{r}+\frac{8r}{r^{2}+1}\right)\partial_{r}G+\left(\frac{20}{r^{2}+1}-\frac{16}{(r^{2}+1)^{2}}\right)G=0,

(A.8)

and second that solutions to (
A.7
) with
G\equiv 0
are given by

\displaystyle F_{1}=2r^{2}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad F_{2}=r^{4}+4r^{2}\log r-1.

(A.9)

Construction of a particular solution to (
A.8
)
.
We claim that there exists a solution to (
A.8
) of the form

G(r)=r^{5-\sqrt{5}}+cr^{3-\sqrt{5}}+\tilde{G},\quad c\in\mathbb{R},\quad|\nabla^{k}\tilde{G}(r)|\lesssim r^{1-\sqrt{5}-k}\quad\mbox{as }r\to\infty.

(A.10)

Indeed, we decompose

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}-\left(\frac{1}{r}+\frac{8r}{r^{2}+1}\right)\partial_{r}+\left(\frac{20}{r^{2}+1}-\frac{16}{(r^{2}+1)^{2}}\right)=L+\tilde{L},

\displaystyle L=\partial_{r}^{2}-\frac{9}{r}\partial_{r}+\frac{20}{r^{2}},

\displaystyle\tilde{L}=-\frac{8}{r(r^{2}+1)}\partial_{r}+\left(\frac{16}{(r^{2}+1)^{2}}+\frac{20}{r^{2}(r^{2}+1)}\right)=\frac{8}{r^{3}}\partial_{r}-\frac{36}{r^{4}}+O(r^{-5})\partial_{r}+O(r^{-6}).

Hence for the choice
c=\frac{8\sqrt{5}-4}{6+4\sqrt{5}}
we have

H(r)=\left(\partial_{r}^{2}-\left(\frac{1}{r}+\frac{8r}{r^{2}+1}\right)\partial_{r}+\left(\frac{20}{r^{2}+1}-\frac{16}{(r^{2}+1)^{2}}\right)\right)(r^{5-\sqrt{5}}+cr^{3-\sqrt{5}})=O(r^{-1-\sqrt{5}}).

as
r\to\infty
. The kernel of
L
is spanned by
r^{5-\sqrt{5}}
and
r^{5+\sqrt{5}}
so that for Schwartz functions
f
a particular solution
u=L^{-1}f
to
Lu=f
is given by

L^{-1}f(r)=-\frac{r^{5+\sqrt{5}}}{2\sqrt{5}}\int_{r}^{\infty}\rho^{-4-\sqrt{5}}f(\rho)d\rho+\frac{r^{5+\sqrt{5}}}{2\sqrt{5}}\int_{r}^{\infty}\rho^{-4+\sqrt{5}}f(\rho)d\rho.

We compute, by an integration by parts, that for Schwartz functions
f
,

\displaystyle L^{-1}\tilde{L}f

\displaystyle=\sum_{\pm}\mp\frac{r^{5\pm\sqrt{5}}}{2\sqrt{5}}\int_{r}^{\infty}\left[\rho^{-4\mp\sqrt{5}}\left(\frac{16}{(\rho^{2}+1)^{2}}+\frac{20}{\rho^{2}(\rho^{2}+1)}\right)+8\partial_{\rho}\left(\frac{\rho^{-5\mp\sqrt{5}}}{\rho^{2}+1}\right)\right]d\rho.

A solution of (
A.8
) is of the form (
A.10
) if and only if
\tilde{G}
solves
(L+\tilde{L})\tilde{G}=+H
. We obtain the solution
\tilde{G}
of this latter equation by solving the fixed point equation
L\tilde{G}=-L^{-1}\tilde{L}\tilde{F}+L^{-1}H
, i.e.

\displaystyle\tilde{G}(r)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\pm}\mp\frac{r^{5\pm\sqrt{5}}}{2\sqrt{5}}\int_{r}^{\infty}\rho^{-4\mp\sqrt{5}}H(\rho)d\rho

\displaystyle+\sum_{\pm}\pm\frac{r^{5\pm\sqrt{5}}}{2\sqrt{5}}\int_{r}^{\infty}\left[\rho^{-4\mp\sqrt{5}}\left(\frac{16}{(\rho^{2}+1)^{2}}+\frac{20}{\rho^{2}(\rho^{2}+1)}\right)+8\partial_{\rho}\left(\frac{\rho^{-5\mp\sqrt{5}}}{\rho^{2}+1}\right)\right]G(\rho)d\rho.

The above defines a contraction in
L^{\infty}([R,\infty),r^{-1+\sqrt{5}}dr)
for
R
large enough, providing the existence of the desired function
\tilde{G}
satisfying the inequality in (
A.10
) for
k=0
. The estimates of higher order derivatives
k\geq 1
of
\tilde{G}
are obtained by induction from the above formula.

Construction of a particular corresponding solution to (
A.7
)
. We claim that for
G
given by (
A.10
) there exists a solution to (
A.7
) of the form

F(r)=r^{5-\sqrt{5}}+c^{\prime}r^{3-\sqrt{5}}+\tilde{F},\quad c^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R},\quad|\nabla^{k}\tilde{F}(r)|\lesssim r^{1-\sqrt{5}-k}\quad\mbox{as }r\to\infty.

(A.11)

Indeed, a direct computation shows

\displaystyle I=

\displaystyle-\left(\partial_{r}^{2}-\left(\frac{1}{r}+\frac{4r}{r^{2}+1}\right)\partial_{r}+\frac{8}{r^{2}+1}\right)\left(r^{5-\sqrt{5}}+c^{\prime}r^{3-\sqrt{5}}\right)+\frac{4}{r^{2}+1}(r\partial_{r}G-3G)

\displaystyle=(-12+4\sqrt{5}+6c^{\prime}-8+4\sqrt{5}(1-c))r^{1-\sqrt{5}}+O(r^{-1-\sqrt{5}}).

By the above identity and (
A.10
), choosing
c^{\prime}
such that
-12+4\sqrt{5}+6c^{\prime}-8+4\sqrt{5}(1-c)=0
yields
|\nabla^{k}I(r)|=O(r^{-1-\sqrt{5}-k})
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
as
r\to\infty
. A function of the form (
A.11
) then solves (
A.7
) if and only if
\tilde{F}
solves
\partial_{r}^{2}\tilde{F}-\left(\frac{1}{r}+\frac{4r}{r^{2}+1}\right)\partial_{r}\tilde{F}+\frac{8}{r^{2}+1}\tilde{F}=I
. Given the two particular solutions (
A.9
), a solution
\tilde{F}
is provided by the formula

\tilde{F}=\frac{r^{2}}{2}\int_{r}^{\infty}\frac{\rho^{4}+4\rho^{2}\log r-1}{\rho(\rho^{2}+1)^{2}}I(\rho)d\rho-\frac{r^{4}+4r^{2}\log r-1}{2}\int_{r}^{\infty}\frac{\rho}{(\rho^{2}+1)^{2}}I(\rho)d\rho.

The above identity gives the desired function
\tilde{F}
that satisfies the inequality in (
A.11
).

Construction of
(h_{1,4},g_{1,4})
. We let
h_{1,4}=\frac{1}{2r(r^{2}+1)^{3}}(F+G)
and
g_{1,4}=\frac{1}{16r(r^{2}+1)}(F-G)
. Then by (
A.10
) and (
A.11
) the function
h_{1,4}
satisfies the desired asymptotics
h_{1,4}(r)=r^{-\sqrt{5}-2}+O(r^{-4-\sqrt{5}})
as
\to\infty
. We also infer
g_{1,4}=c^{\prime\prime}r^{-\sqrt{5}}+O(r^{-\sqrt{5}-2})
. Since
g_{1,4}
solves
\partial_{r}^{2}g_{1,4}+\dfrac{\partial_{r}g_{1,4}}{r}-\dfrac{\ell^{2}}{r^{2}}g_{1,4}+h_{1,4}=0
we get
c^{\prime\prime}=-\frac{1}{4}
hence the desired result.

Step 2
.
Remaining identities
. The identity (
A.5
) is obtained in Proposition 3.5 in
[
SSV13
]
. This proposition also proves the identity (
A.6
) for
\ell\geq 2
, but with
(h_{\ell,4},g_{\ell,4})
replaced by
(\tilde{h}_{\ell,4},\tilde{g}_{\ell,4})
in the last row. Since
(h_{\ell,4},g_{\ell,4})=(\tilde{h}_{\ell,4},\tilde{g}_{\ell,4})-\frac{C_{\ell}\kappa_{\ell}}{\ell+1}(h_{\ell,2},g_{\ell,2})
, these determinants with
(h_{\ell,4},g_{\ell,4})
or
(\tilde{h}_{\ell,4},\tilde{g}_{\ell,4})
in the last row are actually equal, which shows (
A.6
).

There only remains to prove (
A.6
) for
\ell=1
. By a direct computation,
W_{1}
solves
W_{1}^{\prime}=-(1/r^{2}+4r/(1+r^{2}))W_{1}
so that
W_{1}=E_{1}/(r^{2}(1+r^{2})^{2})
for some
E_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
. We compute as
r\to\infty
using the asymptotics of
(h_{1,k},g_{1,k})
for
k=1,2,3,4
that
W_{1}(r)=-128\sqrt{5}K_{1}r^{-6}+o(r^{-8})
, and deduce
E_{1}=-128\sqrt{5}K_{1}
.
∎

Lemma A.3

(Variation of parameters formula for (
A.4
))
.

For
\ell\geq 1
the solution to the inhomogeneous system (
A.4
) is given by

\Big(\psi(r),\omega(r)\Big)=\sum_{k=1}^{4}\Big(h_{\ell,k}(r),g_{\ell,k}(r)\Big)(-1)^{k+1}\int\frac{f(s)W_{\ell,k}(s)}{W_{\ell}(s)}ds,

(A.12)

where

W_{\ell,k}(r)=\left|\begin{array}[]{ccc}h_{\ell,i}(r)&h_{\ell,j}(r)&h_{\ell,m}(r)\\
g_{\ell,i}(r)&g_{\ell,j}(r)&g_{\ell,m}(r)\\
g_{\ell,i}^{\prime}(r)&g_{\ell,j}^{\prime}(r)&g_{\ell,m}^{\prime}(r)\end{array}\right|,\quad i,j,m\in\{1,2,3,4\}\setminus\{k\},\;\;i<j<m.

(A.13)

We have the identity

W_{1,2}=\frac{1}{8}W_{1}r^{2}.

(A.14)

The function
W_{\ell,k}
admits the asymptotic behaviors:

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}W_{1,1}(r)\\
W_{1,2}(r)\\
W_{1,3}(r)\\
W_{1,4}(r)\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\frac{2(c_{1,4}-8c_{1,4}^{\prime})}{r^{2}}+O(1)\\
-64c_{1,4}^{\prime}+O(r^{2})\\
\frac{4(c_{1,4}-8c_{1,4}^{\prime})}{r^{2}}+O(1)\\
-64+O(r^{2})\end{array}\right),\quad\textup{as}\quad r\to 0,

(A.15)

and for
\ell\geq 2
,

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}W_{\ell,1}(r)\\
W_{\ell,2}(r)\\
W_{\ell,3}(r)\\
W_{\ell,4}(r)\end{array}\right)=-\frac{32\ell}{r}\left(\begin{array}[]{l}(\ell-1)r^{-\ell}+o(r^{-\ell})\\
(\ell+1)r^{\ell}+o(r^{\ell})\\
(\ell^{2}-1)r^{-\ell}+o(r^{-\ell})\\
(\ell^{2}-1)r^{\ell}+o(r^{\ell})\end{array}\right),\quad\textup{as}\quad r\to 0,

(A.16)

and as
r\to+\infty
,

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}W_{1,1}(r)\\
W_{1,2}(r)\\
W_{1,3}(r)\\
W_{1,4}(r)\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}-16K_{1}\sqrt{5}r^{-2}\Big[1+\mathcal{O}(r^{-(\sqrt{5}-1)})\Big]\\
-16K_{1}\sqrt{5}r^{-4}\Big[1+\mathcal{O}(r^{1-\sqrt{5}})\Big]\\
4r^{-\sqrt{5}-3}\Big[1+\mathcal{O}(r^{-2}\ln r)\Big]\\
64K_{1}r^{\sqrt{5}-3}\big[1+\mathcal{O}(r^{-4})\big]\end{array}\right),

(A.17)

and for
\ell\geq 2
,

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}W_{\ell,1}(r)\\
W_{\ell,2}(r)\\
W_{\ell,3}(r)\\
W_{\ell,4}(r)\end{array}\right)=-\frac{32\ell}{r^{3}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}4(\ell+1)r^{-\ell}\Big[1+\mathcal{O}(r^{\ell-\sqrt{\ell^{2}+4}})\Big]\\
4(\ell-1)r^{\ell}\Big[1+\mathcal{O}(r^{\ell-\sqrt{\ell^{2}+4}})\Big]\\
(\ell^{2}-1)C_{\ell}r^{-\sqrt{\ell^{2}+4}}\Big[1+\mathcal{O}(r^{\ell-\sqrt{\ell^{2}+4}})\Big]\\
(\ell^{2}-1)K_{\ell}r^{\sqrt{\ell^{2}+4}}\Big[1+\mathcal{O}(r^{\ell-\sqrt{\ell^{2}+4}})\Big]\end{array}\right),

(A.18)

where
C_{\ell},K_{\ell}
and
\kappa_{\ell}
are the constants introduced in Lemma
A.2
. These estimates propagate for higher order derivatives.

Proof.

The formula (
A.12
) can be found using Cramer’s formula to solve an ODE, what is done in the proof of Lemma 3.2. in
[
SSV13
]
. The asymptotic behavior of
W_{\ell,k}
is a direct computation using Lemma
A.2
, using that
\sqrt{\ell^{2}+4}-\ell<\ell+2-\sqrt{\ell^{2}+4}
for
\ell\geq 2
in order to estimate the
O()
’s.

There remains to prove the identity (
A.14
). Let
f
be a smooth compactly supported function of the form
f(x)=\mathsf{f}(r)\cos\theta
, such that
\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{W_{1,2}(r)}{W_{1}(r)}\mathsf{f}(r)dr=0
. Let then
(u,v)(x)=(\mathsf{u}(r)\cos\theta,\mathsf{v}(r)\cos\theta)
be given by
(\mathsf{u}(r),\mathsf{v}(r))=\sum_{k=1}^{4}\gamma_{1,k}(r)(h_{1,k}(r),g_{1,k}(r))
where

\displaystyle\gamma_{1,1}(r)=-\int_{r}^{\infty}\frac{W_{1,1}(s)}{W_{1}(s)}\mathsf{f}(s)ds,\qquad\gamma_{1,2}(r)=-\int_{0}^{r}\frac{W_{1,2}(s)}{W_{1}(s)}\mathsf{f}(s)ds,

\displaystyle\gamma_{1,3}(r)=-\int_{r}^{\infty}\frac{W_{1,3}(s)}{W_{1}(s)}\mathsf{f}(s)ds,\qquad\gamma_{1,k}(r)=-\int_{0}^{r}\frac{W_{1,4}(s)}{W_{1}(s)}\mathsf{f}(s)ds.

Then by (
A.18
) and (
A.15
)
(u,v)
is smooth, and by (
A.12
) it is a solution of
\mathscr{L}_{0}(u,v)=f
and
-\Delta v=u
. Since
f
is compactly supported and
\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{W_{1,2}(r)}{W_{1}(r)}\mathsf{f}(r)dr=0
, we have for all
r
large enough that
(\mathsf{u}(r),\mathsf{v}(r))=\left(-\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{W_{1,4}(s)}{W_{1}(s)}\mathsf{f}(s)ds\right)(h_{1,4}(r),g_{1,4}(r))
. We deduce from Lemma
A.2
that for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
there holds
|\nabla^{k}u(x)|\lesssim|x|^{-\sqrt{5}-2-k}
and
|\nabla^{k}v(x)|\lesssim|x|^{-\sqrt{5}-k}
for
|x|\geq 1
. By a uniqueness argument for the Laplace equation, this decay implies that
v=\Phi_{u}
. In particular, this implies that
u
solves
\mathscr{L}_{0}u=f
. We then compute using polar coordinates that on the one hand

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}y_{1}fdy=\pi\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{2}\mathsf{f}(r)dr.

On the other hand, by performing integration by parts, which is permitted by the decay of
u
and
\Phi_{u}=v
as
|x|\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}y_{1}fdy

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}y_{1}\mathscr{L}_{0}udy=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathscr{L}_{0}^{*}(y_{1})udy

where
\mathscr{L}_{0}^{*}\varepsilon=\mathscr{M}_{0}\nabla.(U\nabla\varepsilon)
. We recall that
\mathscr{L}_{0}^{*}(y_{1})=0
from
[
RS14a
]
, Lemma 2.4., so that

\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{2}\mathsf{f}(r)dr=0.

We have proved that any smooth compactly supported function
\mathsf{f}
that satisfies
\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{W_{1,2}(r)}{W_{1}(r)}\mathsf{f}(r)dr=0
must satisfy
\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{2}\mathsf{f}(r)dr=0
, which is only possible if
\frac{W_{1,2}}{W_{1}}=Cr^{2}
for some
C\in\mathbb{R}
. The asymptotic behaviours (
A.6
) and (
A.18
) of
W_{1}
and
W_{1,2}
as
r\to\infty
then imply
C=\frac{1}{8}
. The desired identity (
A.14
) follows.

∎

Appendix B
Estimates related to the Poisson field

We claim the following pointwise estimate of Poison field

Lemma B.1
.

Let
\gamma\in(1,2)
and
f\in L^{\infty}_{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, where

L^{\infty}_{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})=\{f\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2}),\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}=\|\langle x\rangle^{\gamma}f(x)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}<+\infty\}.

Then, it holds

\forall z\in\mathbb{R}^{2},\quad|\nabla\Phi_{f}(z)|\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\big(|z|^{-\sigma}+\langle z\rangle^{-\gamma}|z|\big),

for
\sigma\in(0,\gamma-1)
.

Proof.

The proof is straightforward from the convolution formula

\displaystyle|\nabla\Phi_{f}(z)|\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{|f(x)|}{|x-z|}dx

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{|x-z|\leq\frac{|z|}{2}}\frac{|f(x)|}{|x-z|}dx+\int_{|x-z|\geq\frac{|z|}{2}}\frac{|f(x)|}{|x-z|}dx.

Since
|x-z|\leq\frac{|z|}{2}
implies
\frac{|z|}{2}\leq|x|\leq\frac{3|z|}{2}
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{|x-z|\leq\frac{|z|}{2}}\frac{|f(x)|}{|x-z|}dx

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|f(z)\big\|_{L_{\gamma}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\int_{|x-z|\leq\frac{|z|}{2}}\frac{\langle x\rangle^{-\gamma}}{|x-z|}dx\lesssim\big\|f\big\|_{L_{\gamma}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\langle z\rangle^{-\gamma}|z|.

The second integral is estimated by Holder inequality,

\displaystyle\int_{|x-z|\geq\frac{|z|}{2}}\frac{|f(x)|}{|x-z|}dx

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|f\big\|_{L_{\gamma}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\int_{|x-z|\geq\frac{|z|}{2}}\frac{\langle x\rangle^{-\gamma}}{|x-z|}dx

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|f\big\|_{L_{\gamma}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\Big(\int_{|x-z|\geq\frac{|z|}{2}}|x-z|^{-p}dx\Big)^{\frac{1}{p}}\Big(\int_{|x-z|\geq\frac{|z|}{2}}\langle x\rangle^{-\gamma q}dx\Big)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|f\big\|_{L_{\gamma}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}|z|^{-\sigma},

where

\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}=1,\quad\gamma q-2>0,\quad\sigma=1-\frac{2}{p}\in(0,\gamma-1).

This concludes the proof of Lemma
B.1
.
∎

We Taylor expanse the Poison field from the other bubble in order to refine the expansion of the inner approximate eigenfunction.

Proof of Lemma
3.3
.

Recall that
\nabla\Phi_{U}(y)=-\frac{4y}{1+|y|^{2}}
and let
b=\pm 2a
, we write

\nabla\Phi_{U}(y\pm\frac{2a}{\nu})=-4\frac{y+\frac{b}{\nu}}{1+|y+\frac{b}{\nu}|^{2}}=\frac{-4\nu(b+\nu y)}{|b|^{2}+2\nu y.b+\nu^{2}(1+|y|^{2})}.

Let
A=2\nu y.b+\nu^{2}(1+|y|^{2})
, we expand

\frac{1}{|b|^{2}+A}=\frac{1}{|b|^{2}}\left(1-\frac{1}{|b|^{2}}A+\frac{1}{|b|^{4}}A^{2}-\frac{1}{|b|^{6}}A^{3}+O(\nu^{4}|y|^{4})\right),

where

\displaystyle A^{2}=4\nu^{2}(y.b)^{2}+4\nu^{3}(y.b)(1+|y|^{2})+O(\nu^{4}|y|^{4}),

\displaystyle A^{3}=8\nu^{3}(y.b)^{3}+O(\nu^{4}|y|^{4}).

Thus,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|b|^{2}+2\nu y.b+\nu^{2}(1+|y|^{2})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|b|^{2}}\left[1-\nu\frac{2y.b}{|b|^{2}}+\nu^{2}\left(4\frac{(y.b)^{2}}{|b|^{4}}-\frac{1+|y|^{2}}{|b|^{2}}\right)+\nu^{3}\left(\frac{4(y.b)(1+|y|^{2})}{|b|^{4}}-\frac{8(y.b)^{3}}{|b|^{6}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+O(\nu^{4}|y|^{4}),

and hence,

\displaystyle\nabla\Phi_{U}\big(y\pm\frac{2a}{\nu}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{-4\nu b-4\nu^{2}y}{|b|^{2}+2\nu y.b+\nu^{2}(1+|y|^{2})}

\displaystyle=-\frac{4\nu b}{|b|^{2}}+4\frac{\nu^{2}}{|b|^{2}}\left(\frac{2y.b}{|b|^{2}}b-y\right)+4\frac{\nu^{3}}{|b|^{3}}\left(\left(1+|y|^{2}-4\frac{(y.b)^{2}}{|b|^{2}}\right)\frac{b}{|b|}+\frac{2y.b}{|b|}y\right)

\displaystyle+4\frac{\nu^{4}}{|b|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{8(y.b)^{3}}{|b|^{3}}-\frac{4(y.b)(1+|y|^{2})}{|b|}\right)\frac{b}{|b|}+\left(1+|y|^{2}\right)y-4\frac{(y.b)^{2}}{|b|^{2}}y\right).

Recalling that
b=\pm 2a=(\pm 2|a|,0)
with
a^{2}=2/\beta
, we obtain

-4\nu\frac{b}{|b|^{2}}=\mp\beta\nu a,

and

\displaystyle\frac{2y.b}{|b|^{2}}b-y=2(y.(\pm 1,0))(\pm 1,0)-y=(2y_{1},0)-y=(y_{1},-y_{2}),

and

\displaystyle\left(1+|y|^{2}-4\frac{(y.b)^{2}}{|b|^{2}}\right)\frac{b}{|b|}+\frac{2y.b}{|b|}y

\displaystyle=\left(1+|y|^{2}-4y_{1}^{2}\right)(\pm 1,0)+2(y.(\pm 1,0))y

\displaystyle=\pm\left((1-3y_{1}^{2}+y_{2}^{2},0)+(2y_{1}^{2},2y_{1}y_{2})\right)

\displaystyle=\pm\left((1-y_{1}^{2}+y_{2}^{2},2y_{1}y_{2})\right),

and

\displaystyle\left(\frac{8(y.b)^{3}}{|b|^{3}}-\frac{4(y.b)(1+|y|^{2})}{|b|}\right)\frac{b}{|b|}+\left(1+|y|^{2}\right)y-4\frac{(y.b)^{2}}{|b|^{2}}y

\displaystyle=\left(8y_{1}^{3}-4y_{1}(1+|y|^{2})\right)(1,0)+\Big((1+|y|^{2})y_{1},(1+|y|^{2})y_{2}\Big)-4\big(y_{1}^{3},y_{1}^{2}y_{2}\big)

\displaystyle=(-3y_{1}-3y_{1}y_{2}^{2}+y_{1}^{3},y_{2}-3y_{1}^{2}y_{2}+y_{2}^{3}).

We substitute
b=\pm 2a
and arrive at

\displaystyle\nabla\Phi_{U}\big(y\pm\frac{2a}{\nu}\big)

\displaystyle=\mp\beta\nu a+\frac{\nu^{2}}{|a|^{2}}(y_{1},-y_{2})\pm\frac{\nu^{3}}{2|a|^{3}}\left((1-y_{1}^{2}+y_{2}^{2},2y_{1}y_{2})\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\nu^{4}}{4|a|^{4}}(-3y_{1}-3y_{1}y_{2}^{2}+y_{1}^{3},y_{2}-3y_{1}^{2}y_{2}+y_{2}^{3})+O(\nu^{5}|y|^{4}),

which concludes the proof of Lemma
3.3
.
∎

Appendix C
Functional analysis

Lemma C.1

(Hardy inequality in
H^{1}(\langle y\rangle^{4}dy)
)
.

There exists
C>0
such that for any
u\in H^{1}(U^{-1}dy)
one has
