\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}u^{2}\langle y\rangle^{2}dy\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\nabla u|^{2}\langle y\rangle^{4}dy.

(C.1)

Proof.

Consider the vector field
\phi(y)=(|y|^{3}+1)\frac{y}{|y|}
. A direct computation shows that it satisfies

|\phi|\lesssim\langle y\rangle^{3}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\nabla.\phi\leq c\langle y\rangle^{2}

(C.2)

for some
c>0
. For
u
a Schwartz function we have by integration by parts and (
C.2
):

-2\int u\nabla u.\phi=\int u^{2}\nabla.\phi\geq c\int\langle y\rangle^{2}u^{2}dy.

On the other hand, by Cauchy-Schwarz and (
C.2
):

\displaystyle-2\int u\nabla u.\phi

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int|\nabla u|^{2}|\phi|\langle y\rangle dy\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int|u|^{2}|\phi|\langle y\rangle^{-1}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\int|\nabla u|^{2}\langle y\rangle^{4}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int|u|^{2}\langle y\rangle^{2}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Combining, we get
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}u^{2}\langle y\rangle^{2}dy\leq C^{2}c^{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\nabla u|^{2}\langle y\rangle^{4}dy
as desired.
∎

Lemma C.2

(Poincaré inequality in
H^{1}(\langle y\rangle^{8}e^{-|y|^{2}/4}dy)
)
.

We have for all
u\in H^{1}(\langle y\rangle^{8}e^{-|y|^{2}/4}dy)
:

\int u^{2}\langle y\rangle^{10}e^{-|y|^{2}/4}dy\lesssim\int(u^{2}+|\nabla u|^{2})\langle y\rangle^{8}e^{-|y|^{2}/4}dy.

(C.3)

Proof.

An integration by parts shows that

\int u^{2}|y|^{10}e^{-|y|^{2}/4}dy=20\int u^{2}|y|^{8}e^{-|y|^{2}/4}dy+4\int u\nabla u.y|y|^{8}e^{-|y|^{2}/4}dy.

By Cauchy’ inequality we have
4u\nabla u.y|y|^{8}\leq u^{2}|y|^{10}/2+8|\nabla u|^{2}|y|^{8}
so we deduce from the above identity that

\int u^{2}|y|^{10}e^{-|y|^{2}/4}dy\leq\int(40u^{2}+16|\nabla u|^{2})|y|^{4}e^{-|y|^{2}/4}dy.

This implies (
C.3
).
∎

Lemma C.3

(Estimates for the Poisson field)
.

For any
\epsilon>0
, there exists
C_{\epsilon}>0
such that for any Schwartz function
u
we have

\int|\Phi_{u}|^{2}\langle y\rangle^{-2-\epsilon}dy\leq C_{\epsilon}\int u^{2}\langle y\rangle^{2+\epsilon}dy.

(C.4)

There exists
C>0
such that if
u
and
v
are Schwartz functions with
u
taking values in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
For any
\lambda>0
,

\left|\int\nabla.u\Phi_{v}dy\right|\leq C\left(\int|u|^{2}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int(\lambda+|y|)^{2}v^{2}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+C\int\frac{|u|}{\lambda+|y|}dy\int vdy.

(C.5)

Remark C.4
.

Note that we need
\epsilon>0
in both the left-hand side and right-hand side of the inequality (
C.4
). Indeed, since
|\Phi_{u}|
can have logarithmic growth at infinity, the integral
\int|\Phi_{u}|^{2}\langle y\rangle^{-2}dy
may diverge. For similar reasons, the quantity
\int_{|y|<1}|\Phi_{u}|^{2}dy
cannot be bounded by
(\int u^{2}\langle y\rangle^{2}dy)^{1/2}
.

The inequality (
C.5
) somehow corresponds to the case
\epsilon=0
in (
C.4
). This is permitted because of the divergence structure of the first term.

Proof.

Proof of (
C.4
)
. We perform a dyadic partition
1=\sum_{k\geq 0}\chi_{k}(y)
with
\chi_{0}
being supported on
\{|y|<2\}
and
\chi_{k}
being supported on
\{2^{k}<|y|<2^{k+2}\}
for
k\geq 1
, which are smooth functions such that
\nabla^{j}\chi_{k}\lesssim 2^{-jk}
. We decompose
f=\sum_{k}f_{k}
where
f_{k}=\chi_{k}f
. We have
\Phi_{u}=\sum_{k,l}(\Phi_{u_{k}})_{l}
. For
y
in the support of
(\Phi_{u_{k}})_{l}
we have
2^{l}<|y|<2^{l+2}
and we estimate by Cauchy-Schwarz

\displaystyle|(\Phi_{u_{k}})_{l}(y)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{2^{k}<|y^{\prime}|<2^{k+2}}|\ln|y-y^{\prime}||u_{k}(y^{\prime})dy^{\prime}\lesssim\|\ln|y-y^{\prime}|\|_{L^{2}(2^{k}<|y^{\prime}|<2^{k+2})}\|u_{k}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{k}\max(l,k)\|u_{k}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-k\epsilon}\max(l,k)\|u\langle y\rangle^{1+\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}}

Hence

\|(\Phi_{u_{k}})_{l}\langle y\rangle^{-1-\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-l\epsilon}\|(\Phi_{u_{k}})_{l}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-k\epsilon-l\epsilon}\max(l,k)\|u\langle y\rangle^{1+\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}}.

Therefore,

\|\Phi_{u}\langle y\rangle^{-1-\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\sum_{k,l}\|(\Phi_{u_{k}})_{l}\langle y\rangle^{-1-\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|u\langle y\rangle^{1+\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}}\sum_{k,l}2^{-k\epsilon-l\epsilon}\max(l,k)\leq C_{\epsilon}\|u\langle y\rangle^{1+\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}}

as desired.

Proof of (
C.5
)
. We decompose:

\int\nabla.u\Phi_{v}=\sum_{k<l-2}\int\nabla.u_{k}\Phi_{v_{l}}+\sum_{k>l+2}\int\nabla.u_{k}\Phi_{v_{l}}+\sum_{l-2\leq k\leq 2+2}\int\nabla.u_{k}\Phi_{v_{l}}.

If
k<l-2
we integrate by parts and bound by Hölder

\left|\int\nabla.u_{k}\Phi_{v_{l}}\right|=\left|\int u_{k}.\nabla\Phi_{v_{l}}\right|\lesssim\|u_{k}\|_{L^{1}}\|\nabla\Phi_{v_{l}}\|_{L^{\infty}(2^{k}<|y|<2^{k+2})}\lesssim 2^{k-l}\|\langle y\rangle^{-1}u_{k}\|_{L^{1}}\|v_{l}\|_{L^{1}}

(C.6)

where we used that for
2^{k}<|y|<2^{k+2}
there holds

|\nabla\Phi_{v_{l}}(y)|\lesssim\int_{2^{l}<|y^{\prime}|<2^{l+2}}|y-y^{\prime}|^{-1}|v_{l}(y^{\prime})|dy^{\prime}\lesssim 2^{-l}\|v_{l}\|_{L^{1}}.

If
k>l+2
we integrate by parts and bound similarly

\left|\int\nabla.u_{k}\Phi_{v_{l}}\right|=\left|\int u_{k}.\nabla\Phi_{v_{l}}\right|\lesssim\|u_{k}\|_{L^{1}}\|\nabla\Phi_{v_{l}}\|_{L^{\infty}(2^{k}<|y|<2^{k+2})}\lesssim\|\langle y\rangle^{-1}u_{k}\|_{L^{1}}\|v_{l}\|_{L^{1}}

(C.7)

where we used that for
2^{k}<|y|<2^{k+2}
there holds

|\nabla\Phi_{v_{l}}(y)|\lesssim\int_{2^{l}<|y^{\prime}|<2^{l+2}}|y-y^{\prime}|^{-1}|v_{l}(y^{\prime})|dy^{\prime}\lesssim 2^{-k}\|v_{l}\|_{L^{1}}

Therefore, after summation of (
C.6
) and (
C.7
) we get

\left|\sum_{k<l-2}\int\nabla.u_{k}\Phi_{v_{l}}\right|+\left|\sum_{k>l+2}\int\nabla.u_{k}\Phi_{v_{l}}\right|\lesssim\|\langle y\rangle^{-1}u\|_{L^{1}}\|v\|_{L^{1}}.

(C.8)

If
k=l+j
for
-2\leq j\leq 2
we rescale variables defining
z=2^{-k}y
and let
\tilde{u}_{k}(z)=u_{k}(y)
and
\tilde{v}_{l}(z)=v_{l}(y)
. Then

\displaystyle\Phi_{\tilde{v}_{l}}(z)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int\ln|z-z^{\prime}|v_{l}(2^{k}z^{\prime})dz^{\prime}=-\frac{1}{2\pi}2^{-2k}\int\ln|2^{-k}y-2^{-k}y^{\prime}|v_{l}(y^{\prime})dy^{\prime}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\pi}2^{-2k}\int(-k\ln 2+\ln|y-y^{\prime}|)v_{l}(y^{\prime})dy^{\prime}=\frac{k2^{-2k}}{2\pi}\int v_{l}+2^{-2k}\Phi_{v_{l}}(y).

Thus, changing variables and using that
\tilde{u}_{k}
has support in
\{|z|<4\}
,

\displaystyle\left|\int\nabla.u_{k}\Phi_{v_{l}}dy\right|

\displaystyle=2^{3k}\left|\int\nabla.\tilde{u}_{k}(z)\left(\Phi_{\tilde{v}_{l}}(z)-\frac{k}{2\pi}\int v_{l}\right)dz\right|\ =2^{3k}\left|\int\nabla.\tilde{u}_{k}(z)\Phi_{\tilde{v}_{l}}(z)\right|

\displaystyle=2^{3k}\left|\int\tilde{u}_{k}(z).\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{v}_{l}}(z)\right|\ \lesssim 2^{3k}\left|(\int|\tilde{u}_{k}(z)|^{2}dz\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{|z|<10}|\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{v}}(z)|^{2}dz\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

By Hölder,
\int_{|z|<10}|\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{v}}(z)|^{2}dz\lesssim\|\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{v}_{l}}\|_{L^{6}}^{2}
and
\|\tilde{v}_{l}\|_{L^{3/2}}\lesssim\|\tilde{v}_{l}\|_{L^{2}}
, where we used that
\tilde{v}_{l}
has support inside
\{|z|<16\}
. We have
\|\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{v}}\|_{L^{6}}\lesssim\|\nabla^{2}\Phi_{\tilde{v}_{l}}\|_{L^{3/2}}
by Sobolev. Since
-\Delta\Phi_{\tilde{v}}=\tilde{v}
, by elliptic regularity estimates we have
\|\nabla^{2}\Phi_{\tilde{v}_{l}}\|_{L^{3/2}}\lesssim\|\tilde{v}_{l}\|_{L^{3/2}}
. Combining, we get
\int_{|z|<10}|\nabla\Phi_{\tilde{v}}(z)|^{2}dz\lesssim\|\tilde{v}_{l}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}
. Using these inequality and changing back variables
z\mapsto y
we get

\left|\int\nabla.u_{k}\Phi_{v_{l}}dy\right|\lesssim 2^{3k}\|\tilde{u}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}\|\tilde{v}_{l}\|_{L^{2}}=2^{k}\|u_{k}\|_{L^{2}}\|v_{l}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|u_{k}\|_{L^{2}}\|\langle y\rangle v_{l}\|_{L^{2}}.

Therefore, by Cauchy-Schwarz,

\left|\sum_{l-2\leq k\leq 2+2}\int\nabla.u_{k}\Phi_{v_{l}}\right|\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}}\|\langle y\rangle v\|_{L^{2}}.

(C.9)

Combining (
C.8
) and (
C.9
) shows (
C.5
) with parameter
\lambda=1
. Let now
\lambda>0
. We change variables
z=\lambda^{-1}y
and define
\tilde{u}(z)=u(y)
and
\tilde{v}(z)=v(y)
. Then by (
C.5
) with parameter
\lambda=1
,

\displaystyle\left|\int\nabla.u\Phi_{v}dy\right|

\displaystyle=\lambda^{3}\left|\int\nabla.\tilde{u}(z)\Phi_{\tilde{v}}(z)dz\right|

\displaystyle\leq\lambda^{3}C\left(\int|\tilde{u}|^{2}dz\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int(1+|z|)^{2}\tilde{v}^{2}dz\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+C\lambda^{3}\int\frac{|\tilde{u}|}{1+|z|}dz\int|\tilde{v}|dz.

\displaystyle=C\left(\int|\tilde{u}|^{2}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int(\lambda+|y|)^{2}\tilde{v}^{2}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+C\int\frac{|\tilde{u}|}{\lambda+|y|}dy\int|\tilde{v}|dy.

This is the desired inequality (
C.5
).
∎
